# Fresh Translation Draft

Scope: Tobit-Psalms (19 books)

## Tobit

### Chapter 1

**Tobit 1:1**

Book of the words of Tobit son of Tobiel son of Ananiel son of Aduel son of Gabael, from the seed of Asiel, from the tribe of Naphtali,

**Tobit 1:2**

who was taken captive in the days of Enemessar, king of the Assyrians, from Thisbe, which is to the right of Kydios of Naphtali in Galilee above Asher. I, Tobit, walked in ways of truth and righteousness all the days of my life,

**Tobit 1:3**

and I did many acts of mercy for my brothers and for the nation, those who went before with me into the country of the Assyrians, to Nineveh.

**Tobit 1:4**

And when I was in my country, in the land of Israel, while I was younger, the whole tribe of Naphtali my father withdrew from the house of Jerusalem, which had been chosen from all the tribes of Israel for all the tribes to sacrifice there; and the temple of the dwelling of the Most High was sanctified and built for all generations of the age.

**Tobit 1:5**

And all the tribes that had withdrawn together sacrificed to Baal the heifer, and the house of Naphtali my father.

**Tobit 1:6**

And I alone went often to Jerusalem at the feasts, just as it has been written for all Israel by an eternal ordinance, having the firstfruits and the tithes of the produce and the first shearings.

**Tobit 1:7**

And I gave them to the priests, the sons of Aaron, toward the altar, from all the produce. The tithe I gave to the sons of Levi who minister in Jerusalem, and the second tithe I sold off, and I went and spent them in Jerusalem each year.

**Tobit 1:8**

And the third tithe I gave to those to whom it belongs, just as Deborah, the mother of my father, commanded, because I was left an orphan by my father.

**Tobit 1:9**

And when I became a man, I took Anna as wife from the seed of our father's house, and from her I fathered Tobias.

**Tobit 1:10**

And when we were taken captive to Nineveh, all my brothers and those from my race were eating from the breads of the nations;

**Tobit 1:11**

but I kept my soul from eating,

**Tobit 1:12**

because I remembered God with my whole soul.

**Tobit 1:13**

And the Most High gave favor and form before Enemessar, and I was his buyer;

**Tobit 1:14**

and I went to Media and deposited with Gabael, the brother of Gabrias, in Rages of Media, ten talents of silver.

**Tobit 1:15**

And when Enemessar died, Sennacherim his son reigned instead of him, and his ways were unsettled, and I was no longer able to go to Media.

**Tobit 1:16**

And in the days of Enemessar I was doing many acts of mercy for my brothers;

**Tobit 1:17**

I gave my breads to the hungry and garments to the naked, and if I saw anyone from my race dead and thrown behind the wall of Nineveh, I buried him.

**Tobit 1:18**

And if King Sennacherim killed anyone, when he came fleeing from Judea, I buried them by stealth; for he killed many in his wrath, and the bodies were sought by the king and were not found.

**Tobit 1:19**

But one of those in Nineveh went and informed the king about me, that I was burying them, and I hid myself; and knowing that I was being sought to die, I was afraid and withdrew.

**Tobit 1:20**

And all my possessions were plundered, and nothing was left to me except Anna my wife and Tobias my son.

**Tobit 1:21**

And fifty days did not pass until his two sons killed him and fled into the mountains of Ararat, and Sacherdonos his son reigned instead of him, and he appointed Achiacharos, Anael son of my brother, over all the accounting of his kingdom and over all the administration.

**Tobit 1:22**

And Achiacharos made request concerning me, and I came to Nineveh. Now Achiacharos was the cupbearer and over the ring and administrator and accountant, and Sacherdonos appointed him second; and he was my cousin.

### Chapter 2

**Tobit 2:1**

But when I came down to my house and Anna my wife was restored to me, and Tobias my son, at the feast of Pentecost, which is the holy feast of seven weeks, a good meal was prepared for me, and I reclined to eat.

**Tobit 2:2**

And I saw many dishes and said to my son, Go and bring whomever you may find of our brothers in need, who remembers the Lord, and behold, I am waiting for you.

**Tobit 2:3**

And when he came, he said, Father, one from our race has been strangled and thrown in the marketplace.

**Tobit 2:4**

And before I tasted anything, I leaped up and took him away into some room until the sun set.

**Tobit 2:5**

And returning, I washed and ate my bread in grief;

**Tobit 2:6**

and I remembered the prophecy of Amos, just as he said, Your feasts will be turned into mourning and all your gladnesses into lamentation,

**Tobit 2:7**

and I wept. And when the sun set, I went away and dug and buried him.

**Tobit 2:8**

And the neighbors kept laughing, saying, Does he no longer fear being murdered concerning this matter? He ran away, and behold, again he is burying the dead.

**Tobit 2:9**

And in that same night I returned after burying him and slept defiled by the wall of the courtyard, and my face was uncovered.

**Tobit 2:10**

And I did not know that sparrows were in the wall, and while my eyes were open, the sparrows defecated hot dung into my eyes, and white spots came to be in my eyes. And I went to physicians, and they did not help me; but Achiacharos fed me until I went to Elymais.

**Tobit 2:11**

And Anna my wife worked for wages in women's work;

**Tobit 2:12**

and she sent it to the owners, and they paid her the wage, adding also a young goat.

**Tobit 2:13**

But when it came to me, it began to cry out; and I said to her, From where is the young goat? Is it not stolen? Return it to the owners; for it is not lawful to eat what is stolen.

**Tobit 2:14**

But she said, A gift has been given to me in addition to the wage. And I did not believe her and kept telling her to return it to the owners, and I was ashamed before her. But answering, she said to me, Where are your acts of mercy and your righteousness? Behold, all things are known with you.

### Chapter 3

**Tobit 3:1**

And being grieved, I wept and prayed with anguish, saying,

**Tobit 3:2**

You are righteous, Lord, and all your works and all your ways are acts of mercy and truth, and you judge true and righteous judgment forever.

**Tobit 3:3**

Remember me and look upon me; do not take vengeance on me for my sins and my ignorances and those of my fathers, which they sinned before you;

**Tobit 3:4**

for they disobeyed your commandments, and you gave us to plunder and captivity and death and a parable of reproach to all the nations among whom we have been scattered.

**Tobit 3:5**

And now your judgments are many and true, to do with me concerning my sins and those of my fathers, because we did not do your commandments; for we did not walk in truth before you.

**Tobit 3:6**

And now do with me according to what is pleasing before you; command that my spirit be taken up, so that I may be released and become earth; because it is better for me to die than to live, because I have heard false reproaches, and much grief is in me. Command that I be released now from distress into the eternal place; do not turn your face away from me.

**Tobit 3:7**

On the same day it also happened to Sarah, daughter of Raguel, in Ecbatana of Media, to be reproached by her father's maidservants,

**Tobit 3:8**

because she had been given to seven men, and Asmodeus the evil demon killed them before they came to be with her as with women. And they said to her, Do you not understand that you are strangling your husbands? You have already had seven, and you were not named by one of them;

**Tobit 3:9**

why do you scourge us? If they died, go with them. May we not see son or daughter of yours forever.

**Tobit 3:10**

Having heard these things, she was grieved exceedingly, so as to hang herself. And she said, I am my father's only one; if I do this, it will be a reproach to him, and I will bring down his old age with anguish to Hades.

**Tobit 3:11**

And she prayed by the window and said, Blessed are you, Lord my God, and blessed is your holy and honorable name forever; may all your works bless you forever.

**Tobit 3:12**

And now, Lord, I have given my eyes and my face to you;

**Tobit 3:13**

say to release me from the earth and not hear reproach anymore.

**Tobit 3:14**

You know, Lord, that I am pure from every sin with a man,

**Tobit 3:15**

and I did not defile my name nor the name of my father in the land of my captivity. I am only-begotten to my father, and no child exists for him who will inherit him, nor a near brother, nor a son existing for him, for whom I should keep myself as wife. Already seven have perished for me; why should I live? And if it does not seem good to you to kill me, command to look upon me and have mercy on me, and for me no longer to hear reproach.

**Tobit 3:16**

And the prayer of both was heard before the glory of the great Raphael,

**Tobit 3:17**

and he was sent to heal the two: to peel away the white spots of Tobit and to give Sarah, daughter of Raguel, to Tobias son of Tobit as wife, and to bind Asmodeus the evil demon, because it falls to Tobias to inherit her. At that same time, Tobit returned and entered his house, and Sarah daughter of Raguel came down from her upper room.

### Chapter 4

**Tobit 4:1**

On that day Tobit remembered concerning the silver that he had deposited with Gabael in Rages of Media,

**Tobit 4:2**

and he said in himself, I asked for death; why do I not call Tobias my son, so that I may disclose it to him before I die?

**Tobit 4:3**

And calling him, he said, Child, if I die, bury me, and do not overlook your mother; honor her all the days of your life, and do what is pleasing to her, and do not grieve her.

**Tobit 4:4**

Remember, child, that she has seen many dangers over you in the womb; when she dies, bury her beside me in one tomb.

**Tobit 4:5**

All the days, child, remember the Lord our God, and do not desire to sin and transgress his commandments. Do righteousness all the days of your life, and do not walk in the ways of unrighteousness;

**Tobit 4:6**

because when you do the truth, there will be good success in your works and for all those who do righteousness.

**Tobit 4:7**

From the things that exist to you, do mercy, and let your eye not be grudging when you do mercy. Do not turn your face away from every poor person, and the face of God will certainly not be turned away from you.

**Tobit 4:8**

As things exist to you according to abundance, do mercy from them; if little exists to you, according to the little do not fear to do mercy.

**Tobit 4:9**

For you are treasuring a good deposit for yourself for a day of distress;

**Tobit 4:10**

because mercy rescues from death and does not allow one to enter into darkness;

**Tobit 4:11**

for mercy is a good gift to all those who do it before the Most High.

**Tobit 4:12**

Pay attention to yourself, child, from all sexual immorality, and first take a wife from the seed of your fathers. Do not take a foreign wife who is not from the tribe of your father, because we are sons of prophets. Noah, Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, our fathers from the age: remember, child, that all of them took wives from their brothers and were blessed in their children, and their seed will inherit land.

**Tobit 4:13**

And now, child, love your brothers, and do not become proud in your heart away from your brothers and the sons and daughters of your people, to take for yourself a wife from them; because in pride there is destruction and much disorder, and in worthlessness there is diminishment and great lack; for worthlessness is mother of famine.

**Tobit 4:14**

The wage of every person who works with you must not lodge overnight, but repay him immediately, and if you serve God, it will be repaid to you. Pay attention to yourself, child, in all your works, and be disciplined in all your conduct.

**Tobit 4:15**

And what you hate, do to no one. Do not drink wine to drunkenness, and let drunkenness not go with you on your way.

**Tobit 4:16**

From your bread give to the hungry, and from your garments to the naked; everything, whatever abounds to you, do as mercy, and let your eye not be grudging when you do mercy.

**Tobit 4:17**

Pour out your breads upon the tomb of the righteous, and do not give to sinners.

**Tobit 4:18**

Seek counsel from every prudent person, and do not despise any useful counsel.

**Tobit 4:19**

And at every time bless the Lord God, and ask from him that your ways may become straight, and all your paths and counsels may prosper; because not every nation has counsel, but the Lord himself gives all good things, and whom he wills he humbles, just as he wills. And now, child, remember my commandments, and let them not be blotted out from your heart.

**Tobit 4:20**

And now I disclose to you the ten talents of silver that I deposited with Gabael son of Gabrias in Rages of Media.

**Tobit 4:21**

And do not fear, child, because we became poor; many things exist for you if you fear God and depart from every sin and do what is pleasing before him.

### Chapter 5

**Tobit 5:1**

And answering, Tobias said to him, Father, I will do everything, as much as you have commanded me;

**Tobit 5:2**

but how will I be able to receive the silver, since I do not know him?

**Tobit 5:3**

And he gave him the handwritten document and said to him, Seek for yourself a man who will travel with you, and I will give him wages as long as I live; and go receive the silver.

**Tobit 5:4**

And he went to seek a man and found Raphael, who was an angel, and he did not know.

**Tobit 5:5**

And he said to him, Can I go with you to Rages of Media, and are you experienced with the places?

**Tobit 5:6**

And the angel said to him, I will go with you, and I am experienced with the way, and I lodged with Gabael our brother.

**Tobit 5:7**

And Tobias said to him, Wait for me, and I will tell my father.

**Tobit 5:8**

And he said to him, Go, and do not delay.

**Tobit 5:9**

And entering, he said to his father, Behold, I have found one who will travel with me. But he said, Call him to me, so that I may know of what tribe he is and whether he is trustworthy to go with you.

**Tobit 5:10**

And he called him, and he entered, and they greeted one another.

**Tobit 5:11**

And Tobit said to him, Brother, from what tribe and from what father's house are you? Disclose it to me.

**Tobit 5:12**

And he said to him, Are you seeking tribe and father's house, or a hired man who will travel with your son? And Tobit said to him, I wish, brother, to know your race and name.

**Tobit 5:13**

And he said, I am Azarias, son of Ananias the Great, from your brothers.

**Tobit 5:14**

And he said to him, May you come in health, brother, and do not be angry with me because I sought to know your tribe and your father's house. And you happen to be my brother from the beautiful and good generation; for I knew Ananias and Jonathan, the sons of Semei the Great, as we were going together to Jerusalem to worship, carrying up the firstborn and the tithes of the produce, and they were not led astray in the error of our brothers. You are from a good root, brother,

**Tobit 5:15**

but tell me what wage I am to give you: a drachma per day and the things necessary for you, as also for my son.

**Tobit 5:16**

And I will still add to your wage if you return in health.

**Tobit 5:17**

And they were pleased thus. And he said to Tobias, Become ready for the way, and may you be prospered. And his son prepared the things for the way. And his father said to him, Go with this man, and God who dwells in heaven will prosper your way, and may his angel travel with you. And both went out to depart, and the young man's dog with them.

**Tobit 5:18**

But Anna his mother wept and said to Tobit, Why have you sent away our child? Is he not the staff of our hand as he goes in and goes out before us?

**Tobit 5:19**

Do not let silver overtake silver, but let it become refuse for our child;

**Tobit 5:20**

for as it has been given to us to live by the Lord, this exists as enough for us.

**Tobit 5:21**

And Tobit said to her, Do not have a word, sister; he will come in health, and your eyes will see him;

**Tobit 5:22**

for a good angel will travel with him, and his way will be prospered, and he will return in health. And she stopped crying.

### Chapter 6

**Tobit 6:1**

And those traveling on the way came in the evening to the Tigris River, and they lodged there.

**Tobit 6:2**

And the young man went down to wash himself, and a fish leaped up from the river and wanted to swallow the young man.

**Tobit 6:3**

But the angel said to him, Take hold of the fish. And the young man seized the fish and threw it up onto the land.

**Tobit 6:4**

And the angel said to him, Cut open the fish, and taking the heart and the liver and the gall, set them safely aside.

**Tobit 6:5**

And the young man did just as the angel said to him, and roasting the fish, they ate.

**Tobit 6:6**

And both traveled until they drew near to Ecbatana.

**Tobit 6:7**

And the young man said to the angel, Azarias, brother, what is the heart and the liver and the gall of the fish?

**Tobit 6:8**

And he said to him, The heart and the liver: if a demon or evil spirit troubles anyone, these must be smoked before a man or woman, and he will certainly no longer be troubled.

**Tobit 6:9**

And the gall: anoint a person who has white spots in the eyes, and he will be healed.

**Tobit 6:10**

And as they drew near to Rages,

**Tobit 6:11**

the angel said to the young man, Brother, today we will lodge with Raguel, and he is your kinsman, and he has a daughter named Sarah;

**Tobit 6:12**

I will speak concerning her, that she be given to you as wife, because her inheritance falls to you, and you alone are from her race, and the girl is beautiful and prudent.

**Tobit 6:13**

And now listen to me, and I will speak to her father; and when we return from Rages, we will make the marriage; because I know Raguel, that he will certainly not give her to another man according to the law of Moses, or he will be liable to death, because it belongs to you to receive the inheritance rather than any person.

**Tobit 6:14**

Then the young man said to the angel, Azarias, brother, I have heard that the girl has been given to seven men, and that all perished in the bridal chamber.

**Tobit 6:15**

And now I am only one to my father, and I fear that entering in I may die just as the former ones also did, because a demon loves her, which wrongs no one except those who approach her. And now I fear that I may die and bring down the life of my father and my mother with anguish over me into their tomb; and no other son exists for them who will bury them.

**Tobit 6:16**

But the angel said to him, Do you not remember the words that your father commanded you, concerning taking for yourself a wife from your race? And now listen to me, brother, because she will be yours as wife, and have no word concerning the demon, because this night she will be given to you as wife.

**Tobit 6:17**

And when you enter into the bridal chamber, you will take ashes of incense and put some of the heart and liver of the fish on them and smoke it,

**Tobit 6:18**

and the demon will smell it and flee and will not come back forever and ever. But when you go near to her, both rise and cry to the merciful God, and he will save you and have mercy. Do not fear, because she was prepared for you from the age, and you will save her, and she will go with you, and I suppose that children will be yours from her.

**Tobit 6:19**

And when Tobias heard these things, he loved her, and his soul was joined exceedingly to her.

### Chapter 7

**Tobit 7:1**

And they came to Ecbatana and arrived at the house of Raguel. And Sarah met them and greeted them, and they greeted her, and she brought them into the house.

**Tobit 7:2**

And Raguel said to Edna his wife, How like the young man is to Tobit my cousin!

**Tobit 7:3**

And Raguel asked them, From where are you, brothers? And they said to him, From the sons of Naphtali, those captive from Nineveh.

**Tobit 7:4**

And he said to them, Do you know Tobit our brother? And they said, We know him. And he said to them, Is he in health?

**Tobit 7:5**

And they said, He both lives and is in health. And Tobias said, He is my father.

**Tobit 7:6**

And Raguel leaped up and kissed him and wept,

**Tobit 7:7**

and blessed him and said to him, Son of the beautiful and good man. And hearing that Tobit had lost his own eyes, he was grieved and wept.

**Tobit 7:8**

And Edna his wife and Sarah his daughter wept, and they received them eagerly;

**Tobit 7:9**

and they sacrificed a ram of the sheep and set before them many dishes. But Tobias said to Raphael, Azarias, brother, speak concerning the things you were saying on the journey, and let the matter be completed.

**Tobit 7:10**

And he shared the word with Raguel; and Raguel said to Tobias, Eat, drink, and become glad, for it belongs to you to take my child. But I will disclose the truth to you.

**Tobit 7:11**

I gave my child to seven men, and whenever they went in to her, they died during the night. But for the present, become glad. And Tobias said, I will taste nothing here until you set the matter and stand with me. And Raguel said, Receive her from now according to the judgment. You are her brother, and she is yours; and may the merciful God prosper the most beautiful things for you.

**Tobit 7:12**

And he called Sarah his daughter, and taking her by the hand, he delivered her to Tobias as wife and said, Behold, according to the law of Moses, receive her and lead her away to your father. And he blessed them.

**Tobit 7:13**

And he called Edna his wife; and taking a book, he wrote a contract and sealed it.

**Tobit 7:14**

And they began to eat.

**Tobit 7:15**

And Raguel called Edna his wife and said to her, Sister, prepare the other chamber and bring her in.

**Tobit 7:16**

And she did as he said and brought her in there, and she wept; and she received the tears of her daughter and said to her,

**Tobit 7:17**

Take courage, child; may the Lord of heaven and earth give you favor in place of this grief of yours. Take courage, daughter.

### Chapter 8

**Tobit 8:1**

And when they finished dining, they brought Tobias in to her.

**Tobit 8:2**

And as he was going, he remembered the words of Raphael and took the ashes of incense and put the heart of the fish and the liver on it, and smoked it.

**Tobit 8:3**

And when the demon smelled the odor, it fled into the uppermost parts of Egypt, and the angel bound it.

**Tobit 8:4**

And when both had been shut in together, Tobias rose from the bed and said, Rise up, sister, and let us pray, so that the Lord may have mercy on us.

**Tobit 8:5**

And Tobias began to say, Blessed are you, the God of our fathers, and blessed is your holy and glorious name forever. Let the heavens and all your created things bless you.

**Tobit 8:6**

You made Adam and gave him Eve as a helper, his wife, as support. From these the seed of humans was born. You said, It is not good for the human to be alone; let us make for him a helper like him.

**Tobit 8:7**

And now, Lord, not because of fornication do I take this my sister, but in truth. Give command to have mercy on me and to grow old together with her.

**Tobit 8:8**

And she said with him, Amen.

**Tobit 8:9**

And they both slept the night.

**Tobit 8:10**

And Raguel, rising, went and dug a tomb, saying, Lest this one also die?

**Tobit 8:11**

And Raguel came to his own house,

**Tobit 8:12**

and said to Edna his wife, Send one of the maidservants, and let them see whether he lives; but if not, so that we may bury him and no one may know.

**Tobit 8:13**

And the maidservant entered, having opened the door, and found the two sleeping.

**Tobit 8:14**

And going out, she reported to them that he lives.

**Tobit 8:15**

And Raguel blessed God, saying, Blessed are you, O God, with every pure and holy blessing, and let your holy ones and all your created things bless you, and let all your angels and your elect bless you forever.

**Tobit 8:16**

Blessed are you because you gladdened me, and it did not happen to me as I suspected, but according to your great mercy you acted with us.

**Tobit 8:17**

Blessed are you because you had mercy on two only children. Do mercy for them, Master; complete their life in health with gladness and mercy.

**Tobit 8:18**

And he commanded the servants to fill in the tomb.

**Tobit 8:19**

And he made a wedding for them of fourteen days.

**Tobit 8:20**

And Raguel said to him with an oath, before the days of the wedding were completed, that he should not go out unless the fourteen days of the wedding should be fulfilled.

**Tobit 8:21**

And then, having taken half of his possessions, he was to go in health to his father; and the rest, when I and my wife die.

### Chapter 9

**Tobit 9:1**

And Tobias called Raphael and said to him,

**Tobit 9:2**

Azarias, brother, take with yourself a servant and two camels, and go to Rages of Media, to Gabael, and bring me the silver, and bring him to me to the wedding;

**Tobit 9:3**

because Raguel swore that I should not go out,

**Tobit 9:4**

and my father counts the days, and if I delay greatly, he will be grieved exceedingly.

**Tobit 9:5**

And Raphael went and lodged with Gabael, and he gave him the handwritten document. And he brought out the bags with the seals and gave them to him.

**Tobit 9:6**

And they rose early together and came to the wedding. And Tobias blessed his wife.

### Chapter 10

**Tobit 10:1**

And Tobit his father was reckoning each day; and when the days of the journey were fulfilled and they were not coming,

**Tobit 10:2**

he said, Perhaps they have been put to shame? Or perhaps Gabael has died, and no one gives him the silver?

**Tobit 10:3**

And he was grieved exceedingly.

**Tobit 10:4**

And his wife said to him, The child has perished, because he has delayed; and she began to mourn him and said,

**Tobit 10:5**

I care for nothing, child, because I let you go, the light of my eyes.

**Tobit 10:6**

And Tobit says to her, Be silent; have no word; he is in health.

**Tobit 10:7**

And she said to him, Be silent; do not deceive me; my child has perished. And she went every day outside to the road by which he had gone away, and by day she ate no bread, and by night she did not cease mourning Tobias her son, until the fourteen days of the wedding were completed, which Raguel swore to make him there. And Tobias said to Raguel, Send me away, because my father and my mother no longer hope to see me.

**Tobit 10:8**

But his father-in-law said to him, Stay with me, and I will send to your father, and they will report to him the things concerning you.

**Tobit 10:9**

And Tobias says, Send me away to my father.

**Tobit 10:10**

And Raguel, rising, gave him Sarah his wife and half of his possessions: servants and livestock and silver,

**Tobit 10:11**

and blessing them, he sent them away, saying, The God of heaven will prosper you, children, before I die.

**Tobit 10:12**

And he said to his daughter, Honor your parents-in-law; they are now your parents. May I hear a good report of you. And he kissed her. And Edna said to Tobias, Beloved brother, may the Lord of heaven restore you, and may he grant me to see children of yours from Sarah my daughter, so that I may be gladdened before the Lord. And behold, I entrust my daughter to you as a deposit; do not grieve her.

**Tobit 10:13**

After these things Tobias also went, blessing God because he prospered his way, and he blessed Raguel and Edna his wife.

### Chapter 11

**Tobit 11:1**

And they went until they drew near to Nineveh. And Raphael said to Tobias, Do you not know, brother, how you left your father?

**Tobit 11:2**

Let us run ahead before your wife and prepare the house;

**Tobit 11:3**

but take the gall of the fish in hand. And they went, and the dog went along behind them.

**Tobit 11:4**

And Anna was sitting, looking around toward the road for her child;

**Tobit 11:5**

and she noticed him coming and said to his father, Behold, my son is coming, and the man who went with him.

**Tobit 11:6**

And Raphael said, I know that your father will open the eyes.

**Tobit 11:7**

You anoint the gall into his eyes, and being stung, he will rub and throw off the white spots, and he will see you.

**Tobit 11:8**

And Anna ran up and fell upon the neck of her son and said to him, I have seen you, child; from now I will die. And they both wept.

**Tobit 11:9**

And Tobit was going out toward the door and was stumbling, but his son ran to him

**Tobit 11:10**

and took hold of his father and applied the gall upon the eyes of his father, saying, Take courage, father.

**Tobit 11:11**

And when they were stung, he rubbed his eyes, and the white spots were scaled off from the corners of his eyes.

**Tobit 11:12**

And seeing his son, he fell upon his neck

**Tobit 11:13**

and wept and said, Blessed are you, O God, and blessed is your name forever, and blessed are all your holy angels; because you scourged me and had mercy on me. Behold, I see Tobias my son.

**Tobit 11:14**

And his son entered rejoicing and reported to his father the great things that happened to him in Media.

**Tobit 11:15**

And Tobit went out to meet his daughter-in-law, rejoicing and blessing God, at the gate of Nineveh. And those seeing him going were marveling, because he saw.

**Tobit 11:16**

And Tobit was confessing before him that God had mercy on them. And when Tobit drew near to Sarah his daughter-in-law, he blessed her, saying, May you come in health, daughter. Blessed be God, who brought you to us, and your father and your mother. And joy came to all his brothers in Nineveh.

**Tobit 11:17**

And Achiacharos and Nasbas his cousin came,

**Tobit 11:18**

and the wedding of Tobias was held with gladness seven days.

### Chapter 12

**Tobit 12:1**

And Tobit called Tobias his son and said to him, See, child, give wages to the man who went with you, and it is necessary to add for him.

**Tobit 12:2**

And he said, Father, I am not harmed by giving him half of what I have brought,

**Tobit 12:3**

because he brought me to you in health and healed my wife and brought my silver and likewise healed you.

**Tobit 12:4**

And the elder said, It is due to him.

**Tobit 12:5**

And he called the angel and said to him, Take half of all that you have brought, and go in health.

**Tobit 12:6**

Then, having called the two privately, he said to them, Bless God, and confess to him, and give greatness to him, and confess to him before all the living concerning what he did with you. It is good to bless God and exalt his name, honorably showing the words of the works of God, and do not hesitate to confess to him.

**Tobit 12:7**

A king's mystery is good to hide, but the works of God to reveal gloriously. Do good, and evil will not find you.

**Tobit 12:8**

Good is prayer with fasting and almsgiving and righteousness; good is little with righteousness rather than much with injustice. It is good to make almsgiving rather than to treasure up gold;

**Tobit 12:9**

for almsgiving rescues from death, and it will cleanse every sin. Those doing almsgivings and righteousnesses will be filled with life,

**Tobit 12:10**

but those sinning are enemies of their own life.

**Tobit 12:11**

I will certainly not hide any word from you. I have indeed said, A king's mystery is good to hide, but the works of God to reveal gloriously.

**Tobit 12:12**

And now, when you and your daughter-in-law Sarah prayed, I brought the memorial of your prayer before the Holy One; and when you buried the dead, likewise I was present with you.

**Tobit 12:13**

And when you did not hesitate to rise and leave your meal, so that going you might wrap the dead, you did not escape my notice doing good, but I was with you.

**Tobit 12:14**

And now God sent me to heal you and your daughter-in-law Sarah.

**Tobit 12:15**

I am Raphael, one of the seven holy angels who bring up the prayers of the holy ones and enter before the glory of the Holy One.

**Tobit 12:16**

And the two were troubled and fell upon their face, because they feared.

**Tobit 12:17**

And he said to them, Do not fear; peace shall be to you. But bless God forever,

**Tobit 12:18**

because not by my own favor, but by the will of our God, I came; therefore bless him forever.

**Tobit 12:19**

All the days I appeared to you, and I did not eat nor drink, but you were beholding a vision.

**Tobit 12:20**

And now confess to God, because I am ascending to the one who sent me, and write all the things completed in a book.

**Tobit 12:21**

And they rose up, and no longer saw him.

**Tobit 12:22**

And they confessed his great and wondrous works, and how the angel of the Lord appeared to them.

### Chapter 13

**Tobit 13:1**

And Tobit wrote a prayer for rejoicing and said, Blessed be the living God forever, and his kingdom,

**Tobit 13:2**

because he scourges and has mercy, brings down to Hades and brings up, and there is no one who will escape his hand.

**Tobit 13:3**

Confess to him, sons of Israel, before the nations, because he scattered us among them;

**Tobit 13:4**

there show his greatness; exalt him before every living one, because he is our Lord and God; he is our Father for all the ages.

**Tobit 13:5**

And he will scourge us in our injustices and again have mercy and gather us from all the nations, wherever you may be scattered among them.

**Tobit 13:6**

If you return to him with all your heart and with all your soul, to do truth before him, then he will return to you and will certainly not hide his face from you. And behold what he will do with you, and confess to him with all your mouth; and bless the Lord of righteousness and exalt the King of the ages. I, in the land of my captivity, confess to him and show his strength and his greatness to a nation of sinners. Turn back, sinners, and do righteousness before him; who knows whether he will desire you and do mercy for you?

**Tobit 13:7**

I will exalt my God, and my soul will exalt the King of heaven and will rejoice in his greatness.

**Tobit 13:8**

Let all speak and confess to him in Jerusalem:

**Tobit 13:9**

Jerusalem, holy city, he will scourge you for the works of your sons and again have mercy on the sons of the righteous.

**Tobit 13:10**

Confess to the Lord well and bless the King of the ages, so that again his tabernacle may be built in you with joy, and he may gladden the captives in you and love the afflicted in you for all the generations of the age.

**Tobit 13:11**

Many nations from far away will come to the name of the Lord God, having gifts in their hands and gifts for the King of heaven; generations of generations will give you rejoicing.

**Tobit 13:12**

Accursed are all those hating you; blessed shall be all those loving you forever.

**Tobit 13:13**

Rejoice and exult over the sons of the righteous, because they will be gathered and will bless the Lord of the righteous.

**Tobit 13:14**

O blessed are those loving you; they will rejoice over your peace. Blessed are as many as were grieved over all your scourges, because over you they will rejoice, having seen all your glory, and they will be gladdened forever.

**Tobit 13:15**

Let my soul bless God, the great King,

**Tobit 13:16**

because Jerusalem will be built with sapphire and emerald and precious stone; your walls and towers and battlements with pure gold,

**Tobit 13:17**

and the broad places of Jerusalem will be paved with beryl and carbuncle and stone from Souphir.

**Tobit 13:18**

And all her streets will say, Alleluia, and they will praise, saying, Blessed be God, who exalted her for all the ages.

### Chapter 14

**Tobit 14:1**

And Tobit ceased confessing.

**Tobit 14:2**

And he was fifty-eight years old when he lost his sight, and after eight years he looked up again. And he did almsgivings and continued to fear the Lord God and confessed to him.

**Tobit 14:3**

And he grew very old; and he called his son and his sons and said to him, Child, take your sons; behold, I have grown old and am near to departing from life.

**Tobit 14:4**

Go away into Media, child, because I am persuaded of as many things as Jonah the prophet spoke concerning Nineveh, that it will be overthrown; but in Media there will be peace rather, until a time, and our brothers in the land will be scattered from the good land, and Jerusalem will be desolate, and the house of God in it will be burned down and will be desolate until a time.

**Tobit 14:5**

And again God will have mercy on them and return them to the land, and they will build the house, not like the former one, until the times of the age are fulfilled. And after these things they will return from the captivities and build Jerusalem honorably, and the house of God in it will be built for all the generations of the age with a glorious building, just as the prophets spoke concerning it.

**Tobit 14:6**

And all the nations will return truly to fear the Lord God and will bury their idols, and all the nations will bless the Lord.

**Tobit 14:7**

And his people will confess to God, and the Lord will exalt his people, and all those loving the Lord God in truth and righteousness will rejoice, doing mercy to our brothers.

**Tobit 14:8**

And now, child, go away from Nineveh, because certainly the things that Jonah the prophet spoke will be.

**Tobit 14:9**

But you, keep the law and the commandments, and become mercy-loving and righteous, so that it may be well with you. And bury me well, and your mother with me, and no longer lodge in Nineveh.

**Tobit 14:10**

Child, see what Aman did to Achiacharos, the one who raised him, how he led him from the light into the darkness, and how much he repaid him. And Achiacharos was saved, but to that one repayment was given, and he himself went down into the darkness. Manasseh did almsgiving and was saved from a snare of death which he had set for him, but Aman fell into the snare and perished.

**Tobit 14:11**

And now, children, see what almsgiving does, and what righteousness rescues. And as he was saying these things, his soul failed upon the bed; and he was 158 years old, and they buried him gloriously.

**Tobit 14:12**

And when Anna died, he buried her with his father, and Tobias went away with his wife and his sons to Ecbatana, to Raguel his father-in-law,

**Tobit 14:13**

and he grew old honorably and buried his parents-in-law gloriously and inherited their substance and that of Tobit his father.

**Tobit 14:14**

And he died at 127 years in Ecbatana of Media.

**Tobit 14:15**

And before he died, he heard of the destruction of Nineveh, which Nebuchadnezzar and Asueros captured; and before dying he rejoiced over Nineveh.

## Judith

### Chapter 1

**Judith 1:1**

In the twelfth year of the reign of Nebuchadnezzar, who reigned over the Assyrians in Nineveh, the great city, in the days of Arphaxad, who reigned over the Medes in Ecbatana,

**Judith 1:2**

and he built walls around Ecbatana from hewn stones, three cubits in width and six cubits in length, and made the height of the wall seventy cubits and its width fifty cubits,

**Judith 1:3**

and he set its towers upon its gates, one hundred cubits, and founded its width at sixty cubits,

**Judith 1:4**

and he made its gates, gates rising to a height of seventy cubits and their width forty cubits, for exits of his mighty forces and formations of his foot soldiers.

**Judith 1:5**

And in those days King Nebuchadnezzar made war against King Arphaxad in the great plain, that is, in the borders of Ragau.

**Judith 1:6**

And all those dwelling in the hill country met with him, and all those dwelling by the Euphrates and the Tigris and the Hydaspes, and the plains of Arioch king of the Elymaeans; and very many nations assembled into the battle line of the sons of Cheleoud.

**Judith 1:7**

And Nebuchadnezzar king of the Assyrians sent to all those dwelling in Persis and to all those dwelling toward the west, those dwelling in Cilicia and Damascus, Lebanon and Anti-Lebanon, and all those dwelling along the face of the coastland,

**Judith 1:8**

and those among the nations of Carmel and Gilead and upper Galilee and the great plain of Esdrelon,

**Judith 1:9**

and all those in Samaria and its cities and beyond the Jordan as far as Jerusalem and Betane and Chellous and Kadesh and the river of Egypt and Taphnas and Ramesses and all the land of Gesem,

**Judith 1:10**

until coming above Tanis and Memphis, and all those dwelling in Egypt until coming to the borders of Ethiopia.

**Judith 1:11**

And all those dwelling in all the land treated lightly the word of Nebuchadnezzar king of the Assyrians and did not assemble with him for war, because they did not fear him, but he was before them as one man; and they turned back his messengers empty, with dishonor before their face.

**Judith 1:12**

And Nebuchadnezzar was exceedingly angry over all this land and swore by his throne and his kingdom that he would surely take vengeance on all the borders of Cilicia and Damascus and Syria, to destroy with his sword all those dwelling in the land of Moab and the sons of Ammon and all Judea and all those in Egypt until coming to the borders of the two seas.

**Judith 1:13**

And he arrayed himself with his force against King Arphaxad in the seventeenth year, and he grew strong in his war and turned back all the force of Arphaxad and all his cavalry and all his chariots,

**Judith 1:14**

and he became lord of his cities and came as far as Ecbatana and seized the towers and plundered its streets and set its adornment as its reproach.

**Judith 1:15**

And he took Arphaxad in the mountains of Ragau and pierced him with his javelins and utterly destroyed him until that day.

**Judith 1:16**

And he returned with them, he and all his mixed multitude, a very great multitude of warrior men; and he was there taking ease and feasting, he and his force, for 120 days.

### Chapter 2

**Judith 2:1**

And in the eighteenth year, on the twenty-second day of the first month, word came in the house of Nebuchadnezzar king of the Assyrians to take vengeance on all the land, just as he had spoken.

**Judith 2:2**

And he called together all his attendants and all his great ones, and set with them the mystery of his counsel, and completed all the evil of the land from his mouth.

**Judith 2:3**

And they judged to utterly destroy all flesh, whoever had not followed the word of his mouth.

**Judith 2:4**

And it happened, when he completed his counsel, Nebuchadnezzar king of the Assyrians called Holofernes, chief general of his force, being second after him, and said to him,

**Judith 2:5**

Thus says the great king, the lord of all the earth: Behold, you shall go out from my face and take with yourself men trusting in their strength, 120,000 foot soldiers and a multitude of horses with riders, twelve myriads,

**Judith 2:6**

and you shall go out to meet all the land toward the west, because they disobeyed the word of my mouth.

**Judith 2:7**

And you shall report to them to prepare earth and water, because I will go out in my wrath against them and cover all the face of the earth with the feet of my force, and I will give them to them for plunder;

**Judith 2:8**

and their wounded will fill their ravines and their torrents, and an overflowing river will be filled with their dead;

**Judith 2:9**

and I will lead their captivity to the extremities of all the earth.

**Judith 2:10**

But you, going out, shall seize beforehand for me every border of theirs, and they will give themselves up to you, and you shall keep them for me until the day of their reproof;

**Judith 2:11**

but upon those disobeying, your eye shall not spare to give them to slaughter and plunder in all your land.

**Judith 2:12**

For as I live, and as the might of my kingdom lives, I have spoken, and I will do these things by my hand.

**Judith 2:13**

And you also shall not transgress one thing of the words of your lord, but completing, you shall complete just as I have commanded you, and you shall not delay to do them.

**Judith 2:14**

And Holofernes went out from before his lord and called all the rulers and the generals and overseers of the force of Assur,

**Judith 2:15**

and he numbered chosen men for battle line, just as his lord commanded him, to twelve myriads, and horsemen archers, twelve thousand,

**Judith 2:16**

and he arranged them in the manner that a multitude for war is arranged.

**Judith 2:17**

And he took camels and donkeys and mules for their equipment, an exceedingly great multitude, and sheep and oxen and goats for their provision, of which there was no number,

**Judith 2:18**

and food supply for every man in abundance, and exceedingly much gold and silver from the house of the king.

**Judith 2:19**

And he went out, he and all his force, for the journey, to go before King Nebuchadnezzar and to cover all the face of the land toward the west with their chariots and horsemen and chosen footmen.

**Judith 2:20**

And a great mixed multitude went out together with them like a locust and like the sand of the earth, for there was no number because of their multitude.

**Judith 2:21**

And they went away from Nineveh, a journey of three days, toward the face of the plain of Bectileth, and they encamped from Bectileth near the mountain that is on the left of upper Cilicia.

**Judith 2:22**

And he took all his force, the foot soldiers and the horsemen and his chariots, and went away from there into the hill country.

**Judith 2:23**

And he cut down Phud and Lud and plundered all the sons of Rassis and the sons of Ishmael who were before the wilderness, toward the south of Cheleon.

**Judith 2:24**

And he passed through the Euphrates and went through Mesopotamia and dug down all the high cities that were upon the torrent Abronah, until coming to the sea.

**Judith 2:25**

And he seized the borders of Cilicia and cut down all those resisting him, and came as far as the borders of Japheth toward the south, before Arabia.

**Judith 2:26**

And he encircled all the sons of Midian and burned their tent-dwellings and plundered their folds.

**Judith 2:27**

And he came down into the plain of Damascus in the days of wheat harvest and burned all their fields, and he gave the flocks and the cattle herds to annihilation, and despoiled their cities, and winnowed out their children, and struck all their young men with the mouth of the sword.

**Judith 2:28**

And his fear and terror fell upon those dwelling along the coastland, those who were in Sidon and Tyre, and those dwelling in Sur and Okina, and all those dwelling in Jemnaan; and those dwelling in Azotus and Ascalon feared him exceedingly.

### Chapter 3

**Judith 3:1**

And they sent messengers to him with peaceful words, saying,

**Judith 3:2**

Behold, we, servants of Nebuchadnezzar the great king, lie before you; use us as it is pleasing before your face.

**Judith 3:3**

Behold, our farmsteads and every plain of wheat and the flocks and the cattle herds and all the folds of our tents lie before your face; use them according to whatever may please you.

**Judith 3:4**

Behold also our cities and those dwelling in them are your slaves; come and meet them as is good in your eyes.

**Judith 3:5**

And the men came to Holofernes and reported to him according to these words.

**Judith 3:6**

And he went down upon the coastland, he and his force, and garrisoned the high cities and took from them selected men as allies;

**Judith 3:7**

and they received him, they and all their surrounding country, with wreaths and dances and tambourines.

**Judith 3:8**

And he razed all their borders and cut down their sacred groves, and it had been given to him to utterly destroy all the gods of the land, so that all the nations should serve Nebuchadnezzar alone, and all their tongues and all their tribes should call upon him as god.

**Judith 3:9**

And he came opposite Esdrelon near Dothaea, which is across from the great saw-ridge of Judea,

**Judith 3:10**

and he encamped between Gabai and the city of the Scythians, and he was there a month of days to gather all the equipment of his force.

### Chapter 4

**Judith 4:1**

And the sons of Israel dwelling in Judea heard all that Holofernes had done to the nations, the chief general of Nebuchadnezzar king of the Assyrians, and how he despoiled all their sacred things and gave them to annihilation,

**Judith 4:2**

and they feared exceedingly exceedingly from his face and were troubled concerning Jerusalem and the temple of the Lord their God.

**Judith 4:3**

For recently they had come up from the captivity, and newly all the people of Judea had been gathered together, and the vessels and the altar and the house had been sanctified out of profanation.

**Judith 4:4**

And they sent into every border of Samaria and Kona and Beth-horon and Belmain and Jericho and into Choba and Aesora and the valley of Salem,

**Judith 4:5**

and they seized beforehand all the tops of the high mountains and walled the villages in them and set aside food supply for preparation of war, because their fields had recently been harvested.

**Judith 4:6**

And Joakim the high priest, who was in Jerusalem in those days, wrote to those dwelling in Bethulia and Betomesthaim, which is opposite Esdrelon, before the plain near Dothaim,

**Judith 4:7**

telling them to hold fast the ascents of the hill country, because through them was the entrance into Judea, and it was easy to hinder those approaching, since the approach was narrow for all the men, two at a time.

**Judith 4:8**

And the sons of Israel did as Joakim the high priest and the council of elders of all the people of Israel, who sat in Jerusalem, commanded them.

**Judith 4:9**

And every man of Israel cried out to God with great earnestness, and they humbled their souls with great earnestness.

**Judith 4:10**

They and their women and their infants and their livestock and every resident alien or hired worker and their bought-with-silver ones put sackcloths upon their loins.

**Judith 4:11**

And every man of Israel and woman and the children and those dwelling in Jerusalem fell before the face of the temple and sprinkled ashes on their heads and stretched out their sackcloths before the face of the Lord;

**Judith 4:12**

and they wrapped the altar with sackcloth and cried out to the God of Israel with one accord earnestly, that he not give their infants to plunder, and their wives to spoil, and the cities of their inheritance to annihilation, and the holy things to profanation and reproach, a cause for rejoicing to the nations.

**Judith 4:13**

And the Lord heard their voice and looked upon their affliction; and the people were fasting many days in all Judea and Jerusalem before the holy things of the Lord Almighty.

**Judith 4:14**

And Joakim the high priest and all those standing before the Lord, priests and those ministering to the Lord, with sackcloths girded around their loins, offered the continual whole burnt offering and the vows and the voluntary gifts of the people,

**Judith 4:15**

and ash was upon their turbans. And they cried to the Lord from all their strength, to visit all the house of Israel for good.

### Chapter 5

**Judith 5:1**

And it was reported to Holofernes, chief general of the force of Assur, that the sons of Israel had prepared for war and closed the passes of the hill country and walled every top of a high mountain and put stumbling blocks in the plains.

**Judith 5:2**

And he was angry with exceedingly great wrath and called all the rulers of Moab and the generals of Ammon and all the satraps of the coastland,

**Judith 5:3**

and said to them, Tell me now, sons of Canaan, who is this people sitting in the hill country, and what cities do they inhabit, and what is the multitude of their force, and in what is their might and their strength, and who has stood over them as king, leading their army,

**Judith 5:4**

and why have they turned their backs, so as not to come to meet me, beyond all those dwelling in the west?

**Judith 5:5**

And Achior, leader of all the sons of Ammon, said to him, Let my lord hear a word from the mouth of your servant, and I will report to you the truth concerning the people that dwells in this hill country near you, and no falsehood will come out from the mouth of your servant.

**Judith 5:6**

This people are descendants of Chaldeans,

**Judith 5:7**

and formerly they sojourned in Mesopotamia, because they were not willing to follow the gods of their fathers, who were in the land of Chaldeans;

**Judith 5:8**

and they departed from the way of their parents and worshiped the God of heaven, the God whom they knew, and they cast them out from before their gods, and they fled to Mesopotamia and sojourned there many days.

**Judith 5:9**

And their God told them to go out from their sojourning and to go into the land of Canaan, and they dwelt there and were multiplied with gold and silver and with very many livestock.

**Judith 5:10**

And they went down into Egypt, for famine covered the face of the land of Canaan, and they sojourned there until they were nourished; and they came to be there as a great multitude, and there was no number of their race.

**Judith 5:11**

And the king of Egypt rose up against them, and they dealt craftily with them in labor and in brick, and humbled them and set them as slaves;

**Judith 5:12**

and they cried out to their God, and he struck all the land of Egypt with plagues in which there was no healing; and the Egyptians cast them out from before their face.

**Judith 5:13**

And God dried up the Red Sea before them,

**Judith 5:14**

and led them into the way of Sinai and Kadesh Barne; and they cast out all those dwelling in the wilderness,

**Judith 5:15**

and they dwelt in the land of the Amorites and utterly destroyed all the Heshbonites by their strength. And crossing the Jordan, they inherited all the hill country,

**Judith 5:16**

and they cast out from before them the Canaanite and the Perizzite and the Jebusite and Sychem and all the Gergesites, and they dwelt in it many days.

**Judith 5:17**

And as long as they did not sin before their God, good things were with them, because God, who hates injustice, is with them.

**Judith 5:18**

But when they departed from the way that he arranged for them, they were utterly destroyed in many wars for a very long time and were taken captive into a land not their own, and the temple of their God came to be as ground, and their cities were seized by their adversaries.

**Judith 5:19**

And now, having returned to their God, they came up from the dispersion where they had been scattered, and they took possession of Jerusalem, where their sanctuary is, and they were settled in the hill country, because it was desolate.

**Judith 5:20**

And now, master lord, if there is an unwitting sin in this people and they are sinning against their God, and we inspect that this stumbling block is among them, then we will go up and war against them.

**Judith 5:21**

But if there is no lawlessness in their nation, let my lord pass by, lest their Lord and their God shield them, and we shall be a reproach before all the earth.

**Judith 5:22**

And it happened, when Achior ceased speaking these words, that all the people surrounding the tent and standing around murmured, and the great ones of Holofernes and all those dwelling along the coastland and in Moab said to cut him down;

**Judith 5:23**

for we shall not fear the sons of Israel; for behold, they are a people in whom there is no force nor might for a strong battle line.

**Judith 5:24**

Therefore indeed we will go up, and they will be food for all your army, master Holofernes.

### Chapter 6

**Judith 6:1**

And when the uproar of the men around the council ceased, Holofernes, chief general of the force of Assur, said to Achior before all the people of the foreigners and to all the sons of Moab,

**Judith 6:2**

And who are you, Achior, and the hirelings of Ephraim, that you prophesied among us as today and said not to war against the race of Israel, because their God will shield them? And who is God except Nebuchadnezzar? This one will send his might and utterly destroy them from the face of the earth, and their God will not rescue them;

**Judith 6:3**

but we his slaves will strike them as one man, and they will not withstand the might of our horses.

**Judith 6:4**

For we will burn them up among themselves, and their mountains will be made drunk with their blood, and their plains will be filled with their dead, and the track of their feet will not stand before our face, but by destruction they will be destroyed, says King Nebuchadnezzar, lord of all the earth; for he spoke, and the words of his sayings will not be made vain.

**Judith 6:5**

But you, Achior, hireling of Ammon, who spoke these words in the day of your injustice, you shall no longer see my face from this day until I take vengeance on the race from Egypt;

**Judith 6:6**

and then the iron of my army and the people of my attendants will pass through your sides, and you will fall among their wounded when I return.

**Judith 6:7**

And my slaves will restore you to the hill country and set you in one of the cities of the ascents,

**Judith 6:8**

and you will not perish until you are utterly destroyed with them.

**Judith 6:9**

And if indeed you hope in your heart that they will not be taken, do not let your face fall; I have spoken, and none of my words will fall through.

**Judith 6:10**

And Holofernes commanded his slaves who were standing in his tent to seize Achior and restore him to Bethulia and hand him over into the hands of the sons of Israel.

**Judith 6:11**

And his slaves seized him and led him outside the camp into the plain, and they departed from the middle of the lowland into the hill country and came to the springs that were below Bethulia.

**Judith 6:12**

And when the men of the city saw them upon the top of the mountain, they took up their weapons and went outside the city upon the top of the mountain, and every slinger held their ascent and threw stones upon them.

**Judith 6:13**

And slipping below the mountain, they bound Achior and left him thrown at the root of the mountain and went away to their lord.

**Judith 6:14**

But the sons of Israel came down from their city and stood over him, and loosing him, they led him away to Bethulia and set him before the rulers of their city,

**Judith 6:15**

who were in those days: Ozias son of Micha from the tribe of Simeon, and Abris son of Gothoniel, and Charmis son of Melchiel.

**Judith 6:16**

And they called together all the elders of the city, and every young man of theirs and the women ran together into the assembly, and they set Achior in the midst of all their people, and Ozias asked him what had happened.

**Judith 6:17**

And answering, he reported to them the words of Holofernes' council and all the words that he spoke in the midst of the rulers of the sons of Assur, and as much as Holofernes boasted greatly against the house of Israel.

**Judith 6:18**

And falling down, the people worshiped God and cried out, saying,

**Judith 6:19**

Lord God of heaven, look down upon their arrogances, and have mercy on the humiliation of our race, and look upon the face of those sanctified to you in this day.

**Judith 6:20**

And they comforted Achior and praised him exceedingly.

**Judith 6:21**

And Ozias took him from the assembly into his house and made a drinking feast for the elders, and they called upon the God of Israel for help all that night.

### Chapter 7

**Judith 7:1**

And on the next day Holofernes commanded all his army and all his people who had come for his alliance to break camp against Bethulia and to seize beforehand the ascents of the hill country and to make war against the sons of Israel.

**Judith 7:2**

And on that day every mighty man of theirs broke camp; and their force of warrior men was 170,000 foot soldiers and 12,000 horsemen, apart from the baggage and the men who were on foot among them, an exceedingly great multitude.

**Judith 7:3**

And they encamped in the valley near Bethulia by the spring and stretched out in width to Dothaim and as far as Belbaim, and in length from Bethulia as far as Cyamon, which is opposite Esdrelon.

**Judith 7:4**

But the sons of Israel, when they saw their multitude, were greatly troubled, and each said to his neighbor, Now these will lick up the face of the whole land, and neither the high mountains nor the ravines nor the hills will withstand their weight.

**Judith 7:5**

And each took up their weapons of war, and kindling fires upon their towers, they remained guarding all that night.

**Judith 7:6**

But on the second day Holofernes led out all his cavalry before the face of the sons of Israel who were in Bethulia,

**Judith 7:7**

and inspected the ascents of their city and reconnoitered the springs of their waters and seized them beforehand, and he stationed camps of warrior men over them, and he himself broke camp to his people.

**Judith 7:8**

And the rulers of the sons of Esau and all the leaders of the people of Moab and the generals of the coastland came near to him and said,

**Judith 7:9**

Let our master hear a word, so that there may not be a fracture in your force;

**Judith 7:10**

for this people of the sons of Israel do not trust in their spears, but in the heights of their mountains in which they dwell; for it is not easy to go up to the tops of their mountains.

**Judith 7:11**

And now, master, do not war against them as battle-line war happens, and not one man from your people will fall.

**Judith 7:12**

Remain in your camp, guarding every man from your force, and let your servants take possession of the spring of water that goes out from the root of the mountain,

**Judith 7:13**

because from there all those dwelling in Bethulia draw water, and thirst will destroy them, and they will surrender their city. And we and our people will go up to the nearby tops of the mountains and camp upon them as a guard, so that not one man may go out from the city.

**Judith 7:14**

And they will melt away in famine, they and their wives and their children, and before the sword comes upon them, they will be strewn in the streets of their dwelling.

**Judith 7:15**

And you will repay them an evil repayment, because they rebelled and did not meet your face in peace.

**Judith 7:16**

And their words were pleasing before Holofernes and before all his attendants, and they commanded to do just as they spoke.

**Judith 7:17**

And the camp of the sons of Ammon departed, and with them five thousand sons of Assur, and they encamped in the valley and seized beforehand the waters and the springs of the waters of the sons of Israel.

**Judith 7:18**

And the sons of Esau and the sons of Ammon went up and encamped in the hill country opposite Dothaim. And they sent some of them toward the south and east opposite Egrebel, which is near Chous, which is upon the torrent Mochmour. And the remaining army of the Assyrians encamped in the plain and covered all the face of the land; and their tents and equipment encamped in a great crowd, and they were an exceedingly great multitude.

**Judith 7:19**

And the sons of Israel cried out to the Lord their God, because their spirit fainted, because all their enemies surrounded them and there was no escaping from their midst.

**Judith 7:20**

And the whole camp of Assur remained around them, their foot soldiers and chariots and horsemen, thirty-four days. And all the vessels of water failed for all those dwelling in Bethulia,

**Judith 7:21**

and the cisterns were being emptied, and they did not have water to drink to fullness for one day, because they were giving them drink by measure.

**Judith 7:22**

And their infants became faint, and their women and young men failed from thirst and fell in the streets of the city and in the passages of the gates, and there was no strengthening still in them.

**Judith 7:23**

And all the people were gathered against Ozias and the rulers of the city, the young men and the women and the children, and they cried out with a great voice and said before all the elders,

**Judith 7:24**

May God judge between us and you, because you have done great injustice among us by not speaking peaceful things with the sons of Assur.

**Judith 7:25**

And now there is no helper for us, but God has sold us into their hands, to be strewn before them in thirst and great destruction.

**Judith 7:26**

And now call upon them and hand over the whole city as spoil to the people of Holofernes and to all his force;

**Judith 7:27**

for it is better for us to become plunder for them, for we will be slaves, and our soul will live, and we will not see the death of our infants with our eyes, and our wives and our children failing in their souls.

**Judith 7:28**

We call to witness against you heaven and earth and our God and Lord of our fathers, who avenges us according to our sins and according to the sins of our fathers, so that he may not do according to these words on this day.

**Judith 7:29**

And there was great weeping in the midst of the assembly of all with one accord, and they cried out to the Lord God with a great voice.

**Judith 7:30**

And Ozias said to them, Take courage, brothers; let us endure yet five days, in which the Lord our God will turn his mercy upon us, for he will not abandon us to the end.

**Judith 7:31**

But if these pass and help does not come upon us, I will do according to your words. And he scattered the people to their own camp, and they went away upon the walls and towers of their city, and he sent the women and the children into their houses; and they were in much humiliation in the city.

### Chapter 8

**Judith 8:1**

And Judith heard in those days, daughter of Merari, son of Ox, son of Joseph, son of Oziel, son of Elkia, son of Eliou, son of Chelkias, son of Eliab, son of Nathanael, son of Salamiel, son of Sarasadai, son of Israel.

**Judith 8:2**

And her husband was Manasseh, of her tribe and of her family, and he died in the days of barley harvest;

**Judith 8:3**

for he stood over the one binding the sheaf in the field, and the burning heat came upon his head, and he fell upon the bed and died in Bethulia his city, and they buried him with his fathers in the field between Dothaim and Balamon.

**Judith 8:4**

And Judith was in her house as a widow three years and four months.

**Judith 8:5**

And she made herself a tent upon the roof of her house and put sackcloth upon her loin, and the garments of her widowhood were upon her.

**Judith 8:6**

And she fasted all the days of her widowhood, except the eves of Sabbaths and Sabbaths and eves of new moons and new moons and feasts and rejoicings of the house of Israel.

**Judith 8:7**

And she was beautiful in form and very lovely in appearance; and Manasseh her husband had left her gold and silver and male servants and female servants and livestock and fields, and she remained upon them.

**Judith 8:8**

And there was no one who brought an evil word against her, because she feared God exceedingly.

**Judith 8:9**

And she heard the evil words of the people against the ruler, because they had fainted over the scarcity of the waters; and Judith heard all the words that Ozias spoke to them, how he swore to them to hand over the city to the Assyrians after five days.

**Judith 8:10**

And sending her maid, who stood over all her possessions, she called Ozias and Chabris and Charmis, the elders of her city,

**Judith 8:11**

and they came to her, and she said to them, Hear me now, rulers of those dwelling in Bethulia, because your word is not straight, which you spoke before the people today, and you set the oath that you spoke between God and you, and said to hand over the city to our enemies if within those days the Lord does not return to help us.

**Judith 8:12**

And now who are you, who tested God on this day and stand over God in the midst of sons of men?

**Judith 8:13**

And now you examine the Lord Almighty, and you will know nothing forever,

**Judith 8:14**

because you will not find the depth of a human heart, and you will not apprehend the words of his mind. And how will you search out God, who made all these things, and know his mind and understand his reasoning? By no means, brothers; do not provoke the Lord our God.

**Judith 8:15**

Because if he does not wish to help us within the five days, he himself has authority in whatever days he wishes to shelter us, or even to destroy us before the face of our enemies.

**Judith 8:16**

But you, do not put the counsels of the Lord our God under pledge, because God is not as a man to be threatened, nor as a son of man to be arbitrated.

**Judith 8:17**

Therefore, waiting for the salvation from him, let us call upon him for our help, and he will hear our voice, if it is pleasing to him.

**Judith 8:18**

For there has not risen in our generations, nor is there today, either tribe or family or people or city from us, who worship handmade gods, just as happened in former days;

**Judith 8:19**

for their sake our fathers were given to sword and to plunder and fell a great fall before our enemies.

**Judith 8:20**

But we have known no other god except him; therefore we hope that he will not overlook us, nor any of our race.

**Judith 8:21**

Because when we are taken, all Judea will sit this way, and our holy things will be plundered, and he will seek their profanation from our blood,

**Judith 8:22**

and the slaughter of our brothers and the captivity of the land and the desolation of our inheritance he will turn upon our head among the nations, wherever we serve there, and we shall be a stumbling block and a reproach before those who possess us.

**Judith 8:23**

Because our slavery will not be directed to favor, but the Lord our God will set it for dishonor.

**Judith 8:24**

And now, brothers, let us show our brothers that their soul hangs from us, and the holy things and the house and the altar rest upon us.

**Judith 8:25**

Besides all these things, let us give thanks to the Lord our God, who tests us just as also our fathers.

**Judith 8:26**

Remember what he did with Abraham, and what he tested in Isaac, and what happened to Jacob in Mesopotamia of Syria, while he was shepherding the sheep of Laban, the brother of his mother.

**Judith 8:27**

For he has not refined us by fire as he did those men for the testing of their heart, nor has he avenged himself on us; but for admonition the Lord scourges those who draw near to him.

**Judith 8:28**

And Ozias said to her, All things, as many as you said, you have spoken with a good heart, and there is no one who will resist your words;

**Judith 8:29**

for not today only is your wisdom evident, but from the beginning of your days all the people have known your understanding, because the formation of your heart is good.

**Judith 8:30**

But the people thirsted exceedingly and forced us to do as we spoke to them and to bring an oath upon ourselves, which we will not transgress.

**Judith 8:31**

And now pray concerning us, because you are a devout woman, and the Lord will send the rain for the filling of our cisterns, and we shall not fail anymore.

**Judith 8:32**

And Judith said to them, Hear me, and I will do a matter that will reach to generations of generations for the sons of our race.

**Judith 8:33**

You shall stand at the gate this night, and I will go out with my maid, and in the days after which you said you would hand over the city to our enemies, the Lord will visit Israel by my hand;

**Judith 8:34**

but you shall not search out my action, for I will not tell you until the things I do are completed.

**Judith 8:35**

And Ozias and the rulers said to her, Go in peace, and may the Lord God be before you for vengeance on our enemies.

**Judith 8:36**

And turning back from the tent, they went to their stations.

### Chapter 9

**Judith 9:1**

But Judith fell upon her face and put ashes upon her head and uncovered the sackcloth that she had put on. And just then, in Jerusalem, in the house of God, the incense of that evening was being offered, and Judith cried out with a great voice to the Lord and said,

**Judith 9:2**

Lord God of my father Simeon, to whom you gave a sword in hand for vengeance on foreigners, who loosed the womb of a virgin for defilement and uncovered a thigh for shame and profaned a womb for reproach; for you said, It shall not be so, and they did it.

**Judith 9:3**

For this you gave their rulers to slaughter and their bed, which was ashamed at their deceit, to blood, and you struck slaves upon rulers and rulers upon their thrones.

**Judith 9:4**

And you gave their wives to spoil and their daughters to captivity and all the spoils to division among sons beloved by you, who also were zealous with your zeal and abhorred the defilement of their blood and called upon you as helper. O God, my God, hear me, the widow;

**Judith 9:5**

for you made the former things of those and those things and the things after, and the things now and the things coming you considered, and the things you intended came to be,

**Judith 9:6**

and the things you counseled stood by and said, Behold, we are present; for all your ways are prepared, and your judgment is in foreknowledge.

**Judith 9:7**

For behold, the Assyrians were multiplied in their force; they were exalted upon horse and rider; they boasted in the arm of foot soldiers; they hoped in shield and javelin and bow and sling, and they did not know that you are the Lord who crushes wars.

**Judith 9:8**

Lord is your name. You, shatter their strength by your power and break down their might in your wrath; for they planned to profane your holy things, to defile the tent of the rest of the name of your glory, and to throw down the horn of your altar with iron.

**Judith 9:9**

Look upon their arrogance; send your wrath upon their heads; give into my hand, the widow, the might that I have planned.

**Judith 9:10**

Strike a slave by the lips of my deceit against a ruler, and a ruler against his attendant; break their stature by the hand of a female.

**Judith 9:11**

For your might is not in multitude, nor your dominion among the strong, but you are God of the humble, helper of the lesser, supporter of the weak, shelterer of those given up, savior of those without hope.

**Judith 9:12**

Yes, yes, God of my father and God of the inheritance of Israel, Master of the heavens and the earth, Creator of the waters, King of all your creation, you, hear my supplication

**Judith 9:13**

and give my word and deceit for their wound and welt, who planned harsh things against your covenant and your sanctified house and the summit of Zion and the house of possession of your sons.

**Judith 9:14**

And make upon all your nation and every tribe an acknowledgment to know that you are the God of all power and might, and there is no other shielding the race of Israel except you.

### Chapter 10

**Judith 10:1**

And it happened, when she ceased crying out to the God of Israel and completed all these words,

**Judith 10:2**

that she rose up from her fall and called her maid and went down into the house in which she used to spend time on the Sabbath days and on her feast days;

**Judith 10:3**

and she took off the sackcloth that she had put on and stripped off the garments of her widowhood and washed her body all around with water and anointed herself with thick perfume and arranged the hairs of her head and put a turban upon it and put on the garments of her gladness, with which she was adorned in the days of the life of her husband Manasseh,

**Judith 10:4**

and she took sandals for her feet and put on the anklets and the bracelets and the rings and the earrings and all her adornment, and she made herself exceedingly beautiful for the deception of the eyes of men, as many as might see her.

**Judith 10:5**

And she gave her maid a skin-bottle of wine and a flask of oil, and filled a bag with groats and a fig-cake and clean breads, and wrapped up all her vessels and put them upon her.

**Judith 10:6**

And they went out to the gate of the city of Bethulia and found Ozias and the elders of the city, Chabris and Charmin, standing at it.

**Judith 10:7**

And when they saw her, and her face was altered and her apparel changed, they marveled at her beauty exceedingly much and said to her,

**Judith 10:8**

May the God, the God of our fathers, give you for favor and complete your undertakings for the boasting of the sons of Israel and the exaltation of Jerusalem. And she worshiped God

**Judith 10:9**

and said to them, Give orders to open the gate of the city for me, and I will go out for the completion of the words that you spoke with me. And they commanded the young men to open for her, just as she had spoken.

**Judith 10:10**

And they did so. And Judith went out, she and her maid with her; and the men of the city watched her until she went down the mountain, until she passed through the valley and they no longer saw her.

**Judith 10:11**

And they were going straight in the valley, and an advance guard of the Assyrians met her.

**Judith 10:12**

And they seized her and questioned her, Whose are you, and from where do you come, and where are you going? And she said, I am a daughter of the Hebrews, and I am fleeing from before them, because they are about to be given to you for food;

**Judith 10:13**

and I am coming before the face of Holofernes, chief general of your force, to report words of truth, and I will show before his face a way by which he will go and master all the hill country, and not one flesh nor spirit of life among his men will fail.

**Judith 10:14**

And when the men heard her words and observed her face, and she was before them exceedingly marvelous in beauty, they said to her,

**Judith 10:15**

You have saved your soul by hurrying to come down before the face of our lord. And now come near to his tent, and some of us will escort you until they hand you over into his hands;

**Judith 10:16**

but if you stand before him, do not fear in your heart, but report according to your words, and he will do good to you.

**Judith 10:17**

And they selected from themselves one hundred men and yoked them alongside her and her maid, and they led them to the tent of Holofernes.

**Judith 10:18**

And there was a running together in the whole camp, for her presence was shouted through the tents; and coming, they surrounded her as she stood outside the tent of Holofernes, until they reported to him concerning her.

**Judith 10:19**

And they marveled at her beauty and marveled at the sons of Israel because of her, and each said to his neighbor, Who will despise this people, which has such women among itself? For it is not good to leave from them one man, who, if left, will be able to outwit all the earth.

**Judith 10:20**

And those sleeping beside Holofernes went out, and all his attendants, and they brought her into the tent.

**Judith 10:21**

And Holofernes was resting upon his bed in the canopy, which was woven of purple and gold and emerald and costly stones.

**Judith 10:22**

And they reported to him concerning her, and he went out into the front tent, and silver lamps were going before him.

**Judith 10:23**
