And when Judith came before his face and before his attendants, all marveled at the beauty of her face; and falling upon her face, she worshiped him, and his slaves raised her.

### Chapter 11

**Judith 11:1**

And Holofernes said to her, Take courage, woman; do not fear in your heart, because I have not harmed a human who has chosen to serve King Nebuchadnezzar of all the earth.

**Judith 11:2**

And now, your people dwelling in the hill country, if they had not treated me lightly, I would not have lifted my spear against them; but they did these things to themselves.

**Judith 11:3**

And now tell me for what reason you escaped from them and came to us. For you have come for salvation; take courage, in this night you will live and henceforth.

**Judith 11:4**

For there is no one who will wrong you, but he will do good to you, just as happens to the slaves of my lord King Nebuchadnezzar.

**Judith 11:5**

And Judith said to him, Receive the words of your slave woman, and let your maidservant speak before your face, and I will not report a lie to my lord in this night.

**Judith 11:6**

And if you follow closely the words of your maidservant, God will accomplish a thing perfectly with you, and my lord will not fall short of his undertakings.

**Judith 11:7**

For King Nebuchadnezzar of all the earth lives, and his might lives, he who sent you for the setting right of every soul, because not only humans serve him through you, but also the wild beasts of the field and the cattle and the birds of heaven will live through your strength for Nebuchadnezzar and all his house.

**Judith 11:8**

For we have heard your wisdom and the crafty deeds of your soul, and it has been reported in all the earth that you alone are good in all the kingdom and mighty in knowledge and marvelous in armies of war.

**Judith 11:9**

And now the word that Achior spoke in your council, we have heard his words, because the men of Bethulia preserved him, and he reported to them all that he spoke out in your presence.

**Judith 11:10**

Therefore, master lord, do not pass by his word, but lay it down in your heart, because it is true; for vengeance is not taken on our race, nor does a sword prevail against them, unless they sin against their God.

**Judith 11:11**

And now, so that my lord may not become cast out and without result, death will fall upon their face; and sin has overtaken them, by which they will provoke their God whenever they do an improper thing.

**Judith 11:12**

For since foods failed them and all water became scarce, they took counsel to lay hands on their cattle, and all the things that God ordered them in his laws not to eat, they determined to consume.

**Judith 11:13**

And the firstfruits of the grain and the tithes of the wine and the oil, which they kept, having sanctified them for the priests who stand by in Jerusalem before the face of our God, they have judged to consume completely, things of which it was not fitting for anyone of the people even to touch with the hands.

**Judith 11:14**

And they have sent to Jerusalem, because those dwelling there also have done these things, to bring back permission for them from the council of elders.

**Judith 11:15**

And it will be, whenever it is reported to them and they do it, they will be given to you for destruction in that day.

**Judith 11:16**

Therefore I, your slave woman, having learned all these things, fled from before their face, and God sent me to do things with you at which all the earth, as many as hear them, will be astonished.

**Judith 11:17**

Because your slave woman is God-fearing and serves the God of heaven night and day. And now I will remain beside you, my lord, and your slave woman will go out by night into the ravine and pray to God, and he will tell me when they have done their sins.

**Judith 11:18**

And coming, I will disclose it to you, and you will go out with all your force, and there is no one among them who will resist you.

**Judith 11:19**

And I will lead you through the midst of Judea until coming opposite Jerusalem, and I will set your seat in its midst, and you will lead them like sheep for which there is no shepherd, and no dog will growl with its tongue before you, because these things were spoken to me according to my foreknowledge and were reported to me, and I was sent to report to you.

**Judith 11:20**

And her words were pleasing before Holofernes and before all his attendants, and they marveled at her wisdom and said,

**Judith 11:21**

There is no such woman from one end to the other end of the earth with a beautiful face and understanding of words.

**Judith 11:22**

And Holofernes said to her, God did well in sending you before the people, so that might may come to be in our hands, and destruction among those who treated my lord lightly.

**Judith 11:23**

And now you are beautiful in your appearance and good in your words; because if you do just as you spoke, your God will be my God, and you will sit in the house of King Nebuchadnezzar and be renowned through all the earth.

### Chapter 12

**Judith 12:1**

And he commanded them to bring her in where his silver things were laid, and he ordered them to spread for her from his delicacies and to drink from his wine.

**Judith 12:2**

And Judith said, I will not eat from them, lest there be an offense, but from the things that came with me provision will be made.

**Judith 12:3**

And Holofernes said to her, But if the things with you fail, from where shall we bring out to give you things like them? For there is no one with us from your nation.

**Judith 12:4**

And Judith said to him, Your soul lives, my lord, because your slave woman will not consume the things with me until the Lord does by my hand the things that he has planned.

**Judith 12:5**

And the attendants of Holofernes led her into the tent, and she slept until the middle of the night; and she rose toward the morning watch,

**Judith 12:6**

and sent to Holofernes, saying, Let my lord give order now to allow your slave woman to go out for prayer.

**Judith 12:7**

And Holofernes commanded the bodyguards not to hinder her. And she remained in the camp three days, and she went out by night into the ravine of Bethulia and immersed herself in the camp at the spring of water;

**Judith 12:8**

and when she came up, she entreated the Lord God of Israel to direct her way for the raising up of the sons of his people.

**Judith 12:9**

And entering clean, she remained in the tent until her food was brought toward evening.

**Judith 12:10**

And it happened on the fourth day, Holofernes made a drinking feast for his slaves alone and did not call into the use any of those attending to needs.

**Judith 12:11**

And he said to Bagoas the eunuch, who was set over all his things, Go now and persuade the Hebrew woman who is beside you to come to us and eat and drink with us;

**Judith 12:12**

for behold, it is shameful before our face if we let such a woman pass by without consorting with her, because if we do not draw her in, she will laugh us to scorn.

**Judith 12:13**

And Bagoas went out from before Holofernes and entered to her and said, Let this beautiful maidservant not hesitate now to come to my lord, to be glorified before his face and to drink wine with us for gladness and to become on this day as one daughter of the sons of Assur, who stand by in the house of Nebuchadnezzar.

**Judith 12:14**

And Judith said to him, And who am I to contradict my lord? For everything that will be pleasing in his eyes, I will hasten to do, and this will be exultation until the day of my death.

**Judith 12:15**

And having arisen, she adorned herself with clothing and with all womanly adornment, and her slave woman approached and spread out for her opposite Holofernes, on the ground, the fleeces that she had received from Bagoas for her daily way of life, for eating while reclining upon them.

**Judith 12:16**

And Judith entered and reclined, and the heart of Holofernes was astonished over her, and his soul was shaken, and he was exceedingly desirous to come together with her; and he was watching for a time to deceive her from the day he saw her.

**Judith 12:17**

And Holofernes said to her, Drink now, and become with us for gladness.

**Judith 12:18**

And Judith said, I will drink now, lord, because my life has been magnified in me today beyond all the days of my birth.

**Judith 12:19**

And taking, she ate and drank before him what her slave woman had prepared.

**Judith 12:20**

And Holofernes was made glad because of her and drank very much wine, as much as he had never drunk in one day from when he was born.

### Chapter 13

**Judith 13:1**

And when evening came, his slaves hurried to depart. And Bagoas closed the tent from outside and shut out those standing by from before the face of his lord, and they went away to their beds; for they were all weary because the drinking had gone on longer.

**Judith 13:2**

But Judith was left alone in the tent, and Holofernes had fallen forward upon his bed, for the wine had overwhelmed him.

**Judith 13:3**

And Judith had told her slave woman to stand outside her bedchamber and watch for her going out, just as day by day; for she said that she would go out for her prayer, and she spoke to Bagoas according to these words.

**Judith 13:4**

And all went away from before his face, and no one was left in the bedchamber, from small to great. And Judith, standing beside his bed, said in her heart, Lord, God of all power, look in this hour upon the works of my hands for the exaltation of Jerusalem;

**Judith 13:5**

because now is the time to take hold of your inheritance and to do my undertaking for the shattering of enemies who rose up against us.

**Judith 13:6**

And coming to the rail of the bed, which was near the head of Holofernes, she took down his short sword from it,

**Judith 13:7**

and drawing near to the bed, she grasped the hair of his head and said, Strengthen me, God of Israel, in this day.

**Judith 13:8**

And she struck into his neck twice with her strength and took away his head from him,

**Judith 13:9**

and rolled his body from the bedding and removed the canopy from the pillars. And after a little while she went out and handed the head of Holofernes to her maid,

**Judith 13:10**

and she put it into her food bag. And the two went out together according to their custom for prayer; and passing through the camp, they circled around that battle line and went up the mountain of Bethulia and came to its gates.

**Judith 13:11**

And Judith said from afar to those guarding at the gates, Open, open now the gate; God is with us, our God, to do still strength in Israel and might against the enemies, just as he also did today.

**Judith 13:12**

And it happened, when the men of her city heard her voice, they hurried to go down to the gate of their city and called together the elders of the city.

**Judith 13:13**

And all ran together, from small to great, because it was extraordinary to them that she had come, and they opened the gate and welcomed them; and kindling a fire for light, they surrounded them.

**Judith 13:14**

But she said to them with a great voice, Praise God, praise; praise God, who did not withdraw his mercy from the house of Israel, but shattered our enemies through my hand in this night.

**Judith 13:15**

And taking the head out of the bag, she showed it and said to them, Behold the head of Holofernes, chief general of the force of Assur, and behold the canopy in which he lay in his drunkenness; and the Lord struck him by the hand of a female.

**Judith 13:16**

And the Lord lives, who preserved me in my way in which I went, because my face deceived him for his destruction, and he did not commit sin with me for defilement and shame.

**Judith 13:17**

And all the people were exceedingly astonished, and bowing down they worshiped God and said with one accord, Blessed are you, our God, who brought to nothing today the enemies of your people.

**Judith 13:18**

And Ozias said to her, Blessed are you, daughter, by God Most High beyond all the women upon the earth, and blessed is the Lord God, who created the heavens and the earth, who directed you toward the wound of the head of the ruler of our enemies;

**Judith 13:19**

because your hope will not depart from the heart of humans remembering the strength of God forever.

**Judith 13:20**

And may God make these things for you into eternal exaltation, to visit you with good things, because you did not spare your soul because of the humiliation of our race, but went out against our fall, having gone straight before our God. And all the people said, May it be, may it be.

### Chapter 14

**Judith 14:1**

And Judith said to them, Hear me now, brothers, and taking this head, hang it upon the battlement of your wall.

**Judith 14:2**

And it will be, whenever dawn shines through and the sun comes out upon the earth, each of you shall take up your weapons of war, and every strong man shall go outside the city, and you shall set a leader over them, as though going down upon the plain toward the advance guard of the sons of Assur; but you shall not go down.

**Judith 14:3**

And these, taking up their full armor, will go into their camp and wake the generals of the force of Assur; and they will run together to the tent of Holofernes and will not find him, and fear will fall upon them, and they will flee from before your face.

**Judith 14:4**

And you, following after, and all those dwelling in every border of Israel, lay them low in their roads.

**Judith 14:5**

But before doing these things, call Achior the Ammonite for me, so that seeing, he may recognize the one who treated lightly the house of Israel and sent him to us as for death.

**Judith 14:6**

And they called Achior from the house of Ozias; and when he came and saw the head of Holofernes in the hand of one man in the assembly of the people, he fell upon his face, and his spirit failed.

**Judith 14:7**

And when they raised him up, he fell at the feet of Judith and worshiped before her face and said, Blessed are you in every tent of Judah and in every nation; those who hear your name will be troubled.

**Judith 14:8**

And now report to me all that you did in these days. And Judith reported to him in the midst of the people all that she had done, from the day she went out until she was speaking to them.

**Judith 14:9**

And when she ceased speaking, the people shouted with a great voice and gave a glad voice in their city.

**Judith 14:10**

And Achior, seeing all that the God of Israel did, believed God exceedingly and circumcised the flesh of his foreskin and was added to the house of Israel until this day.

**Judith 14:11**

And when dawn rose, they hung the head of Holofernes from the wall, and every man of Israel took up his arms, and they went out by companies to the ascents of the mountain.

**Judith 14:12**

And when the sons of Assur saw them, they sent to their leaders; and they came to the generals and thousand-commanders and to every ruler of theirs.

**Judith 14:13**

And they came to the tent of Holofernes and said to the one over all his things, Wake our lord now, because the slaves dared to come down against us for war, so that they may be utterly destroyed to the end.

**Judith 14:14**

And Bagoas entered and struck the curtain of the tent, for he supposed him to be sleeping with Judith;

**Judith 14:15**

but when no one answered, having drawn it apart, he entered into the bedchamber and found him thrown dead upon the footstool, and his head had been taken away from him.

**Judith 14:16**

And he cried out with a great voice, with weeping and groaning and a strong shout, and tore his garments.

**Judith 14:17**

And he entered into the tent where Judith had been lodging, and he did not find her; and he leapt out to the people, crying,

**Judith 14:18**

The slaves acted faithlessly; one woman of the Hebrews has made shame for the house of King Nebuchadnezzar, because behold, Holofernes is on the ground, and the head is not upon him.

**Judith 14:19**

And when the rulers of the force of Assur heard these words, they tore their tunics, and their soul was exceedingly troubled, and their cry and a very great shout came to be in the midst of the camp.

### Chapter 15

**Judith 15:1**

And when those who were in the tents heard, they were astonished at what had happened,

**Judith 15:2**

and fear and trembling fell upon them, and there was no human remaining before the face of his neighbor any longer, but pouring out with one accord, they fled upon every road of the plain and the hill country;

**Judith 15:3**

and those encamped in the hill country around Bethulia also turned to flight. And then the sons of Israel, every warrior man from among them, poured out upon them.

**Judith 15:4**

And Ozias sent to Betomesthaim and Bebai and Chola and to every border of Israel those who would report concerning the things completed, and so that all should pour themselves upon the enemies for their destruction.

**Judith 15:5**

And when the sons of Israel heard, they all with one accord fell upon them and cut them down as far as Choba. Likewise also those from Jerusalem arrived, and from all the hill country, for the things that happened to the camp of their enemies had been reported to them; and those in Galaad and those in Galilee outflanked them with a great blow, until they passed Damascus and its borders.

**Judith 15:6**

And the remaining people dwelling in Bethulia fell upon the camp of Assur and plundered them and became exceedingly rich.

**Judith 15:7**

And the sons of Israel, turning back from the slaughter, took possession of the rest, and the villages and farmsteads in the hill country and plain took hold of many spoils, for there was an exceedingly great multitude.

**Judith 15:8**

And Joakim the great priest and the council of the sons of Israel, those dwelling in Jerusalem, came to behold the good things that the Lord had done for Israel, and to see Judith and speak peace with her.

**Judith 15:9**

And when they entered to her, they all blessed her with one accord and said to her, You are the exaltation of Israel, you are the great pride of Israel, you are the great boast of our race;

**Judith 15:10**

you did all these things in your hand, you did good things with Israel, and God took pleasure in them; become blessed with the Almighty Lord for age-long time. And the people said, May it be.

**Judith 15:11**

And all the people plundered the camp for thirty days; and they gave Judith the tent of Holofernes and all his silver things and beds and vessels and all his equipment. And taking them, she put them upon her mule and yoked her wagons and piled them upon them.

**Judith 15:12**

And every woman of Israel ran together to see her and blessed her and made a dance for her from among them, and she took festival wands in her hands and gave them to the women who were with her.

**Judith 15:13**

And they crowned themselves with olive, she and those with her, and she went before all the people in dance, leading all the women, and every man of Israel followed, armed, with crowns and hymns in their mouth.

**Judith 15:14**

And Judith led out this thanksgiving in all Israel, and all the people answered with this praise.

### Chapter 16

**Judith 16:1**

And Judith said, Begin for my God with tambourines; sing to my Lord with cymbals; fit to him a new psalm; exalt and call upon his name,

**Judith 16:2**

because God who crushes wars is Lord, because into his camps in the midst of the people he rescued me from the hand of those pursuing me.

**Judith 16:3**

Assur came from the mountains, from the north; he came with myriads of his force, whose multitude blocked torrents, and their horse covered hills.

**Judith 16:4**

He said he would burn my borders and destroy my young men with the sword, and put my nursing children to the ground, and give my infants for plunder, and despoil my virgins.

**Judith 16:5**

The Almighty Lord nullified them by the hand of a female.

**Judith 16:6**

For their mighty one did not fall under young men, nor did sons of Titans strike him, nor did tall giants set upon him, but Judith daughter of Merari disabled him by the beauty of her face;

**Judith 16:7**

for she stripped off the garment of her widowhood for the exaltation of those suffering in Israel; she anointed her face with perfume

**Judith 16:8**

and bound her hair in a turban and took a linen garment for his deception;

**Judith 16:9**

her sandal seized his eye, and her beauty captured his soul; the short sword passed through his neck.

**Judith 16:10**

Persians shuddered at her boldness, and Medes were shattered by her daring.

**Judith 16:11**

Then my humbled ones shouted, and my weak ones gave a voice; but they were astonished. They lifted their voice, and they were overturned.

**Judith 16:12**

Sons of maidens pierced them through and wounded them as children of deserters; they perished from the battle line of my Lord.

**Judith 16:13**

I will hymn to my God a new hymn. Lord, you are great and glorious, marvelous in strength, unsurpassable.

**Judith 16:14**

Let all your creation serve you; because you spoke, and they came to be; you sent your spirit, and it built; and there is no one who will resist your voice.

**Judith 16:15**

For mountains from their foundations will be shaken with waters, and rocks before your face will melt like wax; but still, to those fearing you, you are merciful to them.

**Judith 16:16**

Because every sacrifice for a smell of fragrance is small, and all fat for a whole burnt offering to you is least; but the one fearing the Lord is great through all.

**Judith 16:17**

Woe to nations rising up against my race. The Almighty Lord will take vengeance on them in the day of judgment, to give fire and worms into their flesh, and they will weep in sensation forever.

**Judith 16:18**

And when they came to Jerusalem, they worshiped God. And when the people had been cleansed, they brought up their whole burnt offerings and their voluntary offerings and their gifts.

**Judith 16:19**

And Judith dedicated all the vessels of Holofernes, as many as the people gave her, and the canopy that she had taken from his bedchamber she gave as a dedicated offering to God.

**Judith 16:20**

And the people were rejoicing in Jerusalem before the face of the holy things for three months, and Judith remained with them.

**Judith 16:21**

And after these days each departed to his inheritance, and Judith went away to Bethulia and remained upon her estate; and she became glorious in all the land in her time.

**Judith 16:22**

And many desired her, and no man knew her all the days of her life, from the day Manasseh her husband died and was added to his people.

**Judith 16:23**

And she was advancing very greatly and grew old in the house of her husband, one hundred five years; and she let her maid go free. And she died in Bethulia, and they buried her in the cave of her husband Manasseh,

**Judith 16:24**

and the house of Israel mourned her seven days. And she divided her possessions before she died to all those nearest to Manasseh her husband and to those nearest of her race.

**Judith 16:25**

And there was no longer anyone frightening the sons of Israel in the days of Judith and for many days after her death.

## Greek Esther

### Chapter 1

**Greek Esther 1:1α**

In the second year, when Artaxerxes the great king was reigning, on the first day of Nisan, Mordecai son of Jairus, son of Semeios, son of Kisaios, from the tribe of Benjamin, saw a dream,

**Greek Esther 1:1β**

a Jewish human dwelling in Susa the city, a great human, serving in the court of the king.

**Greek Esther 1:1γ**

And he was from the captivity that Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon took captive from Jerusalem with Jeconiah king of Judea.

**Greek Esther 1:1δ**

And this was his dream: and behold, voices and uproar, thunder and earthquake, disturbance upon the earth.

**Greek Esther 1:1ε**

And behold, two great dragons came forth, both ready to wrestle, and their great voice came to be;

**Greek Esther 1:1ζ**

and at their voice every nation was prepared for war, so as to war against a nation of righteous ones.

**Greek Esther 1:1η**

And behold, a day of darkness and gloom, affliction and constraint, mistreatment and great disturbance upon the earth;

**Greek Esther 1:1θ**

and every righteous nation was troubled, fearing their own evils, and they were prepared to perish and cried out to God.

**Greek Esther 1:1ι**

But from their cry there came to be, as from a small spring, a great river, much water;

**Greek Esther 1:1κ**

and light and sun rose, and the humble were exalted and devoured the glorious ones.

**Greek Esther 1:1λ**

And Mordecai, having awakened, the one who had seen this dream and what God had planned to do, kept it in his heart and in every matter desired to understand it until the night.

**Greek Esther 1:1μ**

And Mordecai rested in the court with Gabatha and Thara, the two eunuchs of the king, those guarding the court,

**Greek Esther 1:1ν**

and he heard their thoughts and searched out their anxieties and learned that they were preparing their hands to lay upon King Artaxerxes, and he disclosed concerning them to the king.

**Greek Esther 1:1ξ**

And the king examined the two eunuchs, and after confessing, they were led away.

**Greek Esther 1:1ο**

And the king wrote these words for a memorial, and Mordecai wrote concerning these words.

**Greek Esther 1:1π**

And the king ordered Mordecai to serve in the court and gave him gifts concerning these things.

**Greek Esther 1:1ρ**

And Haman son of Hamadathos, the Bougaian, was glorious before the king; and he sought to do evil to Mordecai and his people because of the two eunuchs of the king.

**Greek Esther 1:1**

And it happened after these words, in the days of Artaxerxes, this Artaxerxes held 127 countries from India.

**Greek Esther 1:2**

In those days, when King Artaxerxes was enthroned in Susa the city,

**Greek Esther 1:3**

in the third year of his reign, he made a reception for his friends and for the remaining nations and for the glorious ones of the Persians and Medes and for the rulers of the satraps.

**Greek Esther 1:4**

And after these things, after showing them the wealth of his kingdom and the glory of the gladness of his wealth for one hundred eighty days,

**Greek Esther 1:5**

when the days of the feast were fulfilled, the king made a drinking feast for the nations found in the city for six days in the court of the house of the king,

**Greek Esther 1:6**

adorned with fine linen and cotton stretched upon linen and purple cords, upon gold and silver blocks, upon Parian and stone pillars; golden and silver couches were upon a pavement of emerald stone and mother-of-pearl and Parian stone, and transparent coverings variegated with many flowers; roses had been sprinkled around.

**Greek Esther 1:7**

There were gold and silver drinking cups, and a small carbuncle cup set out worth thirty thousand talents; there was much sweet wine, which the king himself drank.

**Greek Esther 1:8**

But this drinking feast did not happen according to an established law; thus the king willed and commanded the stewards to do his will and that of the humans.

**Greek Esther 1:9**

And Astin the queen made a drinking feast for the women in the palace where King Artaxerxes was.

**Greek Esther 1:10**

And on the seventh day, when the king had become merry, he said to Haman and Bazan and Thara and Barazi and Zatholtha and Abataza and Tharaba, the seven eunuchs, ministers of King Artaxerxes,

**Greek Esther 1:11**

to bring the queen to him, to enthrone her and put the diadem around her and show the rulers and the nations her beauty, because she was beautiful.

**Greek Esther 1:12**

And Astin the queen did not listen to him to come with the eunuchs. And the king was grieved and angered,

**Greek Esther 1:13**

and said to his friends, According to these things Astin spoke; therefore make a law and judgment concerning this.

**Greek Esther 1:14**

And Arkesaios and Sarsathaios and Maleseear drew near to him, rulers of Persians and Medes, those near the king, the first sitting beside the king,

**Greek Esther 1:15**

and they reported to him according to the laws how it is necessary to do to Astin the queen, because she did not do the things commanded by the king through the eunuchs.

**Greek Esther 1:16**

And Mouchaios said to the king and the rulers, Astin the queen has not wronged the king only, but also all the rulers and the leaders of the king,

**Greek Esther 1:17**

for he told them the words of the queen and how she contradicted the king. As therefore Astin contradicted King Artaxerxes,

**Greek Esther 1:18**

so today the remaining ruling women of the rulers of the Persians and Medes, when they hear the things said by her to the king, will dare likewise to dishonor their husbands.

**Greek Esther 1:19**

If therefore it seems good to the king, let him command a royal order, and let it be written according to the laws of the Medes and Persians; and let it not be handled otherwise, nor let the queen still come to him, and let the king give her kingship to a woman better than her.

**Greek Esther 1:20**

And let the law made by the king be heard, whatever he may make in his kingdom, and thus all the women will place honor around their own husbands, from poor to rich.

**Greek Esther 1:21**

And the word was pleasing to the king and to the rulers, and the king did just as Mouchaios spoke;

**Greek Esther 1:22**

and he sent into every kingdom according to country, according to their language, so that there would be fear for them in their houses.

### Chapter 2

**Greek Esther 2:1**

And after these words, the king ceased from wrath and no longer remembered Astin, remembering what she had spoken and how he had condemned her.

**Greek Esther 2:2**

And the ministers of the king said, Let undefiled girls, beautiful in appearance, be sought for the king;

**Greek Esther 2:3**

and let the king appoint village-rulers in all the countries of his kingdom, and let them select virgin girls, beautiful in appearance, for Susa the city, for the women's quarters; and let them be handed over to the eunuch of the king, the keeper of the women, and let cleansing preparation and the remaining care be given.

**Greek Esther 2:4**

And the woman who pleases the king will reign instead of Astin. And the matter pleased the king, and he did so.

**Greek Esther 2:5**

And there was a Jewish human in Susa the city, and his name was Mordecai son of Jairus, son of Semeios, son of Kisaios, from the tribe of Benjamin,

**Greek Esther 2:6**

who was a captive from Jerusalem, which Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon had taken captive.

**Greek Esther 2:7**

And he had a foster child, daughter of Aminadab, brother of his father, and her name was Esther; and when her parents passed away, he brought her up for himself as a wife, and the girl was beautiful in appearance.

**Greek Esther 2:8**

And when the ordinance of the king was heard, many girls were gathered to Susa the city under the hand of Gai, and Esther was led to Gai, the keeper of the women.

**Greek Esther 2:9**

And the girl pleased him and found favor before him, and he hastened to give her the cleansing preparation and the portion and the seven girls appointed to her from the royal house, and he treated her well and her maids in the women's quarters.

**Greek Esther 2:10**

And Esther did not disclose her race nor her fatherland, for Mordecai had commanded her not to report it.

**Greek Esther 2:11**

And on each day Mordecai walked along the women's court, watching what would happen to Esther.

**Greek Esther 2:12**

Now this was the time for a girl to enter to the king, whenever she completed twelve months; for thus the days of treatment are completed: six months being anointed with myrrh oil and six months with spices and with the cleansing preparations of the women,

**Greek Esther 2:13**

and then she enters to the king. And whatever she should say, he will hand it over to her to go in with her from the women's quarters to the palace.

**Greek Esther 2:14**

In the evening she enters, and toward day she returns to the second women's quarters, where Gai the eunuch of the king, the keeper of the women, is; and she no longer enters to the king unless she is called by name.

**Greek Esther 2:15**

And when the time was fulfilled for Esther, daughter of Aminadab, brother of Mordecai's father, to enter to the king, she set aside nothing of what the eunuch, the keeper of the women, commanded; for Esther was finding favor from all those seeing her.

**Greek Esther 2:16**

And Esther entered to King Artaxerxes in the twelfth month, which is Adar, in the seventh year of his kingdom.

**Greek Esther 2:17**

And the king loved Esther, and she found favor beyond all the virgins, and he placed the womanly diadem upon her.

**Greek Esther 2:18**

And the king made a drinking feast for all his friends and forces for seven days, and he exalted the wedding of Esther and made a release for those under his kingdom.

**Greek Esther 2:19**

And Mordecai was serving in the court.

**Greek Esther 2:20**

But Esther did not disclose her fatherland, for thus Mordecai had commanded her: to fear God and to do his ordinances, just as she was with him; and Esther did not change her way of life.

**Greek Esther 2:21**

And the two eunuchs of the king, the chief bodyguards, were grieved because Mordecai had been promoted, and they sought to kill King Artaxerxes.

**Greek Esther 2:22**

And the word was made clear to Mordecai, and he signaled Esther, and she disclosed the things of the plot to the king.

**Greek Esther 2:23**

And the king examined the two eunuchs and hanged them; and the king commanded it to be recorded for a memorial in the royal library concerning the favor of Mordecai, with praise.

### Chapter 3

**Greek Esther 3:1**

After these things King Artaxerxes glorified Haman son of Hamadathos, the Bougaian, and exalted him, and he set him first in rank of all his friends.

**Greek Esther 3:2**

And all those in the court worshiped him, for thus the king had commanded to do; but Mordecai did not worship him.

**Greek Esther 3:3**

And those in the court of the king spoke to Mordecai, Mordecai, why do you disobey the things spoken by the king?

**Greek Esther 3:4**

Day by day they spoke to him, and he did not obey them; and they disclosed to Haman that Mordecai was opposing the words of the king, and Mordecai disclosed to them that he was a Jew.

**Greek Esther 3:5**

And when Haman learned that Mordecai was not worshiping him, he was exceedingly enraged,

**Greek Esther 3:6**

and he took counsel to make vanish all the Jews under the kingdom of Artaxerxes.

**Greek Esther 3:7**

And he made a decree in the twelfth year of the kingdom of Artaxerxes and cast lots, day by day and month by month, so as to destroy the race of Mordecai in one day; and the lot fell on the fourteenth of the month, which is Adar.

**Greek Esther 3:8**

And he spoke to King Artaxerxes, saying, There exists a nation scattered among the nations in all your kingdom, and their laws are exceptional beyond all the nations, and they disobey the laws of the king, and it is not profitable for the king to allow them.

**Greek Esther 3:9**

If it seems good to the king, let him decree to destroy them, and I will assign ten thousand talents of silver to the treasury of the king.

**Greek Esther 3:10**

And the king, taking off the ring, gave it into the hands of Haman to seal against the things written against the Jews.

**Greek Esther 3:11**

And the king said to Haman, Keep the silver, and use the nation as you wish.

**Greek Esther 3:12**

And the scribes of the king were called in the first month, on the thirteenth, and they wrote, as Haman commanded, to the generals and to the rulers by every country from India to Ethiopia, to the 127 countries, and also to the rulers of the nations according to their language, through King Artaxerxes.

**Greek Esther 3:13**

And it was sent through letter-carriers into the kingdom of Artaxerxes to make vanish the race of the Jews in one day of the twelfth month, which is Adar, and to plunder their possessions.

**Greek Esther 3:13α**

And this is the copy of the letter: Great King Artaxerxes writes these things to the rulers and local governors subject to him from India to Ethiopia, 127 countries:

**Greek Esther 3:13β**

Having ruled over many nations and become master of the whole inhabited world, I wished, not being lifted up by the boldness of authority, but always conducting things more moderately and with gentleness, to establish the lives of those subject to me as untroubled through all time, and to provide a kingdom gentle and open to travel as far as the borders, and to renew the peace longed for by all humans.

**Greek Esther 3:13γ**

But when I inquired of the counselors how this might be brought to completion, Haman, who has surpassed among us in sound-mindedness and has been demonstrated in unchanging goodwill and firm faith, and who has received the second honor of the kingdoms,

**Greek Esther 3:13δ**

showed us that among all the tribes throughout the inhabited world a certain hostile people has been mixed in, opposed in laws to every nation and continually disregarding the ordinances of kings, so that the government directed blamelessly by us is not established.

**Greek Esther 3:13ε**

Therefore, having understood that this nation alone is set in opposition to every human through all time, changing by a strange way of laws and ill-minded toward our affairs, accomplishing the worst evils, and preventing the kingdom from obtaining stability,

**Greek Esther 3:13ζ**

therefore we have commanded that those indicated to you in the writings by Haman, the one appointed over the affairs and our second father, all of them with wives and children, be destroyed root and branch by the swords of enemies, without any mercy or sparing, on the fourteenth of the twelfth month Adar of the present year,

**Greek Esther 3:13η**

so that those long ago and now hostile, having violently gone down to Hades in one day, may for the time afterward provide our affairs stable and undisturbed through to the end.

**Greek Esther 3:14**

And the copies of the letters were set out by country, and all the nations were commanded to be ready for that day.

**Greek Esther 3:15**

And the matter was hastened also into Susa; and the king and Haman were drinking, but the city was troubled.

### Chapter 4

**Greek Esther 4:1**

But Mordecai, learning what was being completed, tore his own garments and put on sackcloth and sprinkled ashes, and rushing through the broad street of the city, he cried out with a great voice, A nation that has done no wrong is being taken away.

**Greek Esther 4:2**

And he came as far as the gate of the king and stood, for it was not permitted for him to enter the court while having sackcloth and ashes.

**Greek Esther 4:3**

And in every country where the letters were set out, there was outcry and beating and great mourning for the Jews; they spread sackcloth and ashes for themselves.

**Greek Esther 4:4**

And the maids and eunuchs of the queen entered and reported to her, and she was troubled when she heard what had happened, and she sent to clothe Mordecai and to remove his sackcloth; but he was not persuaded.

**Greek Esther 4:5**

And Esther called Achrathaios her eunuch, who stood beside her, and sent to learn from Mordecai the precise matter.

**Greek Esther 4:7**

And Mordecai disclosed to him what had happened and the promise that Haman had promised to the king for the treasury, ten thousand talents, in order to destroy the Jews;

**Greek Esther 4:8**

and the copy set out in Susa concerning their destruction he gave him to show Esther. And he told him to command her, when she entered, to entreat the king and ask him concerning the people, remembering the days of your humiliation, how you were raised in my hand, because Haman, the one second to the king, has spoken against us for death; call upon the Lord and speak to the king concerning us, to rescue us from death.

**Greek Esther 4:9**

And Achrathaios entered and spoke all these words to her.

**Greek Esther 4:10**

And Esther said to Achrathaios, Go to Mordecai and say,

**Greek Esther 4:11**

All the nations of the kingdom know that every human or woman who enters to the king into the inner court uncalled, there is no salvation for that one, except to whom the king stretches out the golden rod; this one will be saved. And I have not been called to enter to the king these thirty days.

**Greek Esther 4:12**

And Achrathaios reported all the words of Esther to Mordecai,

**Greek Esther 4:13**

and Mordecai said to Achrathaios, Go and say to her, Esther, do not say to yourself that you alone will be saved in the kingdom, beyond all the Jews;

**Greek Esther 4:14**

for if you disobey at this time, help and shelter will come to the Jews from another place, but you and the house of your father will perish. And who knows whether for this time you became queen?

**Greek Esther 4:15**

And Esther sent away the one who came to her, to Mordecai, saying,

**Greek Esther 4:16**

Go, gather the Jews who are in Susa and fast for me, and do not eat nor drink for three days, night and day; and I also and my maids will be without food, and then I will enter to the king contrary to the law, even if it is necessary for me to perish.

**Greek Esther 4:17**

And Mordecai went and did whatever Esther commanded him. [17α] And he entreated the Lord, remembering all the works of the Lord, and said, [17β] Lord, Lord, King ruling all, because the whole is in your authority, and there is no one opposing you when you wish to save Israel; [17γ] because you made heaven and earth and every wondered-at thing under heaven, and you are Lord of all, and there is no one who will oppose you, the Lord. [17δ] You know all things; you know, Lord, that not in insolence nor in pride nor in love of glory did I do this, not worshiping arrogant Haman, because I would have been pleased to kiss the soles of his feet for the salvation of Israel; [17ε] but I did this so that I might not place the glory of a human above the glory of God, and I will worship no one except you, my Lord, and I will not do these things in pride. [17ζ] And now, Lord God, King, God of Abraham, spare your people, because they look upon us for destruction and have desired to destroy your inheritance from the beginning; [17η] do not overlook your portion, which you redeemed for yourself from the land of Egypt; [17θ] hear my petition and be propitious to your lot, and turn our mourning into feasting, so that while living we may hymn your name, Lord; and do not make vanish the mouth of those praising you, Lord. [17ι] And all Israel cried out from their strength, because their death was in their eyes. [17κ] And Esther the queen fled for refuge to the Lord, being seized in a struggle of death, and taking off the garments of her glory she put on garments of constraint and mourning; and instead of proud sweet spices, she filled her head with ashes and dung, and humbled her body exceedingly, and every place of adornment of her gladness she filled with the twists of her hair; and she entreated the Lord God of Israel and said, [17λ] My Lord, you alone are our king; help me, the lone one and having no helper except you, because my danger is in my hand. [17μ] I heard from my birth in the tribe of my family that you, Lord, took Israel from all the nations and our fathers from all their ancestors for an eternal inheritance, and did for them all that you spoke. [17ν] And now we sinned before you, and you handed us into the hands of our enemies, because we glorified their gods. You are righteous, Lord. [17ξ] And now they were not satisfied with the bitterness of our slavery, but they set their hands upon the hands of their idols to remove the decree of your mouth and to make vanish your inheritance and to stop up the mouth of those praising you and to extinguish the glory of your house and your altar, [17ο] and to open the mouth of nations for the praises of worthless things and for a fleshly king to be admired forever. [17π] Do not hand over your scepter, Lord, to those who are not, and let them not laugh at our fall, but turn their counsel upon themselves, and make an example of the one who began against us. [17ρ] Remember, Lord; be made known in the time of our affliction, and embolden me, King of the gods and master of every rule. [17σ] Give ordered speech into my mouth before the lion, and change his heart to hatred of the one warring against us, for his ending and the ending of those agreeing with him. [17τ] But rescue us by your hand and help me, the lone one and having none except you, Lord. [17υ] You have knowledge of all things, and you know that I hated the glory of lawless ones and abhor the bed of uncircumcised ones and of every foreigner. [17φ] You know my necessity, because I abhor the sign of my pride, which is upon my head in the days of my appearing; I abhor it like a menstruation rag, and I do not wear it in the days of my quiet. [17χ] And your slave woman has not eaten the table of Haman, and I did not glorify the banquet of the king, nor did I drink wine of libations; [17ψ] and your slave woman has not rejoiced from the day of my change until now, except in you, Lord, God of Abraham. [17ω] O God, the one strong over all, listen to the voice of those without hope and rescue us from the hand of those doing evil, and rescue me from my fear.

### Chapter 5

**Greek Esther 5:1**

And it happened on the third day, when she ceased praying, she stripped off the garments of her service and put on her glory. [1α] And having become splendid, having called upon God the overseer and savior of all, she took along two maids; and on one she leaned as one being delicate, and the other followed, lightening her clothing. [1β] And she herself was blushing in the bloom of her beauty, and her face was cheerful as beloved, but her heart was constricted from fear. [1γ] And entering through all the doors, she stood before the king, and he was sitting upon the throne of his kingdom and had put on every robe of his splendor, all through with gold and costly stones, and he was exceedingly fearful. [1δ] And lifting his face, fired with glory in the height of wrath, he looked, and the queen fell and changed her color in faintness and bowed down upon the head of the maid going before. [1ε] And God changed the spirit of the king to gentleness, and in agony he leapt up from his throne and took her into his arms until she recovered, and he comforted her with peaceful words and said to her, [1ζ] What is it, Esther? I am your brother. Take courage; you shall not die, because our ordinance is common. Draw near.

**Greek Esther 5:2**

And lifting the golden rod, he placed it upon her neck and kissed her and said, Speak to me. [2α] And she said to him, I saw you, lord, as an angel of God, and my heart was troubled from fear of your glory, because you are marvelous, lord, and your face is full of favors. [2β] And while she was speaking, she fell from her faintness, and the king was troubled, and all his attendants comforted her.

**Greek Esther 5:3**

And the king said, What do you wish, Esther? And what is your request? Up to half of my kingdom, and it will be yours.

**Greek Esther 5:4**

And Esther said, My day is notable today. If therefore it seems good to the king, let him come, he and Haman, to the reception that I will make today.

**Greek Esther 5:5**

And the king said, Hasten Haman, so that we may do the word of Esther; and both came to the reception that Esther had spoken of.

**Greek Esther 5:6**

And during the drinking feast, the king said to Esther, What is it, Queen Esther? And whatever you ask will be yours.

**Greek Esther 5:7**

And she said, My request and my petition:

**Greek Esther 5:8**

If I have found favor before the king, let the king and Haman come still tomorrow to the reception that I will make for them, and tomorrow I will do the same things.

**Greek Esther 5:9**

And Haman went out from the king exceedingly joyful and rejoicing; but when Haman saw Mordecai the Jew in the court, he was exceedingly enraged,

**Greek Esther 5:10**

and entering into his own house, he called his friends and Zosara his wife,

**Greek Esther 5:11**

and disclosed to them his wealth and the glory that the king had put around him, and how he had made him be first and lead the kingdom.

**Greek Esther 5:12**

And Haman said, The queen has called no one with the king to the reception except me, and for tomorrow I have been called.

**Greek Esther 5:13**

And these things do not please me whenever I see Mordecai the Jew in the court.

**Greek Esther 5:14**

And Zosara his wife and the friends said to him, Let wood of fifty cubits be cut for you, and at dawn speak to the king, and let Mordecai be hanged upon the wood; but you enter into the reception with the king and rejoice. And the word pleased Haman, and the wood was prepared.

### Chapter 6

**Greek Esther 6:1**

But the Lord removed sleep from the king that night, and he told his minister to bring in memorial writings of the days to read to him.

**Greek Esther 6:2**

And he found the writings written about Mordecai, how he reported to the king concerning the two eunuchs of the king, while they were guarding, and their seeking to lay hands upon Artaxerxes.

**Greek Esther 6:3**

And the king said, What glory or favor have we done for Mordecai? And the ministers of the king said, You have done nothing for him.

**Greek Esther 6:4**

And while the king was inquiring concerning the goodwill of Mordecai, behold, Haman was in the court. And the king said, Who is in the court? Now Haman had entered to tell the king to hang Mordecai upon the wood that he had prepared.

**Greek Esther 6:5**

And the ministers of the king said, Behold, Haman is standing in the court. And the king said, Call him.

**Greek Esther 6:6**

And the king said to Haman, What shall I do for the human whom I wish to glorify? And Haman said in himself, Whom does the king wish to glorify except me?

**Greek Esther 6:7**

And he said to the king, A human whom the king wishes to glorify,

**Greek Esther 6:8**

let the servants of the king bring a fine linen robe that the king puts around himself, and a horse upon which the king rides,

**Greek Esther 6:9**

and let him give it to one of the glorious friends of the king, and let him clothe the human whom the king loves, and let him mount him upon the horse and proclaim through the broad street of the city, saying, Thus it will be for every human whom the king glorifies.

**Greek Esther 6:10**

And the king said to Haman, You spoke well. Do so to Mordecai the Jew, the one serving in the court, and let no word of yours fall aside from what you spoke.

**Greek Esther 6:11**

And Haman took the robe and the horse and clothed Mordecai, and mounted him upon the horse and went through the broad street of the city and proclaimed, saying, Thus it will be for every human whom the king wishes to glorify.

**Greek Esther 6:12**

And Mordecai returned to the court. But Haman returned to his own house grieving, with his head down.

**Greek Esther 6:13**

And Haman told the things that had happened to him to Zosara his wife and to the friends, and the friends and his wife said to him, If Mordecai is from the race of Jews, you have begun to be humbled before him; falling, you will fall, and you surely will not be able to defend against him, because the living God is with him.

**Greek Esther 6:14**

While they were still speaking, the eunuchs arrived, hurrying Haman to the drinking feast that Esther had prepared.

### Chapter 7

**Greek Esther 7:1**

And the king and Haman entered to drink together with the queen.

**Greek Esther 7:2**

And on the second day, during the drinking feast, the king said to Esther, What is it, Queen Esther, and what is your request, and what is your petition? Let it be yours, up to half of my kingdom.

**Greek Esther 7:3**

And answering, she said, If I have found favor before the king, let my soul be given to my request and my people to my petition;

**Greek Esther 7:4**

for we have been sold, both I and my people, for destruction and plunder and slavery, we and our children as male slaves and female slaves, and I kept silent; for the slanderer is not worthy of the court of the king.

**Greek Esther 7:5**

And the king said, Who is this, who dared to do this thing?

**Greek Esther 7:6**

And Esther said, A hostile human: Haman, this evil one. And Haman was troubled before the king and the queen.

**Greek Esther 7:7**

And the king rose up from the banquet into the garden; but Haman entreated the queen, for he saw himself being in evils.

**Greek Esther 7:8**

And the king returned from the garden, and Haman had fallen upon the couch, petitioning the queen. And the king said, So you even force the woman in my house? And when Haman heard, his face was turned.

**Greek Esther 7:9**

And Bugathan, one of the eunuchs, said to the king, Behold, Haman also prepared wood for Mordecai, the one who spoke concerning the king, and wood of fifty cubits has been set upright among Haman's things. And the king said, Let him be staked upon it.

**Greek Esther 7:10**

And Haman was hanged upon the wood that had been prepared for Mordecai. And then the king ceased from wrath.

### Chapter 8

**Greek Esther 8:1**

And on that same day King Artaxerxes gifted Esther whatever belonged to Haman the slanderer, and Mordecai was called by the king, for Esther had disclosed that he was kin to her.

**Greek Esther 8:2**

And the king took the ring that he had taken from Haman and gave it to Mordecai, and Esther appointed Mordecai over all the things of Haman.

**Greek Esther 8:3**

And adding, she spoke to the king and fell at his feet and was asking him to take away the evil of Haman and all that he had done to the Jews.

**Greek Esther 8:4**

And the king stretched out the golden rod to Esther, and Esther was raised to stand beside the king,

**Greek Esther 8:5**

and Esther said, If it seems good to you and I have found favor, let it be sent to turn back the letters sent by Haman, the ones written to destroy the Jews who are in your kingdom;

**Greek Esther 8:6**

for how will I be able to see the mistreatment of my people, and how will I be able to be saved in the destruction of my fatherland?

**Greek Esther 8:7**

And the king said to Esther, If I have given and granted you all the possessions of Haman, and hanged him on wood because he brought his hands against the Jews, what do you still seek?

**Greek Esther 8:8**

Write also yourselves in my name, as it seems good to you, and seal with my ring; for whatever you write by command of the king and is sealed with my ring, it is not possible for them to contradict.

**Greek Esther 8:9**

And the scribes were called in the first month, which is Nisan, on the twenty-third of the same year, and it was written to the Jews whatever he commanded to the stewards and the rulers of the satraps, from India to Ethiopia, to 127 satraps, country by country, according to their own language.

**Greek Esther 8:10**

And it was written through the king and sealed with his ring, and they sent out the letters through letter-carriers,

**Greek Esther 8:11**

as he commanded them to use their laws in every city, and to help themselves and to use their adversaries and those opposing them as they wished,

**Greek Esther 8:12**

on one day in all the kingdom of Artaxerxes, on the thirteenth of the twelfth month, which is Adar.

**Greek Esther 8:12α**

Of these, the things written below are a copy of the letter:

**Greek Esther 8:12β**

Great King Artaxerxes, to the rulers of the 127 satrapies of countries from India to Ethiopia and to those mindful of our interests, greetings.

**Greek Esther 8:12γ**

Many, being honored more often by the greatest kindness of benefactors, became more proud and seek not only to harm those subject to us, but being unable to bear satiety, they attempt to scheme against their own benefactors;

**Greek Esther 8:12δ**

and not only taking away gratitude from humans, but also being lifted up by the boasts of those inexperienced in good things, they suppose they will escape the evil-hating justice of God, who always watches all things.

**Greek Esther 8:12ε**

And often the encouragement of friends entrusted to handle affairs, making many of those appointed over authorities partners in innocent blood, has wrapped them in irremediable calamities,

**Greek Esther 8:12ζ**

by the false deception of evil character, deceiving the sincere goodwill of those holding power.

**Greek Esther 8:12η**

And it is possible to observe, not so much from the older histories that we have handed down, as from the things near at hand to you, as you seek out what things have been impiously completed by the pestilence of those unworthily exercising power,

**Greek Esther 8:12θ**

and to pay attention to the things after these, so that we may provide the kingdom undisturbed with peace for all humans,

**Greek Esther 8:12ι**

using changes and always discerning the things coming under sight with a more equitable response.

**Greek Esther 8:12κ**

For Haman son of Hamadathos, a Macedonian, truly a foreigner to the blood of the Persians and much removed from our kindness, having been received as a guest by us,

**Greek Esther 8:12λ**

obtained so much of the love for humanity that we have toward every nation that he was proclaimed our father and continued as the second person of the royal throne, worshiped by all;

**Greek Esther 8:12μ**

but not bearing the arrogance, he undertook to deprive us of rule and of spirit,

**Greek Esther 8:12ν**

having requested for destruction our savior and continual benefactor Mordecai and Esther, the blameless sharer of the kingdom, with all their nation, by many-woven deceptions of methods;

**Greek Esther 8:12ξ**

for through these ways he thought that, having taken us bereft, he would transfer the dominion of the Persians to the Macedonians.

**Greek Esther 8:12ο**

But we find that the Jews, handed over to destruction by the thrice-criminal one, are not evildoers, but are governed by most righteous laws,

**Greek Esther 8:12π**

and are sons of the Most High, greatest, living God, who directs the kingdom for us and for our ancestors in the fairest arrangement.

**Greek Esther 8:12ρ**

Therefore you will do well not making use of the letters sent by Haman son of Hamadathos, because he himself who produced these things has been staked at the gates of Susa with his whole household, God who rules all having swiftly given him the judgment he deserved.

**Greek Esther 8:12σ**

And setting out the copy of this letter openly in every place, allow the Jews to use their own customs and strengthen them together, so that on the thirteenth of the twelfth month Adar, the same day, they may defend themselves against those attacking them in time of affliction;

**Greek Esther 8:12τ**

for this day God who rules all made for them gladness instead of the destruction of the chosen race.

**Greek Esther 8:12υ**

And therefore, among your named feasts, you also shall celebrate a notable day with every feasting, so that both now and after these things there may be salvation for us and for the well-disposed Persians, but for those plotting against us a memorial of destruction.

**Greek Esther 8:12φ**

But every city or country as a whole, whoever does not do according to these things, will be consumed with spear and fire in wrath; it will be made not only untrodden by humans, but also most hateful to wild beasts and birds for all time.

**Greek Esther 8:13**

And let the copies be set out visibly in all the kingdom, and let all the Jews be ready for this day, to war against their opponents.

**Greek Esther 8:14**

Therefore the horsemen went out, hurrying to complete the things spoken by the king; and the ordinance was also set out in Susa.

**Greek Esther 8:15**

And Mordecai went out clothed in the royal robe and having a golden crown and a purple fine-linen diadem; and those in Susa saw and rejoiced.

**Greek Esther 8:16**

And for the Jews there came to be light and gladness;

**Greek Esther 8:17**

by city and country, wherever the ordinance was set out, wherever the posted decree was set out, there was joy and gladness for the Jews, a drinking feast and gladness. And many of the nations were circumcised and Judaized because of the fear of the Jews.

### Chapter 9

**Greek Esther 9:1**

For in the twelfth month, on the thirteenth of the month, which is Adar, the letters written by the king were present.

**Greek Esther 9:2**

On that same day those opposing the Jews perished, for no one stood against them, fearing them.

**Greek Esther 9:3**

For the rulers of the satraps and the local rulers and the royal scribes were honoring the Jews, for the fear of Mordecai lay upon them.

**Greek Esther 9:4**

For the ordinance of the king came to be named in all the kingdom, and it was being magnified.

**Greek Esther 9:5**

And the Jews struck a blow among all their enemies, a blow of sword and killing and destruction, and did among those hating them according to their will.

**Greek Esther 9:6**

And in Susa the city, the Jews killed five hundred men,

**Greek Esther 9:7**

both Pharsannes and Delphon and Phasga,

**Greek Esther 9:8**

and Pharadatha and Barea and Sarbaka,

**Greek Esther 9:9**

and Marmasima and Rouphaios and Arsaios and Zabouthaios,

**Greek Esther 9:10**

the ten sons of Haman son of Hamadathos, the Bougaian, the enemy of the Jews, and they plundered.

**Greek Esther 9:11**

On that same day the number of those destroyed in Susa was handed over to the king.

**Greek Esther 9:12**

And the king said to Esther, "The Jews destroyed five hundred men in Susa the city; but in the surrounding region, how do you think they dealt with them? What then do you still ask, and it will be yours?"

**Greek Esther 9:13**

And Esther said to the king, "Let it be given to the Jews to deal likewise tomorrow, so as to hang the ten sons of Haman."

**Greek Esther 9:14**

And he permitted it to be so and set out for the Jews of the city the bodies of the sons of Haman to hang.

**Greek Esther 9:15**

And the Jews in Susa were gathered on the fourteenth of Adar and killed three hundred men and plundered nothing.

**Greek Esther 9:16**

But the rest of the Jews in the kingdom were gathered and were helping themselves and rested from the enemies; for they destroyed fifteen thousand of them on the thirteenth of Adar and plundered nothing.

**Greek Esther 9:17**

And they rested on the fourteenth of the same month and kept it as a day of rest with joy and gladness.

**Greek Esther 9:18**

But the Jews in Susa the city were gathered also on the fourteenth and rested; and they kept also the fifteenth with joy and gladness.

**Greek Esther 9:19**

Therefore the Jews scattered in every outside country keep the fourteenth of Adar as a good day with gladness, each sending portions to his neighbor.

**Greek Esther 9:20**

And Mordecai wrote these words in a book and sent it out to the Jews, as many as were in the kingdom of Artaxerxes, to those near and those far,

**Greek Esther 9:21**

to establish these days as good, and to keep both the fourteenth and the fifteenth of Adar. [22] For in these days the Jews rested from their enemies, and the month in which it was turned for them, which was Adar, from mourning into joy and from pain into a good day, to keep the whole as good days of weddings and gladness, sending portions to friends and to the poor.

**Greek Esther 9:23**

And the Jews received it, just as Mordecai had written to them,

**Greek Esther 9:24**

how Haman son of Hamadathos, the Macedonian, made war on them, how he set a decree and lot to make them vanish,

**Greek Esther 9:25**

and how he entered to the king, saying to hang Mordecai; but whatever evils he attempted to bring upon the Jews came upon him, and he himself was hanged, and his children.

**Greek Esther 9:26**

Therefore these days were called Phrourai because of the lots, because in their dialect they are called Phrourai, because of the words of this letter and all that they suffered because of these things and all that happened to them,

**Greek Esther 9:27**

and he established it; and the Jews received it for themselves and for their seed and for those joined to them, and surely they will not use other days. And these days are a memorial being performed by generation and generation and city and family and country.

**Greek Esther 9:28**

And these days of Phrourai will be kept for all time, and their memorial shall surely not fail from the generations.

**Greek Esther 9:29**

And Queen Esther daughter of Aminadab and Mordecai the Jew wrote all that they had done, and the confirmation of the letter of Phrourai.

**Greek Esther 9:30**

And Mordecai and Esther the queen established it for themselves by themselves, and then, having established it for their own welfare and their counsel,

**Greek Esther 9:31**

And Esther established it by word forever, and it was written for a memorial.

### Chapter 10

**Greek Esther 10:1**

And the king wrote concerning the kingdom of both the land and the sea,

**Greek Esther 10:2**

and his strength and manly valor, and the wealth and glory of his kingdom, behold, it is written in the book of the kings of the Persians and Medes for a memorial.

**Greek Esther 10:3**

And Mordecai stood next after King Artaxerxes and was great in the kingdom and glorified by the Jews; and being loved, he described his way of life to all his nation.

**Greek Esther 10:3α**

And Mordecai said, "These things came from God. [3β] For I remembered concerning the dream that I saw concerning these matters; for no word from them passed by."

**Greek Esther 10:3γ**

The small spring, which became a river, and there was light and sun and much water: Esther is the river, whom the king married and made queen.

**Greek Esther 10:3δ**

And the two dragons are I and Haman.

**Greek Esther 10:3ε**

And the nations are those gathered together to destroy the name of the Jews.

**Greek Esther 10:3ζ**

And my nation, this is Israel, those who cried out to God and were saved; and the Lord saved his people, and the Lord rescued us from all these evils. And God did the signs and the great wonders that have not happened among the nations.

**Greek Esther 10:3η**

Because of this he made two lots, one for the people of God and one for all the nations;

**Greek Esther 10:3θ**

and these two lots came to hour and time and day of judgment before God and all the nations,

**Greek Esther 10:3ι**

and God remembered his people and justified his inheritance;

**Greek Esther 10:3κ**

and these days will be for them in the month Adar, on the fourteenth and fifteenth of the month, with gathering and joy and gladness before God by generations forever among his people Israel.

**Greek Esther 10:3λ**

In the fourth year when Ptolemy and Cleopatra were reigning, Dositheos, who said he was a priest and Levite, and Ptolemy his son brought in the preceding letter of Phrourai, which they said was authentic and that Lysimachos son of Ptolemy, of those in Jerusalem, had translated.

## Greek Esther Additions

### Chapter 1

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1α**

In the second year, when Artaxerxes the great king was reigning, on the first day of Nisan, Mordecai son of Jairus, son of Semeios, son of Kisaios, from the tribe of Benjamin, saw a dream,

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1β**

a Jewish human dwelling in Susa the city, a great human, serving in the court of the king.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1γ**

And he was from the captivity that Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon took captive from Jerusalem with Jeconiah king of Judea.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1δ**

And this was his dream: and behold, voices and uproar, thunder and earthquake, disturbance upon the earth.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ε**

And behold, two great dragons came forth, both ready to wrestle, and their great voice came to be;

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ζ**

and at their voice every nation was prepared for war, so as to war against a nation of righteous ones.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1η**

And behold, a day of darkness and gloom, affliction and constraint, mistreatment and great disturbance upon the earth;

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1θ**

and every righteous nation was troubled, fearing their own evils, and they were prepared to perish and cried out to God.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ι**

But from their cry there came to be, as from a small spring, a great river, much water;

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1κ**

and light and sun rose, and the humble were exalted and devoured the glorious ones.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1λ**

And Mordecai, having awakened, the one who had seen this dream and what God had planned to do, kept it in his heart and in every matter desired to understand it until the night.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1μ**

And Mordecai rested in the court with Gabatha and Thara, the two eunuchs of the king, those guarding the court,

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ν**

and he heard their thoughts and searched out their anxieties and learned that they were preparing their hands to lay upon King Artaxerxes, and he disclosed concerning them to the king.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ξ**

And the king examined the two eunuchs, and after confessing, they were led away.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ο**

And the king wrote these words for a memorial, and Mordecai wrote concerning these words.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1π**

And the king ordered Mordecai to serve in the court and gave him gifts concerning these things.

**Greek Esther Additions 1:1ρ**

And Haman son of Hamadathos, the Bougaian, was glorious before the king; and he sought to do evil to Mordecai and his people because of the two eunuchs of the king.

### Chapter 3

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13α**

And this is the copy of the letter: Great King Artaxerxes writes these things to the rulers and local governors subject to him from India to Ethiopia, 127 countries:

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13β**

Having ruled over many nations and become master of the whole inhabited world, I wished, not being lifted up by the boldness of authority, but always conducting things more moderately and with gentleness, to establish the lives of those subject to me as untroubled through all time, and to provide a kingdom gentle and open to travel as far as the borders, and to renew the peace longed for by all humans.

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13γ**

But when I inquired of the counselors how this might be brought to completion, Haman, who has surpassed among us in sound-mindedness and has been demonstrated in unchanging goodwill and firm faith, and who has received the second honor of the kingdoms,

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13δ**

showed us that among all the tribes throughout the inhabited world a certain hostile people has been mixed in, opposed in laws to every nation and continually disregarding the ordinances of kings, so that the government directed blamelessly by us is not established.

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13ε**

Therefore, having understood that this nation alone is set in opposition to every human through all time, changing by a strange way of laws and ill-minded toward our affairs, accomplishing the worst evils, and preventing the kingdom from obtaining stability,

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13ζ**

therefore we have commanded that those indicated to you in the writings by Haman, the one appointed over the affairs and our second father, all of them with wives and children, be destroyed root and branch by the swords of enemies, without any mercy or sparing, on the fourteenth of the twelfth month Adar of the present year,

**Greek Esther Additions 3:13η**

so that those long ago and now hostile, having violently gone down to Hades in one day, may for the time afterward provide our affairs stable and undisturbed through to the end.

### Chapter 8

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12α**

Of these, the things written below are a copy of the letter:

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12β**

Great King Artaxerxes, to the rulers of the 127 satrapies of countries from India to Ethiopia and to those mindful of our interests, greetings.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12γ**

Many, being honored more often by the greatest kindness of benefactors, became more proud and seek not only to harm those subject to us, but being unable to bear satiety, they attempt to scheme against their own benefactors;

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12δ**

and not only taking away gratitude from humans, but also being lifted up by the boasts of those inexperienced in good things, they suppose they will escape the evil-hating justice of God, who always watches all things.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ε**

And often the encouragement of friends entrusted to handle affairs, making many of those appointed over authorities partners in innocent blood, has wrapped them in irremediable calamities,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ζ**

by the false deception of evil character, deceiving the sincere goodwill of those holding power.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12η**

And it is possible to observe, not so much from the older histories that we have handed down, as from the things near at hand to you, as you seek out what things have been impiously completed by the pestilence of those unworthily exercising power,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12θ**

and to pay attention to the things after these, so that we may provide the kingdom undisturbed with peace for all humans,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ι**

using changes and always discerning the things coming under sight with a more equitable response.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12κ**

For Haman son of Hamadathos, a Macedonian, truly a foreigner to the blood of the Persians and much removed from our kindness, having been received as a guest by us,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12λ**

obtained so much of the love for humanity that we have toward every nation that he was proclaimed our father and continued as the second person of the royal throne, worshiped by all;

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12μ**

but not bearing the arrogance, he undertook to deprive us of rule and of spirit,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ν**

having requested for destruction our savior and continual benefactor Mordecai and Esther, the blameless sharer of the kingdom, with all their nation, by many-woven deceptions of methods;

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ξ**

for through these ways he thought that, having taken us bereft, he would transfer the dominion of the Persians to the Macedonians.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ο**

But we find that the Jews, handed over to destruction by the thrice-criminal one, are not evildoers, but are governed by most righteous laws,

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12π**

and are sons of the Most High, greatest, living God, who directs the kingdom for us and for our ancestors in the fairest arrangement.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12ρ**

Therefore you will do well not making use of the letters sent by Haman son of Hamadathos, because he himself who produced these things has been staked at the gates of Susa with his whole household, God who rules all having swiftly given him the judgment he deserved.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12σ**

And setting out the copy of this letter openly in every place, allow the Jews to use their own customs and strengthen them together, so that on the thirteenth of the twelfth month Adar, the same day, they may defend themselves against those attacking them in time of affliction;

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12τ**

for this day God who rules all made for them gladness instead of the destruction of the chosen race.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12υ**

And therefore, among your named feasts, you also shall celebrate a notable day with every feasting, so that both now and after these things there may be salvation for us and for the well-disposed Persians, but for those plotting against us a memorial of destruction.

**Greek Esther Additions 8:12φ**

But every city or country as a whole, whoever does not do according to these things, will be consumed with spear and fire in wrath; it will be made not only untrodden by humans, but also most hateful to wild beasts and birds for all time.

### Chapter 10

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3α**

And Mordecai said, "These things came from God. [3β] For I remembered concerning the dream that I saw concerning these matters; for no word from them passed by."

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3γ**

The small spring, which became a river, and there was light and sun and much water: Esther is the river, whom the king married and made queen.

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3δ**

And the two dragons are I and Haman.

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3ε**

And the nations are those gathered together to destroy the name of the Jews.

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3ζ**

And my nation, this is Israel, those who cried out to God and were saved; and the Lord saved his people, and the Lord rescued us from all these evils. And God did the signs and the great wonders that have not happened among the nations.

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3η**

Because of this he made two lots, one for the people of God and one for all the nations;

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3θ**

and these two lots came to hour and time and day of judgment before God and all the nations,

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3ι**

and God remembered his people and justified his inheritance;

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3κ**

and these days will be for them in the month Adar, on the fourteenth and fifteenth of the month, with gathering and joy and gladness before God by generations forever among his people Israel.

**Greek Esther Additions 10:3λ**

In the fourth year when Ptolemy and Cleopatra were reigning, Dositheos, who said he was a priest and Levite, and Ptolemy his son brought in the preceding letter of Phrourai, which they said was authentic and that Lysimachos son of Ptolemy, of those in Jerusalem, had translated.

## Wisdom

### Chapter 1

**Wisdom 1:1**

Love righteousness, you who judge the earth; think concerning the Lord in goodness, and seek him in simplicity of heart;

**Wisdom 1:2**

because he is found by those who do not test him, and appears to those who do not disbelieve him.

**Wisdom 1:3**

For crooked reasonings separate from God, and power, when tested, reproves the foolish.

**Wisdom 1:4**

because wisdom will not enter into an evil-working soul, nor dwell in a body under debt to sin;

**Wisdom 1:5**

for a holy spirit of discipline will flee deceit, and will depart from senseless reasonings, and will be reproved when unrighteousness comes upon it.

**Wisdom 1:6**

For wisdom is a man-loving spirit, and it will not acquit a blasphemer from his lips; because God is witness of his kidneys and a true overseer of his heart and a hearer of his tongue;

**Wisdom 1:7**

because the Spirit of the Lord has filled the inhabited world, and that which holds all things together has knowledge of voice.

**Wisdom 1:8**

Therefore no one speaking unrighteous things shall surely hide, nor shall justice, when reproving, pass him by.

**Wisdom 1:9**

For in the deliberations of an ungodly man there will be examination, and a hearing of his words will come to the Lord for reproof of his lawless deeds;

**Wisdom 1:10**

because an ear of zeal hears all things, and a murmur of grumblings is not hidden.
