**Wisdom 1:11**

Therefore guard yourselves from unprofitable grumbling, and spare your tongue from slander; because a secret utterance will not go empty, and a mouth that lies kills a soul.

**Wisdom 1:12**

Do not seek death with zeal in the wandering of your life, nor draw destruction upon yourselves by the works of your hands;

**Wisdom 1:13**

because God did not make death, nor does he delight in destruction of the living.

**Wisdom 1:14**

For he created all things for being, and the generations of the world are saving, and there is no poison of destruction in them, nor kingdom of Hades upon earth.

**Wisdom 1:15**

For righteousness is immortal.

**Wisdom 1:16**

But ungodly ones called him to themselves with hands and words; counting him a friend, they wasted away, and made a covenant with him, because they are worthy to belong to his portion.

### Chapter 2

**Wisdom 2:1**

For they said among themselves, reasoning not rightly, "Our life is short and sorrowful, and there is no healing at a man's end, and no one has been known who released from Hades.

**Wisdom 2:2**

For we were born by chance, and after this we shall be as not having existed; because the breath in our nostrils is smoke, and speech is a spark in the movement of our heart,

**Wisdom 2:3**

when it is quenched, the body will turn out as ashes, and the spirit will be poured out like loose air.

**Wisdom 2:4**

And our name will be forgotten in time, and no one will remember our works; and our life will pass by like traces of a cloud, and like mist it will be scattered, driven by rays of the sun and weighed down by its heat.

**Wisdom 2:5**

For our life is a shadow's passing, and there is no turning back of our end, because it was sealed, and no one returns.

**Wisdom 2:6**

Come then, and let us enjoy the good things that exist, and let us use creation eagerly as in youth.

**Wisdom 2:7**

Let us be filled with costly wine and perfumes, and let no flower of air pass us by.

**Wisdom 2:8**

Let us crown ourselves with rosebuds before they wither.

**Wisdom 2:9**

Let none of us be without share in our proud revelry; let us leave tokens of gladness everywhere, because this is our portion and this lot.

**Wisdom 2:10**

Let us oppress a righteous poor man; let us not spare a widow, nor show reverence for an elder's gray hairs of many years.

**Wisdom 2:11**

But let our strength be law of righteousness, for the weak thing is proved useless.

**Wisdom 2:12**

And let us lie in wait for the righteous man, because he is troublesome to us and opposes our works and reproaches us with sins of law and reports against us sins of our discipline;

**Wisdom 2:13**

he promises to have knowledge of God and names himself child of the Lord;

**Wisdom 2:14**

he became to us a reproof of our thoughts; he is burdensome to us even when seen,

**Wisdom 2:15**

because his life is unlike others, and his paths have been changed;

**Wisdom 2:16**

we were counted to him as counterfeit, and he keeps away from our ways as from impurities; he blesses the last things of the righteous and boasts of God as father.

**Wisdom 2:17**

Let us see if his words are true, and let us test the things at his outcome;

**Wisdom 2:18**

for if the righteous one is son of God, he will help him and rescue him out of the hand of those standing against him.

**Wisdom 2:19**

Let us test him with insult and torture, so that we may know his gentleness and test his endurance of evil;

**Wisdom 2:20**

let us condemn him to a shameful death, for there will be visitation for him according to his words."

**Wisdom 2:21**

These things they reasoned, and they were led astray; for their wickedness blinded them,

**Wisdom 2:22**

and they did not know mysteries of God, nor hope for a wage of holiness, nor judge the prize of blameless souls.

**Wisdom 2:23**

because God created man for incorruption and made him an image of his own nature;

**Wisdom 2:24**

but by envy of the devil death entered into the world,

**Wisdom 2:25**

and those who are of his portion experience him.

### Chapter 3

**Wisdom 3:1**

But souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and torment shall surely not touch them.

**Wisdom 3:2**

In the eyes of foolish ones they seemed to have died, and their departure was reckoned harm,

**Wisdom 3:3**

and their going from us a crushing, but they are in peace.

**Wisdom 3:4**

For even if in the sight of men they are chastened, their hope is full of immortality;

**Wisdom 3:5**

and having been disciplined a little, they will be greatly benefited, because God tested them and found them worthy of himself;

**Wisdom 3:6**

as gold in a furnace he tested them, and as a whole-burnt offering of sacrifice he accepted them.

**Wisdom 3:7**

And in the time of their visitation they will shine forth and run like sparks in stubble;

**Wisdom 3:8**

they will judge nations and rule peoples, and the Lord will reign over them into the ages.

**Wisdom 3:9**

Those who trust upon him will understand truth, and the faithful in love will remain with him, because grace and mercy are among his holy ones, and visitation among his chosen ones.

**Wisdom 3:10**

But the ungodly will have punishment according as they reasoned, those who neglected the righteous one and departed from the Lord.

**Wisdom 3:11**

For the one who despises wisdom and discipline is miserable, and their hope is empty, and their labors unprofitable, and their works useless;

**Wisdom 3:12**

their women are foolish, and their children wicked; cursed is their generation.

**Wisdom 3:13**

because blessed is the barren woman who is undefiled, who did not know a bed in transgression; she will have fruit in visitation of souls,

**Wisdom 3:14**

and the eunuch who did not work a lawless deed with hand, nor consider evil things against the Lord; for chosen grace of faith will be given to him and a more delightful lot in the temple of the Lord.

**Wisdom 3:15**

For the fruit of good labors is renowned, and the root of prudence is unfailing.

**Wisdom 3:16**

But children of adulterers will be incomplete, and seed from a lawless bed will vanish.

**Wisdom 3:17**

For even if they become long-lived, they will be reckoned as nothing, and their old age at last will be without honor;

**Wisdom 3:18**

and if they die quickly, they will have no hope, nor consolation in a day of discernment;

**Wisdom 3:19**

for the ends of an unrighteous generation are hard.

### Chapter 4

**Wisdom 4:1**

Better is childlessness with virtue; for immortality is in its memory, because it is known both by God and by men;

**Wisdom 4:2**

while present they imitate it, and when departed they long for it; and in the age, wearing a crown, it marches in procession, having won the contest of undefiled contests.

**Wisdom 4:3**

But a many-childed multitude of ungodly ones will not be useful, and from bastard shoots it will not give root into depth, nor establish a secure foundation;

**Wisdom 4:4**

for even if for a time it sprouts in branches, standing unsafely it will be shaken by wind and uprooted by force of winds.

**Wisdom 4:5**

Incomplete branches will be broken off, and their fruit useless, unripe for food and fit for nothing;

**Wisdom 4:6**

for children born from lawless sleeps are witnesses of wickedness against parents in their examination.

**Wisdom 4:7**

But a righteous one, if he comes early to death, will be in rest;

**Wisdom 4:8**

for honorable old age is not the long-lasting one, nor is it measured by number of years;

**Wisdom 4:9**

but prudence is gray hair for men, and an unstained life is age of old age.

**Wisdom 4:10**

Having become pleasing to God, he was loved, and while living among sinners he was transferred;

**Wisdom 4:11**

he was caught away, lest wickedness change his understanding or deceit deceive his soul;

**Wisdom 4:12**

for the evil eye of worthlessness darkens good things, and the wandering of desire changes an innocent mind.

**Wisdom 4:13**

Having been perfected in a little time, he fulfilled long times,

**Wisdom 4:14**

for his soul was pleasing to the Lord; therefore he hastened from the midst of wickedness. But the peoples saw and did not understand, nor put such a thing in mind,

**Wisdom 4:15**

that grace and mercy are with his chosen ones, and visitation among his holy ones.

**Wisdom 4:16**

And a righteous one who has died will condemn the living ungodly, and youth quickly completed will condemn the many-year old age of an unrighteous one;

**Wisdom 4:17**

for they will see the end of a wise man and will not understand what the Lord purposed concerning him and for what he secured him.

**Wisdom 4:18**

They will see and despise, but the Lord will laugh them to scorn,

**Wisdom 4:19**

and they will be after this for a dishonored fall and for outrage among the dead forever, because he will burst them speechless, face down, and shake them from foundations, and to the last they will be made desolate, and they will be in pain, and their memory will perish.

**Wisdom 4:20**

They will come cowardly in a reckoning of their sins, and their lawless deeds will convict them face to face.

### Chapter 5

**Wisdom 5:1**

Then the righteous one will stand in great boldness before the face of those who afflicted him and those setting his labors at naught.

**Wisdom 5:2**

Having seen, they will be troubled with terrible fear and will be amazed at the wonder of salvation.

**Wisdom 5:3**

They will say among themselves, repenting, and through distress of spirit they will groan and say,

**Wisdom 5:4**

This was he whom we fools once had for laughter and for a parable of reproach; we reckoned his life madness and his end dishonored.

**Wisdom 5:5**

How was he counted among sons of God, and his lot is among holy ones?

**Wisdom 5:6**

So then we wandered from the way of truth, and the light of righteousness did not shine for us, and the sun did not rise for us;

**Wisdom 5:7**

we were filled with paths of lawlessness and destruction, and we journeyed through trackless deserts, but the way of the Lord we did not know.

**Wisdom 5:8**

What did arrogance profit us? And what has wealth with boastfulness contributed to us?

**Wisdom 5:9**

All those things passed by as a shadow and as a message running by;

**Wisdom 5:10**

as a ship passing through billowy water, of which, when it has crossed, no trace can be found, nor path of its keel in waves;

**Wisdom 5:11**

or as when a bird flies through air, no proof of its going is found; but the light breath, whipped by blow of pinions and split by force of whirring, was crossed by moving wings, and after this no sign of passage was found in it;

**Wisdom 5:12**

or as when an arrow is shot at a mark, the cut air immediately dissolved back into itself, so that its passage is unknown.

**Wisdom 5:13**

So also we, having been born, came to an end, and we had no sign of virtue to show, but in our wickedness we were consumed.

**Wisdom 5:14**

Because hope of an ungodly man is like chaff borne by wind and like thin frost driven by storm and like smoke diffused by wind, and it passed by like remembrance of a one-day guest.

**Wisdom 5:15**

But righteous ones live forever, and their reward is in the Lord, and care for them is with the Most High.

**Wisdom 5:16**

Therefore they will receive the royal ornament of dignity and the diadem of beauty from the hand of the Lord, because with his right hand he will shelter them and with his arm he will defend them.

**Wisdom 5:17**

He will take his zeal as full armor and will make creation into weapons for defense against enemies;

**Wisdom 5:18**

he will put on righteousness as a breastplate and put around himself sincere judgment as a helmet;

**Wisdom 5:19**

he will take holiness as an invincible shield,

**Wisdom 5:20**

and he will sharpen severe wrath into a sword, and the world will fight together with him against the senseless.

**Wisdom 5:21**

Well-aimed bolts of lightning will go, and as from a well-rounded bow of clouds they will leap to the target,

**Wisdom 5:22**

and hailstones full of wrath will be thrown as from a stone-thrower. Sea water will rage against them, and rivers will wash them away violently.

**Wisdom 5:23**

A spirit of power will stand against them and like a storm winnow them away. And lawlessness will lay waste all the earth, and evil-doing will overturn thrones of rulers.

### Chapter 6

**Wisdom 6:1**

Hear, then, kings, and understand; learn, judges of the ends of earth.

**Wisdom 6:2**

Give ear, you who hold a multitude and boast over crowds of nations;

**Wisdom 6:3**

because dominion was given to you from the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High, who will examine your works and search out your counsels;

**Wisdom 6:4**

because being servants of his kingdom, you did not judge rightly, nor keep law, nor walk according to the counsel of God.

**Wisdom 6:5**

Dreadfully and quickly he will stand upon you, because sharp judgment happens among those who excel.

**Wisdom 6:6**

For the least is pardonable with mercy, but mighty ones will be mightily tested;

**Wisdom 6:7**

for the Master of all will not shrink from a face, nor will he respect greatness, because he made small and great alike and takes forethought for all likewise;

**Wisdom 6:8**

but for the mighty, strong searching stands near.

**Wisdom 6:9**

To you therefore, O rulers, are my words, so that you may learn wisdom and not fall aside;

**Wisdom 6:10**

for those who holy keep holy things will be made holy, and those taught them will find a defense.

**Wisdom 6:11**

Therefore desire my words, long for them, and you will be disciplined.

**Wisdom 6:12**

Bright and unfading is wisdom, and she is easily beheld by those who love her and found by those who seek her;

**Wisdom 6:13**

she goes before those who desire her, to be known beforehand.

**Wisdom 6:14**

The one who rises early toward her will not toil, for he will find her sitting beside his gates.

**Wisdom 6:15**

For to think concerning her is perfection of prudence, and the one who keeps watch because of her will quickly be without care;

**Wisdom 6:16**

because she goes around seeking those worthy of her, and in the paths she appears to them favorably, and in every thought she meets them.

**Wisdom 6:17**

For her beginning is the truest desire of discipline, and care for discipline is love,

**Wisdom 6:18**

and love is keeping of her laws, and attention to laws is confirmation of incorruption,

**Wisdom 6:19**

and incorruption makes one to be near God.

**Wisdom 6:20**

Therefore desire of wisdom leads up to kingdom.

**Wisdom 6:21**

If then you delight in thrones and scepters, rulers of peoples, honor wisdom, so that you may reign forever.

**Wisdom 6:22**

But what wisdom is and how she came to be, I will report, and I will not hide mysteries from you, but from the beginning of creation I will trace her out and set her knowledge into the open, and surely I will not pass by the truth.

**Wisdom 6:23**

Neither indeed will I travel with wasting envy, because this will not share with wisdom.

**Wisdom 6:24**

But a multitude of wise ones is salvation of the world, and a prudent king is stability of a people.

**Wisdom 6:25**

Therefore be disciplined by my sayings, and you will be benefited.

### Chapter 7

**Wisdom 7:1**

I also am a mortal man, equal to all, and offspring of the earthborn first-formed one; and in my mother's womb I was molded as flesh,

**Wisdom 7:2**

in a ten-month time, coagulated in blood from seed of a man and pleasure that came together with sleep.

**Wisdom 7:3**

And I also, when born, drew the common air and fell upon the same-suffering earth, crying the first voice like all, equally;

**Wisdom 7:4**

in swaddling cloths I was nourished and in cares;

**Wisdom 7:5**

for no king had another beginning of birth,

**Wisdom 7:6**

but one entrance into life belongs to all, and an equal departure.

**Wisdom 7:7**

Because of this I prayed, and prudence was given to me; I called upon, and a spirit of wisdom came to me.

**Wisdom 7:8**

I preferred her before scepters and thrones, and counted wealth as nothing in comparison with her;

**Wisdom 7:9**

nor did I liken a priceless stone to her, because all gold in her sight is a little sand, and silver will be reckoned as clay before her.

**Wisdom 7:10**

Above health and beauty I loved her, and chose to have her instead of light, because sleepless is the radiance from her.

**Wisdom 7:11**

But all good things came to me together with her, and uncountable wealth was in her hands.

**Wisdom 7:12**

And I rejoiced over all things, because wisdom leads them; but I did not know her to be their begetter.

**Wisdom 7:13**

Without deceit I learned, and without envy I share; I do not hide her wealth;

**Wisdom 7:14**

for she is an unfailing treasure for men, which those who used it stored up friendship toward God, being commended through the gifts from discipline.

**Wisdom 7:15**

But may God grant me to speak according to judgment and to think worthily of what has been given, because he is both guide of wisdom and corrector of the wise.

**Wisdom 7:16**

For in his hand are both we and our words, and all prudence and knowledge of crafts.

**Wisdom 7:17**

For he himself gave me unerring knowledge of the things that are, to know the constitution of the world and operation of elements,

**Wisdom 7:18**

beginning and end and middle of times, changes of turnings and changes of seasons,

**Wisdom 7:19**

cycles of years and positions of stars,

**Wisdom 7:20**

natures of living creatures and tempers of wild beasts, forces of winds and reasonings of men, differences of plants and powers of roots,

**Wisdom 7:21**

both hidden and manifest things I knew; for wisdom, the craftswoman of all things, taught me.

**Wisdom 7:22**

For in her is a spirit intelligent, holy, only-born, manifold, subtle, easily moved, clear, undefiled, plain, unharmed, loving good, keen, unhindered, beneficent,

**Wisdom 7:23**

man-loving, firm, secure, without care, all-powerful, all-overseeing, and passing through all intelligent, pure, most subtle spirits.

**Wisdom 7:24**

For wisdom is more moving than every motion; and through her purity she penetrates and passes through all things.

**Wisdom 7:25**

For she is vapor of the power of God and a sincere outflow of the glory of the Almighty; therefore nothing defiled falls into her.

**Wisdom 7:26**

For she is radiance of everlasting light and a spotless mirror of the operation of God and image of his goodness.

**Wisdom 7:27**

And being one she can do all things, and remaining in herself she renews all things, and by generations, passing into holy souls, she prepares friends of God and prophets;

**Wisdom 7:28**

for God loves nothing except the one who dwells together with wisdom.

**Wisdom 7:29**

For she is more beautiful than the sun and beyond every position of stars; compared with light, she is found before it;

**Wisdom 7:30**

for night succeeds this, but wickedness does not prevail against wisdom.

### Chapter 8

**Wisdom 8:1**

And she reaches from end to end mightily and administers all things well.

**Wisdom 8:2**

This one I loved and sought out from my youth, and I sought to bring her as a bride for myself, and I became a lover of her beauty.

**Wisdom 8:3**

She glorifies noble birth, having life together with God, and the Master of all loved her;

**Wisdom 8:4**

for she is an initiate of the knowledge of God and chooser of his works.

**Wisdom 8:5**

But if wealth is a desirable possession in life, what is richer than wisdom, who works all things?

**Wisdom 8:6**

And if prudence works, who of the things that are is more a craftswoman than she?

**Wisdom 8:7**

And if someone loves righteousness, her labors are virtues; for she teaches self-control and prudence, righteousness and courage, than which nothing is more useful in life for men.

**Wisdom 8:8**

And if someone also longs for much experience, she knows ancient things and infers things about to be; she understands turns of sayings and solutions of riddles, she foreknows signs and wonders and outcomes of seasons and times.

**Wisdom 8:9**

Therefore I judged to bring her into life together, knowing that she will be counselor of good things and encouragement for cares and grief.

**Wisdom 8:10**

Because of her I will have glory among crowds and honor before elders, though young;

**Wisdom 8:11**

I will be found keen in judgment, and in the sight of rulers I will be wondered at;

**Wisdom 8:12**

when I am silent they will wait for me, and when I speak they will attend, and while I speak at greater length they will put a hand upon their mouth.

**Wisdom 8:13**

Because of her I will have immortality and leave everlasting memory to those after me.

**Wisdom 8:14**

I will administer peoples, and nations will be subject to me;

**Wisdom 8:15**

dread rulers, when they hear, will fear me; among a multitude I will appear good and in war courageous.

**Wisdom 8:16**

When I enter into my house I will rest together with her; for association with her has no bitterness, nor does life together with her have pain, but gladness and joy.

**Wisdom 8:17**

Having reasoned these things in myself and considered in my heart that immortality is in kinship with wisdom,

**Wisdom 8:18**

and in friendship with her there is good delight, and in the labors of her hands unfailing wealth, and in training together in her conversation prudence, and good fame in fellowship of her words, I went around seeking how I might take her to myself.

**Wisdom 8:19**

But I was a well-natured child and obtained a good soul,

**Wisdom 8:20**

or rather, being good, I came into an undefiled body.

**Wisdom 8:21**

But knowing that I would not otherwise be self-controlled unless God gave it, and this also was prudence, to know whose grace it was, I approached the Lord and entreated him and said from my whole heart:

### Chapter 9

**Wisdom 9:1**

God of fathers and Lord of mercy, who made all things by your word,

**Wisdom 9:2**

and by your wisdom formed man, that he might be master of creatures made by you,

**Wisdom 9:3**

and govern the world in holiness and righteousness and judge judgment in uprightness of soul,

**Wisdom 9:4**

give me wisdom, the one seated beside your thrones, and do not reject me from your children.

**Wisdom 9:5**

because I am your slave and son of your maidservant, a weak and short-lived man, and lesser in understanding of judgment and laws;

**Wisdom 9:6**

for even if one is perfect among sons of men, when wisdom from you is absent, he will be reckoned as nothing.

**Wisdom 9:7**

You chose me beforehand as king of your people and judge of your sons and daughters;

**Wisdom 9:8**

you said to build a temple on your holy mountain and an altar in the city of your encampment, an imitation of the holy tent which you prepared from the beginning.

**Wisdom 9:9**

And with you is wisdom, who knows your works and was present when you were making the world, and understands what is pleasing in your eyes and what is straight in your commandments.

**Wisdom 9:10**

Send her out from holy heavens, and from the throne of your glory send her, so that being present with me she may labor, and I may know what is well-pleasing with you.

**Wisdom 9:11**

For she knows and understands all things, and she will guide me prudently in my actions and guard me in her glory;

**Wisdom 9:12**

and my works will be acceptable, and I will discern your people righteously and be worthy of the thrones of my father.

**Wisdom 9:13**

For what man will know the counsel of God? Or who will consider what the Lord wills?

**Wisdom 9:14**

For reasonings of mortals are cowardly, and our thoughts are unsafe.

**Wisdom 9:15**

For a corruptible body weighs down a soul, and the earthy tent weighs down a much-concerned mind.

**Wisdom 9:16**

And barely do we infer things upon earth, and things in our hands we find with labor; but things in heavens who has traced out?

**Wisdom 9:17**

But your counsel, who has known it, unless you gave wisdom and sent your holy spirit from highest places?

**Wisdom 9:18**

And thus the paths of those upon earth were corrected, and men were taught the things pleasing to you,

**Wisdom 9:19**

and by wisdom they were saved.

### Chapter 10

**Wisdom 10:1**

She preserved the first-formed father of the world, who alone was created, and rescued him from his own transgression,

**Wisdom 10:2**

and gave him strength to rule all things.

**Wisdom 10:3**

But an unrighteous man, having departed from her in his anger, perished together with fratricidal passions;

**Wisdom 10:4**

because of him, when the earth was being flooded, wisdom again saved it, steering the righteous man by cheap wood.

**Wisdom 10:5**

She also, when nations were confounded in agreement of wickedness, knew the righteous man and kept him blameless to God, and preserved him strong against compassion for a child.

**Wisdom 10:6**

She rescued a righteous man when ungodly ones were being destroyed, as he fled fire descending upon the Pentapolis;

**Wisdom 10:7**

of whose wickedness a smoking waste still stands as witness, and plants bearing fruit in unfinished seasons, and a pillar of salt standing as memorial of a disbelieving soul.

**Wisdom 10:8**

For passing by wisdom, they were harmed not only by not knowing good things, but also left behind for life a memorial of foolishness, so that in the things in which they stumbled, they could not even be hidden.

**Wisdom 10:9**

But wisdom rescued those who attended to her out of labors.

**Wisdom 10:10**

She guided a righteous fugitive from a brother's wrath in straight paths; she showed him a kingdom of God and gave him knowledge of holy things; she prospered him in toils and multiplied his labors;

**Wisdom 10:11**

she stood beside him against those overpowering him in greed, and made him rich;

**Wisdom 10:12**

she preserved him from enemies, and secured him from those lying in wait, and awarded him a strong contest, so that he might know that godliness is more powerful than everything.

**Wisdom 10:13**

She did not abandon a righteous man who had been sold, but rescued him from sin;

**Wisdom 10:14**

she went down with him into a pit and did not leave him in bonds, until she brought to him scepters of kingdom and authority over those ruling him as tyrants; she also showed those who blamed him to be false and gave him everlasting glory.

**Wisdom 10:15**

She rescued a holy people and blameless seed from a nation of oppressors;

**Wisdom 10:16**

she entered into the soul of a servant of the Lord and resisted fearsome kings with wonders and signs.

**Wisdom 10:17**

She gave back to holy ones the wage of their labors, guided them in a wonderful way, and became for them a shelter by day and a flame of stars by night.

**Wisdom 10:18**

She brought them through the Red Sea and led them through much water;

**Wisdom 10:19**

but their enemies she flooded and bubbled them up from the depth of the abyss.

**Wisdom 10:20**

Because of this righteous ones despoiled ungodly ones, and they hymned, Lord, your holy name, and praised with one accord your defending hand;

**Wisdom 10:21**

because wisdom opened the mouth of mute ones and made tongues of infants clear.

### Chapter 11

**Wisdom 11:1**

She prospered their works by the hand of a holy prophet.

**Wisdom 11:2**

They journeyed through an uninhabited wilderness and pitched tents in trackless places;

**Wisdom 11:3**

they stood against enemies and defended themselves from enemies.

**Wisdom 11:4**

They thirsted and called upon you, and water was given to them from a sharp-edged rock, and healing of thirst from hard stone.

**Wisdom 11:5**

For through things by which their enemies were punished, through these they themselves, being at a loss, were benefited.

**Wisdom 11:6**

Instead of a spring of an ever-flowing river troubled with bloody filth,

**Wisdom 11:7**

for reproof of an infant-killing decree, you gave them abundant water unexpectedly,

**Wisdom 11:8**

showing through the thirst then how you punished the opponents.

**Wisdom 11:9**

For when they were tested, though being disciplined in mercy, they knew how ungodly ones, judged in wrath, were tormented;

**Wisdom 11:10**

for these, as a father admonishing, you tested; but those, as a severe king condemning, you examined.

**Wisdom 11:11**

And both absent and present they were similarly worn down;

**Wisdom 11:12**

for double grief took them, and a groaning at memories of the past things.

**Wisdom 11:13**

For when they heard that through their own punishments they were being benefited, they perceived the Lord;

**Wisdom 11:14**

for the one long before thrown out in exposure, whom they rejected with mockery, at the end of the outcomes they admired, having thirsted unlike the righteous.

**Wisdom 11:15**

But instead of senseless reasonings of their unrighteousness, in which, being deceived, they worshiped irrational reptiles and worthless vermin, you sent upon them a multitude of irrational living creatures for vengeance,

**Wisdom 11:16**

so that they might know that by the things through which one sins, through these he is punished.

**Wisdom 11:17**

For your almighty hand, which created the world out of unformed matter, was not at a loss to send upon them a multitude of bears or bold lions,

**Wisdom 11:18**

or newly-created wild beasts, unknown, full of rage, either breathing out fiery breath or scattering roarings of smoke or flashing terrible sparks from eyes,

**Wisdom 11:19**

of which not only the harm could crush them all together, but also the sight, terrifying them, could destroy them.

**Wisdom 11:20**

And even apart from these, they could fall by one breath, pursued by justice and winnowed by spirit of your power; but you ordered all things by measure and number and weight.

**Wisdom 11:21**

For to have great strength is present to you always, and who will resist the might of your arm?

**Wisdom 11:22**

because the whole world before you is like a tilt from scales and like a drop of morning dew that came down upon earth.

**Wisdom 11:23**

But you show mercy to all, because you are able to do all things, and you overlook sins of men for repentance.

**Wisdom 11:24**

For you love all things that are and abhor nothing of what you made; for you would not have formed anything while hating it.

**Wisdom 11:25**

But how would anything have remained, unless you willed? Or what not called by you would have been preserved?

**Wisdom 11:26**

But you spare all things, because they are yours, Master who loves souls.

### Chapter 12

**Wisdom 12:1**

For your incorruptible spirit is in all things.

**Wisdom 12:2**

Therefore those who fall aside you reprove little by little and, reminding them in the things by which they sin, you admonish them, so that being released from wickedness they may trust upon you, Lord.

**Wisdom 12:3**

For even the ancient inhabitants of your holy land, hating them,

**Wisdom 12:4**

because they practiced most hateful works of sorceries and unholy rites,

**Wisdom 12:5**

and merciless murderers of children, and a feast of entrail-eaters of human flesh and blood, initiates from the midst of a revel-band,

**Wisdom 12:6**

and murderous parents of helpless souls, you intended to destroy by the hands of our fathers,

**Wisdom 12:7**

so that the land most precious of all beside you might receive a worthy colony of God's children.

**Wisdom 12:8**

But even these, as men, you spared, and sent wasps as forerunners of your army, so that they might destroy them little by little.

**Wisdom 12:9**

not being unable in battle array to give ungodly ones subject to righteous ones, or to crush them at once by terrible beasts or by sharp word,

**Wisdom 12:10**

but judging little by little, you gave a place of repentance, not being ignorant that their generation was wicked and their evil inborn and that their reasoning would surely not be changed forever.

**Wisdom 12:11**

For they were accursed seed from the beginning, nor from fearing anyone did you give license for the things they sinned.

**Wisdom 12:12**

For who will say, What have you done? Or who will stand against your judgment? And who will accuse you concerning nations that have perished, which you made? Or who will come into position against you as avenger for unrighteous men?

**Wisdom 12:13**

For neither is there God besides you, who cares for all, so that you should show that you did not judge unjustly,

**Wisdom 12:14**

nor will king or ruler be able to face you concerning those whom you punished.

**Wisdom 12:15**

But being righteous, you administer all things righteously, considering it alien to your power to condemn the one who ought not be punished.

**Wisdom 12:16**

For your strength is beginning of righteousness, and your mastering all things makes you spare all things.

**Wisdom 12:17**

For you show strength when you are disbelieved in perfection of power, and among those who know, you convict boldness.

**Wisdom 12:18**

But you, mastering strength, judge in gentleness and administer us with much sparing; for power is present to you whenever you will.

**Wisdom 12:19**

But you taught your people through such works that the righteous must be man-loving; and you made your sons of good hope, because you give repentance for sins.

**Wisdom 12:20**

For if enemies of your children, owed to death, you punished with so much attention and leniency, giving times and place through which they might be released from wickedness,

**Wisdom 12:21**

with how much exactness did you judge your sons, to whose fathers you gave oaths and covenants of good promises?

**Wisdom 12:22**

Therefore while disciplining us, you scourge our enemies by tens of thousands, so that when judging we may be anxious for your goodness, and when being judged we may expect mercy.

**Wisdom 12:23**

Therefore also those unrighteous who lived in folly of life you tormented through their own abominations;

**Wisdom 12:24**

for they wandered farther than ways of error, taking as gods even the dishonored things among living creatures of enemies, deceived like foolish infants.

**Wisdom 12:25**

Because of this, as to unreasonable children, you sent judgment for mockery.

**Wisdom 12:26**

But those not admonished by playthings of rebuke will experience judgment worthy of God.

**Wisdom 12:27**

For in the things at which they themselves, suffering, were indignant, by these, which they supposed to be gods, being punished in them, they saw and recognized as true God the one whom long ago they denied knowing; therefore also the end of condemnation came upon them.

### Chapter 13

**Wisdom 13:1**

For all men were vain by nature, in whom ignorance of God was present, and from the seen good things they did not have strength to know the One who is, nor by attending to works did they recognize the craftsman;

**Wisdom 13:2**

but either fire or spirit or swift air or circle of stars or violent water or luminaries of heaven they supposed to be gods, rulers of the world.

**Wisdom 13:3**

If indeed, delighting in beauty, they assumed these things to be gods, let them know how much better than these the Master is, for the origin-ruler of beauty created them;

**Wisdom 13:4**

but if, astonished at power and operation, let them understand from them how much more powerful the one who made them is;

**Wisdom 13:5**

for from the greatness and beauty of created things, analogously their origin-maker is contemplated.

**Wisdom 13:6**

But nevertheless, for these there is slight blame, for perhaps they themselves wander while seeking God and wanting to find him;

**Wisdom 13:7**

for living among his works, they search carefully and are persuaded by sight, because the things seen are beautiful.

**Wisdom 13:8**

But again neither are they pardonable;

**Wisdom 13:9**

for if they had strength to know so much, so that they could aim at the age, how did they not more quickly find the Master of these things?

**Wisdom 13:10**

But miserable, and among the dead are their hopes, are those who called gods works of men's hands, gold and silver, practice of art, and likenesses of living creatures, or useless stone, work of an ancient hand.

**Wisdom 13:11**

But if some woodcutter, sawing down a suitable tree, skillfully scraped off all its bark and, working it handsomely, made a useful vessel for service of life,

**Wisdom 13:12**

and spending the castoffs of the work for preparation of food, was filled;

**Wisdom 13:13**

but the castoff from them, useful for nothing, crooked wood and grown together with knots, taking it, he carved it in care of his idleness and by experience of understanding shaped it; he likened it to an image of man,

**Wisdom 13:14**

or made it like some worthless living creature, smearing it with red ochre and reddening its surface with rouge, and smearing over every stain in it,

**Wisdom 13:15**

and making for it a dwelling worthy of it, he set it in a wall, securing it with iron.

**Wisdom 13:16**

So then, so it would not fall, he took forethought for it, knowing that it is unable to help itself; for it is an image and has need of help.

**Wisdom 13:17**

But praying about his possessions and his marriages and children, he is not ashamed to speak to the soulless thing, and about health he calls upon the weak thing,

**Wisdom 13:18**

and about life he petitions the dead thing, and about aid he entreats the most inexperienced thing, and about travel the thing unable even to use a footstep,

**Wisdom 13:19**

and about gain and work and success of hands he asks good activity from the thing most inactive in hands.

### Chapter 14

**Wisdom 14:1**

Again, someone preparing to sail and about to journey through wild waves cries out to wood more rotten than the ship that carries him.

**Wisdom 14:2**

For desire of gains devised that one, and craftswoman wisdom constructed it;

**Wisdom 14:3**

but your providence, Father, steers it, because you gave a way even in sea and a secure path in waves,

**Wisdom 14:4**

showing that you can save from every thing, so that even without skill someone may embark.

**Wisdom 14:5**

But you want the works of your wisdom not to be idle; therefore men entrust souls even to smallest wood, and passing through surge by raft they were saved.

**Wisdom 14:6**

For also in the beginning, when proud giants were perishing, the hope of the world, taking refuge upon a raft, left to the age seed of generation, steered by your hand.

**Wisdom 14:7**

For blessed is wood through which righteousness happens;

**Wisdom 14:8**

but the handmade thing is accursed, itself and the one who made it, because he worked it, and the corruptible thing was named god.

**Wisdom 14:9**

For equally hateful to God are both the ungodly one and his ungodliness;

**Wisdom 14:10**

for what was done will be punished together with the one who did it.

**Wisdom 14:11**

Because of this also in idols of nations there will be visitation, because in a creature of God they became an abomination and snares to souls of men and a trap to feet of foolish ones.

**Wisdom 14:12**

For beginning of fornication is device of idols, and invention of them corruption of life.

**Wisdom 14:13**

For they were not from the beginning, nor will they be forever;

**Wisdom 14:14**

for through empty glory of men they entered into the world, and because of this a short end for them was devised.

**Wisdom 14:15**

For a father worn down by untimely mourning, having made an image of a child quickly taken away, then honored the dead man as god and handed down mysteries and rites to those under his hand.

**Wisdom 14:16**

Then in time the ungodly custom, strengthened, was kept as law, and by commands of rulers carved images were worshiped,

**Wisdom 14:17**

whom men, not being able to honor in sight because they lived far away, making a copy of the distant appearance, made a manifest image of the honored king, so that through zeal they might flatter the absent as present.

**Wisdom 14:18**

And the ambition of the craftsman urged even those ignorant into intensification of worship;

**Wisdom 14:19**

for he, perhaps wanting to please the ruler, forced the likeness by art toward greater beauty;

**Wisdom 14:20**

and the multitude, drawn by grace of the workmanship, now reckoned as object of worship the man honored a little before.

**Wisdom 14:21**

And this became an ambush for life, because men, enslaved either to misfortune or tyranny, put the incommunicable name upon stones and woods.

**Wisdom 14:22**

Then it was not enough to be deceived concerning the knowledge of God, but also, living in great war of ignorance, they address so many evils as peace.

**Wisdom 14:23**

For either conducting child-murdering rites or hidden mysteries or frenzied revels from other ordinances,

**Wisdom 14:24**

they no longer guard either lives or marriages pure, but one kills another by lying in wait, or pains him by adulterating.

**Wisdom 14:25**

And all things mixed together have blood and murder, theft and deceit, corruption, faithlessness, disturbance, perjury, tumult of good things,

**Wisdom 14:26**

forgetfulness of grace, defilement of souls, interchange of birth, disorder of marriages, adultery and wantonness.

**Wisdom 14:27**

For the worship of nameless idols is beginning and cause and end of every evil;

**Wisdom 14:28**

for either rejoicing they are mad, or they prophesy lies, or live unrighteously, or quickly perjure themselves;

**Wisdom 14:29**

for having trusted in soulless idols, having sworn wickedly, they do not expect to be harmed.

**Wisdom 14:30**

But just things will pursue them both, because they thought wickedly concerning God by attending to idols and swore unrighteously in deceit, despising holiness;

**Wisdom 14:31**

for not the power of the things by which they swear, but the justice of those who sin, always pursues the transgression of the unrighteous.

### Chapter 15

**Wisdom 15:1**

But you, our God, are kind and true, longsuffering and administering all things in mercy.

**Wisdom 15:2**

For even if we sin, we are yours, knowing your dominion; but we will not sin, knowing that we have been reckoned to you.

**Wisdom 15:3**

For to know you is whole righteousness, and to know your dominion is root of immortality.

**Wisdom 15:4**

For neither did an evil-working device of men deceive us, nor fruitless labor of shadow-painters, a form stained with varied colors,

**Wisdom 15:5**

whose sight comes to foolish ones for reproach, and he longs for the breathless form of a dead image.

**Wisdom 15:6**

Lovers of evils are worthy of such hopes, both those doing and those longing and those revering.

**Wisdom 15:7**

For also a potter, pressing soft earth, laboriously molds each thing for our service; but from the same clay he formed vessels that serve clean works and the opposite, all alike; but of each of these, what the use is for each, the clay-worker is judge.

**Wisdom 15:8**

And with evil toil he molds a vain god from the same clay, who, having been born from earth shortly before, after a little goes to where he was taken from, when demanded back for the debt of soul.

**Wisdom 15:9**

But his concern is not that he is about to be weary, nor that he has a short-ended life, but he competes with goldsmiths and silversmiths, and imitates bronze-molders, and considers it glory that he molds counterfeits.

**Wisdom 15:10**

Ash is his heart, and his hope more worthless than earth, and his life more dishonored than clay,

**Wisdom 15:11**

because he was ignorant of the one who molded him and breathed into him an active soul and breathed in a life-giving spirit;

**Wisdom 15:12**

but they reckoned our life to be a game and life a profitable festival; for he says one must get gain from wherever, even from evil.

**Wisdom 15:13**

For this one beyond all knows that he sins, making brittle vessels and carved images from earthy matter.

**Wisdom 15:14**

But most foolish of all and wretched beyond an infant's soul were enemies of your people, having oppressed it,

**Wisdom 15:15**

because they also reckoned all idols of the nations as gods, which have neither use of eyes for sight, nor nostrils for drawing in air, nor ears to hear, nor fingers of hands for touching, and their feet are idle for stepping.

**Wisdom 15:16**

For man made them, and having borrowed spirit he formed them; for no man has strength to form a god like himself.

**Wisdom 15:17**

But being mortal, he works a dead thing with lawless hands; for he is better than his revered things, since he lived, but they never.

**Wisdom 15:18**

And they revere the most hateful living creatures; for compared in foolishness they are worse than the others;

**Wisdom 15:19**

nor are they beautiful enough to desire, as happens in the sight of living creatures; but they have missed both the praise of God and his blessing.

### Chapter 16

**Wisdom 16:1**

Because of this, by similar things they were punished worthily and by a multitude of vermin they were tormented.

**Wisdom 16:2**

Instead of this punishment, benefiting your people, you prepared for desire of appetite a strange taste, food of quail,

**Wisdom 16:3**

so that those, desiring food, because of the loathsomeness of things sent upon them might turn away even necessary appetite, but these, having become needy for a little, might partake of a strange taste.

**Wisdom 16:4**

For it was necessary for inescapable want to come upon those tyrannizing, but for these only to be shown how their enemies were tormented.

**Wisdom 16:5**

For also when terrible rage of beasts came upon them and they were being destroyed by bites of crooked serpents, your wrath did not remain to the end;

**Wisdom 16:6**

but for admonition they were troubled for a little, having a counselor of salvation for remembrance of the commandment of your law;

**Wisdom 16:7**

for the one who turned was not saved through the thing seen, but through you, the Savior of all.

**Wisdom 16:8**

And in this also you persuaded our enemies that you are the one rescuing from every evil;

**Wisdom 16:9**

for bites of locusts and flies killed those, and no healing for their soul was found, because they were worthy to be punished by such things;

**Wisdom 16:10**

but teeth of venomous dragons did not overcome your sons, for your mercy came opposite and healed them.

**Wisdom 16:11**

For for reminder of your oracles they were pierced and quickly saved, lest, falling into deep forgetfulness, they become severed from your good deed.

**Wisdom 16:12**

For neither herb nor poultice healed them, but your word, Lord, that heals all things.

**Wisdom 16:13**

For you have authority of life and death, and you bring down to gates of Hades and bring up.

**Wisdom 16:14**

But a man kills by his wickedness, but a spirit that has gone out he does not turn back, nor does he release a soul taken away.

**Wisdom 16:15**

But it is impossible to flee your hand;

**Wisdom 16:16**

for ungodly ones who denied knowing you were scourged by the strength of your arm, pursued by strange rains and hails and inescapable showers and consumed by fire.

**Wisdom 16:17**

For the most paradoxical thing: in water that quenches all things fire worked more; for the world is defender of righteous ones.

**Wisdom 16:18**

For at one time flame was tamed, so that it might not burn up the living creatures sent against ungodly ones, but that they themselves, seeing, might know that they are driven by judgment of God;

**Wisdom 16:19**

but at another time, even in the midst of water, it burns beyond the power of fire, so that it might destroy produce of an unrighteous land.

**Wisdom 16:20**

Instead of these things you fed your people with food of angels and sent them prepared bread from heaven without toil, able to provide every pleasure and suited to every taste;

**Wisdom 16:21**

for your substance manifested your sweetness toward children, and serving the desire of the one who took it, it changed itself toward whatever anyone wished.

**Wisdom 16:22**

But snow and crystal endured fire and did not melt, so that they might know that fire blazing in hail and flashing in rains destroyed fruits of enemies;

**Wisdom 16:23**

but this again, so that righteous ones might be fed, forgot its own power.

**Wisdom 16:24**

For creation, serving you who made it, strains for punishment against unrighteous ones and relaxes for benefit on behalf of those who trust in you.

**Wisdom 16:25**

Because of this also then, being changed into all things, it served your all-nourishing gift according to the will of those in need,

**Wisdom 16:26**

so that your sons, whom you loved, Lord, might learn that not the births of fruits nourish man, but your word preserves those who trust you.

**Wisdom 16:27**

For what was not destroyed by fire, simply warmed by a brief ray of sun, melted,

**Wisdom 16:28**

so that it might be known that one must rise before the sun for thanksgiving to you and meet you at the rising of light.

**Wisdom 16:29**

For hope of an ungrateful one will melt like wintry frost and flow away like useless water.

### Chapter 17

**Wisdom 17:1**

For your judgments are great and hard to explain; because of this undisciplined souls wandered.

**Wisdom 17:2**

For lawless ones, supposing they dominated a holy nation, prisoners of darkness and fettered by long night, shut under roofs, lay as fugitives from eternal providence.

**Wisdom 17:3**

For supposing they could escape notice in hidden sins, they were scattered under a lightless covering of forgetfulness, terribly amazed and disturbed by apparitions;

**Wisdom 17:4**

for not even the hidden place holding them kept them without fear, but sounds crashing around them rang about, and gloomy phantoms with unsmiling faces appeared.

**Wisdom 17:5**

And no force of fire had strength to illuminate, nor did brilliant flames of stars endure to brighten that hateful night.

**Wisdom 17:6**

But there appeared to them only self-kindled fire full of fear, and being terrified by that sight not seen, they considered the things seen to be worse.

**Wisdom 17:7**

And mockeries of magic art lay powerless, and a shameful reproof of boasting in prudence;

**Wisdom 17:8**

for those promising to drive away fears and disturbances of a sick soul, these themselves were sick with laughable fear.

**Wisdom 17:9**

For even if nothing troubling frightened them, scared out of their senses by passing of vermin and hissing of reptiles, they were perishing trembling and refusing to look at air that could nowhere be fled.

**Wisdom 17:10**

For wickedness, condemned by its own witness, is cowardly, and, being constrained by conscience, always takes up the difficult things;

**Wisdom 17:11**

for fear is nothing except betrayal of helps from reasoning.

**Wisdom 17:12**

But expectation, being weaker within, reckons ignorance greater than the cause that provides the torment.

**Wisdom 17:13**

But they, sleeping the same sleep that night, truly powerless and having come upon them from depths of powerless Hades,

**Wisdom 17:14**

were partly driven by monstrosities of phantoms, and partly paralyzed by betrayal of soul; for sudden and unexpected fear came upon them.

**Wisdom 17:15**

Then in this way, whoever was there falling down, was guarded shut into a prison not made of iron;

**Wisdom 17:16**

for whether someone was a farmer or shepherd or worker of labors in wilderness, being overtaken he remained under inescapable necessity,

**Wisdom 17:17**

for all were bound with one chain of darkness; whether whistling wind, or melodious sound of birds around spreading branches, or rhythm of water going by force, or harsh crash of rocks thrown down,

**Wisdom 17:18**

or unseen running of leaping animals, or voice of most harsh roaring beasts, or echo reflected from hollows of mountains, it paralyzed them by terrifying.

**Wisdom 17:19**

For the whole world was shining with bright light and held together by unhindered works;

**Wisdom 17:20**

but upon those alone heavy night was stretched, image of darkness about to receive them; but to themselves they were heavier than darkness.

### Chapter 18

**Wisdom 18:1**

But for your holy ones there was greatest light; hearing their voice but not seeing form, because they also had not suffered, they blessed them,

**Wisdom 18:2**

and because those previously wronged were not harming them, they gave thanks and begged favor for their difference.

**Wisdom 18:3**

Instead of these things you provided a fire-flaming pillar, guide of an unknown journey, and harmless sun of honorable sojourning.

**Wisdom 18:4**

For those were worthy to be deprived of light and imprisoned in darkness, who had kept your sons shut up, through whom the incorruptible light of law was about to be given to the age.

**Wisdom 18:5**

But when they had resolved to kill infants of holy ones, and one child had been exposed and saved, for reproof you took away a multitude of their children and destroyed them with one accord in violent water.

**Wisdom 18:6**

That night was foreknown to our fathers, so that knowing safely by what oaths they trusted, they might be cheerful.

**Wisdom 18:7**

By your people there was expected salvation of righteous ones and destruction of enemies;

**Wisdom 18:8**

for by what you punished the opponents, by this, having called us to yourself, you glorified us.

**Wisdom 18:9**

For holy children of good ones were sacrificing in secret, and with one mind they appointed a law of divinity, that holy ones would share alike in the same good things and dangers, already beforehand singing praises of fathers.

**Wisdom 18:10**

But a discordant cry of enemies echoed back, and a piteous voice of lamenting children was carried abroad;

**Wisdom 18:11**

and by like penalty slave was punished together with master and commoner suffering the same things with king,

**Wisdom 18:12**

and with one accord all under one name of death had uncountable dead; for living ones were not enough even to bury them, since in one turn their more honored generation was destroyed.

**Wisdom 18:13**

For disbelieving all things because of sorceries, at the destruction of firstborn they confessed the people to be God's son.

**Wisdom 18:14**

For while quiet silence held all things and night was in the middle of its own swiftness,

**Wisdom 18:15**

your almighty word leaped from heavens, from royal thrones, a severe warrior, into the middle of the destructive land,

**Wisdom 18:16**

bearing your sincere command as a sharp sword, and standing, filled all things with death; and it touched heaven, yet had stepped upon earth.

**Wisdom 18:17**

Then immediately visions of dreams terribly troubled them, and unexpected fears stood upon them,

**Wisdom 18:18**

and each, thrown here and there half-dead, revealed for what cause he was dying;

**Wisdom 18:19**

for dreams that disturbed them foretold this, so that they might not perish ignorant of why they suffer evil.

**Wisdom 18:20**

But a trial of death also touched righteous ones, and a breaking of multitude happened in the wilderness. But wrath did not remain long;

**Wisdom 18:21**

for a blameless man, hastening, fought as champion, bringing the weapon of his own ministry, prayer and propitiation of incense; he stood against anger and put an end to calamity, showing that he was your servant.

**Wisdom 18:22**

And he conquered the crowd not by strength of body, nor by working of weapons, but by word he subjected the one punishing, reminding him of oaths and covenants of fathers.

**Wisdom 18:23**

For when dead had already fallen heap upon one another, standing between, he cut off wrath and split through the way to the living.

**Wisdom 18:24**

For upon his full-length garment was the whole world, and glories of fathers upon a four-rowed carving of stones, and your majesty upon the diadem of his head.

**Wisdom 18:25**

To these the destroyer yielded, and these they feared; for only a trial of wrath was enough.

### Chapter 19

**Wisdom 19:1**

But upon ungodly ones merciless passion stood until the end; for he foreknew also their future things,

**Wisdom 19:2**

that they themselves, after permitting them to depart and sending them forth with haste, would pursue them after changing their mind.

**Wisdom 19:3**

For while still having mournings in hands and lamenting at graves of dead, they drew upon themselves another reasoning of folly, and those whom with entreaties they cast out, these they pursued as fugitives.

**Wisdom 19:4**

For worthy necessity drew them to this end and cast forgetfulness of things that had happened, so that they might fill up in advance the punishment lacking to the torments,

**Wisdom 19:5**

and your people might pass through a paradoxical journey, but those might find strange death.

**Wisdom 19:6**

For the whole creation in its own kind was again formed anew from above, serving your commands, so that your children might be kept unharmed.

**Wisdom 19:7**

The cloud shadowing the camp was seen, and from water that stood before, an emergence of dry land, an unhindered way from Red Sea and a grass-bearing plain from violent surge;

**Wisdom 19:8**

through which those sheltered by your hand passed through as a whole nation, having seen wonderful wonders.

**Wisdom 19:9**

For like horses they grazed, and like lambs they skipped, praising you, Lord, the one rescuing them.

**Wisdom 19:10**

For they still remembered the things in their sojourning, how instead of birth of living creatures, earth brought out gnats, and instead of water-creatures, river spewed out a multitude of frogs.

**Wisdom 19:11**

And afterward they also saw a new birth of birds, because being led by desire they asked for delicacies of luxury;

**Wisdom 19:12**

for quail came up to them from sea for comfort.

**Wisdom 19:13**

And punishments came upon sinners, not without prior signs by force of thunderbolts; for they were suffering justly by their own wickednesses, for they practiced harsher hatred of strangers.

**Wisdom 19:14**

For those did not receive unknown ones when present, but these enslaved foreign benefactors.

**Wisdom 19:15**

And not only so, but some visitation of them will be, since they received strangers hatefully;

**Wisdom 19:16**

but these, after receiving with feasts those who already shared in the same rights, afflicted them with terrible labors.

**Wisdom 19:17**

And they were also struck with blindness, as those at the doors of the righteous one, when, surrounded with yawning darkness, each sought passage through his own doors.

**Wisdom 19:18**

For elements, changing order among themselves, just as in a psaltery notes change name of rhythm, always remaining in sound, which is possible to infer exactly from sight of the things that happened.

**Wisdom 19:19**

For land animals were changed into water creatures, and swimming things crossed over upon land;

**Wisdom 19:20**

fire had strength in water beyond its own power, and water forgot its quenching power;

**Wisdom 19:21**

flames, on the contrary, did not wither flesh of easily destroyed living creatures walking among them, nor melt the easily melted kind of crystal-like ambrosial food.

**Wisdom 19:22**

For according to all things, Lord, you magnified your people and glorified them, and did not overlook them, standing beside them in every time and place.

## Sirach

### Chapter 1

**Sirach 1:1**

All wisdom is from the Lord and is with him forever.

**Sirach 1:2**

Sand of seas and drops of rain and days of an age, who will count out?

**Sirach 1:3**

Height of heaven and breadth of earth and abyss and wisdom, who will trace out?

**Sirach 1:4**

Wisdom was created before all things, and understanding of prudence from eternity. [5] A spring of wisdom is the word of God in the highest, and her ways are eternal commandments.

**Sirach 1:6**

Root of wisdom, to whom was it revealed? And her clever devices, who has known? [7] Knowledge of wisdom, to whom was it manifested? And her much experience, who understood?

**Sirach 1:8**

There is one wise, very fearsome, sitting upon his throne.

**Sirach 1:9**

The Lord himself created her and saw and numbered her and poured her out upon all his works,

**Sirach 1:10**

with all flesh according to his gift, and he supplied her to those who love him.

**Sirach 1:11**

Fear of the Lord is glory and boast and gladness and a crown of rejoicing.

**Sirach 1:12**

Fear of the Lord will delight a heart and will give gladness and joy and length of days.

**Sirach 1:13**

For the one fearing the Lord, it will be well at last things, and in the day of his death he will find grace.

**Sirach 1:14**

Beginning of wisdom is to fear the Lord, and with faithful ones in the womb she was created together with them.

**Sirach 1:15**

With men she nested as a foundation of an age, and with their seed she will be entrusted.

**Sirach 1:16**

Fullness of wisdom is to fear the Lord, and she intoxicates them from her fruits;

**Sirach 1:17**

she will fill all her house with desirable things and her storehouses from her produce.

**Sirach 1:18**

A crown of wisdom is fear of the Lord, making peace and health of healing sprout.

**Sirach 1:19**

And he saw and numbered her; he rained down science and knowledge of understanding and exalted the glory of those holding her.

**Sirach 1:20**

Root of wisdom is to fear the Lord, and her branches are length of days. [21] Fear of the Lord drives away sins, and remaining, it will turn away wrath.

**Sirach 1:22**

Unrighteous anger will not be able to be justified, for the inclination of his anger is a fall to him.

**Sirach 1:23**

Until a time a long-suffering one will hold out, and afterward gladness will spring up for him;

**Sirach 1:24**

until a time he will hide his words, and lips of faithful ones will tell out his understanding.

**Sirach 1:25**

In treasures of wisdom is a parable of knowledge, but godliness is an abomination to a sinner.

**Sirach 1:26**

If you desired wisdom, keep commandments, and the Lord will supply her to you.

**Sirach 1:27**

For wisdom and discipline are fear of the Lord, and his good pleasure is faith and meekness.

**Sirach 1:28**

Do not disobey fear of the Lord, and do not approach him with a double heart.

**Sirach 1:29**

Do not act hypocritically in mouths of men, and take heed with your lips.

**Sirach 1:30**

Do not exalt yourself, lest you fall and bring dishonor to your soul, and the Lord reveal your hidden things and throw you down in the midst of the congregation, because you did not approach fear of the Lord and your heart is full of deceit.

### Chapter 2

**Sirach 2:1**

Child, if you come to serve the Lord God, prepare your soul for testing;

**Sirach 2:2**

make your heart straight and endure, and do not hurry in a time of calamity;

**Sirach 2:3**

cling to him and do not depart, so that you may be increased at your last things.

**Sirach 2:4**

Whatever is brought upon you, accept, and in changes of your humiliation be long-suffering;

**Sirach 2:5**

because in fire gold is tested, and acceptable men in a furnace of humiliation.

**Sirach 2:6**

Trust him, and he will help you; make your ways straight and hope upon him.

**Sirach 2:7**

You who fear the Lord, wait for his mercy and do not turn aside, lest you fall.

**Sirach 2:8**

You who fear the Lord, trust him, and your reward shall surely not stumble.

**Sirach 2:9**

You who fear the Lord, hope for good things and for gladness of an age and mercy.

**Sirach 2:10**

Look into ancient generations and see: who trusted the Lord and was ashamed? Or who remained in his fear and was abandoned? Or who called upon him, and he overlooked him?

**Sirach 2:11**

For compassionate and merciful is the Lord, and he forgives sins and saves in time of affliction.

**Sirach 2:12**

Woe to cowardly hearts and slack hands and to a sinner stepping upon two paths.

**Sirach 2:13**

Woe to a slack heart, because it does not trust; therefore it will not be sheltered.

**Sirach 2:14**

Woe to you who have destroyed endurance; and what will you do when the Lord visits?

**Sirach 2:15**

Those who fear the Lord will not disobey his words, and those who love him will keep his ways.

**Sirach 2:16**

Those who fear the Lord will seek his good pleasure, and those who love him will be filled with the law.

**Sirach 2:17**

Those who fear the Lord will prepare their hearts and humble their souls before him.

**Sirach 2:18**

We will fall into the hands of the Lord and not into hands of men; for as his greatness is, so also is his mercy.

### Chapter 3

**Sirach 3:1**

Hear me your father, children, and do thus, so that you may be saved;

**Sirach 3:2**

for the Lord glorified a father over children and established judgment of a mother over sons.

**Sirach 3:3**

The one honoring a father will make atonement for sins,

**Sirach 3:4**

and as one storing up treasure, so is the one glorifying his mother.

**Sirach 3:5**

The one honoring a father will be gladdened by children, and in the day of his prayer he will be heard.

**Sirach 3:6**

The one glorifying a father will live long, and the one obeying the Lord will give rest to his mother;

**Sirach 3:7**

and as to masters he will serve those who begot him.

**Sirach 3:8**

In work and word honor your father, so that blessing may come upon you from him;

**Sirach 3:9**

for blessing of a father supports houses of children, but curse of a mother uproots foundations.

**Sirach 3:10**

Do not glorify yourself in dishonor of your father, for dishonor of a father is not glory to you;

**Sirach 3:11**

for glory of a man is from honor of his father, and a mother in dishonor is reproach to children.

**Sirach 3:12**

Child, help your father in old age, and do not grieve him in his life;

**Sirach 3:13**

even if he lacks understanding, have forgiveness, and do not dishonor him with all your strength.

**Sirach 3:14**

For mercy to a father will not be forgotten, and instead of sins it will be added to build you up.

**Sirach 3:15**

In the day of your affliction it will be remembered of you; as fair weather upon frost, so your sins will be dissolved.

**Sirach 3:16**

Like a blasphemer is the one forsaking a father, and cursed by the Lord is the one provoking his mother.

**Sirach 3:17**

Child, conduct your works in meekness, and you will be loved by an acceptable man.

**Sirach 3:18**

As much as you are great, humble yourself by so much, and before the Lord you will find grace;

**Sirach 3:20**

because great is the dominion of the Lord, and by the humble he is glorified.

**Sirach 3:21**

Do not seek things too hard for you, and do not examine things too strong for you;

**Sirach 3:22**

what was commanded to you, think on these things, for you have no need of hidden things.

**Sirach 3:23**

In the excessive things of your works do not be meddlesome; for more than the understanding of men has been shown to you;

**Sirach 3:24**

for their assumption deceived many, and evil suspicion caused their minds to slip. [25] Not having pupils, you will lack light; but being without share of knowledge, do not profess.

**Sirach 3:26**

A hard heart will be harmed at last things, and the one loving danger will fall in it.

**Sirach 3:27**

A hard heart will be weighed down with labors, and the sinner will add sin upon sins.

**Sirach 3:28**

There is no healing for the calamity of a proud one, for a plant of wickedness has taken root in him.

**Sirach 3:29**

The heart of an understanding one will consider a parable, and an ear of a listener is desire of a wise man.

**Sirach 3:30**

Water will quench blazing fire, and mercy will atone for sins.

**Sirach 3:31**

The one repaying favors remembers for things after these, and in time of fall he will find support.

### Chapter 4

**Sirach 4:1**

Child, do not deprive the poor of life, and do not delay needy eyes.

**Sirach 4:2**

Do not grieve a hungry soul, and do not provoke a man in his want.

**Sirach 4:3**

Do not further trouble an angered heart, and do not delay the gift of one needing.

**Sirach 4:4**

Do not reject a suppliant who is afflicted, and do not turn your face from a poor man.

**Sirach 4:5**

Do not turn an eye from one needing, and do not give place to a man to curse you;

**Sirach 4:6**

for if he curses you in bitterness of his soul, the one who made him will hear his petition.

**Sirach 4:7**

Make yourself beloved to a congregation, and humble your head to a great man.

**Sirach 4:8**

Incline your ear to a poor man and answer him peacefully in meekness.

**Sirach 4:9**

Rescue one wronged from the hand of the wrongdoer, and do not be faint-hearted when you judge.

**Sirach 4:10**

Become as a father to orphans and instead of a husband to their mother; and you will be as a son of the Most High, and he will love you more than your mother.

**Sirach 4:11**

Wisdom exalted her sons and takes hold of those seeking her.

**Sirach 4:12**

The one loving her loves life, and those who rise early to her will be filled with gladness.

**Sirach 4:13**

The one holding her will inherit glory, and where he enters, the Lord will bless.

**Sirach 4:14**

Those serving her will minister to a holy one, and the Lord loves those who love her.

**Sirach 4:15**

The one obeying her will judge nations, and the one attending to her will dwell confidently.

**Sirach 4:16**

If he trusts, he will inherit her, and his generations will be in possession;

**Sirach 4:17**

because at first she walks with him perversely, and will bring fear and cowardice upon him, and will torment him in her discipline until she trusts his soul and tests him in her ordinances.

**Sirach 4:18**

And again she will come back straight to him and gladden him and reveal her hidden things to him.

**Sirach 4:19**

If he goes astray, she will abandon him and hand him over into hands of his fall.

**Sirach 4:20**

Keep time and guard from evil, and concerning your soul do not be ashamed;

**Sirach 4:21**

for there is shame bringing sin, and there is shame that is glory and grace.

**Sirach 4:22**

Do not accept a face against your soul, and do not be ashamed to your fall.

**Sirach 4:23**

Do not hinder a word in time of salvation;

**Sirach 4:24**

for in a word wisdom will be known, and discipline in an utterance of the tongue.

**Sirach 4:25**

Do not contradict the truth, and be ashamed concerning your lack of discipline.

**Sirach 4:26**

Do not be ashamed to confess upon your sins, and do not force a river's flow.

**Sirach 4:27**

And do not subject yourself to a foolish man, and do not accept the face of a ruler.

**Sirach 4:28**

Until death contend for the truth, and the Lord God will fight for you.

**Sirach 4:29**

Do not become swift in your tongue and sluggish and slack in your works.

**Sirach 4:30**

Do not be as a lion in your house and playing terror among your household servants.

**Sirach 4:31**

Do not let your hand be stretched out to receive and drawn in when giving back.

### Chapter 5

**Sirach 5:1**

Do not set your heart upon your possessions, and do not say that they are sufficient for me.

**Sirach 5:2**

Do not follow your soul and your strength, to walk in the desires of your heart,

**Sirach 5:3**

and do not say, Who will rule over me? For the Lord, avenging, will avenge you.

**Sirach 5:4**

Do not say, I sinned, and what happened to me? For the Lord is long-suffering.

**Sirach 5:5**

Concerning atonement do not become fearless, to add sin upon sins;

**Sirach 5:6**

and do not say, His compassion is much; he will atone for the multitude of my sins; for mercy and wrath are from him, and upon sinners his anger will rest.

**Sirach 5:7**

Do not wait to turn to the Lord, and do not put off day from day; for suddenly wrath of the Lord will come out, and in time of vengeance you will be destroyed.

**Sirach 5:8**

Do not set your heart upon unrighteous possessions; for nothing will benefit you in the day of calamity.

**Sirach 5:9**

Do not winnow in every wind, and do not walk in every path; so is the sinner who is double-tongued.

**Sirach 5:10**

Be established in your understanding, and let your word be one.

**Sirach 5:11**

Be swift in your hearing, and in long-suffering utter an answer.

**Sirach 5:12**

If there is understanding in you, answer your neighbor; but if not, let your hand be upon your mouth.

**Sirach 5:13**

Glory and dishonor are in speech, and the tongue of a man is a fall to him.

**Sirach 5:14**

Do not be called a whisperer, and with your tongue do not lie in wait; for shame is upon the thief, and evil condemnation upon the double-tongued.

**Sirach 5:15**

In great and in small do not be ignorant.

### Chapter 6

**Sirach 6:1**

And instead of a friend do not become an enemy; for an evil name will inherit shame and reproach; so is the sinner who is double-tongued.

**Sirach 6:2**

Do not lift yourself up in counsel of your soul, lest your soul be torn apart like a bull;

**Sirach 6:3**

you will eat up your leaves and destroy your fruits and leave yourself like a dry tree.

**Sirach 6:4**

An evil soul will destroy the one who acquired it and make him an object of joy to enemies.

**Sirach 6:5**

A sweet throat will multiply his friends, and a well-speaking tongue will multiply friendly greetings.

**Sirach 6:6**

Let those at peace with you be many, but let your counselors be one from a thousand.

**Sirach 6:7**

If you acquire a friend, acquire him in testing, and do not quickly trust him;

**Sirach 6:8**

for there is a friend in his own time, and he shall surely not remain in the day of your affliction.

**Sirach 6:9**

And there is a friend who turns into hostility and will reveal a quarrel to your reproach.

**Sirach 6:10**

And there is a friend who is a companion of tables, and he shall surely not remain in the day of your affliction.

**Sirach 6:11**

And in your good things he will be as you, and over your servants he will speak freely;

**Sirach 6:12**

if you are humbled, he will be against you, and from your face he will hide.

**Sirach 6:13**

Separate yourself from your enemies, and take heed from your friends.

**Sirach 6:14**

A faithful friend is a strong shelter, and the one who finds him found a treasure.

**Sirach 6:15**

For a faithful friend there is no exchange, and there is no weight of his beauty.

**Sirach 6:16**

A faithful friend is medicine of life, and those fearing the Lord will find him.

**Sirach 6:17**

The one fearing the Lord makes his friendship straight, because according to him, so also is his neighbor.

**Sirach 6:18**

Child, from your youth choose discipline, and until gray hairs you will find wisdom.

**Sirach 6:19**

As one plowing and one sowing approach her and wait for her good fruits; for in her work you will labor a little and quickly eat from her produce.

**Sirach 6:20**

How rough she is to the undisciplined, and a heartless one will not remain in her;

**Sirach 6:21**

as a strong test stone she will be upon him, and he will not delay to throw her away.

**Sirach 6:22**

For wisdom is according to her name, and she is not manifest to many.

**Sirach 6:23**

Listen, child, and receive my judgment, and do not reject my counsel.

**Sirach 6:24**

And bring your feet into her fetters and your neck into her collar.

**Sirach 6:25**

Put under your shoulder and carry her, and do not be irritated by her bonds.

**Sirach 6:26**

With all your soul approach her and with all your power keep her ways.

**Sirach 6:27**

Trace out and seek, and she will be known to you, and having become self-controlled, do not let her go;

**Sirach 6:28**

for at last things you will find her rest, and she will be turned to gladness for you.

**Sirach 6:29**

And her fetters will be for you a shelter of strength, and her collars for a robe of glory.

**Sirach 6:30**

For golden ornament is upon her, and her bonds are a hyacinth cord;

**Sirach 6:31**

you will put her on as a robe of glory, and you will set her around yourself as a crown of rejoicing.

**Sirach 6:32**

If you want, child, you will be disciplined, and if you give your soul, you will be skillful.

**Sirach 6:33**

If you love to hear, you will receive, and if you incline your ear, you will be wise.

**Sirach 6:34**

Stand in the multitude of elders, and whoever is wise, cling to him.

**Sirach 6:35**

Want to listen to every divine narrative, and let not proverbs of understanding escape you.

**Sirach 6:36**

If you see an understanding one, rise early to him, and let your foot wear out the steps of his doors.

**Sirach 6:37**

Think in the ordinances of the Lord and meditate in his commandments continually; he will establish your heart, and the desire of your wisdom will be given to you.

### Chapter 7

**Sirach 7:1**

Do not do evils, and evil shall surely not overtake you;

**Sirach 7:2**

depart from unrighteousness, and it will turn aside from you.

**Sirach 7:3**

Son, do not sow upon furrows of unrighteousness, and you shall surely not reap them sevenfold.

**Sirach 7:4**

Do not seek leadership from the Lord, nor a seat of glory from a king.

**Sirach 7:5**

Do not justify yourself before the Lord, and before a king do not play the wise man.

**Sirach 7:6**

Do not seek to become a judge, lest you not have strength to take away injustices; lest perhaps you fear before the face of a ruler and set a stumbling block in your uprightness.

**Sirach 7:7**

Do not sin against a multitude of a city, and do not throw yourself down in a crowd.

**Sirach 7:8**

Do not bind sin twice, for in the one you will not be innocent.

**Sirach 7:9**

Do not say that he will look upon the multitude of my gifts, and when I bring to God Most High he will accept.

**Sirach 7:10**

Do not be fainthearted in your prayer, and do not neglect to do mercy.

**Sirach 7:11**

Do not laugh down a man who is in bitterness of his soul, for there is one who humbles and exalts.

**Sirach 7:12**

Do not plow a lie against your brother, nor do the like to a friend.

**Sirach 7:13**

Do not desire to lie any lie, for its continuance is not for good.

**Sirach 7:14**

Do not babble in the multitude of elders, and do not repeat a word in your prayer.

**Sirach 7:15**

Do not hate laborious work and farming created by the Most High.

**Sirach 7:16**

Do not count yourself in the multitude of sinners. Remember that wrath will not delay.

**Sirach 7:17**

Humble your soul greatly, because vengeance of an ungodly one is fire and worm.

**Sirach 7:18**

Do not exchange a friend for profit, nor a genuine brother for gold of Souphir.

**Sirach 7:19**

Do not miss a wise and good wife, for her grace is above gold.

**Sirach 7:20**

Do not mistreat a servant working in truth, nor a hireling giving his soul.

**Sirach 7:21**

Let your soul love an understanding servant; do not deprive him of freedom.

**Sirach 7:22**

If livestock are yours, look after them, and if they are useful to you, let them remain with you.

**Sirach 7:23**

If children are yours, discipline them, and bend their neck from youth.

**Sirach 7:24**

If daughters are yours, pay attention to their body, and do not brighten your face toward them.

**Sirach 7:25**

Give a daughter in marriage, and you will have completed a great work, and give her as gift to an understanding man.

**Sirach 7:26**

If a wife is yours according to soul, do not cast her out; and to a hated woman do not entrust yourself.

**Sirach 7:27**

With whole heart glorify your father, and do not forget birth pains of your mother;

**Sirach 7:28**
