Take courage, children, and cry out to God, for there will be remembrance of you by the one who brought it.

**Baruch 4:28**

For just as your mind came to wander from God, multiply tenfold, having turned back to seek him.

**Baruch 4:29**

For the one who brought evils upon you will bring upon you eternal gladness with your salvation.

**Baruch 4:30**

Take courage, Jerusalem; the one who named you will comfort you.

**Baruch 4:31**

Cowardly are those who harmed you and rejoiced over your fall;

**Baruch 4:32**

cowardly are the cities in which your children served; cowardly is the one who received your sons.

**Baruch 4:33**

For just as she rejoiced over your fall and was gladdened over your ruin, so she will be grieved over her own desolation.

**Baruch 4:34**

And I will take away from her the exultation of her crowd, and her boasting will become mourning.

**Baruch 4:35**

For fire will come upon her from the Eternal One for long days, and she will be inhabited by demons for the greater time.

**Baruch 4:36**

Look around toward the east, Jerusalem, and see the gladness coming to you from God.

**Baruch 4:37**

Behold, your sons are coming, whom you sent away; they are coming, gathered from east to west by the word of the Holy One, rejoicing in the glory of God.

### Chapter 5

**Baruch 5:1**

Strip off, Jerusalem, the robe of your mourning and mistreatment, and put on the beauty of glory from God forever.

**Baruch 5:2**

Wrap around yourself the double cloak of righteousness from God; put upon your head the turban of glory of the Eternal One.

**Baruch 5:3**

For God will show your brightness to all under heaven.

**Baruch 5:4**

For your name will be called by God forever: Peace of righteousness and glory of god-reverence.

**Baruch 5:5**

Rise up, Jerusalem, and stand upon the high place, and look around toward the east, and see your children gathered from setting of sun to risings by the word of the Holy One, rejoicing in the remembrance of God.

**Baruch 5:6**

For they went out from you on foot, being led by enemies, but God brings them in to you, being lifted with glory as a throne of kingdom.

**Baruch 5:7**

For God appointed every high mountain and everlasting mound to be humbled, and ravines to be filled for leveling of the earth, so that Israel may walk securely in the glory of God.

**Baruch 5:8**

And the woods and every tree of sweet fragrance shaded Israel by command of God;

**Baruch 5:9**

for God will lead Israel with gladness by the light of his glory, with mercy and righteousness from him.

## Letter of Jeremiah

### Chapter 1

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:1**

Because of the sins that you sinned before God, you will be led away into Babylon as captives by Nebuchadnezzar king of the Babylonians.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:2**

Therefore, when you enter into Babylon, you will be there many years and a long time, until seven generations; but after this I will bring you out from there with peace.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:3**

But now you will see in Babylon gods of silver and gold and wood being lifted upon shoulders, showing fear to the nations.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:4**

Therefore take care, lest you also, being made like the foreigners, be made like them, and fear take hold of you concerning them,

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:5**

when you see a crowd before and behind them worshiping them. But say in your mind, You must be worshiped, Master.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:6**

For my angel is with you, and he himself is seeking out your souls.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:7**

For their tongue has been scraped smooth by a carpenter, and they are overlaid with gold and overlaid with silver, but they are false and cannot speak.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:8**

And as for a maiden loving adornment, taking gold they prepare crowns upon heads of their gods.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:9**

And there is also a time when the priests, secretly taking gold and silver from their gods, will spend it on themselves, and they will also give some from them to the prostitutes on the roof.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:10**

And they adorn them like men with garments, gods of silver and gods of gold and wood; but these are not preserved from rust and eating things.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:11**

When they have been wrapped in purple clothing, their face is wiped because of the dust from the house, which is abundant upon them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:12**

And he has a scepter like a man, a judge of a country, who will not kill the one sinning against him.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:13**

And he has a dagger in his right hand and an axe, but he will not deliver himself from war and robbers.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:14**

From this they are known not to be gods. Therefore do not fear them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:15**

For just as a man's vessel, when shattered, becomes useless, such are their gods, when they have been set up in the houses.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:16**

Their eyes are full of dust from the feet of those entering in.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:17**

And just as for someone who has wronged a king the courts are fenced around, as for one led away to death, their priests fortify their houses with doors and bars and bolts, so that they may not be plundered by robbers.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:18**

They burn lamps, even more than for themselves, of which they cannot see one.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:19**

It is indeed like a beam from the house, but they say their hearts are licked out; when creeping things from the earth eat both them and their clothing, they do not perceive.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:20**

Their face has been blackened from the smoke from the house.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:21**

Upon their body and upon their head bats, swallows, and birds fly down, and likewise also cats.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:22**

From this you will know that they are not gods. Therefore do not fear them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:23**

For the gold that lies around them for beauty, unless someone wipes off the rust, they shall surely not shine; for not even when they were being cast did they perceive.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:24**

They were bought at every price, in whom there is no spirit.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:25**

Without feet they are carried upon shoulders, showing their own dishonor to men, and those serving them are ashamed,

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:26**

because if it ever falls to the ground, it does not rise by them; neither if someone sets it upright will it move by itself; neither if it is tilted will it surely be straightened, but gifts are set before them as before dead people.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:27**

And their priests sell their sacrifices and use them up; likewise also their wives preserve some from them and will not share with poor or powerless.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:28**

The woman sitting apart and the woman who has given birth touch their sacrifices. Therefore, knowing from these things that they are not gods, do not fear them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:29**

For from where could they be called gods? Because women set things before gods of silver and gold and wood.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:30**

And in their houses the priests sit on stools, having their tunics torn and their heads and beards shaved, whose heads are uncovered;

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:31**

and they roar, shouting before their gods, like some at a funeral meal for a dead man.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:32**

Taking away from their clothing, the priests will clothe their wives and children.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:33**

Neither if they suffer evil from someone nor if good will they be able to repay; neither can they appoint a king nor take one away.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:34**

Likewise, neither wealth nor copper will they surely be able to give; if someone vows a vow to them and does not pay it, they will surely not seek it out.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:35**

They shall surely not rescue a man from death, nor deliver a weaker one from a strong one.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:36**

They shall surely not restore a blind man to sight; they shall surely not deliver a man who is in distress.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:37**

They shall surely not have mercy on a widow, nor do good to an orphan.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:38**

The wooden things and the things overlaid with gold and overlaid with silver are likened to stones from the mountain, and those serving them will be put to shame.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:39**

How then must they be regarded or called as being gods?

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:40**

And still, even the Chaldeans dishonor them, who, whenever they see a mute unable to speak, bring Bel near and ask him to give voice, as if he were able to perceive,

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:41**

and they themselves, having understood, cannot leave them, for they have no perception.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:42**

And the women, putting cords around themselves, sit in the roads burning bran as incense.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:43**

But whenever one of them, being drawn away by one of those passing by, sleeps with him, she reproaches her neighbor because she was not considered worthy like herself and because her cord was not torn.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:44**

All the things coming to be among them are false. How then must they be regarded or called as being gods?

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:45**

They have been made by carpenters and goldsmiths; they will become nothing else than what the craftsmen want them to become.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:46**

And those making them shall surely not become long-lived.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:47**

And how then are the things made by them going to be gods? For they left lies and reproach to those coming after.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:48**

For whenever war and evils come upon them, the priests deliberate among themselves where they may hide together with them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:49**

How then is it not to be perceived that they are not gods, who neither save themselves from war nor from evils?

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:50**

For being wooden and overlaid with gold and overlaid with silver, after these things it will be known that they are false; to all the nations and kings it will be clear that they are not gods, but works of men's hands, and no work of God is in them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:51**

To whom then must it be known that they are not gods?

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:52**

For they shall surely not raise up a king of a country, nor shall they surely give rain to men.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:53**

They shall surely not decide their own judgment, nor rescue one being wronged, being powerless; for they are like crows between heaven and earth.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:54**

For also whenever fire falls into a house of wooden gods or ones overlaid with gold or silver, their priests will flee and be preserved, but they themselves will be burned up like beams in the middle.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:55**

And they shall surely not stand against a king and enemies.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:56**

How then must it be accepted or regarded that they are gods? Neither from thieves nor from robbers shall wooden gods and those overlaid with silver and overlaid with gold surely be preserved,

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:57**

from whom the strong will strip off the gold and silver and will go away having the clothing wrapped around them, nor shall they surely help themselves.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:58**

So it is better to be a king displaying his own courage than false gods, or a useful vessel in a house, which the one possessing it will use, than false gods; or even a door in a house, preserving the things in it, than false gods; and a wooden pillar in palaces than false gods.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:59**

For sun and moon and stars, being bright and sent out for uses, are obedient;

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:60**

likewise also lightning, when it appears, is easily seen, and the same also wind blows in every country.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:61**

And when clouds are commanded by God to travel over the whole inhabited world, they complete what was appointed; and fire sent from above to consume mountains and woods does what was commanded.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:62**

But these are likened to them neither in appearances nor in powers.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:63**

Therefore they must neither be regarded nor called as being gods, since they are not able either to judge judgment or to do good to men.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:64**

Therefore, knowing that they are not gods, do not fear them;

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:65**

for they shall surely neither curse kings nor bless them.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:66**

And signs among nations in heaven they shall surely not show; neither will they shine like the sun nor give light like the moon.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:67**

The wild beasts are better than they are, which can escape into shelter and help themselves.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:68**

Therefore in no way is it clear to us that they are gods. Therefore do not fear them;

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:69**

for just as a scarecrow in a cucumber bed guards nothing, so their gods are wooden and overlaid with gold and overlaid with silver.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:70**

In the same way their wooden gods and those overlaid with gold and overlaid with silver are also likened to a thornbush in a garden, on which every bird perches, and likewise also to a dead body thrown in darkness.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:71**

And from the purple and the marble-colored cloth rotting upon them they will be known that they are not gods; and they themselves will finally be eaten, and there will be reproach in the country.

**Letter of Jeremiah 1:72**

Better, then, is a righteous man having no idols, for he will be far from reproach.

## Song of the Three Young Men

### Chapter 1

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:1**

And Azariah, standing up, prayed thus, and opening his mouth in the midst of the fire, said:

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:2**

Blessed are you, Lord God of our fathers, and praised, and your name has been glorified into the ages,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:3**

because you are righteous in all things that you did to us, and all your works are true, and your ways are straight, and all your judgments are truth,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:4**

and you made judgments of truth according to all things that you brought upon us and upon the holy city of our fathers, Jerusalem, because in truth and judgment you brought all these things because of our sins.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:5**

Because we sinned and acted lawlessly by departing from you,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:6**

and we sinned exceedingly in all things, and we did not listen to your commandments, nor keep them together, nor do just as you commanded us, so that it might go well with us.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:7**

And all things, as many as you brought upon us and all things, as many as you did to us, you did in true judgment,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:8**

and you handed us into hands of lawless enemies, most hateful apostates, and to an unrighteous king and most wicked beyond all the earth.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:9**

And now there is no opening the mouth for us; we became shame and reproach to your servants and to those revering you.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:10**

Therefore do not hand us over to the end because of your name, and do not scatter your covenant,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:11**

and do not make your mercy depart from us, because of Abraham loved by you and because of Isaac your servant and Israel your holy one,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:12**

to whom you spoke, to multiply their seed as the stars of Heaven and as the sand by the lip of the sea.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:13**

Because, Master, we were made small beyond all the nations and are humble in all the earth today because of our sins,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:14**

and there is in this time no ruler and prophet and leader, nor whole-burnt offering nor sacrifice nor offering nor incense, nor place to make an offering before you and find mercy;

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:15**

but with a shattered soul and a spirit of humiliation may we be received,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:16**

as with whole-burnt offerings of rams and bulls and as with ten thousands of fat lambs; so may our sacrifice come to be before you today, and may we fully follow after you, because there will be no shame for those trusting upon you.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:17**

And now we follow out with whole heart and fear you and seek your face.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:18**

Do not put us to shame, but do with us according to your fairness and according to the multitude of your mercy,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:19**

and deliver us according to your wonders and give glory to your name, Lord. And let all those showing evils to your servants be turned back in shame,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:20**

and let them be put to shame from all domination, and let their strength be crushed;

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:21**

and let them know that you alone are Lord God and glorious over the whole inhabited world.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:22**

And the king's servants throwing them in did not stop burning the furnace with naphtha and pitch and tow and brushwood.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:23**

And the flame was poured out above the furnace forty-nine cubits.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:24**

And it passed through and set on fire those of the Chaldeans whom it found around the furnace.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:25**

But the angel of the Lord went down together with those around Azariah into the furnace and shook the flame of the fire out from the furnace,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:26**

and made the middle of the furnace like a wind of dew whistling through, and the fire did not touch them at all and did not grieve nor trouble them.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:27**

Then the three, as from one mouth, were hymning and glorifying and blessing God in the furnace, saying:

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:28**

Blessed are you, Lord God of our fathers, and praised and highly exalted into the ages,

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:29**

and blessed is the name of your holy glory, and praised above and highly exalted into all the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:30**

Blessed are you in the temple of your holy glory, and hymned above and exceedingly glorious into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:31**

Blessed are you, the one looking upon abysses, sitting upon Cherubim, and praised and highly exalted into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:32**

Blessed are you upon the throne of your kingdom, and hymned above and highly exalted into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:33**

Blessed are you in the firm span of Heaven, and hymned and glorified into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:34**

Bless the Lord, all works of the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:35**

Bless the Lord, heavens; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:36**

Bless the Lord, angels of the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:37**

Bless the Lord, all waters above Heaven; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:38**

Bless the Lord, all powers of the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:39**

Bless the Lord, sun and moon; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:40**

Bless the Lord, stars of Heaven; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:41**

Let every shower and dew bless the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:42**

Bless the Lord, all winds; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:43**

Bless the Lord, fire and burning heat; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages. [44 Bless the Lord, cold and scorching heat; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:45**

Bless the Lord, dews and snowfalls; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.]

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:46**

Bless the Lord, nights and days; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:47**

Bless the Lord, light and darkness; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:48**

Bless the Lord, cold and burning heat; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:49**

Bless the Lord, frosts and snows; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:50**

Bless the Lord, lightnings and clouds; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:51**

Let the earth bless the Lord; let it hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:52**

Bless the Lord, mountains and hills; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:53**

Bless the Lord, all things growing in the earth; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:54**

Bless the Lord, sea and rivers; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:55**

Bless the Lord, springs; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:56**

Bless the Lord, sea-monsters and all things moving in the waters; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:57**

Bless the Lord, all birds of Heaven; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:58**

Bless the Lord, all wild beasts and cattle; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:59**

Bless the Lord, sons of men; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:60**

Bless the Lord, Israel; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:61**

Bless the Lord, priests of the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:62**

Bless the Lord, servants of the Lord; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:63**

Bless the Lord, spirits and souls of righteous ones; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:64**

Bless the Lord, devout ones and humble in heart; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:65**

Bless the Lord, Hananiah, Azariah, Mishael; hymn and highly exalt him into the ages, because he delivered us out of Hades and saved us from hand of death; he rescued us from midst of a furnace of burning flame, and from midst of fire he rescued us.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:66**

Confess to the Lord, because he is kind, because his mercy is forever.

**Song of the Three Young Men 1:67**

Bless the Lord, all those revering the Lord, the God of gods; hymn and confess, because his mercy is forever.

## Susanna

### Chapter 1

**Susanna 1:1**

And there was a man dwelling in Babylon, and his name was Joakim.

**Susanna 1:2**

And he took a wife, whose name was Susanna, daughter of Hilkiah, very beautiful and fearing the Lord;

**Susanna 1:3**

and her parents were righteous and taught their daughter according to the law of Moses.

**Susanna 1:4**

And Joakim was very rich, and he had a garden adjoining his house; and the Jews were coming to him because he was more glorious than all.

**Susanna 1:5**

And two elders from the people were appointed as judges in that year, concerning whom the Master spoke, that lawlessness came out from Babylon, from elder judges who seemed to govern the people.

**Susanna 1:6**

These were devoting themselves in the house of Joakim, and all those being judged were coming to them.

**Susanna 1:7**

And it came to pass, when the people withdrew at midday, Susanna would enter and walk in her husband's garden.

**Susanna 1:8**

And the two elders were watching her day by day entering and walking, and they came to be in desire for her.

**Susanna 1:9**

And they twisted their own mind and turned aside their eyes so as not to look into Heaven nor remember righteous judgments.

**Susanna 1:10**

And both were pierced concerning her, and they did not report their pain to one another,

**Susanna 1:11**

because they were ashamed to report their desire, that they wished to come together with her.

**Susanna 1:12**

And they were watching eagerly day by day to see her.

**Susanna 1:13**

And one said to the other, Let us now go into the house, because it is meal hour. And going out, they separated from one another,

**Susanna 1:14**

and turning back, they came to the same place, and questioning one another for the cause, they confessed their desire. And then together they arranged a time when they would be able to find her alone.

**Susanna 1:15**

And it came to pass as they watched for a suitable day, she entered once, as yesterday and the day before, with two young girls only, and desired to bathe in the garden, because it was hot.

**Susanna 1:16**

And no one was there except the two elders, hidden and watching her.

**Susanna 1:17**

And she said to the young girls, Bring me oil and soaps now, and close the doors of the garden, so that I may bathe.

**Susanna 1:18**

And they did just as she said and shut the doors of the garden and went out by the side doors to bring the things commanded to them, and they did not see the elders, because they were hidden.

**Susanna 1:19**

And it came to pass when the young girls went out, the two elders rose up and ran upon her

**Susanna 1:20**

and said, Behold, the doors of the garden have been shut, and no one sees us, and we are in desire for you; therefore agree with us and come to be with us.

**Susanna 1:21**

But if not, we will testify against you that a young man was with you and because of this you sent the young girls away from you.

**Susanna 1:22**

And Susanna groaned and said, Straights are mine from every side; for if I do this, it is death for me, and if I do not do it, I will not escape your hands.

**Susanna 1:23**

It is more choice for me, not having done it, to fall into your hands than to sin before the Lord.

**Susanna 1:24**

And Susanna shouted with a great voice, and the two elders also shouted against her.

**Susanna 1:25**

And one, running, opened the doors of the garden.

**Susanna 1:26**

And when those from the house heard the outcry in the garden, they leaped in through the side door to see what had happened to her.

**Susanna 1:27**

But when the elders said their words, the servants were greatly ashamed, because never had such a word been spoken concerning Susanna.

**Susanna 1:28**

And it came to pass on the next day, when the people assembled to her husband Joakim, the two elders came full of lawless intent against Susanna, to put her to death, and said before the people:

**Susanna 1:29**

Send for Susanna daughter of Hilkiah, who is wife of Joakim. And they sent.

**Susanna 1:30**

And she came, and her parents and her children and all her relatives.

**Susanna 1:31**

And Susanna was very delicate and beautiful in appearance.

**Susanna 1:32**

But the lawless men commanded her to be uncovered, for she had been veiled, so that they might be filled with her beauty.

**Susanna 1:33**

And those with her were weeping, and all those who saw her.

**Susanna 1:34**

And standing up, the two elders in the midst of the people placed their hands upon her head.

**Susanna 1:35**

But she, weeping, looked up into Heaven, because her heart was trusting upon the Lord.

**Susanna 1:36**

And the elders said, While we were walking in the garden alone, this woman entered with two maidservants and shut the doors of the garden and released the maidservants;

**Susanna 1:37**

and a young man who had been hidden came to her and lay down with her.

**Susanna 1:38**

But we, being in the corner of the garden, seeing the lawlessness, ran upon them; and seeing them coming together,

**Susanna 1:39**

we were not able to become masters of that one because he was stronger than we, and having opened the doors, he leaped out.

**Susanna 1:40**

But seizing this woman, we questioned her, Who was the young man?

**Susanna 1:41**

And she did not wish to tell us. These things we testify. And the congregation believed them, as elders of the people and judges, and condemned her to die.

**Susanna 1:42**

But Susanna shouted with a great voice and said, O eternal God, knower of hidden things, the one knowing all things before their coming to be,

**Susanna 1:43**

you understand that they testified falsely against me; and behold, I am dying, having done none of the things that these men wickedly devised against me.

**Susanna 1:44**

And the Lord heard her voice.

**Susanna 1:45**

And as she was being led away to be destroyed, God raised up the holy spirit of a younger child, whose name was Daniel,

**Susanna 1:46**

and he shouted with a great voice, I am innocent from this woman's blood.

**Susanna 1:47**

And all the people turned back toward him and said, What is this word that you have spoken?

**Susanna 1:48**

But he, standing in the midst of them, said, Are you so foolish, sons of Israel? Without examining and without knowing the clear thing, you condemned a daughter of Israel?

**Susanna 1:49**

Return to the place of judgment, for these testified falsely against her.

**Susanna 1:50**

And all the people returned with haste. And the elders said to him, Come, sit in the midst of us and report to us, because God has given you the rank of elder.

**Susanna 1:51**

And Daniel said to them, Separate them far from one another, and I will examine them.

**Susanna 1:52**

And when they were separated one from the other, he called one of them and said to him, O one grown old in evil days, now your sins have arrived, which you were doing formerly,

**Susanna 1:53**

judging unrighteous judgments and condemning the innocent but releasing the guilty, while the Lord says, You shall not kill innocent and righteous.

**Susanna 1:54**

Now therefore, if you saw this woman, say: Under what tree did you see them conversing with one another? And he said, Under a mastic tree.

**Susanna 1:55**

And Daniel said, Rightly you lied against your own head; for already an angel of God, having received sentence from God, will split you in the middle.

**Susanna 1:56**

And removing him, he commanded the other to be brought near. And he said to him, Seed of Canaan and not Judah, beauty deceived you, and desire twisted your heart.

**Susanna 1:57**

Thus you were doing to daughters of Israel, and those, fearing, were conversing with you; but a daughter of Judah did not endure your lawlessness.

**Susanna 1:58**

Now therefore tell me: Under what tree did you catch them conversing with one another? And he said, Under a holm oak.

**Susanna 1:59**

And Daniel said to him, Rightly you also lied against your own head; for the angel of God waits, having the sword, to saw you in the middle, so that he may utterly destroy you.

**Susanna 1:60**

And all the congregation shouted with a great voice and blessed God, the one saving those hoping upon him.

**Susanna 1:61**

And they rose up against the two elders, because Daniel established from their own mouth that they had borne false witness, and they did to them in the way that they had wickedly devised against the neighbor,

**Susanna 1:62**

to act according to the law of Moses, and they killed them. And innocent blood was saved in that day.

**Susanna 1:63**

And Hilkiah and his wife praised God concerning their daughter, with Joakim her husband and all the relatives, because no shameful matter was found in her.

**Susanna 1:64**

And Daniel became great before the people from that day and beyond.

## Bel and the Dragon

### Chapter 1

**Bel and the Dragon 1:1**

And King Astyages was added to his fathers, and Cyrus the Persian received his kingdom.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:2**

And Daniel was companion of the king and honored beyond all his friends.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:3**

And there was an idol among the Babylonians, whose name was Bel, and for him each day twelve artabas of fine flour and forty sheep and six measures of wine were spent.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:4**

And the king revered him and went each day to worship him, but Daniel worshiped his God. And the king said to him, Why do you not worship Bel?

**Bel and the Dragon 1:5**

And he said, Because I do not revere handmade idols, but the living God, the one who created Heaven and earth and has lordship of all flesh.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:6**

And the king said to him, Does Bel not seem to you to be a living god? Or do you not see how much he eats and drinks each day?

**Bel and the Dragon 1:7**

And Daniel, laughing, said, Do not be deceived, king; for this is clay within and bronze outside, and he has never eaten nor drunk.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:8**

And becoming angry, the king called his priests and said to them, Unless you tell me who is eating up this expense,

**Bel and the Dragon 1:9**

you will die. But if you show that Bel eats them, Daniel will die, because he blasphemed against Bel. And Daniel said to the king, Let it come to be according to your word.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:10**

And there were seventy priests of Bel, besides wives and children. And the king came with Daniel into the house of Bel.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:11**

And the priests of Bel said, Behold, we are going away outside; but you, king, set out the foods and mix the wine and set it there, and shut the door and seal it with your ring. And coming in the morning, if you do not find everything eaten by Bel, we will die, or Daniel, the one lying against us.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:12**

But they were despising it, because they had made a hidden entrance under the table, and through it they were entering continually and consuming them.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:13**

And it came to pass when those men went out, the king set out the foods for Bel.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:14**

And Daniel ordered his young servants, and they brought ashes and spread them over the whole temple before the king alone; and going out, they shut the door and sealed it with the king's ring and went away.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:15**

But the priests came by night according to their custom, and their wives and their children, and they ate up everything and drank it out.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:16**

And the king rose early in the morning, and Daniel with him.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:17**

And the king said, Are the seals intact, Daniel? And he said, Intact, king.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:18**

And it came to pass, as soon as he opened the doors, the king, looking upon the table, shouted with a great voice, You are great, Bel, and there is not even one deceit with you.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:19**

And Daniel laughed and held the king back so that he would not enter inside, and said, Now see the floor and know whose tracks these are.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:20**

And the king said, I see tracks of men and women and children.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:21**

And becoming angry, the king then seized the priests and their wives and children, and they showed him the hidden doors through which they were entering and spending the things upon the table.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:22**

And the king killed them and gave Bel handed over to Daniel, and he tore down him and his temple.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:23**

And there was a great dragon, and the Babylonians revered it.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:24**

And the king said to Daniel, Will you also say that this one is bronze? Behold, he lives and eats and drinks. You cannot say that this is not a living god; so worship him.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:25**

And Daniel said, I will worship the Lord my God, because this one is the living God.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:26**

But you, king, give me authority, and I will kill the dragon without sword or rod. And the king said, I give it to you.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:27**

And Daniel took pitch and fat and hairs and boiled them together and made lumps and gave them into the mouth of the dragon, and after eating, the dragon burst apart. And he said, See the things you revere.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:28**

And it came to pass when the Babylonians heard, they were greatly indignant and turned together against the king and said, The king has become a Jew; he tore down Bel and killed the dragon and slaughtered the priests.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:29**

And coming to the king, they said, Hand Daniel over to us; but if not, we will kill you and your house.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:30**

And the king saw that they were pressing him greatly, and being compelled, the king handed Daniel over to them.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:31**

And they threw him into the den of lions, and he was there six days.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:32**

And there were seven lions in the den, and each day two bodies and two sheep were given to them; but then it was not given to them, so that they might eat Daniel up.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:33**

And Habakkuk the prophet was in Judea, and he had boiled stew and broken bread into a bowl, and he was going into the plain to carry it away to the harvesters.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:34**

And the angel of the Lord said to Habakkuk, Carry away the meal that you have into Babylon for Daniel, into the den of lions.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:35**

And Habakkuk said, Lord, I have not seen Babylon, and I do not know the den.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:36**

And the angel of the Lord took hold of the crown of his head, and carrying him by the hair of his head, set him in Babylon above the den in the rush of his wind.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:37**

And Habakkuk shouted, saying, Daniel, Daniel, take the meal that God sent you.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:38**

And Daniel said, For you remembered me, O God, and did not forsake those loving you.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:39**

And Daniel rose up and ate. And the angel of God restored Habakkuk immediately to his place.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:40**

But the king came on the seventh day to mourn for Daniel, and he came to the den and looked in, and behold, Daniel was sitting.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:41**

And shouting with a great voice, he said, You are great, Lord God of Daniel, and there is no other besides you.

**Bel and the Dragon 1:42**

And he drew him up, but those responsible for his destruction he threw into the den, and they were eaten up immediately before him.

## 1 Maccabees

### Chapter 1

**1 Maccabees 1:1**

And it came to pass after Alexander son of Philip, the Macedonian, who came out from the land of Kittim, struck Darius king of Persians and Medes and reigned in his place, formerly over Greece.

**1 Maccabees 1:2**

And he waged many wars and gained mastery of many strongholds and slaughtered kings of the earth.

**1 Maccabees 1:3**

And he went through as far as the ends of the earth and took spoils of a multitude of nations. And the earth was quiet before him, and he was lifted up, and his heart was raised up.

**1 Maccabees 1:4**

And he gathered a very strong force and ruled countries and nations and tyrants, and they became tribute to him.

**1 Maccabees 1:5**

And after these things he fell upon the bed and knew that he was dying.

**1 Maccabees 1:6**

And he called his servants, the glorious ones, those brought up with him from youth, and divided his kingdom to them while he was still living.

**1 Maccabees 1:7**

And Alexander reigned twelve years and died.

**1 Maccabees 1:8**

And his servants gained mastery, each in his place.

**1 Maccabees 1:9**

And they all put on diadems after he died, and their sons after them for many years, and they multiplied evils in the earth.

**1 Maccabees 1:10**

And from them came out a sinful root, Antiochus Epiphanes, son of King Antiochus, who had been a hostage in Rome; and he reigned in the one hundred thirty-seventh year of the kingdom of Greeks.

**1 Maccabees 1:11**

In those days lawless sons came out from Israel and persuaded many, saying, Let us go and make a covenant with the nations around us, because from the time we were separated from them, many evils found us.

**1 Maccabees 1:12**

And the word was good in their eyes,

**1 Maccabees 1:13**

and some from the people were eager, and they went to the king, and he gave them authority to do the ordinances of the nations.

**1 Maccabees 1:14**

And they built a gymnasium in Jerusalem according to the customs of the nations,

**1 Maccabees 1:15**

and made uncircumcision for themselves and departed from the holy covenant and were yoked to the nations and were sold to do evil.

**1 Maccabees 1:16**

And the kingdom was prepared before Antiochus, and he proposed to reign over Egypt, so that he might reign over the two kingdoms.

**1 Maccabees 1:17**

And he entered into Egypt with a heavy crowd, with chariots and with elephants and with horsemen and with a great fleet,

**1 Maccabees 1:18**

and they waged war against Ptolemy king of Egypt. And Ptolemy was turned away from his face and fled, and many wounded fell.

**1 Maccabees 1:19**

And they seized the strong cities in the land of Egypt, and he took the spoils of the land of Egypt.

**1 Maccabees 1:20**

And Antiochus returned after striking Egypt in the one hundred forty-third year, and he went up against Israel and went up into Jerusalem with a heavy crowd.

**1 Maccabees 1:21**

And they entered into the sanctuary in pride, and he took the golden altar and the lampstand of the light and all its vessels,

**1 Maccabees 1:22**

and the table of presentation and the libation vessels and the bowls and the golden censers and the veil and the crowns and the golden ornament before the face of the temple, and he stripped all things.

**1 Maccabees 1:23**

And he took the silver and the gold and the desirable vessels, and he took the hidden treasures that he found;

**1 Maccabees 1:24**

and taking everything, he went away into his own land. And he made slaughter and spoke great pride.

**1 Maccabees 1:25**

And great mourning came upon Israel in every place of theirs.

**1 Maccabees 1:26**

And rulers and elders groaned; maidens and young men became weak, and the beauty of women was changed.

**1 Maccabees 1:27**

Every bridegroom took up lament, and the woman sitting in the bridal chamber came to be in mourning.

**1 Maccabees 1:28**

And the earth was shaken over those dwelling in it, and all the house of Jacob put on shame.

**1 Maccabees 1:29**

And after two years of days the king sent a ruler of tribute into the cities of Judah, and he came into Jerusalem with a heavy crowd.

**1 Maccabees 1:30**

And he spoke peaceable words to them in deceit, and they trusted him. And he fell upon the city suddenly and struck it with a great blow and destroyed many people from Israel.

**1 Maccabees 1:31**

And he took the spoils of the city and set it on fire with fire and tore down its houses and its walls around.

**1 Maccabees 1:32**

And they took the women and the children captive, and they inherited the livestock.

**1 Maccabees 1:33**

And they built the city of David with a great and strong wall, with fortified towers, and it became a citadel for them.

**1 Maccabees 1:34**

And they placed there a sinful nation, lawless men, and they strengthened themselves in it.

**1 Maccabees 1:35**

And they stored weapons and provisions, and gathering the spoils of Jerusalem, they deposited them there, and they became a great snare.

**1 Maccabees 1:36**

And it became an ambush against the sanctuary and an evil adversary to Israel always.

**1 Maccabees 1:37**

And they poured out innocent blood around the sanctuary and defiled the sanctuary.

**1 Maccabees 1:38**

And the inhabitants of Jerusalem fled because of them, and it became a dwelling of foreigners; and she became foreign to her offspring, and her children forsook her.

**1 Maccabees 1:39**

Her sanctuary was made desolate like a wilderness, her feasts were turned into mourning, her Sabbaths into reproach, her honor into contempt.

**1 Maccabees 1:40**

According to her glory, her dishonor was multiplied, and her height was turned into mourning.

**1 Maccabees 1:41**

And King Antiochus wrote to his whole kingdom that all should be one people

**1 Maccabees 1:42**

and that each should abandon his laws. And all the nations accepted according to the word of the king.

**1 Maccabees 1:43**

And many from Israel took pleasure in his worship and sacrificed to idols and profaned the Sabbath.

**1 Maccabees 1:44**

And the king sent letters by hand of messengers into Jerusalem and the cities of Judah, to go after foreign laws of the land,

**1 Maccabees 1:45**

and to forbid whole-burnt offerings and sacrifice and libation from the sanctuary, and to profane Sabbaths and feasts,

**1 Maccabees 1:46**

and to defile sanctuary and holy ones,

**1 Maccabees 1:47**

and to build altars and sacred precincts and idol shrines, and to sacrifice pigs and common livestock,

**1 Maccabees 1:48**

and to leave their sons uncircumcised, to make their souls abominable with every unclean thing and profanation,

**1 Maccabees 1:49**

so as to forget the law and change all the ordinances;

**1 Maccabees 1:50**

and whoever should not act according to the word of the king would die.

**1 Maccabees 1:51**

According to all these words he wrote to his whole kingdom, and he made overseers over all the people and commanded the cities of Judah to sacrifice, city by city.

**1 Maccabees 1:52**

And many from the people were gathered together to them, everyone who abandoned the law, and they did evils in the land

**1 Maccabees 1:53**

and put Israel in hidden places, in every place of refuge they had.

**1 Maccabees 1:54**

And on the fifteenth day of Chislev, in the one hundred forty-fifth year, they built an abomination of desolation upon the altar, and in the cities of Judah around they built altars;

**1 Maccabees 1:55**

and upon the doors of the houses and in the streets they were burning incense.

**1 Maccabees 1:56**

And the books of the law that they found, tearing them apart, they burned with fire.

**1 Maccabees 1:57**

And wherever a book of covenant was found with someone, and if anyone consented to the law, the judgment of the king put him to death.

**1 Maccabees 1:58**

In their strength they did thus to Israel, to those found month by month in the cities.

**1 Maccabees 1:59**

And on the twenty-fifth of the month, they were sacrificing upon the altar that was upon the altar.

**1 Maccabees 1:60**

And the women who had circumcised their children they put to death according to the ordinance,

**1 Maccabees 1:61**

and they hung the infants from their necks, and plundered their houses, and killed those who had circumcised them.

**1 Maccabees 1:62**

And many in Israel were strengthened and fortified in themselves not to eat common things,

**1 Maccabees 1:63**

and they chose to die, so that they might not be defiled by the foods and might not profane the holy covenant, and they died.

**1 Maccabees 1:64**

And great wrath came upon Israel exceedingly.

### Chapter 2

**1 Maccabees 2:1**

In those days Mattathias son of John son of Simeon, a priest of the sons of Joarib, rose up from Jerusalem and sat in Modein.

**1 Maccabees 2:2**

And he had five sons: John, the one called Gaddis,

**1 Maccabees 2:3**

Simon, the one called Thassi,

**1 Maccabees 2:4**

Judas, the one called Maccabeus,

**1 Maccabees 2:5**

Eleazar, the one called Avaran, and Jonathan, the one called Apphus.

**1 Maccabees 2:6**

And he saw the blasphemies that were happening in Judah and in Jerusalem,

**1 Maccabees 2:7**

and said, Woe to me! Why was I born for this, to see the crushing of my people and the crushing of the holy city, and to sit there when she was given into hand of enemies and the sanctuary into hand of foreigners?

**1 Maccabees 2:8**

Her temple became like a dishonored man;

**1 Maccabees 2:9**

the vessels of her glory were led away captive; her infants were killed in the streets, her young men by sword of enemy.

**1 Maccabees 2:10**

What nation did not inherit her kingdom and gain mastery of her spoils?

**1 Maccabees 2:11**

All her adornment was taken away; instead of a free woman she became a slave woman.

**1 Maccabees 2:12**

And behold, our holy things and our beauty and our glory were made desolate, and the nations profaned them.

**1 Maccabees 2:13**

Why still live for us?

**1 Maccabees 2:14**

And Mattathias and his sons tore their garments and wrapped themselves in sackcloth and mourned exceedingly.

**1 Maccabees 2:15**

And those from the king, those compelling the apostasy, came into Modein the city, so that they might sacrifice.

**1 Maccabees 2:16**

And many from Israel came near to them, and Mattathias and his sons were gathered together.

**1 Maccabees 2:17**

And those from the king answered and spoke to Mattathias, saying, You are a ruler and honored and great in this city and established with sons and brothers.

**1 Maccabees 2:18**

Now therefore come near first and do the ordinance of the king, as all the nations and the men of Judah and those left in Jerusalem have done, and you and your house will be among the friends of the king, and you and your sons will be glorified with silver and gold and many gifts.

**1 Maccabees 2:19**

And Mattathias answered and said with a great voice, If all the nations that are in the house of the kingdom of the king listen to him, to depart each from the worship of his fathers, and have chosen his commandments,

**1 Maccabees 2:20**

nevertheless I and my sons and my brothers will walk in the covenant of our fathers.

**1 Maccabees 2:21**

May it be far from us to abandon law and ordinances.

**1 Maccabees 2:22**

We will not listen to the words of the king, to turn aside from our worship to the right or left.

**1 Maccabees 2:23**

And when he ceased speaking these words, a Judean man came near in the eyes of all, to sacrifice upon the altar in Modein according to the ordinance of the king.

**1 Maccabees 2:24**

And Mattathias saw and was zealous, and his kidneys trembled, and he brought up wrath according to the judgment, and running, he slaughtered him upon the altar.

**1 Maccabees 2:25**

And the man of the king, the one compelling sacrifice, he killed at that time, and he tore down the altar.

**1 Maccabees 2:26**

And he was zealous for the law, just as Phinehas did to Zimri son of Salu.

**1 Maccabees 2:27**

And Mattathias cried out in the city with a great voice, saying, Everyone zealous for the law and standing by covenant, let him go out after me.

**1 Maccabees 2:28**

And he and his sons fled into the mountains and abandoned whatever they had in the city.

**1 Maccabees 2:29**

Then many seeking righteousness and judgment went down into the wilderness to sit there,

**1 Maccabees 2:30**

they and their sons and their wives and their livestock, because the evils were multiplied upon them.

**1 Maccabees 2:31**

And it was reported to the men of the king and to the forces that were in Jerusalem, City of David, that men who scattered the commandment of the king had gone down into the hidden places in the wilderness.

**1 Maccabees 2:32**

And many ran after them and overtook them and camped against them and waged war against them on the day of the Sabbaths.

**1 Maccabees 2:33**

And they said to them, Enough until now. Come out and act according to the word of the king, and you will live.

**1 Maccabees 2:34**

And they said, We will not come out, nor will we do the word of the king, to profane the day of the Sabbaths.

**1 Maccabees 2:35**

And they hastened war against them.

**1 Maccabees 2:36**

And they did not answer them, nor throw a stone at them, nor block up the hidden places,

**1 Maccabees 2:37**

saying, Let us all die in our simplicity. Heaven and earth testify against us that you destroy us without judgment.

**1 Maccabees 2:38**

And they rose up against them in the war on the Sabbaths, and they died, they and their wives and their children and their livestock, up to a thousand souls of men.

**1 Maccabees 2:39**

And Mattathias and his friends knew, and they mourned over them exceedingly.

**1 Maccabees 2:40**

And a man said to his neighbor, If we all do as our brothers did and do not fight against the nations for our souls and our ordinances, now they will more quickly utterly destroy us from the earth.

**1 Maccabees 2:41**

And they took counsel on that day, saying, Every man who comes against us for war on the day of the Sabbaths, let us fight opposite him, and let us surely not all die as our brothers died in the hidden places.

**1 Maccabees 2:42**

Then a congregation of Hasideans was gathered to them, strong in power from Israel, everyone volunteering for the law;

**1 Maccabees 2:43**

and all those fleeing from the evils were added to them and became a support for them.

**1 Maccabees 2:44**

And they waged war with a force and struck sinners in their wrath and lawless men in their anger; and the rest fled to the nations to be saved.

**1 Maccabees 2:45**

And Mattathias and his friends went around, and they tore down the altars

**1 Maccabees 2:46**

and circumcised the uncircumcised boys, as many as they found within the borders of Israel, with strength.

**1 Maccabees 2:47**

And they pursued the sons of pride, and the work prospered in their hand.

**1 Maccabees 2:48**

And they laid hold of the law out of hand of the nations and out of hand of the kings, and they did not give a horn to the sinner.

**1 Maccabees 2:49**

And the days of Mattathias drew near to die, and he said to his sons, Now pride and rebuke have been established, and a time of overthrow and wrath of anger.

**1 Maccabees 2:50**

And now, children, be zealous for the law and give your souls for the covenant of our fathers.

**1 Maccabees 2:51**

Remember the works of our fathers, which they did in their generations, and receive great glory and an eternal name.

**1 Maccabees 2:52**

Was not Abraham found faithful in trial, and it was counted to him for righteousness?

**1 Maccabees 2:53**

Joseph, in the time of his distress, kept commandment and became lord of Egypt.

**1 Maccabees 2:54**

Phinehas our father, by being zealous with zeal, received a covenant of eternal priesthood.

**1 Maccabees 2:55**

Joshua, by fulfilling a word, became judge in Israel.

**1 Maccabees 2:56**

Caleb, by bearing witness in the assembly, received an inheritance of land.

**1 Maccabees 2:57**

David, in his mercy, inherited a throne of kingdom into age of age.

**1 Maccabees 2:58**

Elijah, by being zealous with zeal for the law, was taken up as far as Heaven.

**1 Maccabees 2:59**

Hananiah, Azariah, and Mishael, having trusted, were saved from flame.

**1 Maccabees 2:60**

Daniel, in his simplicity, was rescued from mouth of lions.

**1 Maccabees 2:61**

And so consider by generation and generation that all those hoping upon him will not be weak.

**1 Maccabees 2:62**

And do not fear from words of a sinful man, because his glory is for dung and worms;

**1 Maccabees 2:63**

today he will be lifted up, and tomorrow he shall surely not be found, because he returned into his dust, and his reasoning perished.

**1 Maccabees 2:64**

And you, children, be strong and act like men in the law, because in it you will be glorified.

**1 Maccabees 2:65**

And behold, Simon your brother, I know that he is a man of counsel; listen to him all the days. He will be a father to you.

**1 Maccabees 2:66**

And Judas Maccabeus, strong in power from his youth, he will be commander of the army for you and will fight the war of peoples.

**1 Maccabees 2:67**

And you, bring near to yourselves all doers of the law and take vengeance with the vengeance of your people.

**1 Maccabees 2:68**

Repay recompense to the nations, and pay attention to the ordinances of the law.

**1 Maccabees 2:69**

And he blessed them and was added to his fathers.

**1 Maccabees 2:70**

And he died in the one hundred forty-sixth year, and his sons buried him in the tombs of their fathers in Modein, and all Israel beat themselves for him with a great beating.

### Chapter 3

**1 Maccabees 3:1**

And Judas, the one called Maccabeus, his son, rose up in his place.

**1 Maccabees 3:2**

And all his brothers and all who had clung to his father were helping him, and they were fighting the war of Israel with gladness.

**1 Maccabees 3:3**

And he spread glory for his people and put on a breastplate like a giant and girded on his war gear and waged wars, sheltering the camp with sword.

**1 Maccabees 3:4**

And he was likened to a lion in his works and like a lion cub roaring for prey.

**1 Maccabees 3:5**

And he pursued lawless ones, searching them out, and burned those troubling his people.

**1 Maccabees 3:6**

And the lawless were drawn in from fear of him, and all the workers of lawlessness were troubled together, and salvation prospered in his hand.

**1 Maccabees 3:7**

And he embittered many kings and gladdened Jacob by his works, and his memorial is for blessing until the age.

**1 Maccabees 3:8**

And he went through the cities of Judah and utterly destroyed ungodly ones from it and turned wrath away from Israel,

**1 Maccabees 3:9**

and he was named as far as the end of the earth and gathered those perishing.

**1 Maccabees 3:10**

And Apollonius gathered nations and a great force from Samaria to fight against Israel.

**1 Maccabees 3:11**

And Judas knew and went out to meet him and struck him and killed him. And many wounded fell, and the rest fled.

**1 Maccabees 3:12**

And they took their spoils, and Judas took the sword of Apollonius and was fighting with it all the days.

**1 Maccabees 3:13**

And Seron, ruler of the force of Syria, heard that Judas had gathered a gathering and assembly of faithful ones going out with him into war,

**1 Maccabees 3:14**

and he said, I will make a name for myself and be glorified in the kingdom, and I will fight Judas and those with him, those despising the word of the king.

**1 Maccabees 3:15**

And he added to go up; and with him went up a strong camp of ungodly ones to help him and to make vengeance among sons of Israel.

**1 Maccabees 3:16**

And they drew near as far as the ascent of Beth-horon, and Judas went out to meet them with few men.

**1 Maccabees 3:17**

And when they saw the camp coming to meet them, they said to Judas, How will we be able, being few, to fight against such a strong multitude? And we are faint, being without food today.

**1 Maccabees 3:18**

And Judas said, It is easy for many to be shut up in hands of few, and there is no difference before the God of heaven to save by many or by few;

**1 Maccabees 3:19**

because victory of war is not in multitude of force, but strength is from heaven.

**1 Maccabees 3:20**

They are coming against us in multitude of insolence and lawlessness, to remove us and our wives and our children, to plunder us;

**1 Maccabees 3:21**

but we are fighting concerning our souls and our laws.

**1 Maccabees 3:22**

And he himself will crush them before our face; but you, do not fear because of them.

**1 Maccabees 3:23**

And when he ceased speaking, he leaped upon them suddenly, and Seron and his camp were crushed before him.

**1 Maccabees 3:24**

And they pursued him in the descent of Beth-horon as far as the plain; and about eight hundred men from them fell, but the rest fled into land of Philistines.

**1 Maccabees 3:25**

And the fear of Judas and his brothers and the terror began to fall upon the nations around them.

**1 Maccabees 3:26**

And his name drew near as far as the king, and every nation was telling out the battles of Judas.

**1 Maccabees 3:27**

And when King Antiochus heard these words, he was angered with wrath and sent and gathered all the forces of his kingdom, a very strong camp.

**1 Maccabees 3:28**

And he opened his treasury and gave wages to his forces for a year and commanded them to be ready for every need.

**1 Maccabees 3:29**

And he saw that the silver had failed from the treasuries and that the tributes of the country were few, because of the dissension and blow that he had prepared in the land, to remove the laws that were from the first days.

**1 Maccabees 3:30**

And he became cautious lest he not have, as once and twice, for the expenses and the gifts that he had given before with a lavish hand; and he had abounded beyond the kings before him.

**1 Maccabees 3:31**

And he was greatly perplexed in his soul and took counsel to go into Persia and take the tributes of the countries and gather much silver.

**1 Maccabees 3:32**

And he left Lysias, a glorious man and from the royal line, over the affairs of the king from the Euphrates River as far as the borders of Egypt,

**1 Maccabees 3:33**

and to rear Antiochus his son until he returned.

**1 Maccabees 3:34**

And he handed over to him half of the forces and the elephants and commanded him concerning all that he wanted, and concerning those dwelling in Judea and Jerusalem,

**1 Maccabees 3:35**

to send a force against them to wipe out and remove the strength of Israel and the remnant of Jerusalem and to remove their memorial from the place,

**1 Maccabees 3:36**

and to settle sons of foreigners in all their borders and to allot their land.

**1 Maccabees 3:37**

And the king took half of the forces that were left and departed from Antioch, from the city of his kingdom, in the one hundred forty-seventh year, and he crossed the Euphrates River and went through the upper countries.

**1 Maccabees 3:38**

And Lysias chose Ptolemy son of Dorymenes and Nicanor and Gorgias, powerful men of the friends of the king,

**1 Maccabees 3:39**

and sent with them forty thousand men and seven thousand horse to go out into land of Judah and destroy it according to the word of the king.

**1 Maccabees 3:40**

And they departed with all their force and came and camped near Emmaus in the plain land.

**1 Maccabees 3:41**

And the merchants of the country heard their name and took silver and very much gold and fetters and came into the camp to take the sons of Israel as slaves. And a force of Syria and of the land of foreigners was added to them.

**1 Maccabees 3:42**

And Judas and his brothers saw that the evils had been multiplied and that the forces were camping in their borders, and they recognized the words of the king that he had commanded to do to the people for destruction and completion.

**1 Maccabees 3:43**

And each said to his neighbor, Let us raise up the ruin of our people, and let us fight concerning our people and the holy things.

**1 Maccabees 3:44**

And the congregation was gathered together to be ready for war and to pray and ask mercy and compassions.

**1 Maccabees 3:45**

And Jerusalem was uninhabited like a wilderness; there was no one entering and going out from her offspring, and the sanctuary was being trampled, and sons of foreigners were in the citadel, a lodging for the nations; and delight was removed from Jacob, and flute and harp failed.

**1 Maccabees 3:46**

And they were gathered and came to Massepha opposite Jerusalem, because a place of prayer was in Massepha formerly for Israel.

**1 Maccabees 3:47**

And they fasted on that day and wrapped themselves in sackcloth and put ashes upon their heads and tore their garments.

**1 Maccabees 3:48**

And they spread open the book of the law, the one about which the nations were searching out the likenesses of their idols.

**1 Maccabees 3:49**

And they brought the garments of the priesthood and the firstfruits and the tithes, and they raised the Nazirites who had fulfilled the days,

**1 Maccabees 3:50**

and they shouted with a voice into heaven, saying, What shall we do with these, and where shall we lead them away?

**1 Maccabees 3:52**

And your holy things have been trampled and profaned, and your priests are in mourning and humiliation. And behold, the nations have been gathered together against us to remove us; you know what they are reasoning against us.

**1 Maccabees 3:53**

How will we be able to stand before their face, unless you help us?

**1 Maccabees 3:54**

And they trumpeted with the trumpets and shouted with a great voice.

**1 Maccabees 3:55**

And after this Judas appointed leaders of the people, commanders of thousands and commanders of hundreds and commanders of fifties and commanders of tens.

**1 Maccabees 3:56**

And they told those building houses and betrothing wives and planting vineyards and the cowardly to return, each to his house, according to the law.

**1 Maccabees 3:57**

And the camp departed and camped south of Emmaus.

**1 Maccabees 3:58**

And Judas said, Gird yourselves and become mighty sons and be ready for the morning, to fight against these nations gathered together against us, to remove us and our holy things;

**1 Maccabees 3:59**

because it is better for us to die in war than to look upon the evils of our nation and the holy things.

**1 Maccabees 3:60**

But as the will may be in heaven, so he will do.

### Chapter 4

**1 Maccabees 4:1**

And Gorgias took five thousand men and a thousand chosen horse, and the camp departed by night,

**1 Maccabees 4:2**

so as to attack the camp of the Jews and strike them suddenly; and the sons of the citadel were guides for him.

**1 Maccabees 4:3**

And Judas heard, and he and the mighty men departed to strike the force of the king that was in Emmaus,

**1 Maccabees 4:4**

while the forces were still scattered from the camp.

**1 Maccabees 4:5**

And Gorgias came into the camp of Judas by night and found no one, and he was seeking them in the mountains, because he said, These men are fleeing from us.

**1 Maccabees 4:6**

And at daybreak Judas appeared in the plain with three thousand men, except they did not have coverings and swords as they wished.

**1 Maccabees 4:7**

And they saw a strong camp of nations armored, and cavalry surrounding it, and these were trained in war.

**1 Maccabees 4:8**

And Judas said to the men with him, Do not fear their multitude, and do not be cowardly before their rush.

**1 Maccabees 4:9**

Remember how our fathers were saved in the Red Sea, because Pharaoh pursued them with a force.

**1 Maccabees 4:10**

And now let us cry out to heaven, if perhaps he may have mercy on us and remember the covenant of our fathers and crush this camp before our face today,

**1 Maccabees 4:11**

and all the nations will know that there is one redeeming and saving Israel.

**1 Maccabees 4:12**

And the foreigners lifted up their eyes and saw them coming face to face,

**1 Maccabees 4:13**

and they came out from the camp into war; and those with Judas trumpeted,

**1 Maccabees 4:14**

and they joined battle, and the nations were crushed and fled into the plain,

**1 Maccabees 4:15**

but all the last ones fell by sword. And they pursued them as far as Gazara and as far as the plains of Idumea and Azotus and Jamnia, and about three thousand men from them fell.

**1 Maccabees 4:16**

And Judas and the force returned from pursuing after them,

**1 Maccabees 4:17**

and he said to the people, Do not desire the spoils, because war is before us,

**1 Maccabees 4:18**

and Gorgias and the force are in the mountain near us. But now stand before our enemies and fight them, and after these things take the spoils with boldness.

**1 Maccabees 4:19**

While Judas was still speaking these things, a certain portion appeared peering out from the mountain.

**1 Maccabees 4:20**

And he saw that they had been turned to flight and were setting the camp on fire; for the smoke being seen made clear what had happened.

**1 Maccabees 4:21**

But those perceiving these things became very cowardly; and perceiving also the camp of Judas in the plain ready for battle line,

**1 Maccabees 4:22**

they all fled into land of foreigners.

**1 Maccabees 4:23**

And Judas returned to the plundering of the camp, and they took much gold and silver and blue cloth and sea-purple and great wealth.

**1 Maccabees 4:24**

And turning back, they were hymning and blessing toward heaven, because he is good, because his mercy is forever.

**1 Maccabees 4:25**

And great salvation came to Israel on that day.

**1 Maccabees 4:26**

But as many of the foreigners as were preserved came and reported to Lysias all the things that had happened.

**1 Maccabees 4:27**

And when he heard, he was confused and discouraged, because such things as he wanted had not happened to Israel, and such things as the king had commanded him had not turned out.

**1 Maccabees 4:28**

And in the following year Lysias mustered sixty thousand chosen men and five thousand horse, so as to make war against them.

**1 Maccabees 4:29**

And they came into Idumea and camped in Bethsura, and Judas met them with ten thousand men.

**1 Maccabees 4:30**

And he saw the strong camp and prayed and said, Blessed are you, Savior of Israel, who crushed the rush of the mighty one by hand of your servant David and handed the camp of the foreigners into hands of Jonathan son of Saul and the one carrying his gear.

**1 Maccabees 4:31**

So shut up this camp in hand of your people Israel, and let them be ashamed in their force and their horse;

**1 Maccabees 4:32**

give them cowardice and melt the boldness of their strength, and let them be shaken by their crushing.

**1 Maccabees 4:33**

Cast them down by the sword of those loving you, and let all who know your name praise you with hymns.

**1 Maccabees 4:34**

And they engaged one another, and about five thousand men from the camp of Lysias fell, and they fell opposite them.

**1 Maccabees 4:35**

And when Lysias saw the rout that had happened to his own array, and the courage that had happened to Judas's men, and how they were ready either to live or to die nobly, he departed to Antioch and enlisted foreigners. And having made the army that had come to be more numerous, he planned to return again into Judea.

**1 Maccabees 4:36**

And Judas and his brothers said, Behold, our enemies have been crushed; let us go up to cleanse the holy things and dedicate them.

**1 Maccabees 4:37**

And all the camp was gathered, and they went up to Mount Zion.

**1 Maccabees 4:38**

And they saw the sanctuary made desolate and the altar profaned and the gates burned down and plants growing in the courts as in a thicket or as on one of the mountains, and the chambers torn down.

**1 Maccabees 4:39**

And they tore their garments and beat themselves with a great beating and put ashes upon their heads,

**1 Maccabees 4:40**

and they fell upon face upon the ground and trumpeted with the trumpets of signals and shouted into heaven.

**1 Maccabees 4:41**

Then Judas ordered men to fight those in the citadel until he should cleanse the holy things.

**1 Maccabees 4:42**

And he chose blameless priests, willing ones of the law,

**1 Maccabees 4:43**

and they cleansed the holy things and took the stones of defilement into an unclean place.

**1 Maccabees 4:44**

And they took counsel concerning the altar of whole-burnt offering that had been profaned, what they should do with it.

**1 Maccabees 4:45**

And good counsel fell upon them to tear it down, lest it become a reproach to them because the nations defiled it. And they tore down the altar.

**1 Maccabees 4:46**

And they deposited the stones on the mountain of the house in a suitable place until a prophet should come to answer concerning them.

**1 Maccabees 4:47**

And they took whole stones according to the law and built a new altar according to the former one.

**1 Maccabees 4:48**

And they built the holy things and the things inside the house, and they sanctified the courts.

**1 Maccabees 4:49**

And they made new holy vessels and brought the lampstand and the altar of incenses and the table into the temple.

**1 Maccabees 4:50**

And they burned incense upon the altar and lit the lamps upon the lampstand, and they were shining in the temple.

**1 Maccabees 4:51**

And they placed bread upon the table and spread out the veils and completed all the works that they made.

**1 Maccabees 4:52**

And they rose early in the morning on the twenty-fifth of the ninth month, this is the month Chislev, in the one hundred forty-eighth year,

**1 Maccabees 4:53**

and they brought up sacrifice according to the law upon the new altar of whole-burnt offerings that they made.

**1 Maccabees 4:54**

According to the time and according to the day on which the nations profaned it, on that day it was dedicated with songs and lyres and harps and with cymbals.

**1 Maccabees 4:55**

And all the people fell upon face and worshiped and blessed toward heaven the one who prospered them.

**1 Maccabees 4:56**

And they made the dedication of the altar eight days and brought whole-burnt offerings with gladness and sacrificed a sacrifice of salvation and praise.

**1 Maccabees 4:57**

And they adorned the front of the temple with golden crowns and small shields, and they dedicated the gates and the chambers and set doors on them.

**1 Maccabees 4:58**

And very great gladness came to be among the people, and reproach of nations was turned away.

**1 Maccabees 4:59**

And Judas and his brothers and all the assembly of Israel established that the days of dedication of the altar should be kept in their seasons, year by year, eight days from the twenty-fifth of the month Chislev, with gladness and joy.

**1 Maccabees 4:60**

And at that time they built Mount Zion all around with high walls and fortified towers, lest the nations ever come and trample them, as they did formerly.

**1 Maccabees 4:61**

And he appointed a force there to keep it, and they fortified it to keep Bethsura, so that the people might have a stronghold before the face of Idumea.

### Chapter 5

**1 Maccabees 5:1**

And it came to pass, when the nations around heard that the altar had been built and the holy place renewed as formerly,

**1 Maccabees 5:2**

that they took counsel to take away the race of Jacob, the ones who were in their midst, and they began to put some of the people to death and to destroy them.

**1 Maccabees 5:3**

And Judas fought against the sons of Esau in Idumea, in Akrabattene, because they were sitting around Israel, and he struck them with a great blow and checked them and took their spoils.

**1 Maccabees 5:4**

And he remembered the evil of the sons of Baian, who were a snare and stumbling block to the people, lying in wait for them in the roads.

**1 Maccabees 5:5**

And they were shut up by him in the towers, and he camped against them and devoted them to destruction and burned the towers with fire, with all those who were in them.

**1 Maccabees 5:6**

And he crossed over against the sons of Ammon and found a strong hand and a numerous people, and Timothy leading them.

**1 Maccabees 5:7**

And he joined many battles against them, and they were crushed before his face, and he struck them.

**1 Maccabees 5:8**

And he seized Jazer beforehand, and her daughters, and returned into Judea.

**1 Maccabees 5:9**

And the nations in Gilead gathered together against Israel, against the ones who were on their borders, to take them away, and they fled into Dathema the fortress.

**1 Maccabees 5:10**

And they sent letters to Judas and his brothers, saying, The nations around us have been gathered against us to take us away,

**1 Maccabees 5:11**

and they are preparing to come and seize the fortress into which we fled, and Timothy leads their force.

**1 Maccabees 5:12**

Now therefore come and rescue us from their hand, because a multitude from us has fallen,

**1 Maccabees 5:13**

and all our brothers who were in the places of Tobiah have died, and they have taken captive their wives and children and baggage, and there they destroyed about one thousand men.

**1 Maccabees 5:14**

While the letters were still being read, behold, other messengers came from Galilee, having torn their garments, reporting according to these words, saying

**1 Maccabees 5:15**

that they have been gathered against them from Ptolemais and Tyre and Sidon and all Galilee of foreigners, to consume us.

**1 Maccabees 5:16**

And when Judas and the people heard these words, a great assembly was gathered to take counsel about what they should do for their brothers who were in affliction and being warred against by them.

**1 Maccabees 5:17**

And Judas said to Simon his brother, Choose men for yourself, and go and rescue your brothers who are in Galilee; but I and Jonathan my brother will go into Gilead.

**1 Maccabees 5:18**

And he left Joseph son of Zechariah and Azariah, leaders of the people, with the rest of the force in Judea for keeping guard,

**1 Maccabees 5:19**

and he commanded them, saying, Stand over this people, and do not join battle against the nations until we return.

**1 Maccabees 5:20**

And three thousand men were assigned to Simon, to go into Galilee, and eight thousand men to Judas, into Gilead.

**1 Maccabees 5:21**

And Simon went into Galilee and joined many battles against the nations, and the nations were crushed from before his face,

**1 Maccabees 5:22**

and he pursued them as far as the gate of Ptolemais. And about three thousand men from the nations fell, and he took their spoils.

**1 Maccabees 5:23**

And he took along those in Galilee and in Arbattis, with the wives and children and all that belonged to them, and brought them into Judea with great gladness.

**1 Maccabees 5:24**

And Judas Maccabeus and Jonathan his brother crossed over the Jordan and went a journey of three days in the wilderness.

**1 Maccabees 5:25**

And they met the Nabataeans, and they met them peaceably and recounted to them all the things that had happened to their brothers in Gilead.

**1 Maccabees 5:26**

And they said that many of them were shut up in Bosorra and Bosor, in Alema, Chaspho, Maked, and Carnaim; all these cities were strong and great,

**1 Maccabees 5:27**

and that they were shut up in the remaining cities of Gilead, and for tomorrow they were arranging to camp against the fortresses and seize them and take away all these in one day.

**1 Maccabees 5:28**

And Judas and his force turned back suddenly by way of the wilderness to Bosorra, and he seized the city and killed every male with the mouth of the sword and took all their spoils and burned it with fire.

**1 Maccabees 5:29**

And he departed from there by night and went as far as the fortress.

**1 Maccabees 5:30**

And it came to pass at daybreak, they lifted up their eyes, and behold, a numerous people, of whom there was no number, carrying ladders and engines to seize the fortress, and they were fighting against them.

**1 Maccabees 5:31**

And Judas saw that the battle had begun and that the cry of the city had gone up into heaven with trumpets and a great voice,

**1 Maccabees 5:32**

and he said to the men of the force, Fight today for your brothers.

**1 Maccabees 5:33**

And he went out in three divisions behind them, and they trumpeted with the trumpets and cried out in prayer.

**1 Maccabees 5:34**

And the camp of Timothy recognized that he was Maccabeus, and they fled from before his face, and he struck them with a great blow, and about eight thousand men from them fell on that day.

**1 Maccabees 5:35**

And he turned aside to Maapha and fought against it and seized it beforehand, and killed every male of it and took its spoils and burned it with fire.

**1 Maccabees 5:36**

From there he departed and seized Chasphon, Maked, Bosor, and the remaining cities of Gilead beforehand.

**1 Maccabees 5:37**
