And they cried to Simon to receive right hands, and he gave them; and he cast them out from there and cleansed the citadel from the defilements.

**1 Maccabees 13:51**

And they entered into it on the twenty-third of the second month, in the one hundred seventy-first year, with praise and palm branches and with lyres and with cymbals and with harps and with hymns and with songs, because a great enemy had been crushed from Israel.

**1 Maccabees 13:52**

And he established that they should keep this day year by year with gladness. And he fortified the mountain of the temple beside the citadel further, and he lived there, he and those with him.

**1 Maccabees 13:53**

And Simon saw John his son, that he was a man, and he set him as leader of all the forces; and he lived in Gazara.

### Chapter 14

**1 Maccabees 14:1**

And in the one hundred seventy-second year, King Demetrius gathered his forces and went into Media to draw help to him, so that he might fight Tryphon.

**1 Maccabees 14:2**

And Arsaces, king of Persia and Media, heard that Demetrius had come into his borders, and he sent one of his rulers to seize him alive.

**1 Maccabees 14:3**

And he went and struck the camp of Demetrius and seized him and brought him to Arsaces, and he put him in prison.

**1 Maccabees 14:4**

And the land of Judah was quiet all the days of Simon, and he sought good things for his nation, and his authority and his glory pleased them all the days.

**1 Maccabees 14:5**

And with all his glory he took Joppa for a harbor and made an entrance for the islands of the sea.

**1 Maccabees 14:6**

And he widened the borders for his nation and took hold of the country.

**1 Maccabees 14:7**

And he gathered many captives and became master of Gazara and Bethsura and the citadel. And he removed the uncleannesses from it, and there was no one opposing him.

**1 Maccabees 14:8**

And they were working their land with peace, and the land gave its produce, and the trees of the plains gave their fruit.

**1 Maccabees 14:9**

Elders sat in the squares; all were speaking together about good things, and the young men put on glories and robes of war.

**1 Maccabees 14:10**

He supplied the cities with foods and set them with equipment of fortification, until the name of his glory was named as far as the end of the earth.

**1 Maccabees 14:11**

He made peace upon the land, and Israel rejoiced with great rejoicing.

**1 Maccabees 14:12**

And each one sat under his vine and his fig tree, and there was no one frightening them.

**1 Maccabees 14:13**

And the one fighting them ceased upon the land, and the kings were crushed in those days.

**1 Maccabees 14:14**

And he strengthened all the humble of his people; he sought out the law and removed every lawless and evil one.

**1 Maccabees 14:15**

He glorified the holy things and multiplied the vessels of the holy things.

**1 Maccabees 14:16**

And it was heard in Rome that Jonathan had died, and as far as Sparta, and they were deeply grieved.

**1 Maccabees 14:17**

But when they heard that Simon his brother had become high priest instead of him and was holding the country and the cities in it,

**1 Maccabees 14:18**

they wrote to him on bronze tablets to renew with him the friendship and the alliance that they had established with Judas and Jonathan his brothers.

**1 Maccabees 14:19**

And they were read before the assembly in Jerusalem.

**1 Maccabees 14:20**

And this is the copy of the letters that the Spartans sent: The rulers of the Spartans and the city, to Simon the great priest and to the elders and priests and the remaining people of the Jews, brothers, greeting.

**1 Maccabees 14:21**

The ambassadors sent to our people reported to us concerning your glory and honor, and we rejoiced at their arrival.

**1 Maccabees 14:22**

And we recorded the things spoken by them in the counsels of the people thus: Numenius son of Antiochus and Antipater son of Jason, ambassadors of Jews, came to us, renewing the friendship with us.

**1 Maccabees 14:23**

And it pleased the people to receive the men gloriously and to set the copy of their words in the public books appointed for the people, so that the people of the Spartans may have a memorial. And we wrote a copy of these things to Simon the high priest. [24] And after these things Simon sent Numenius to Rome, holding a great golden shield of the weight of one thousand minas, to establish the alliance with them.

**1 Maccabees 14:25**

But when the people heard these words, they said, What thanks shall we repay to Simon and his sons?

**1 Maccabees 14:26**

For he and his brothers and the house of his father strengthened Israel, and they fought the enemies of Israel away from them and established freedom for him. And they wrote on bronze tablets and set them on pillars on Mount Zion.

**1 Maccabees 14:27**

And this is the copy of the writing: On the eighteenth of Elul, in the one hundred seventy-second year, and this is the third year under Simon the high priest,

**1 Maccabees 14:28**

in Asaramel, at a great gathering of priests and people and rulers of the nation and the elders of the country, it was made known to us:

**1 Maccabees 14:29**

Since wars often came to be in the country, Simon son of Mattathias, son of the sons of Joarib, and his brothers gave themselves to danger and stood against the opponents of their nation, so that their holy things and the law might stand, and with great glory they glorified their nation.

**1 Maccabees 14:30**

And Jonathan gathered their nation and became high priest to them and was added to his people,

**1 Maccabees 14:31**

and their enemies wished to set foot in their country to rub out their country and to stretch out hands against their holy things.

**1 Maccabees 14:32**

Then Simon rose up and fought concerning his nation and spent much money from his own things and supplied weapons to the men of the force of his nation and gave them wages.

**1 Maccabees 14:33**

And he fortified the cities of Judea and Bethsura on the borders of Judea, where the weapons of the enemies had been formerly, and he set there a guard of Jewish men.

**1 Maccabees 14:34**

And he fortified Joppa by the sea and Gazara on the borders of Azotus, in which the enemies had lived there formerly; and he settled Jews there, and whatever was fit for their restoration, he put in them.

**1 Maccabees 14:35**

And the people saw the act of Simon and the glory that he had resolved to do for his nation, and they made him their leader and high priest, because he had done all these things and because of the righteousness and the faith that he kept for his nation, and he sought by every way to exalt his people.

**1 Maccabees 14:36**

And in his days it prospered in his hands to remove the nations from their country, and those in the city of David in Jerusalem who had made a citadel for themselves, from which they went out and defiled around the holy things and made a great blow against purity.

**1 Maccabees 14:37**

And he settled Jewish men in it and fortified it for the safety of the country and the city, and he raised the walls of Jerusalem.

**1 Maccabees 14:38**

And King Demetrius established the high priesthood for him according to these things

**1 Maccabees 14:39**

and made him one of his friends and glorified him with great glory.

**1 Maccabees 14:40**

For he had heard that the Jews had been called friends and allies and brothers by the Romans, and that they had met the ambassadors of Simon gloriously,

**1 Maccabees 14:41**

and that the Jews and the priests were pleased for Simon to be leader and high priest forever, until a faithful prophet should arise,

**1 Maccabees 14:42**

and for him to be general over them and to care for the holy things, to appoint them over their works and over the country and over the weapons and over the fortresses,

**1 Maccabees 14:43**

and for him to care for the holy things, and that he should be heard by all, and that all documents in the country should be written in his name, and that he should be clothed in purple and wear gold.

**1 Maccabees 14:44**

And it shall not be permitted to any of the people and the priests to annul any of these things or to speak against the things to be spoken by him or to gather an assembly in the country without him or to be clothed in purple or to fasten a golden clasp.

**1 Maccabees 14:45**

And whoever acts contrary to these things or annuls any of these things shall be liable. [46] And all the people were pleased to set Simon and to do according to these words.

**1 Maccabees 14:47**

And Simon accepted and was pleased to serve as high priest and to be general and ethnarch of the Jews and priests and to be protector of all.

**1 Maccabees 14:48**

And they said to set this writing on bronze tablets and to set them in the precinct of the holy things in a notable place,

**1 Maccabees 14:49**

and to set copies of them in the treasury, so that Simon and his sons might have them.

### Chapter 15

**1 Maccabees 15:1**

And Antiochus son of King Demetrius sent letters from the islands of the sea to Simon, priest and ethnarch of the Jews, and to the whole nation.

**1 Maccabees 15:2**

And they contained in this manner: King Antiochus to Simon, great priest and ethnarch, and to the nation of Jews, greeting.

**1 Maccabees 15:3**

Since pestilent men have taken hold of the kingdom of our fathers, and I wish to claim the kingdom, so that I may restore it as it was before, I have recruited a multitude of forces and prepared ships of war.

**1 Maccabees 15:4**

And I wish to go out against the country, so that I may pursue those who have corrupted our country and those who have made many cities desolate in the kingdom.

**1 Maccabees 15:5**

Now therefore I establish for you all the releases that the kings before me released to you and whatever other gifts they released to you.

**1 Maccabees 15:6**

And I have permitted you to make your own coinage as currency for your country,

**1 Maccabees 15:7**

and for Jerusalem and the holy things to be free. And all the weapons that you have prepared, and the fortresses that you have built, which you hold, let them remain yours.

**1 Maccabees 15:8**

And every royal debt and the royal things to come, from now and for all time, let them be released to you.

**1 Maccabees 15:9**

And whenever we take hold of our kingdom, we will glorify you and your nation and the temple with great glory, so that your glory will become manifest in all the earth. [10] And in the one hundred seventy-fourth year Antiochus went out into the land of his fathers, and all the forces came together to him, so that those left with Tryphon were few.

**1 Maccabees 15:11**

And King Antiochus pursued him, and he came fleeing to Dora by the sea,

**1 Maccabees 15:12**

for he saw that evils had been gathered together against him, and the forces had left him.

**1 Maccabees 15:13**

And Antiochus camped against Dora, and with him were one hundred twenty thousand warrior men and eight thousand horse.

**1 Maccabees 15:14**

And he encircled the city, and the ships joined from the sea, and he pressed the city from the land and the sea, and he did not permit anyone to go out or go in.

**1 Maccabees 15:15**

And Numenius and those with him came from Rome, having letters for the kings and the countries, in which these things had been written:

**1 Maccabees 15:16**

Lucius, consul of Romans, to King Ptolemy, greeting.

**1 Maccabees 15:17**

The ambassadors of the Jews came to us, our friends and allies, renewing the friendship and alliance from the beginning, having been sent from Simon the high priest and the people of the Jews.

**1 Maccabees 15:18**

And they brought a golden shield of one thousand minas.

**1 Maccabees 15:19**

Therefore it pleased us to write to the kings and the countries, so that they should not seek harms against them and should not make war against them and their cities and their country, and that they should not be allies with those who make war against them.

**1 Maccabees 15:20**

And it seemed good to us to receive the shield from them.

**1 Maccabees 15:21**

Therefore if any pestilent men have escaped from their country to you, give them over to Simon the high priest, so that he may take vengeance on them according to their law. [22] And he wrote the same things to King Demetrius and to Attalus, Ariarathes, and Arsaces,

**1 Maccabees 15:23**

and to all the countries, and to Samsame and the Spartans and to Delos and Myndus and Sicyon, and to Caria and to Samos and to Pamphylia and to Lycia and to Halicarnassus and to Rhodes and to Phaselis and to Cos and to Side and to Aradus and to Gortyna and Cnidus and Cyprus and Cyrene.

**1 Maccabees 15:24**

And they wrote a copy of these things to Simon the high priest.

**1 Maccabees 15:25**

But King Antiochus camped against Dora a second time, continually bringing his hands against it and making engines, and he shut Tryphon in so that he could not go in or go out.

**1 Maccabees 15:26**

And Simon sent him two thousand chosen men to be allied with him, and silver and gold and sufficient vessels.

**1 Maccabees 15:27**

And he did not wish to receive them, but annulled everything that he had formerly agreed with him, and became estranged from him.

**1 Maccabees 15:28**

And he sent Athenobius, one of his friends, to him to speak with him, saying, You hold Joppa and Gazara and the citadel in Jerusalem, cities of my kingdom.

**1 Maccabees 15:29**

You made their borders desolate and made a great blow upon the land and became master of many places in my kingdom.

**1 Maccabees 15:30**

Now therefore hand over the cities that you seized and the taxes of the places over which you became master outside the borders of Judea.

**1 Maccabees 15:31**

But if not, give five hundred talents of silver in exchange for them, and for the destruction that you have destroyed and for the taxes of the cities, another five hundred talents. But if not, when we arrive, we will make war against you.

**1 Maccabees 15:32**

And Athenobius, friend of the king, came into Jerusalem and saw the glory of Simon, and a sideboard with gold and silver vessels and a sufficient attendance, and he was astonished, and he reported the words of the king to him.

**1 Maccabees 15:33**

And Simon answered and said to him, We have neither taken foreign land nor taken hold of foreign things, but the inheritance of our fathers, which was held unjustly by our enemies at a certain time.

**1 Maccabees 15:34**

But we, having opportunity, are holding fast to the inheritance of our fathers.

**1 Maccabees 15:35**

And concerning Joppa and Gazara, which you ask for, these were making a great blow among the people throughout our country; for these we will give one hundred talents. And Athenobius did not answer him a word,

**1 Maccabees 15:36**

but returned with anger to the king and reported to him these words and the glory of Simon and all that he had seen, and the king was angry with great anger.

**1 Maccabees 15:37**

But Tryphon embarked in a ship and fled to Orthosia.

**1 Maccabees 15:38**

And the king appointed Cendebeus as general of the coastland and gave him infantry and cavalry forces.

**1 Maccabees 15:39**

And he commanded him to camp before Judea, and he commanded him to build Kedron and fortify the gates, and that he should fight the people. But the king pursued Tryphon.

**1 Maccabees 15:40**

And Cendebeus came to Jamnia and began to provoke the people and to set foot in Judea and to take the people captive and to murder,

**1 Maccabees 15:41**

and he built Kedron and stationed there horsemen and forces, so that going out they might overrun the roads of Judea, just as the king arranged for him.

### Chapter 16

**1 Maccabees 16:1**

And John went up from Gazara and reported to Simon his father what Cendebeus was completing.

**1 Maccabees 16:2**

And Simon called his two older sons, Judas and John, and said to them, I and my brothers and the house of my father have made war against the enemies of Israel from youth until this day, and it has prospered in our hands to rescue Israel many times.

**1 Maccabees 16:3**

But now I have grown old, and you, by mercy, are sufficient in years. Be in place of me and my brother, and go out and fight over our nation, and may the help from Heaven be with you.

**1 Maccabees 16:4**

And he chose from the country twenty thousand warrior men and horsemen, and they went against Cendebeus and slept in Modein.

**1 Maccabees 16:5**

And rising early in the morning, they went into the plain, and behold, a great force was there to meet them, foot soldiers and horsemen, and a torrent was between them.

**1 Maccabees 16:6**

And he camped opposite them, he and his people. And he saw the people being afraid to cross the torrent, and he crossed first; and the men saw him and crossed behind him.

**1 Maccabees 16:7**

And he divided the people and the horsemen in the middle of the foot soldiers, but the horse of the opponents was very great.

**1 Maccabees 16:8**

And they sounded with the holy trumpets, and Cendebeus and his camp were turned to flight, and many wounded ones fell from among them; but those left fled into the fortress.

**1 Maccabees 16:9**

Then Judas the brother of John was wounded, but John pursued them until he came to Kedron, which he had built.

**1 Maccabees 16:10**

And they fled as far as the towers in the fields of Azotus, and he burned it with fire, and about two thousand men fell from among them; and he returned to the land of Judah with peace.

**1 Maccabees 16:11**

And Ptolemy son of Abubus had been appointed general for the plain of Jericho, and he had much silver and gold,

**1 Maccabees 16:12**

for he was the son-in-law of the high priest.

**1 Maccabees 16:13**

And his heart was lifted up, and he wished to take hold of the country, and he plotted with deceit against Simon and his sons to take them away.

**1 Maccabees 16:14**

But Simon was visiting the cities in the country and caring for their oversight; and he went down to Jericho, he and Mattathias and Judas his sons, in the one hundred seventy-seventh year, in the eleventh month, which is the month Shebat.

**1 Maccabees 16:15**

And the son of Abubus received them with deceit into the little fortress called Dok, which he had built, and he made a great drinking feast for them and hid men there.

**1 Maccabees 16:16**

And when Simon and his sons were drunk, Ptolemy and those with him rose up and took their weapons and rushed in upon Simon into the banquet and killed him and his two sons and some of his young servants.

**1 Maccabees 16:17**

And he committed great treachery and repaid evils for good things.

**1 Maccabees 16:18**

And Ptolemy wrote these things and sent to the king, so that he might send him forces for help and hand over to him their country and the cities.

**1 Maccabees 16:19**

And he sent others to Gazara to take John away, and to the captains of thousands he sent letters to come to him, so that he might give them silver and gold and gifts.

**1 Maccabees 16:20**

And he sent others to seize Jerusalem and the mountain of the temple.

**1 Maccabees 16:21**

And someone ran ahead and reported to John in Gazara that his father and his brothers had perished and that he has sent also to kill you.

**1 Maccabees 16:22**

And when he heard, he was greatly astonished and seized the men who had come to destroy him and killed them, for he recognized that they were seeking to destroy him.

**1 Maccabees 16:23**

And the remaining things of the words of John and of his wars and of his manly deeds that he did, and of the building of the walls that he built and of his acts,

**1 Maccabees 16:24**

behold, these things have been written in the book of days of his high priesthood, from when he became high priest after his father.

## 2 Maccabees

### Chapter 1

**2 Maccabees 1:1**

To the brothers, the Jews throughout Egypt, greetings. The brothers, the Jews in Jerusalem and those in the country of Judea: good peace.

**2 Maccabees 1:2**

And may God do good to you, and remember his covenant with Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, his faithful servants.

**2 Maccabees 1:3**

And may he give you all a heart to revere him and to do his wills with a great heart and a willing soul;

**2 Maccabees 1:4**

and may he open your heart in his law and in the commandments, and make peace,

**2 Maccabees 1:5**

and may he listen to your petitions, and be reconciled to you, and not abandon you in an evil time.

**2 Maccabees 1:6**

And now here we are praying for you.

**2 Maccabees 1:7**

When Demetrius was reigning, in the 169th year, we Jews had written to you in the affliction and in the crisis that came upon us in these years, from when Jason and those with him revolted from the holy land and the kingdom;

**2 Maccabees 1:8**

and they set the gate on fire and poured out innocent blood. And we entreated the Lord, and were heard, and offered sacrifice and fine flour, and lit the lamps, and set out the loaves.

**2 Maccabees 1:9**

And now, that you may keep the days of the feast of tabernacles of the month Chaseleu.

**2 Maccabees 1:10**

In the 188th year, those in Jerusalem and those in Judea, and the senate and Judas, to Aristobulus, teacher of King Ptolemy, and being from the race of the anointed priests, and to the Jews in Egypt: greetings and health.

**2 Maccabees 1:11**

Having been saved by God out of great dangers, we give great thanks to him, as men drawn up in battle against a king.

**2 Maccabees 1:12**

For he himself cast out those who drew themselves up in battle in the holy city.

**2 Maccabees 1:13**

For when the leader came into Persia, and the force around him, which seemed to be irresistible, they were cut down in the temple of Nanaia, the priests around Nanaia using deceit.

**2 Maccabees 1:14**

For Antiochus came to the place as though he would live with her, both he and the friends with him, for the sake of receiving the money as a dowry;

**2 Maccabees 1:15**

and after the priests of Nanaia set these things out, and he came near with a few into the enclosure of the precinct, they shut the temple when Antiochus entered,

**2 Maccabees 1:16**

and opening the hidden door of the ceiling, they threw stones and thunder-struck the leader, and making him into pieces and cutting off the heads, they threw them to those outside.

**2 Maccabees 1:17**

In all things blessed is our God, who handed over the impious.

**2 Maccabees 1:18**

Therefore, since we are about to keep the cleansing of the temple on the twenty-fifth of Chaseleu, we thought it necessary to make this plain to you, that you also may keep the feast of tabernacles and of the fire, when Nehemiah, having built both the temple and the altar, offered sacrifice.

**2 Maccabees 1:19**

For when our fathers were being led into Persia, the pious priests of that time secretly took some of the fire from the altar and hid it in the hollow of a well that had a dry chamber, in which they secured it, so that the place was unknown to all.

**2 Maccabees 1:20**

But when enough years had passed, when it seemed good to God, Nehemiah, sent by the king of Persia, sent the descendants of the priests who had hidden it to the fire;

**2 Maccabees 1:21**

but when they reported to us that they had not found fire but thick water, he commanded them to draw it out and bring it. And when the things for the sacrifices were brought up, Nehemiah commanded the priests to sprinkle the water on both the wood and the things lying upon it.

**2 Maccabees 1:22**

And when this had happened, and time had passed, when the sun shone out, being previously under cloud, a great fire was kindled, so that all marveled.

**2 Maccabees 1:23**

And the priests made a prayer while the sacrifice was being consumed, both the priests and all, Jonathan beginning and the rest responding, as Nehemiah did.

**2 Maccabees 1:24**

And the prayer had this manner: Lord, Lord God, creator of all, fearful and mighty and righteous and merciful, the only king and good one,

**2 Maccabees 1:25**

the only giver, the only righteous and almighty and eternal one, who saves Israel from every evil, who made the fathers chosen and sanctified them,

**2 Maccabees 1:26**

receive the sacrifice on behalf of all your people Israel, and guard your portion and sanctify it.

**2 Maccabees 1:27**

Gather together our dispersion, free those enslaved among the nations, look upon those who are despised and abhorred, and let the nations know that you are our God.

**2 Maccabees 1:28**

Torment those who oppress and insolently abuse in arrogance;

**2 Maccabees 1:29**

plant your people in your holy place, just as Moses said.

**2 Maccabees 1:30**

And the priests sang the hymns.

**2 Maccabees 1:31**

And when the things of the sacrifice were consumed, and the remaining water, Nehemiah commanded it to be poured over larger stones.

**2 Maccabees 1:32**

And when this was done, a flame was kindled; but when the light from the altar shone back, it was consumed.

**2 Maccabees 1:33**

And when the matter became evident, and it was reported to the king of the Persians that, in the place where the priests who had been carried away hid the fire, the water appeared, from which those around Nehemiah also purified the things of the sacrifice,

**2 Maccabees 1:34**

the king, having enclosed it, made a sanctuary, having tested the matter.

**2 Maccabees 1:35**

And the king received many various things and distributed them to those to whom he showed favor.

**2 Maccabees 1:36**

And those around Nehemiah called this Nephthar, which is interpreted Purification; but it is called Nephthaei among the many.

### Chapter 2

**2 Maccabees 2:1**

And it is found in the records that Jeremiah the prophet commanded those being carried away to take some of the fire, as has been indicated,

**2 Maccabees 2:2**

and how the prophet, giving them the law, commanded those being carried away not to forget the commandments of the Lord, and not to be led astray in their minds when they saw gold and silver images and the adornment around them.

**2 Maccabees 2:3**

And saying other such things, he exhorted them that the law should not depart from their heart.

**2 Maccabees 2:4**

And it was in the writing how the prophet, after a divine message came to him, commanded the tent and the ark to follow with him, and how he went out to the mountain where Moses, having ascended, beheld the inheritance of God.

**2 Maccabees 2:5**

And Jeremiah came and found a cave-like house, and brought in there the tent and the ark and the altar of incense, and blocked the door.

**2 Maccabees 2:6**

And some of those following came near so as to mark the way, and they were not able to find it.

**2 Maccabees 2:7**

But when Jeremiah knew it, blaming them he said that the place would also be unknown until God gathers together a gathering of the people and becomes gracious.

**2 Maccabees 2:8**

And then the Lord will show these things, and the glory of the Lord and the cloud will be seen, just as it was also shown with Moses, and just as Solomon also asked that the place be greatly sanctified.

**2 Maccabees 2:9**

And it was also explained how, having wisdom, he offered a sacrifice of dedication and of the completion of the temple.

**2 Maccabees 2:10**

Just as Moses also prayed to the Lord, and fire came down from heaven and consumed the things of the sacrifice, so also Solomon prayed, and the fire came down and consumed the whole burnt offerings.

**2 Maccabees 2:11**

And Moses said that because the thing concerning the sin offering had not been eaten, it was consumed.

**2 Maccabees 2:12**

Likewise Solomon also kept the eight days.

**2 Maccabees 2:13**

And the same things were also explained in the records and in the memoirs concerning Nehemiah, and how, establishing a library, he gathered together the things concerning the kings and prophets, and the things of David, and letters of kings concerning offerings.

**2 Maccabees 2:14**

Likewise Judas also gathered together all the things that had fallen apart because of the war that happened to us, and they are with us.

**2 Maccabees 2:15**

Therefore, if you have need of these things, send those who will carry them away to you.

**2 Maccabees 2:16**

Therefore, since we are about to keep the cleansing, we wrote to you; therefore you will do well keeping the days.

**2 Maccabees 2:17**

And God, who saved all his people and restored the inheritance to all, and the kingdom and the priesthood and the sanctification,

**2 Maccabees 2:18**

just as he promised through the law, for we hope in God that he will quickly have mercy on us and gather us together from under heaven into the holy place; for he delivered us out of great evils and cleansed the place.

**2 Maccabees 2:19**

Now the things concerning Judas Maccabeus and his brothers, and the cleansing of the great temple and the dedication of the altar,

**2 Maccabees 2:20**

and also the wars against Antiochus Epiphanes and his son Eupator,

**2 Maccabees 2:21**

and the manifestations that came from heaven to those who fought bravely with honor on behalf of Judaism, so that, though they were few, they plundered the whole country and pursued the barbarian multitudes,

**2 Maccabees 2:22**

and recovered the temple famous throughout the whole inhabited world, and freed the city, and restored the laws that were about to be abolished, the Lord having become gracious to them with all gentleness,

**2 Maccabees 2:23**

the things declared by Jason of Cyrene through five books, we will attempt to abridge in one composition.

**2 Maccabees 2:24**

For seeing the flood of the numbers, and the existing difficulty for those wishing to enter into the narratives of the history because of the abundance of material,

**2 Maccabees 2:25**

we took care to provide delight for those wishing to read, ease for those inclined to take it up by memory, and benefit for all those who encounter it.

**2 Maccabees 2:26**

And for us, those who have taken on the hardship of the abridgment, it is not easy, but the matter is one of sweat and sleeplessness;

**2 Maccabees 2:27**

just as for the one preparing a banquet and seeking the advantage of others it is not easy; nevertheless, because of the gratitude of the many, we will gladly endure the hardship,

**2 Maccabees 2:28**

leaving the exact treatment concerning each thing to the author, but laboring to proceed along the outlines of the abridgment.

**2 Maccabees 2:29**

For just as the architect of a new house must care for the whole foundation, but the one undertaking encaustic work and painting must examine the things suitable for decoration, so I think it is also with us.

**2 Maccabees 2:30**

For entering in and making an account concerning everything, and being busy about the details, belongs to the originator of the history;

**2 Maccabees 2:31**

but to pursue concise expression and to avoid the elaborative treatment of the work must be granted to the one making the reworking.

**2 Maccabees 2:32**

Therefore from here let us begin the narrative, having added this much to what was said before; for it is foolish to be excessive before the history but to abridge the history itself.

### Chapter 3

**2 Maccabees 3:1**

Therefore, while the holy city was inhabited with all peace, and the laws were still kept most beautifully because of the piety and hatred of evil of Onias the high priest,

**2 Maccabees 3:2**

it happened that even the kings themselves honored the place, and glorified the temple with the finest gifts,

**2 Maccabees 3:3**

so that even Seleucus, king of Asia, supplied from his own revenues all the expenses falling to the services of the sacrifices.

**2 Maccabees 3:4**

But a certain Simon from the tribe of Benjamin, having been appointed protector of the temple, came into conflict with the high priest concerning the lawlessness in the city;

**2 Maccabees 3:5**

and being unable to overcome Onias, he came to Apollonius, son of Thrasaeus, who at that time was governor of Coele-Syria and Phoenicia.

**2 Maccabees 3:6**

And he reported that the treasury in Jerusalem was full of untold monies, so that the multitude of its assets was innumerable, and that they did not belong to the account of the sacrifices, but that it was possible for all these things to fall under the authority of the king.

**2 Maccabees 3:7**

And Apollonius, meeting with the king, disclosed concerning the monies made known to him; and he, having selected Heliodorus, the one over the affairs, sent him, giving commands to carry out the removal of the aforementioned monies.

**2 Maccabees 3:8**

And immediately Heliodorus made the journey, in appearance as though he would inspect the cities throughout Coele-Syria and Phoenicia, but in fact to fulfill the king's purpose.

**2 Maccabees 3:9**

And having come to Jerusalem, and having been received kindly by the high priest of the city, he set forth concerning the disclosure that had happened, and made clear for what reason he was present; and he inquired whether these things truly happened to be so.

**2 Maccabees 3:10**

And when the high priest pointed out that they were deposits of widows and orphans,

**2 Maccabees 3:11**

and that some also belonged to Hyrcanus, son of Tobias, a man set in great eminence, not as the impious Simon was slandering, and that the whole amount was four hundred talents of silver and two hundred of gold,

**2 Maccabees 3:12**

and that it was altogether impossible for those who had trusted in the holiness of the place and in the dignity and inviolability of the temple, honored throughout the whole world, to be wronged.

**2 Maccabees 3:13**

But Heliodorus, because of the royal commands he had, kept saying that these things must certainly be taken up into the royal treasury.

**2 Maccabees 3:14**

And having appointed a day, he entered in to arrange the inspection concerning these things; and there was no small agony throughout the whole city.

**2 Maccabees 3:15**

And the priests, throwing themselves before the altar in the priestly robes, called upon the one in heaven who had legislated concerning a deposit, to preserve safely these things for those who had deposited them.

**2 Maccabees 3:16**

And it was, for one looking at the appearance of the high priest, to have the mind wounded; for his face and the change of color made visible the agony according to soul.

**2 Maccabees 3:17**

For around the man a certain fear and shuddering of body had been poured, through which the pain present according to heart became plain to those watching.

**2 Maccabees 3:18**

And those from the houses were leaping out in crowds to a public supplication, because the place was about to come into contempt.

**2 Maccabees 3:19**

And the women, girded with sackcloth under their breasts, filled the streets; and of the shut-in virgins, some ran together to the gates, others to the walls, and some leaned out through the windows.

**2 Maccabees 3:20**

And all, stretching out their hands toward heaven, were making the supplication.

**2 Maccabees 3:21**

And it was pitiable to see the all-mixed falling-forward of the multitude, and the anxious expectation of the high priest as he greatly agonized.

**2 Maccabees 3:22**

Therefore they called upon the Almighty God to preserve the things entrusted to those who had entrusted them, safe with all security.

**2 Maccabees 3:23**

But Heliodorus was completing the thing determined.

**2 Maccabees 3:24**

And there on the spot, when he himself with the bodyguards was already present at the treasury, the Lord of the fathers and ruler of all authority made a great manifestation, so that all who dared to come together, being struck with amazement at the power of God, turned into faintness and fearfulness.

**2 Maccabees 3:25**

For a certain horse appeared to them, having a fearsome rider and adorned with a most beautiful harness, and being carried along violently it shook its front hoofs at Heliodorus; and the one seated on it appeared to have golden full armor.

**2 Maccabees 3:26**

And two other young men appeared before him, excellent in strength, most beautiful in glory, and distinguished in clothing; and they, standing beside him on either side, kept whipping him without ceasing, throwing many blows upon him.

**2 Maccabees 3:27**

And suddenly, having fallen toward the ground and having been poured around with much darkness, they seized him and put him into a litter,

**2 Maccabees 3:28**

the one who had just entered the aforementioned treasury with much escort and every bodyguard, they carried away as one established helpless to himself, plainly recognizing the sovereignty of God.

**2 Maccabees 3:29**

And he, because of the divine working, lay speechless and deprived of every hope and salvation.

**2 Maccabees 3:30**

But they blessed the Lord, who made his own place marvelous; and the temple, a little before full of fear and disturbance, when the Almighty Lord appeared, had been filled with joy and gladness.

**2 Maccabees 3:31**

But quickly some of Heliodorus's close companions were asking Onias to call upon the Most High, to grant life to the one lying wholly at his last breath.

**2 Maccabees 3:32**

And the high priest, becoming suspicious lest the king should get an idea that some evil deed had been completed against Heliodorus by the Jews, brought forward a sacrifice for the man's salvation.

**2 Maccabees 3:33**

And while the high priest was making the propitiation, the same young men again appeared to Heliodorus, clothed in the same garments, and standing said, "Have great thanks toward Onias the high priest, for because of him the Lord has graciously given life to you.

**2 Maccabees 3:34**

But you, having been whipped by him, report to all the majestic power of God." And saying these things, they became unseen.

**2 Maccabees 3:35**

And Heliodorus, having offered sacrifice to the Lord, and having vowed very great vows to the one who preserved life, and having greeted Onias, broke camp to the king.

**2 Maccabees 3:36**

And he bore witness to all of the works of the greatest God, which he had seen before his eyes.

**2 Maccabees 3:37**

And when the king asked Heliodorus what kind of person would be suitable to be sent yet once more to Jerusalem, he said,

**2 Maccabees 3:38**

"If you have any enemy or plotter against affairs, send him there, and you will receive him back whipped, if indeed he should be saved through, because there truly is some power of God about the place.

**2 Maccabees 3:39**

For he himself, the one having the heavenly dwelling, is overseer and helper of that place, and those who come for harm he strikes and destroys."

**2 Maccabees 3:40**

And the things concerning Heliodorus and the keeping of the treasury proceeded in this way.

### Chapter 4

**2 Maccabees 4:1**

But the aforementioned Simon, who had become informer against the monies and the fatherland, was speaking evil of Onias, as if he himself had shaken up Heliodorus and had become the maker of the evils.

**2 Maccabees 4:2**

And the benefactor of the city, and the guardian of his fellow-nationals, and the zealot of the laws, he dared to call a plotter against the affairs.

**2 Maccabees 4:3**

And when the enmity advanced to such a point that murders were even being completed through one of those approved by Simon,

**2 Maccabees 4:4**

Onias, perceiving the harshness of the rivalry, and that Apollonius, as governor of Coele-Syria and Phoenicia, was raging and increasing the evil of Simon,

**2 Maccabees 4:5**

went to the king, not becoming an accuser of the citizens, but looking to the benefit, both common and particular, for all the multitude.

**2 Maccabees 4:6**

For he saw that without royal forethought it was impossible for the affairs still to obtain peace, and that Simon would not receive a pause from his folly.

**2 Maccabees 4:7**

But when Seleucus changed life, and Antiochus, called Epiphanes, took over the kingdom, Jason, the brother of Onias, illicitly procured the high priesthood,

**2 Maccabees 4:8**

promising the king, through a petition, three hundred and sixty talents of silver, and eighty talents from another revenue,

**2 Maccabees 4:9**

and besides these he also promised to register another hundred and fifty, if it should be permitted through his authority to establish a gymnasium and ephebia for himself, and to enroll those in Jerusalem as Antiochenes.

**2 Maccabees 4:10**

And when the king nodded assent, and he took hold of the office, he immediately began transferring his fellow-tribesmen to the Greek character.

**2 Maccabees 4:11**

And the humane royal provisions laid down for the Jews through John, father of Eupolemus, who made the embassy to the Romans for friendship and alliance, he pushed aside; and abolishing the lawful civic orders, he was renewing lawless customs.

**2 Maccabees 4:12**

For gladly he founded a gymnasium right under the acropolis, and, subjecting the strongest of the youths, he led them under a petasus.

**2 Maccabees 4:13**

And there was such a peak of Hellenism and advance of foreignness through the surpassing impurity of Jason, the impious and not high priest,

**2 Maccabees 4:14**

so that the priests were no longer eager about the services of the altar, but despising the shrine and neglecting the sacrifices, they hurried to take part in the unlawful provision in the palaestra after the summons of the discus.

**2 Maccabees 4:15**

And setting the ancestral honors at nothing, they considered the Greek glories most beautiful.

**2 Maccabees 4:16**

For this reason a harsh circumstance took hold of them, and those whose ways of life they zealously followed, and to whom in every way they wished to become like, these they had as enemies and avengers.

**2 Maccabees 4:17**

For to act impiously against the divine laws is no easy thing, but the following time will make these things clear.

**2 Maccabees 4:18**

And when a five-year contest was being held in Tyre, and the king was present,

**2 Maccabees 4:19**

the polluted Jason sent festival-observers from Jerusalem, being Antiochenes, carrying three hundred drachmas of silver to the sacrifice of Heracles; and those carrying them considered it worthy not to use them for sacrifice because it was not fitting, but to lay them out for another expense.

**2 Maccabees 4:20**

Therefore these funds were sent, because of the sender, for the sacrifice of Heracles, but because of those carrying them, for the furnishing of triremes.

**2 Maccabees 4:21**

And when Apollonius, son of Menestheus, was sent into Egypt because of the enthronement ceremonies of Ptolemy Philometor the king, Antiochus, perceiving that he had become alien to his own affairs, was considering his own security; from there, having come to Joppa, he arrived at Jerusalem.

**2 Maccabees 4:22**

And having been received magnificently by Jason and the city, he entered with torch-bearing and shouts; then in this way he encamped into Phoenicia.

**2 Maccabees 4:23**

And after a three-year time Jason sent Menelaus, brother of the aforementioned Simon, carrying the monies to the king and completing memoranda concerning necessary affairs.

**2 Maccabees 4:24**

But he, having been introduced to the king and having glorified him with the appearance of authority, brought the high priesthood to himself, outbidding Jason by three hundred talents of silver.

**2 Maccabees 4:25**

And having received the royal commands he came, bearing nothing worthy of the high priesthood, but having the passions of a cruel tyrant and the rages of a barbarous beast.

**2 Maccabees 4:26**

And Jason, who had illicitly supplanted his own brother, having been illicitly supplanted by another, was driven as a fugitive into the Ammanite country.

**2 Maccabees 4:27**

But Menelaus held the office, but of the monies promised to the king he arranged nothing in good order,

**2 Maccabees 4:28**

while Sostratus, governor of the acropolis, was making the demand; for to him belonged the collection of the taxes; for which reason the two were summoned by the king.

**2 Maccabees 4:29**

And Menelaus left Lysimachus his own brother as successor of the high priesthood, and Sostratus left Crates, the one over the Cypriots.

**2 Maccabees 4:30**

And as such things were standing, it happened that the Tarseans and Mallotes were revolting, because they had been given as a gift to Antiochis, the king's concubine.

**2 Maccabees 4:31**

Therefore the king came quickly to settle the affairs, leaving Andronicus, one of those set in dignity, as the one succeeding him.

**2 Maccabees 4:32**

And Menelaus, thinking he had taken a favorable time, having embezzled certain gold articles from the temple, gave them to Andronicus, and happened to have sold others into Tyre and the surrounding cities.

**2 Maccabees 4:33**

And Onias, clearly recognizing these things, was refuting him, having withdrawn into an asylum place at Daphne, which lies near Antioch.

**2 Maccabees 4:34**

From there Menelaus, taking Andronicus privately, was urging him to lay hands on Onias; and he, coming to Onias and being persuaded toward deceit, and giving right hands with oaths, though he was lying under suspicion, persuaded him to come out from the asylum, and immediately put him away, not showing reverence for the just.

**2 Maccabees 4:35**

For this reason not only Jews, but also many of the other nations, were indignant and displeased over the unjust murder of the man.

**2 Maccabees 4:36**

And when the king returned from the places around Cilicia, the Jews throughout the city, with the Greeks also joining in hatred of evil, petitioned concerning Onias having been killed contrary to reason.

**2 Maccabees 4:37**

Therefore Antiochus, being grieved according to soul, and turning to mercy, and weeping because of the self-control and much good order of the one who had changed life,

**2 Maccabees 4:38**

and being inflamed in his passions, immediately took off Andronicus's purple and tore off his tunics, and after leading him around through the whole city, to that very place where he had acted impiously against Onias, there he stripped the murderer of his life, the Lord repaying him the worthy punishment.

**2 Maccabees 4:39**

And when many sacrileges had happened throughout the city by Lysimachus with the consent of Menelaus, and the report had spread abroad, the multitude gathered against Lysimachus, many gold articles having already been carried off.

**2 Maccabees 4:40**

And when the crowds were rising up and being filled through with angers, Lysimachus, having armed about three thousand, began unjust violence, with a certain Tyrannus leading, one advanced in age and no less in folly.

**2 Maccabees 4:41**

And recognizing also the attack of Lysimachus, some seizing stones, others thick pieces of wood, and some grasping from the nearby ashes, hurled them in confusion at those around Lysimachus.

**2 Maccabees 4:42**

For this reason they made many of them wounded, and some they even threw down, and all they drove together into flight, and the temple-robber himself they put to death beside the treasury.

**2 Maccabees 4:43**

And concerning these things a judgment arose against Menelaus.

**2 Maccabees 4:44**

And when the king arrived at Tyre, the three men sent by the senate made the plea before him.

**2 Maccabees 4:45**

But Menelaus, already being left behind, promised sufficient monies to Ptolemy, son of Dorymenes, to persuade the king.

**2 Maccabees 4:46**

Therefore Ptolemy, taking the king aside into a certain peristyle as though to refresh him, shifted his mind.

**2 Maccabees 4:47**

And Menelaus, the cause of the whole evil, he released from the charges, but upon the miserable men, who, even if they had spoken before Scythians, would have been released uncondemned, upon these he pronounced death.

**2 Maccabees 4:48**

Therefore quickly those who had spoken beforehand for city and peoples and the holy vessels underwent the unjust penalty.

**2 Maccabees 4:49**

For this reason even Tyrians, hating evil, magnificently supplied the things for their burial.

**2 Maccabees 4:50**

But Menelaus, through the greed of those in power, remained in the office, growing in evil, having become a great plotter against the citizens.

### Chapter 5

**2 Maccabees 5:1**

And about this time Antiochus sent the second expedition into Egypt.

**2 Maccabees 5:2**

And it happened throughout the whole city, for almost forty days, that horsemen were seen running through the air, having gold-woven robes and armed with lances in companies,

**2 Maccabees 5:3**

and ordered squadrons of horses, and attacks being made, and charges of both sides, and movements of shields, and multitudes of pikes, and drawings of swords, and shots of missiles, and flashes of golden ornaments, and all sorts of armors.

**2 Maccabees 5:4**

Therefore all deemed that the manifestation had come for good.

**2 Maccabees 5:5**

And when false talk happened, as if Antiochus had changed life, Jason, taking no fewer than a thousand, suddenly completed an attack upon the city; and when those on the wall were driven together, and at last the city was already being seized, Menelaus fled into the acropolis.

**2 Maccabees 5:6**

But Jason was making slaughters of his own citizens unsparingly, not considering that success against kinsmen is the greatest misfortune, but supposing he was throwing down trophies of enemies and not fellow-nationals,

**2 Maccabees 5:7**

he did not gain hold of the office, but receiving shame as the end of his plot, he again went away as a fugitive into the Ammanite country.

**2 Maccabees 5:8**

Therefore he obtained the end of an evil way of life: being shut in by Aretas, the tyrant of the Arabs, fleeing city from city, pursued by all, hated as an apostate from the laws, and abhorred as executioner of fatherland and citizens, he was tossed into Egypt.

**2 Maccabees 5:9**

And the one who had alienated many from the fatherland perished upon foreign soil, having been brought up to the Lacedaemonians as though he would obtain shelter because of kinship.

**2 Maccabees 5:10**

And the one who had thrown out a multitude unburied became unmourned, and did not partake of any funeral, nor of an ancestral tomb.

**2 Maccabees 5:11**

And when the things that had happened fell before the king, he supposed Judea to be rebelling; therefore, breaking camp from Egypt, made beast-like in soul, he took the city by force of spear.

**2 Maccabees 5:12**

And he commanded the soldiers to cut down unsparingly those who fell in their way, and to slaughter those going up into the houses.

**2 Maccabees 5:13**

And there were removals of young and old, destruction of men and women and children, and slaughters of virgins and infants.

**2 Maccabees 5:14**

And eighty thousand were destroyed in all the three days, forty thousand in hand-to-hand ravages, and no fewer than those slain were sold.

**2 Maccabees 5:15**

And not being satisfied with these things, he dared to enter into the holiest temple of all the earth, having Menelaus as guide, who had become traitor both of the laws and of the fatherland.

**2 Maccabees 5:16**

And taking the holy vessels with defiled hands, and dragging together with profane hands the things dedicated by other kings for increase and glory and honor of the place, he was giving them away.

**2 Maccabees 5:17**

And Antiochus was lifted up in mind, not perceiving that because of the sins of those living in the city, the Master had been angered briefly; therefore disregard concerning the place had come to be.

**2 Maccabees 5:18**

But if it had not happened that they were previously involved in many sins, just as Heliodorus, the one sent by King Seleucus for the inspection of the treasury, this one, having been brought forward, would immediately have been whipped and turned back from his boldness.

**2 Maccabees 5:19**

But not because of the place did the Lord choose the nation, but because of the nation, the place.

**2 Maccabees 5:20**

Therefore the place itself, sharing in the hardships that happened to the nation, afterward also shared in benefits from the Lord; and the one left behind in the wrath of the Almighty was again, in the reconciliation of the great Master, restored with all glory.

**2 Maccabees 5:21**

So Antiochus, having carried away 1,800 talents from the temple, quickly departed to Antioch, supposing from arrogance to make the land navigable and the sea passable on foot, because of the lifting up of his heart.

**2 Maccabees 5:22**

And he also left overseers to harm the race: in Jerusalem, Philip, a Phrygian by race but having a manner more barbarous than the one who appointed him;

**2 Maccabees 5:23**

and in Garizim, Andronicus; and besides these, Menelaus, who worse than the others was exalting himself over the citizens, having a hateful disposition toward the Jewish citizens.

**2 Maccabees 5:24**

And he sent the defiling commander Apollonius with an army of twenty-two thousand, commanding him to slaughter all those in age and to sell the women and younger ones.

**2 Maccabees 5:25**

And this one, having come to Jerusalem and pretending to be peaceable, held back until the holy day of the Sabbath; and having taken the Jews at rest, he commanded those under him to be fully armed.

**2 Maccabees 5:26**

And all those who had gone out to the spectacle he pierced through together, and running into the city with weapons, he laid down considerable multitudes.

**2 Maccabees 5:27**

But Judas Maccabeus, becoming about tenth, and withdrawing into the wilderness, lived through in the mountains in the manner of beasts with those with him, and they continued feeding on grassy food, so as not to partake of the pollution.

### Chapter 6

**2 Maccabees 6:1**

But after not much time the king sent out an Athenian elder, to compel the Jews to cross over from the ancestral laws and not conduct citizenship by the laws of God,

**2 Maccabees 6:2**

and also to defile the temple in Jerusalem and to name it additionally as that of Zeus Olympios, and the one in Garizim, as those inhabiting the place happened to desire, as that of Zeus Xenios.

**2 Maccabees 6:3**

And the onset of the evil was harsh and grievous even to the crowds.

**2 Maccabees 6:4**

For the temple had been filled with debauchery and revels by the nations, idling with courtesans and drawing near to women in the holy enclosures, and further bringing inside things not fitting.

**2 Maccabees 6:5**

And the altar had been filled with law-forbidden unlawful things.

**2 Maccabees 6:6**

And it was not possible to keep Sabbath, nor to preserve ancestral feasts, nor simply to confess oneself to be a Jew.

**2 Maccabees 6:7**

And they were led with bitter compulsion, each month on the king's birthday, to a tasting of entrails; and when the feast of Dionysia occurred, the Jews were compelled, having ivy, to march in procession to Dionysus.

**2 Maccabees 6:8**

And a decree went out to the neighboring Greek cities, at Ptolemy's suggestion, to bring the same conduct against the Jews, to lead them and make them taste entrails;

**2 Maccabees 6:9**

and to slaughter those who did not choose to cross over to the Greek things; therefore it was possible to see the present misery.

**2 Maccabees 6:10**

For two women were brought up, having circumcised their children; and hanging the infants from their breasts and leading them publicly around the city, they hurled them down from the wall.

**2 Maccabees 6:11**

And others, having run together into the nearby caves to keep the seventh day secretly, being reported to Philip, were burned together, because they had piously held back from helping themselves according to the glory of the most solemn day.

**2 Maccabees 6:12**

Therefore I exhort those encountering this book not to be compressed because of the calamities, but to reckon the punishments not for destruction, but for discipline of our race.

**2 Maccabees 6:13**

And the fact that those acting impiously are not allowed much time, but immediately fall into penalties, is a sign of great benefaction.

**2 Maccabees 6:14**

For not as with the other nations, with whom the Master waits patiently until they arrive at the fulfillment of sins to punish them, did he judge it to be so also in our case,

**2 Maccabees 6:15**

lest, when our sins had come to an end, he later avenge us.

**2 Maccabees 6:16**

Therefore he never withdraws his mercy from us; but while disciplining with calamity, he does not abandon his own people.

**2 Maccabees 6:17**

Nevertheless let these things have been said by us for reminder; but through a few words we must come to the narrative.

**2 Maccabees 6:18**

A certain Eleazar, one of the leading scribes, a man already advanced in age and happening to be most beautiful in the appearance of his face, having his mouth forced open, was compelled to eat swine flesh.

**2 Maccabees 6:19**

But he, choosing death with good fame rather than life with pollution, went voluntarily to the torture-wheel;

**2 Maccabees 6:20**

and having spit it out, he came forward in the manner in which those ought to come who endure to fend off things it is not lawful to taste because of affection for life.

**2 Maccabees 6:21**

But those appointed over the lawless entrail-feasting, because of acquaintance with the man from old times, taking him aside privately, were urging him to bring meat proper for him to use, prepared by himself, but to pretend as though eating the meats from the sacrifice commanded by the king,

**2 Maccabees 6:22**

so that, doing this, he might be released from death, and because of ancient friendship with them receive humane treatment.

**2 Maccabees 6:23**

But he, having taken up a noble reasoning and one worthy of his age, and the eminence of old age, and the acquired and manifest gray hair, and the most beautiful upbringing from a child, but rather of the holy and God-founded lawgiving, answered accordingly, saying quickly to send him on to Hades.

**2 Maccabees 6:24**

"For it is not worthy of our age to pretend, lest many of the young, supposing Eleazar the ninety-year-old to have crossed over to foreignness,

**2 Maccabees 6:25**

also they, because of my pretense and because of a little and momentary living, be deceived because of me, and I shall acquire defilement and stain of old age.

**2 Maccabees 6:26**

For even if for the present I shall avoid the punishment from men, yet the hands of the Almighty, neither living nor having died, I shall escape.

**2 Maccabees 6:27**

Therefore, courageously now changing life, I will appear worthy of old age,

**2 Maccabees 6:28**

and leaving behind for the young a noble example, to die eagerly and nobly on behalf of the solemn and holy laws." And having said so much, he immediately came to the torture-wheel.

**2 Maccabees 6:29**

And those leading him changed the good will they had toward him a little before into ill will, because the aforementioned words, as they supposed, were madness;

**2 Maccabees 6:30**

and being about to die by the blows, he groaned and said, "To the Lord who has the holy knowledge it is plain that, while able to be released from death, I endure hard pains according to body by being whipped, but according to soul I gladly suffer these things because of his fear."

**2 Maccabees 6:31**

And therefore this one changed life in this manner, leaving behind his own death not only to the young but also to most of the nation as an example of nobility and a memorial of virtue.

### Chapter 7

**2 Maccabees 7:1**

And it happened also that seven brothers, having been arrested with the mother, were compelled by the king to touch unlawful swine flesh, being tortured with whips and thongs.

**2 Maccabees 7:2**

And one of them, becoming spokesman, said thus, "What are you about to ask and learn from us? For we are ready to die rather than transgress the ancestral laws."

**2 Maccabees 7:3**

And the king, becoming enraged, commanded pans and kettles to be heated.

**2 Maccabees 7:4**

And when they were heated, immediately he commanded the one who had become their spokesman to be tongue-cut, and after they had scalped him, to mutilate him, while the remaining brothers and the mother were watching.

**2 Maccabees 7:5**

And when he had become useless in all parts, he commanded him, still breathing, to be brought to the fire and fried in a pan; and when the vapor from the pan was spreading for a considerable time, they, with the mother, exhorted one another to die nobly, saying thus:

**2 Maccabees 7:6**

"The Lord God watches, and in truth is comforted over us, just as Moses made clear through the song bearing witness face to face, saying, And over his servants he will be comforted."

**2 Maccabees 7:7**

And when the first had changed life in this manner, they brought the second for mockery; and after pulling around the skin of his head with the hair, they were asking, "Will you eat before being punished in body limb by limb?"

**2 Maccabees 7:8**

But he, answering in the ancestral voice, said, "No."

**2 Maccabees 7:9**

Therefore this one also received the next torture, as the first. And being at his last breath, he said, "You accursed one, you release us from the present living, but the King of the world will raise us, who die on behalf of his laws, into an eternal reviving of life."

**2 Maccabees 7:10**

And after this one, the third was being mocked; and when asked, he quickly put forward his tongue and courageously stretched out his hands,

**2 Maccabees 7:11**

and nobly said, "From heaven I possess these, and because of his laws I overlook these, and from him I hope to recover these again."

**2 Maccabees 7:12**

So that the king himself and those with him were struck with amazement at the soul of the young man, because he considered the pains as nothing.

**2 Maccabees 7:13**

And after this one also changed life, they were torturing the fourth likewise, abusing him.

**2 Maccabees 7:14**

And as he came to die, he said thus, "It is preferable, when changing life under men, to look for the hopes from God, to be raised again by him; but for you there will not be resurrection to life."

**2 Maccabees 7:15**

And next, bringing the fifth, they abused him.

**2 Maccabees 7:16**

But looking toward him, he said, "Having authority among men, though being corruptible, you do what you wish; but do not think our race has been abandoned by God.

**2 Maccabees 7:17**

But you, endure, and behold his majestic power, how he will torture you and your seed."

**2 Maccabees 7:18**

And after this one they brought the sixth, and when he was about to die, he said, "Do not be led astray vainly, for we suffer these things because of ourselves, having sinned against our own God; therefore things worthy of wonder have happened.

**2 Maccabees 7:19**

But you, do not suppose you will be innocent, having attempted to fight God."

**2 Maccabees 7:20**

But beyond measure the mother was marvelous and worthy of good memory, who, seeing seven sons perishing under one day of time, bore it with good courage because of the hopes upon the Lord.

**2 Maccabees 7:21**

And she exhorted each of them in the ancestral voice, filled with a noble mindset, and stirring up the female reasoning with a male passion, saying to them,

**2 Maccabees 7:22**

"I do not even know how you appeared in my womb, nor did I grant you the spirit and the life, and I did not arrange the formation of each.

**2 Maccabees 7:23**

Therefore the Creator of the world, who formed the generation of man and discovered the genesis of all things, will also give back to you the spirit and the life again with mercy, since now you overlook yourselves because of his laws."

**2 Maccabees 7:24**

But Antiochus, supposing himself to be despised and suspecting the reproachful voice, while the youngest was still remaining, not only made the exhortation through words, but also pledged with oaths that he would at once make him rich and blessed if he changed over from the ancestral laws, and would have him as friend and entrust duties to him.

**2 Maccabees 7:25**

But when the young man paid no attention at all, the king, calling the mother to him, advised her to become counselor of salvation for the lad.

**2 Maccabees 7:26**

And after he exhorted her with many words, she accepted to persuade the son.

**2 Maccabees 7:27**

But bending toward him, mocking the cruel tyrant, she spoke thus in the ancestral voice: "Son, have mercy on me, who carried you around in the womb nine months, and nursed you three years, and nourished you and led you into this age, and bore you as a nurse.

**2 Maccabees 7:28**

I ask you, child, looking up to heaven and earth, and seeing all things in them, to know that God made them from things not existing, and the race of men has come to be in this way;

**2 Maccabees 7:29**

do not fear this executioner, but becoming worthy of the brothers, accept death, so that in mercy I may recover you with your brothers."

**2 Maccabees 7:30**

And while she was still speaking, the young man said, "For whom are you waiting? I do not obey the command of the king, but I obey the command of the law given to our fathers through Moses.

**2 Maccabees 7:31**

But you, having become inventor of all evil against the Hebrews, shall certainly not escape the hands of God.

**2 Maccabees 7:32**

For we suffer because of our own sins.

**2 Maccabees 7:33**

But if for the sake of rebuke and discipline our living Lord has been briefly angered, he will also again be reconciled to his own servants.

**2 Maccabees 7:34**

But you, O unholy one and most defiled of all men, do not be lifted up vainly, raging with uncertain hopes, lifting up a hand against his servants.

**2 Maccabees 7:35**

For you have not yet escaped the judgment of Almighty watching God.

**2 Maccabees 7:36**

For our brothers, having now endured a brief pain, have come under God's covenant of ever-flowing life; but you, by God's judgment, will carry away the just penalties of arrogance.

**2 Maccabees 7:37**

But I, like my brothers, hand over both body and soul concerning the ancestral laws, calling upon God quickly to become gracious to the nation, and for you, with examinations and whips, to confess fully that he alone is God,

**2 Maccabees 7:38**

and for the wrath of the Almighty, justly brought upon our whole race, to stand still in me and my brothers."

**2 Maccabees 7:39**

And the king, becoming enraged, dealt with this one worse than the others, bearing the mockery bitterly.

**2 Maccabees 7:40**

And therefore this one changed life clean, trusting wholly upon the Lord.

**2 Maccabees 7:41**

And last of the sons, the mother died.

**2 Maccabees 7:42**

Therefore let the things concerning entrail-tastings and the surpassing abuses have been declared to this extent.

### Chapter 8

**2 Maccabees 8:1**

But Judas Maccabeus and those with him, slipping secretly into the villages, called on the kinsmen, and taking along those who had remained in Judaism, gathered about six thousand.

**2 Maccabees 8:2**

And they called upon the Lord to look upon the people trampled by all, and to pity also the shrine profaned by impious men,

**2 Maccabees 8:3**

and to have mercy also on the city being destroyed and about to become level with the ground, and to listen to the bloods crying out to him,

**2 Maccabees 8:4**

and to remember also the unlawful destruction of innocent infants, and the blasphemies committed against his name, and to hate evil.

**2 Maccabees 8:5**

And when Maccabeus had come into an organized body, he was already becoming impossible to withstand by the nations, the wrath of the Lord having turned to mercy.

**2 Maccabees 8:6**

And coming unexpectedly upon cities and villages, he burned them, and taking the critical places, he kept turning to flight and overcoming not a few of the enemies.

**2 Maccabees 8:7**

Especially he took the nights as coworkers for such plots; and a certain report of his manliness poured out everywhere.

**2 Maccabees 8:8**

And Philip, perceiving the man coming little by little into progress and advancing more frequently in successes, wrote to Ptolemy, governor of Coele-Syria and Phoenicia, to help the king's affairs.

**2 Maccabees 8:9**

And he, quickly selecting Nicanor, son of Patroclus, of the first friends, sent him, having placed under him no fewer than twenty thousand nations of every race, to root out the whole race of the Jews; and he joined with him also Gorgias, a man, a general, and having experience in military needs.

**2 Maccabees 8:10**

And Nicanor determined to fulfill the tribute owed by the king to the Romans, two thousand talents, from the captivity of the Jews.

**2 Maccabees 8:11**

And immediately he sent to the seacoast cities, calling them to purchase Jewish bodies, promising to hand over ninety bodies for a talent, not expecting the judgment from the Almighty that was about to follow upon him.

**2 Maccabees 8:12**

And word of Nicanor's advance came to Judas; and when he informed those with him of the army's presence,

**2 Maccabees 8:13**

those acting cowardly and distrusting the justice of God were running away and removing themselves from the place.

**2 Maccabees 8:14**

But others sold all the things left over, and at the same time were asking the Lord to rescue those sold by the impious Nicanor before meeting him.

**2 Maccabees 8:15**

And if not because of them, then because of the covenants with their fathers, and because of the invocation upon them of his solemn and magnificent name.

**2 Maccabees 8:16**

And Maccabeus, gathering those around him, six thousand in number, exhorted them not to be struck with amazement by the enemies, nor to fear the great multitude of nations unjustly coming against them, but to contend nobly,

**2 Maccabees 8:17**

having taken before their eyes the insult unlawfully completed by them against the holy place, and the abuse of the mocked city, and further the dissolution of the ancestral civic order.

**2 Maccabees 8:18**

"For they trust in weapons and bold deeds," he said, "but we trust in Almighty God, who is able in one nod to cast down both those coming against us and the whole world."

**2 Maccabees 8:19**

And recounting to them also the helps that happened in the time of the ancestors, and the one under Sennacherib, how 185,000 perished,

**2 Maccabees 8:20**

and the battle in Babylonia against the Galatians, how all came to the need, 8,000 with 4,000 Macedonians, when the Macedonians were at a loss, the 8,000 destroyed 120,000 because of the help that came to them from heaven, and received much benefit.

**2 Maccabees 8:21**

After making them confident on these things and ready to die on behalf of the laws and the fatherland, he made the army into a certain four-part thing;

**2 Maccabees 8:22**

appointing also his brothers as leaders of each order, Simon and Joseph and Jonathan, assigning to each 1,500,

**2 Maccabees 8:23**

and also Eleazar, after reading aloud the holy book and giving the watchword, "God's help," himself leading the first band, he joined battle with Nicanor.

**2 Maccabees 8:24**

And when the Almighty became an ally to them, they slaughtered over 9,000 of the enemies, and made the greatest part of Nicanor's army wounded and disabled in limbs, and forced all to flee.

**2 Maccabees 8:25**

And they took the monies of those who had come for their purchase; and after pursuing them for a considerable time, they turned back, being shut in by the hour.

**2 Maccabees 8:26**

For it was the day before the Sabbath, for which reason they did not continue long pursuing them.

**2 Maccabees 8:27**

And after collecting their weapons and stripping the spoils of the enemies, they spent the Sabbath especially blessing and confessing to the Lord, the one who had saved them through to this day, having appointed for them a beginning of mercy.

**2 Maccabees 8:28**

And after the Sabbath, distributing from the spoils to those abused and to the widows and orphans, they divided the rest among themselves and the children.

**2 Maccabees 8:29**

And having accomplished these things and made a common supplication, they asked the merciful Lord to be reconciled with his servants to the end.

**2 Maccabees 8:30**

And from those around Timotheus and Bacchides who were fighting with them, they took down more than 20,000 of them, and gained control of very high strongholds; and they divided very many spoils, making themselves equal shares with those who had been abused, and orphans and widows, and further also elders.

**2 Maccabees 8:31**

And after collecting their weapons, they carefully stored all in the critical places, but the remaining spoils they brought into Jerusalem.

**2 Maccabees 8:32**

And they killed the tribal leader of those around Timotheus, a most unholy man and one who had grieved the Jews much.

**2 Maccabees 8:33**

And while leading victory celebrations in the fatherland, those who had burned the holy gates, Callisthenes and some others who had fled into one little house, they set on fire; these received a reward worthy of impiety.

**2 Maccabees 8:34**

But the thrice-wretched Nicanor, who had brought the thousand merchants for the sale of the Jews,

**2 Maccabees 8:35**

having been humbled, with the Lord's help, by those deemed least according to him, having put off the glorious clothing, through the inland region, making himself solitary in the manner of a runaway, came to Antioch, having had exceedingly bad success at the destruction of the army.

**2 Maccabees 8:36**

And the one who had undertaken to set right the tribute to the Romans from the captivity of those in Jerusalem reported that the Jews have God as defender, and because of this manner the Jews were unwoundable, because they followed the laws set before them by him.

### Chapter 9

**2 Maccabees 9:1**

And about that time Antiochus happened to have withdrawn disorderly from the regions in Persia.

**2 Maccabees 9:2**

For he had entered the place called Persepolis, and attempted to plunder the temple and hold the city; therefore, when the multitudes rushed, they turned to the help of weapons; and it happened that Antiochus, being put to flight by the locals, made a shameful retreat.

**2 Maccabees 9:3**

And when he was at Ecbatana, the things that had happened concerning Nicanor and those around Timotheus fell to him.

**2 Maccabees 9:4**

And being lifted up by anger, he supposed he would press onto the Jews also the evil of those who had made him flee; therefore he ordered the chariot-driver, driving without ceasing, to finish the journey, the judgment from heaven indeed going with him; for thus proudly he said, "I will make Jerusalem a mass grave of Jews when I arrive there."

**2 Maccabees 9:5**

But the all-watching Lord, the God of Israel, struck him with an incurable and invisible blow; and just as he finished the word, an incurable pain of the bowels took him, and bitter torments of the inner parts,

**2 Maccabees 9:6**

very justly, since he had tortured the bowels of others with many and strange calamities.

**2 Maccabees 9:7**

But he in no way ceased from haughtiness; indeed he was even filled with arrogance, breathing fire in his passions against the Jews and commanding the journey to be hastened. But it happened also that he fell from the chariot as it was carried with a roar, and, having fallen into a grievous fall, all the members of the body were twisted.

**2 Maccabees 9:8**

And he who just now seemed to command the waves of the sea, because of arrogance beyond man, and supposed he could set the heights of the mountains on scales, having come down to the ground, was being carried in a litter, showing to all the manifest power of God;

**2 Maccabees 9:9**

so that even from the body of the impious man worms swarmed up, and while he lived in agonies and pains his flesh fell apart, and by his smell all the camp was burdened with decay.

**2 Maccabees 9:10**

And the one who a little before seemed to touch the heavenly stars, no one was able to carry because of the unbearable weight of the smell.

**2 Maccabees 9:11**

Therefore there, having been crushed, he began to cease from much of his arrogance and to come into recognition, being increased in pains moment by moment by a divine whip.

**2 Maccabees 9:12**

And not even being able to endure his own smell, he said these things: "It is righteous to be subject to God, and, being mortal, not to think things equal to God arrogantly."

**2 Maccabees 9:13**

And the defiled one was praying to the master who would no longer have mercy on him, saying thus,

**2 Maccabees 9:14**

that the holy city, to which he was hurrying to make level and build into a mass grave, he would declare free;

**2 Maccabees 9:15**

and the Jews, whom he had decided not even to deem worthy of burial, but to throw out with the infants to birds and beasts, all of them he would make equal to Athenians;

**2 Maccabees 9:16**

and the holy temple, which he previously had plundered, he would adorn with most beautiful votive offerings, and would restore all the holy vessels many times over, and the expenses falling to the sacrifices he would supply from his own revenues;

**2 Maccabees 9:17**

and besides these things, that he also would become a Jew and would go through every inhabited place proclaiming the power of God.

**2 Maccabees 9:18**

But when the pains in no way ceased, for the just judgment of God had come upon him, despairing of his own affairs, he wrote to the Jews the letter written below, having the form of a supplication and containing thus:

**2 Maccabees 9:19**

To the good Jews, the citizens, much greeting and health and good action, from King and General Antiochus.

**2 Maccabees 9:20**

If you and the children are strong, and your own affairs are according to mind, I make the greatest thanks to God, having hope toward heaven.

**2 Maccabees 9:21**

And I myself was disposed weakly, but would remember affectionately your honor and goodwill; returning from the regions around Persia and having fallen into a sickness having difficulty, I considered it necessary to care for the common safety of all,

**2 Maccabees 9:22**

not despairing of my own affairs, but having much hope to escape the sickness,

**2 Maccabees 9:23**

but seeing that my father also, in the times when he campaigned into the upper regions, appointed the one who would succeed,

**2 Maccabees 9:24**

so that if anything unexpected should turn out, or some troublesome thing should be reported, those throughout the country, knowing to whom the affairs had been left, might not be disturbed;

**2 Maccabees 9:25**

and besides these things, considering that the adjacent rulers and those neighboring the kingdom are watching the times, awaiting what will turn out, I have appointed my son Antiochus king, whom, often when going up into the upper satrapies, I entrusted and commended to most of you; and I have written to him the things written below.

**2 Maccabees 9:26**

Therefore I exhort you and ask that, remembering the benefactions in common and in particular, each of you preserve the existing goodwill toward me and my son.

**2 Maccabees 9:27**

For I am persuaded that he, following my purpose reasonably and humanely, will conduct himself together with you.

**2 Maccabees 9:28**

Therefore the murderer and blasphemer, having suffered the worst things, as he had treated others, in a foreign land in the mountains, by a most pitiable fate, ended his life.

**2 Maccabees 9:29**
