And Philip, his foster-brother, was carrying the body; and he, being wary of Antiochus's son, was conveyed to Ptolemy Philometor in Egypt.

### Chapter 10

**2 Maccabees 10:1**

But Maccabeus and those with him, the Lord leading them forward, recovered the temple and the city,

**2 Maccabees 10:2**

and the altars in the marketplace made by the foreigners, and further the sacred precincts, they tore down.

**2 Maccabees 10:3**

And having cleansed the temple, they made another altar, and striking stones and taking fire from them, they brought up sacrifice after a two-year time, and made incense and lamps and the presentation of the loaves.

**2 Maccabees 10:4**

And doing these things, falling on the belly, they asked the Lord no longer to fall into such evils, but if ever they should sin, to be disciplined by him with fairness, and not to be handed over to blasphemous and barbarian nations.

**2 Maccabees 10:5**

And on the day in which the temple was profaned by foreigners, it happened on the same day that the cleansing of the shrine took place, the twenty-fifth of the same month, which is Chaseleu.

**2 Maccabees 10:6**

And with gladness they kept eight days in the manner of tabernacles, remembering how a little time before they had been feeding in the mountains and in the caves in the manner of beasts during the feast of tents.

**2 Maccabees 10:7**

Therefore, having thyrsi and beautiful branches, and further palms, they brought up hymns to the one who prospered the cleansing of his own place.

**2 Maccabees 10:8**

And they decreed with common command and vote for the whole nation of the Jews to keep these days each year.

**2 Maccabees 10:9**

And the things of the death of Antiochus, called Epiphanes, were thus.

**2 Maccabees 10:10**

But now we will make clear the things concerning Antiochus Eupator, son of the impious one, shortening the evils of the wars themselves.

**2 Maccabees 10:11**

For he, receiving the kingdom, appointed a certain Lysias over the affairs, chief governor of Coele-Syria and Phoenicia.

**2 Maccabees 10:12**

For Ptolemy called Macron, leading in preserving justice toward the Jews because of the injustice that had happened to them, also tried to conduct things toward them peaceably.

**2 Maccabees 10:13**

Therefore, being accused by the friends before Eupator and hearing "traitor" at every turn, because, after being entrusted with Cyprus by Philometor, he left it and withdrew to Antiochus Epiphanes, having no noble authority, under discouragement poisoning himself, he left life.

**2 Maccabees 10:14**

But Gorgias, becoming general of the places, was maintaining foreigners and at every turn nourishing war against the Jews,

**2 Maccabees 10:15**

and together with this, the Idumeans also, being in control of critical strongholds, were harassing the Jews, and taking in those exiled from Jerusalem, were attempting to nourish war.

**2 Maccabees 10:16**

But those around Maccabeus, making a litany and asking God to become ally to them, rushed upon the strongholds of the Idumeans,

**2 Maccabees 10:17**

and attacking them vigorously, they became masters of the places, and warded off all those fighting on the wall; and they slaughtered those falling in, and killed no fewer than 20,000.

**2 Maccabees 10:18**

And when no fewer than 9,000 had fled together into two very strong towers, having all the things for a siege,

**2 Maccabees 10:19**

Maccabeus, leaving Simon and Joseph, and also Zacchaeus and those with him, enough for their siege, in urgent places, himself departed.

**2 Maccabees 10:20**

But those around Simon, loving money, were persuaded by some of those in the towers with silver; and taking 70,000 drachmas, they allowed some to slip away.

**2 Maccabees 10:21**

But when what had happened was reported to Maccabeus, gathering the leaders of the people, he accused them that they had sold the brothers for silver, releasing the enemies against them.

**2 Maccabees 10:22**

Therefore these, having become traitors, he killed, and immediately seized the two towers.

**2 Maccabees 10:23**

And with weapons, prospering in all things in his hands, he destroyed in the two strongholds more than 20,000.

**2 Maccabees 10:24**

But Timotheus, who had formerly been defeated by the Jews, gathering foreign forces in great multitudes and assembling not a few horses from Asia, was present as one about to take Judea spear-captive.

**2 Maccabees 10:25**

But those around Maccabeus, as he drew near, turned to supplication of God, sprinkling earth on their heads and girding their loins with sackcloth,

**2 Maccabees 10:26**

falling upon the step opposite the altar, they asked him, becoming gracious to them, to be enemy to their enemies and to oppose those opposing, just as the law makes clear.

**2 Maccabees 10:27**

And having come from the petition, taking up the weapons, they advanced farther from the city; and drawing near to the enemies, they were by themselves.

**2 Maccabees 10:28**

And when dawn was just succeeding, both sides attacked; the one having as pledge of success and victory, with virtue, the refuge to the Lord, but the others appointing passion as leader of the contests.

**2 Maccabees 10:29**

And when the battle became strong, there appeared to the opponents from heaven, on horses with golden bridles, five distinguished men, and two were leading the Jews,

**2 Maccabees 10:30**

and taking Maccabeus in the middle and covering him with their own full armor, they kept him unwounded; and at the opponents they were throwing arrows and thunderbolts; therefore, confused by lack of sight, they were cut down, filled with disturbance.

**2 Maccabees 10:31**

And 20,500 were slaughtered, and 600 horsemen.

**2 Maccabees 10:32**

But Timotheus himself fled together into a stronghold called Gazara, a very strong fortress, Chereas being general there.

**2 Maccabees 10:33**

And those around Maccabeus gladly sat around the fortress four days.

**2 Maccabees 10:34**

But those inside, trusting in the strength of the place, were blaspheming exceedingly and were sending out unlawful words.

**2 Maccabees 10:35**

And as the fifth day was beginning to appear, twenty young men of those around Maccabeus, inflamed in passions because of the blasphemies, attacking the wall in a manly manner and with beastlike passion, were cutting down the one who fell in,

**2 Maccabees 10:36**

and others similarly climbing up in the distraction against those inside were burning the towers, and kindling fires were burning the blasphemers alive; and others were cutting through the gates, and receiving the rest of the order, they took the city beforehand,

**2 Maccabees 10:37**

and they slaughtered Timotheus, hidden in a certain pit, and Chereas his brother, and Apollophanes.

**2 Maccabees 10:38**

And having accomplished these things, with hymns and confessions they blessed the Lord, who greatly benefacts Israel and gives them victory.

### Chapter 11

**2 Maccabees 11:1**

And after a very short time Lysias, guardian of the king and kinsman, and the one over the affairs, bearing very heavily the things that had happened,

**2 Maccabees 11:2**

gathering about 80,000 and all the cavalry, came against the Jews, calculating to make the city a dwelling-place for Greeks,

**2 Maccabees 11:3**

and the temple taxable, as the remaining sacred precincts of the nations, and to make the high priesthood saleable year by year,

**2 Maccabees 11:4**

in no way considering the power of God, but being maddened by the myriads of footmen and the thousands of horsemen and the eighty elephants.

**2 Maccabees 11:5**

And entering Judea and drawing near to Bethsura, which was a strong place and was about five stadia from Jerusalem, he was pressing this place.

**2 Maccabees 11:6**

But when those around Maccabeus learned that he was besieging the strongholds, with lamentations and tears, together with the crowds, they entreated the Lord to send a good angel for salvation to Israel.

**2 Maccabees 11:7**

And Maccabeus himself first took up the weapons and exhorted the others, together with him taking risks, to help their brothers; and together they rushed out eagerly.

**2 Maccabees 11:8**

And right there, as they were near Jerusalem, one leading them on horseback in white clothing appeared, shaking golden full armor.

**2 Maccabees 11:9**

And all together blessed the merciful God and were strengthened in souls, being ready to wound not only men but also the wildest beasts and iron walls.

**2 Maccabees 11:10**

They advanced in battle order, having the ally from heaven, the Lord having mercy on them.

**2 Maccabees 11:11**

And striking into the enemies lion-like, they laid down 11,000 of them and 1,600 horsemen; and they forced all to flee.

**2 Maccabees 11:12**

And most of them, wounded and naked, escaped; and Lysias himself, shamefully fleeing, escaped.

**2 Maccabees 11:13**

But being not without understanding, casting together to himself the defeat that had happened around him, and considering that the Hebrews were invincible, all-powerful God fighting as ally to them,

**2 Maccabees 11:14**

having sent to them, he persuaded them to make settlement on all just terms, and said that he would also persuade the king, compelling him to become a friend to them.

**2 Maccabees 11:15**

And Maccabeus nodded assent to all the things Lysias entreated, taking thought for the benefit; for as many things as Maccabeus handed to Lysias in writing concerning the Jews, the king conceded.

**2 Maccabees 11:16**

For the letters written to the Jews from Lysias contained this manner: Lysias to the multitude of the Jews, greetings.

**2 Maccabees 11:17**

John and Abessalom, sent by you, having handed over the document written below, were asking concerning the things signified through it.

**2 Maccabees 11:18**

Therefore as many things as also needed to be brought to the king I made clear, and what was admissible he conceded.

**2 Maccabees 11:19**

Therefore if you preserve the goodwill toward the affairs, also for the future I will try to become cause of good things for you.

**2 Maccabees 11:20**

But concerning the particulars, I have commanded both these men and those from me to converse with you.

**2 Maccabees 11:21**

Be strong. Year 148, Dioscorinthius 24th.

**2 Maccabees 11:22**

And the king's letter contained thus: King Antiochus to brother Lysias, greetings.

**2 Maccabees 11:23**

Our father having departed to gods, wishing those from the kingdom to be undisturbed for the care of their own affairs,

**2 Maccabees 11:24**

having heard that the Jews do not consent to our father's changeover to Greek things, but choose their own conduct, and because of this ask that their legal customs be conceded to them;

**2 Maccabees 11:25**

therefore choosing that this nation also be outside disturbance, we judge that the temple be restored to them, and that they conduct citizenship according to the customs of their ancestors.

**2 Maccabees 11:26**

Therefore you will do well, sending to them and giving right hands, so that knowing our purpose, they may be good-spirited and gladly go through with the recovery of their own affairs.

**2 Maccabees 11:27**

And to the nation the king's letter was such: King Antiochus to the senate of the Jews and the other Jews, greetings.

**2 Maccabees 11:28**

If you are strong, it would be as we wish; and we ourselves are healthy.

**2 Maccabees 11:29**

Menelaus has disclosed to us that you wish, having come down, to be with your own affairs.

**2 Maccabees 11:30**

Therefore to those going down until the thirtieth of Xanthicus there will be right hand with security,

**2 Maccabees 11:31**

for the Jews to use their own provisions and laws just as also before, and none of them in any way will be troubled concerning the things done in ignorance.

**2 Maccabees 11:32**

And I have sent Menelaus also to exhort you.

**2 Maccabees 11:33**

Be strong. Year 148, Xanthicus 15th.

**2 Maccabees 11:34**

And the Romans also sent to them a letter having thus: Quintus Memmius, Titus Manlius, elders of the Romans, to the people of the Jews, greetings.

**2 Maccabees 11:35**

Concerning the things Lysias the king's kinsman conceded to you, we also approve.

**2 Maccabees 11:36**

But what he judged to be brought before the king, send someone at once after inspecting these things, so that we may set them out as is fitting for you; for we are going to Antioch.

**2 Maccabees 11:37**

Therefore hurry and send some, so that we also may know what opinion you are of.

**2 Maccabees 11:38**

Be healthy. Year 148, Xanthicus 15th.

### Chapter 12

**2 Maccabees 12:1**

These agreements having happened, Lysias went away to the king, and the Jews were about agriculture.

**2 Maccabees 12:2**

But of the local generals, Timotheus and Apollonius son of Gennaeus, and still Hieronymus and Demophon, and besides these Nicanor the Cyprus-ruler, were not allowing them to be stable and to conduct the things of quietness.

**2 Maccabees 12:3**

But Joppites completed such an impious deed: having invited the Jews living with them to embark into the boats set alongside by them, with wives and children, as no hostility toward them was present,

**2 Maccabees 12:4**

and according to the common vote of the city, and these accepting it as those wishing to be at peace and having nothing suspicious, when they had been brought out to sea, they drowned them, being no fewer than two hundred.

**2 Maccabees 12:5**

And Judas, learning the cruelty that had happened against his fellow-nationals, having commanded the men around him,

**2 Maccabees 12:6**

and having called upon God the righteous judge, came against the murderers of the brothers; and the harbor by night he burned, and the boats he burned up, and those who had fled together there he pierced through.

**2 Maccabees 12:7**

But when the place was closed up, he withdrew, as one who would come again and root out the whole citizenship of the Joppites.

**2 Maccabees 12:8**

And learning also that those in Jamnia wished to carry out the same manner against the Jews dwelling among them,

**2 Maccabees 12:9**

and attacking the Jamnites by night, he set the harbor with the fleet on fire, so that the rays of the light were seen in Jerusalem, 240 stadia away.

**2 Maccabees 12:10**

And having been drawn away from there nine stadia, as they made the journey toward Timotheus, Arabs attacked him, no fewer than 5,000 and 500 cavalry.

**2 Maccabees 12:11**

And when a strong battle happened, and those around Judas prospered because of the help from God, the defeated Nomad Arabs asked Judas to give them right hands, promising both to give livestock and to benefit them in the remaining matters.

**2 Maccabees 12:12**

And Judas, supposing that they would truly be useful in many things, agreed to make peace with them; and receiving right hands, they departed to their tents.

**2 Maccabees 12:13**

And he also made an attack upon a certain bridged strong city, enclosed by walls and inhabited by all-mixed nations, and the name was Caspin.

**2 Maccabees 12:14**

But those inside, trusting in the strength of the walls and the store of foods, behaved more rudely, reviling those around Judas, and furthermore blaspheming and speaking things not lawful.

**2 Maccabees 12:15**

But those around Judas, calling upon the great ruler of the world, who without rams and mechanical engines overthrew Jericho in the times of Joshua, shook the wall beastlike.

**2 Maccabees 12:16**

And seizing the city by the will of God, they made untold slaughters, so that the adjacent lake, having a width of two stadia, appeared filled with blood flowing down.

**2 Maccabees 12:17**

And from there, having pulled away 750 stadia, they completed the journey to Charax, toward the Jews called Tubians.

**2 Maccabees 12:18**

And Timotheus they did not find in the places, since he had withdrawn from the places without result, but had left behind a garrison in a certain very strong place.

**2 Maccabees 12:19**

But Dositheus and Sosipater, of the leaders around Maccabeus, going out, destroyed those left by Timotheus in the fortress, more than 10,000 men.

**2 Maccabees 12:20**

And Maccabeus, arranging his own army by companies, set them over the companies and rushed upon Timotheus, who had around himself 120,000 footmen and 1,500 cavalry.

**2 Maccabees 12:21**

But Timotheus, learning Judas's advance, sent the women and the children and the other baggage ahead into the place called Carnion; for the place was difficult to besiege and difficult to approach because of the narrowness of all the places.

**2 Maccabees 12:22**

And when Judas's first company appeared, and fear came upon the enemies, and terror from the manifestation of the one who watches all came upon them, they rushed into flight, one being carried one way, another another, so that often they were injured by their own and pierced by the points of swords.

**2 Maccabees 12:23**

And Judas made the pursuit more vigorous, stabbing the accursed ones, and he destroyed about 30,000 men.

**2 Maccabees 12:24**

And Timotheus himself, falling into the hands of those around Dositheus and Sosipater, asked with much craft to be released safe, because he had parents of many and brothers of some, and these would happen to be disregarded if he died.

**2 Maccabees 12:25**

And when he confirmed with many words the agreement that he would restore these unharmed, they released him for the salvation of the brothers.

**2 Maccabees 12:26**

And going out upon Carnion and the Atargateion, he slaughtered 25,000 bodies.

**2 Maccabees 12:27**

And after the turning and destruction of these, Judas marched also upon Ephron, a strong city in which Lysias dwelt, and an all-mixed multitude; and strong young men standing before the walls were defending vigorously, and there were many stores of engines and missiles.

**2 Maccabees 12:28**

And calling upon the Ruler, who with power breaks the strengths of the enemies, they took the city under hand and laid down about 25,000 of those inside.

**2 Maccabees 12:29**

And breaking camp from there, they rushed upon the city of Scythians, 600 stadia from Jerusalem.

**2 Maccabees 12:30**

But when the Jews living there testified to the goodwill that the Scythopolitans had toward them, and that in the times of misfortune they made a gentle meeting,

**2 Maccabees 12:31**

having thanked them and further exhorted them also to be well-disposed toward the race in the remaining time, they came to Jerusalem, the feast of Weeks being near.

**2 Maccabees 12:32**

And after the feast called Pentecost, they rushed against Gorgias, governor of Idumea.

**2 Maccabees 12:33**

And he came out with 3,000 footmen and 400 horsemen.

**2 Maccabees 12:34**

And when they drew up in battle, it happened that a few of the Jews fell.

**2 Maccabees 12:35**

And a certain Dositheus, of those of Bacenor, a horseman and strong man, was holding Gorgias, and grasping the cloak, was leading him vigorously, and wishing to take the accursed man alive; when a certain Thracian cavalryman bore down on him and took off the shoulder, Gorgias escaped to Marisa.

**2 Maccabees 12:36**

But when those around Esdris were fighting more and were worn out, Judas called upon the Lord to appear as ally and guide of the war,

**2 Maccabees 12:37**

beginning in the ancestral voice the shout with hymns, crying out, and shaking unexpectedly against those around Gorgias, he made their rout.

**2 Maccabees 12:38**

And Judas, taking up the army, led it to the city Odollam; and as the week was coming on, having purified themselves according to the custom, they spent the Sabbath there.

**2 Maccabees 12:39**

And on the next day, those around Judas came, according as the need had happened, to carry back the bodies of those who had fallen before and to restore them with their relatives into the ancestral tombs.

**2 Maccabees 12:40**

But they found under the tunics of each of the dead sacred tokens of the idols from Jamnia, from which the law bars the Jews; and it became clear to all that for this cause these had fallen.

**2 Maccabees 12:41**

Therefore all, blessing the Lord, the righteous judge, who makes hidden things visible,

**2 Maccabees 12:42**

turned to supplication, asking that the sin that had happened be fully wiped out. And noble Judas exhorted the multitude to keep themselves sinless, having seen under their eyes the things that had happened because of the sin of those who had fallen before.

**2 Maccabees 12:43**

And having made a collection man by man, preparations amounting to 2,000 drachmas of silver, he sent it to Jerusalem to bring a sacrifice concerning sin, acting very beautifully and nobly, reasoning about resurrection;

**2 Maccabees 12:44**

for if he had not expected those who had fallen before to rise, it would have been excessive and foolish to pray concerning dead ones;

**2 Maccabees 12:45**

then looking to the most beautiful gracious gift laid up for those sleeping with piety. Holy and pious was the thought; therefore he made propitiation concerning the dead, for them to be released from sin.

### Chapter 13

**2 Maccabees 13:1**

And in the 149th year it fell to those around Judas that Antiochus Eupator had come with multitudes upon Judea,

**2 Maccabees 13:2**

and with him Lysias, the guardian and the one over the affairs, each having a Greek force of 110,000 footmen, and 5,300 cavalry, and 22 elephants, and 300 scythe-bearing chariots.

**2 Maccabees 13:3**

And Menelaus also joined with them, and with much dissimulation was exhorting Antiochus, not for the salvation of the fatherland, but supposing he would be established over the office.

**2 Maccabees 13:4**

But the King of kings roused the anger of Antiochus against the accursed one, and when Lysias pointed out that this man was the cause of all the evils, he ordered, as is the custom in the place, to bring him to Beroea and kill him.

**2 Maccabees 13:5**

And there is in the place a tower of fifty cubits, full of ashes; and this had a round instrument, steep all around into the ashes.

**2 Maccabees 13:6**

There all thrust into destruction the one who is guilty of temple-robbery or has committed an excess of some other evils.

**2 Maccabees 13:7**

By such a fate the lawless Menelaus happened to die, not even obtaining the earth;

**2 Maccabees 13:8**

very justly. For since he had committed many sins around the altar, whose fire and ashes were pure, in ashes he obtained death.

**2 Maccabees 13:9**

But the king, barbarized in his thoughts, was coming to show the Jews the worst things beyond those that had happened under his father.

**2 Maccabees 13:10**

And Judas, learning these things, commanded the multitude day and night to call upon the Lord, if ever at another time, also now to help those about to be deprived of law and fatherland and holy temple,

**2 Maccabees 13:11**

and not to allow the people, just briefly refreshed, to become subject to the blasphemous nations.

**2 Maccabees 13:12**

And when all did the same together and entreated the merciful Lord with weeping and fastings and prostration for three days without ceasing, Judas exhorted them and commanded them to be present.

**2 Maccabees 13:13**

And having come by himself with the elders, he resolved, before the king's army entered Judea and became masters of the city, that they should go out and decide the affairs by the Lord's help.

**2 Maccabees 13:14**

And having given the commission to the Creator of the world, exhorting those with him to contend nobly until death concerning laws, temple, city, fatherland, and civic order, he made the encampment near Modein.

**2 Maccabees 13:15**

And giving those around him the watchword "God's victory," with selected best young men attacking by night upon the royal court, in the camp he killed about 4,000 men, and the leading elephant with the crowd in its house.

**2 Maccabees 13:16**

And in the end they filled the camp with fear and disturbance, and they departed prospering.

**2 Maccabees 13:17**

And this had happened as the day was already beginning to appear, because the Lord's shelter gave help to him.

**2 Maccabees 13:18**

But the king, having taken a taste of the good courage of the Jews, tested the places through methods.

**2 Maccabees 13:19**

And he advanced upon Bethsura, a strong fortress of the Jews; and he was turned back, stumbled, and was lessened.

**2 Maccabees 13:20**

And to those inside Judas sent in the necessary things.

**2 Maccabees 13:21**

But Rhodocus from the Jewish order reported the mysteries to the enemies; and he was searched out, and seized, and imprisoned.

**2 Maccabees 13:22**

The king spoke a second time with those in Bethsura; he gave right hand, received, went away, attacked those around Judas, and became inferior,

**2 Maccabees 13:23**

learned that Philip in Antioch, the one left over the affairs, had gone mad, and was confused; he entreated the Jews, submitted, and swore upon all just terms; he made settlement and brought sacrifice, honored the temple, and treated the place humanely,

**2 Maccabees 13:24**

and he accepted Maccabeus; he left Hegemonides as general from Ptolemais to the Gerrenians,

**2 Maccabees 13:25**

and came to Ptolemais. The Ptolemaeans were displeased concerning the agreements, for they were indignant over the things by which they wished to nullify the stipulations.

**2 Maccabees 13:26**

Lysias went up to the judgment seat, made as much defense as was possible, persuaded, calmed, made them well-disposed, and broke camp to Antioch. Thus the things of the king's advance and withdrawal proceeded.

### Chapter 14

**2 Maccabees 14:1**

And after a three-year time it fell to those around Judas that Demetrius, son of Seleucus, having sailed in through the harbor at Tripolis with a strong multitude and fleet,

**2 Maccabees 14:2**

had taken control of the country, having removed Antiochus and his guardian Lysias.

**2 Maccabees 14:3**

But a certain Alcimus, who had formerly become high priest, but had been voluntarily defiled in the times of mixing, considering that by any manner there was no salvation for him nor any longer approach to the holy altar,

**2 Maccabees 14:4**

came to King Demetrius in the 151st year, bringing him a golden crown and a palm, and besides these some of the customary branches of the temple; and that day he had quiet.

**2 Maccabees 14:5**

But taking opportunity, a coworker of his own folly, being called into council by Demetrius and asked in what disposition and counsel the Jews were established, he said to these things,

**2 Maccabees 14:6**

"The so-called Hasideans of the Jews, of whom Judas Maccabeus is leader, nourish war and make factions, not allowing the kingdom to obtain stability.

**2 Maccabees 14:7**

Therefore, having been deprived of the ancestral glory, I mean the high priesthood, I have now come here.

**2 Maccabees 14:8**

First, thinking sincerely on behalf of the things belonging to the king, and second, also considering my own citizens; for by the thoughtlessness of those aforementioned, our whole race is not a little deprived.

**2 Maccabees 14:9**

But you, O king, knowing each of these things, provide for the country and our race surrounded, according to the humane kindness that you have, easy to meet, toward all.

**2 Maccabees 14:10**

For until Judas remains, it is impossible for the affairs to obtain peace."

**2 Maccabees 14:11**

And when such things had been said by this man, quickly the remaining friends, being hostile toward Judas, further inflamed Demetrius.

**2 Maccabees 14:12**

And immediately calling Nicanor, who had become elephant-commander, and appointing him general of Judea, he sent him out,

**2 Maccabees 14:13**

giving commands to remove Judas himself, and to scatter those with him, and to establish Alcimus high priest of the greatest temple.

**2 Maccabees 14:14**

And the nations who had fled Judas from Judea were mixing by herds with Nicanor, supposing the misfortunes and calamities of the Jews would be their own successes.

**2 Maccabees 14:15**

And hearing Nicanor's advance and the attack of the nations, sprinkling earth they made litany to the one who established his own people unto the age, and always with manifestation helps his own portion.

**2 Maccabees 14:16**

And when the leader commanded, from there immediately they broke camp and joined them at the village Dessau.

**2 Maccabees 14:17**

And Simon, Judas's brother, had joined battle with Nicanor, but had stumbled a little because of the sudden speechlessness of the opponents.

**2 Maccabees 14:18**

Nevertheless Nicanor, hearing the manly goodness of those around Judas, and their good courage in contests on behalf of the fatherland, was cautious to make the judgment through blood;

**2 Maccabees 14:19**

therefore he sent Posidonius and Theodotus and Mattathias, to give and receive right hands.

**2 Maccabees 14:20**

And when much review had happened concerning these things, and the leader had communicated to the multitudes, and an opinion of one vote appeared, they nodded assent to the agreements.

**2 Maccabees 14:21**

And they appointed a day on which they would privately come into the same place; and he came forward, and from each side they set separate stools.

**2 Maccabees 14:22**

Judas arranged armed men ready in the critical places, lest some evil deed should suddenly happen from the enemies; they made the fitting mutual discussion.

**2 Maccabees 14:23**

And Nicanor spent time in Jerusalem and did nothing out of place; and the crowds gathered in herds he dismissed.

**2 Maccabees 14:24**

And he had Judas before his face continually; he was attached to the man according to soul.

**2 Maccabees 14:25**

He exhorted him to marry and beget children; he married, was settled, and shared life.

**2 Maccabees 14:26**

But Alcimus, perceiving the goodwill toward one another and taking up the agreements that had happened, came to Demetrius and said that Nicanor thought things alien to the affairs; for he had appointed Judas, plotter against the kingdom, as successor to himself.

**2 Maccabees 14:27**

But the king, becoming enraged and provoked by the slanders of the all-wicked man, wrote to Nicanor, saying that he bore heavily over the agreements and commanding him to send Maccabeus bound quickly to Antioch.

**2 Maccabees 14:28**

And when these things fell to Nicanor, he was confused and bore it painfully, if he should nullify the stipulations when the man had wronged nothing.

**2 Maccabees 14:29**

But since acting against the king was not possible, he kept an opportune time to accomplish this by stratagem.

**2 Maccabees 14:30**

But Maccabeus, perceiving Nicanor conducting himself more severely toward him and having the customary meeting more savage, understanding that the severity was not from the best, gathering not a few of those around himself, hid himself from Nicanor.

**2 Maccabees 14:31**

And the other, realizing that he had been nobly outgeneraled by the man, came to the greatest and holy temple, as the priests were bringing the proper sacrifices, and commanded them to deliver the man.

**2 Maccabees 14:32**

But when with oaths they said they did not know where the one being sought was,

**2 Maccabees 14:33**

stretching out his right hand toward the temple, he swore these things: "Unless you deliver Judas bound to me, I will make this shrine of God into a plain, and I will dig down the altar, and here I will build a prominent temple to Dionysus."

**2 Maccabees 14:34**

And having said so much, he departed; and the priests, stretching out their hands to heaven, called upon the constant defender of our nation, saying these things,

**2 Maccabees 14:35**

"You, Lord, existing without need of all things, were pleased for the temple of your tabernacling to be among us.

**2 Maccabees 14:36**

And now, holy Lord of every sanctification, keep this house, recently cleansed, undefiled into the age."

**2 Maccabees 14:37**

But a certain Razis, one of the elders from Jerusalem, was reported to Nicanor, a man who loved the city and was heard very well of, and according to goodwill was called father of the Jews.

**2 Maccabees 14:38**

For in the former times of non-mixing he had brought forward judgment of Judaism, and had risked body and soul on behalf of Judaism with all intensity.

**2 Maccabees 14:39**

But Nicanor, wishing to make plain the hostility that he had toward the Jews, sent over 500 soldiers to arrest him.

**2 Maccabees 14:40**

For it seemed that by arresting him he would work calamity to these.

**2 Maccabees 14:41**

But when the multitudes were about to seize the tower, and were forcing the courtyard door, and commanded fire to be brought and the doors to be set on fire, being surrounded for capture he set a sword under himself,

**2 Maccabees 14:42**

wishing nobly to die rather than become subject to the accursed ones and be insolently abused unworthy of his own nobility.

**2 Maccabees 14:43**

But not hitting straight with the blow because of the haste of the struggle, and the crowds breaking inside the doorways, running up nobly to the wall, he hurled himself bravely into the crowds.

**2 Maccabees 14:44**

But when they quickly stepped back, and a space happened, he came into the middle of the hollow place.

**2 Maccabees 14:45**

And still being breathing and inflamed in passions, he rose up, streams of blood being carried like fountains, and the wounds being grievous; running through the crowds and standing upon a certain steep rock,

**2 Maccabees 14:46**

already having become wholly bloodless, throwing forward the entrails and taking them with both hands, he shook them at the crowds; and calling upon the one who rules life and spirit to give these back to him again, in this manner he changed life.

### Chapter 15

**2 Maccabees 15:1**

But Nicanor, learning that those around Judas were in the places around Samaria, resolved to attack them on the day of rest with all security.

**2 Maccabees 15:2**

But the Jews who were following him by compulsion said, "By no means destroy so savagely and barbarously, but apportion glory to the day honored beforehand with holiness by the one who watches all things."

**2 Maccabees 15:3**

But the thrice-accursed one asked if there is in heaven a ruler who commanded the day of the Sabbaths to be kept.

**2 Maccabees 15:4**

And when they declared, "There is the living Lord himself in heaven, a ruler, who commanded the seventh day to be observed,"

**2 Maccabees 15:5**

the other said, "And I also am a ruler upon earth, who commands weapons to be taken up and the royal needs to be completed." Nevertheless he did not prevail to complete his wretched will.

**2 Maccabees 15:6**

And Nicanor, holding his neck high with all arrogance, had determined to set up a common trophy over those around Judas.

**2 Maccabees 15:7**

But Maccabeus was unceasingly trusting with all hope to obtain help from the Lord.

**2 Maccabees 15:8**

And he exhorted those with him not to be cowardly at the advance of the nations, but having in mind the helps from heaven that had happened before, also now to expect the victory and help that would be from the Almighty.

**2 Maccabees 15:9**

And encouraging them from the law and the prophets, and reminding them also of the contests they had completed, he made them more eager.

**2 Maccabees 15:10**

And stirring them up in passions, he commanded them, at the same time showing the faithlessness of the nations and the transgression of oaths.

**2 Maccabees 15:11**

And arming each of them, not with the security of shields and spears so much as with the encouragement in good words, and additionally narrating a trustworthy dream, a kind of waking vision, he gladdened all.

**2 Maccabees 15:12**

And his vision was such: Onias, who had been high priest, a good and noble man, modest in meeting, gentle in manner, putting forward speech fittingly, and from a child trained in all the proper things of virtue, this one stretching out his hands and praying for the whole body of the Jews.

**2 Maccabees 15:13**

Then in this way a man appeared, distinguished in gray hair and glory, and a certain marvelous and most magnificent preeminence was around him.

**2 Maccabees 15:14**

And Onias answered and said, "This is the brother-loving one who prays much concerning the people and the holy city, Jeremiah the prophet of God."

**2 Maccabees 15:15**

And Jeremiah, stretching out the right hand, handed to Judas a golden sword, and while giving it addressed these things,

**2 Maccabees 15:16**

"Take the holy sword, a gift from God, through which you will shatter the opponents."

**2 Maccabees 15:17**

And having been encouraged by Judas's words, very beautiful and able to stir to virtue and restore souls of young men, they determined not to camp but to bear in nobly, and with all manliness, engaging, to decide the affairs, because the city and the holy things and the temple were in danger.

**2 Maccabees 15:18**

For the contest concerning women and children, and further brothers and kinsmen, was lying in lesser part with them, but the greatest and first fear was concerning the consecrated temple.

**2 Maccabees 15:19**

And for those shut up in the city, the agony was not secondary, being disturbed concerning the attack in the open air.

**2 Maccabees 15:20**

And all already expecting the coming judgment, and the enemies already joining, and the army set in order, and the beasts restored to an opportune side, and the cavalry arranged at the wing,

**2 Maccabees 15:21**

Maccabeus, perceiving the presence of the multitudes and the varied preparation of the weapons and the savagery of the beasts, stretched out his hands to heaven and called upon the wonder-working Lord, the watcher, knowing that victory is not through weapons, but as it is judged by him, he secures victory for the worthy.

**2 Maccabees 15:22**

And invoking, he was saying in this manner: "You, Master, sent your angel in the time of Hezekiah king of Judea, and killed from the camp of Sennacherib up to 185,000.

**2 Maccabees 15:23**

And now, Ruler of the heavens, send a good angel before us for fear and trembling.

**2 Maccabees 15:24**

By the greatness of your arm may those who come with blasphemy against your holy people be struck with amazement." And he indeed ceased in these things.

**2 Maccabees 15:25**

But those around Nicanor were advancing with trumpets and paeans,

**2 Maccabees 15:26**

but those around Judas joined the enemies with invocation and prayers.

**2 Maccabees 15:27**

And fighting with hands but praying with hearts to God, they laid down no fewer than 35,000, being greatly gladdened by the manifestation of God.

**2 Maccabees 15:28**

And when they had come from the need and were returning with joy, they recognized Nicanor fallen before with the full armor.

**2 Maccabees 15:29**

And when a cry and disturbance happened, they blessed the Ruler in the ancestral voice.

**2 Maccabees 15:30**

And the one in every way first champion for the citizens in body and soul, who had preserved the goodwill of his age toward fellow-nationals, commanded that they cut off Nicanor's head and the hand with the shoulder and carry them to Jerusalem.

**2 Maccabees 15:31**

And coming there, and calling together the fellow-nationals, and setting the priests before the altar, he sent for those from the citadel.

**2 Maccabees 15:32**

And he displayed the head of defiled Nicanor and the hand of the blasphemer, which he had stretched out against the holy house of the Almighty while greatly boasting.

**2 Maccabees 15:33**

And cutting out the tongue of the impious Nicanor, he said he would give it to the birds by part, and hang the wages of folly opposite the temple.

**2 Maccabees 15:34**

And all blessed toward heaven the manifest Lord, saying, "Blessed is the one who kept his own place undefiled."

**2 Maccabees 15:35**

And he hung Nicanor's head out from the citadel, a clear and visible sign to all of the Lord's help.

**2 Maccabees 15:36**

And all decreed with common vote in no way to allow this day to be unmarked, but to have as notable the thirteenth of the twelfth month, called Adar in the Syrian voice, one day before Mordecai's day.

**2 Maccabees 15:37**

Therefore, when the things concerning Nicanor had proceeded thus, and from those times the city was held by the Hebrews, I myself will stop the account right here.

**2 Maccabees 15:38**

And if it is well and aptly in the composition, this is what I myself wished; but if cheaply and moderately, this was attainable by me.

**2 Maccabees 15:39**

For just as to drink wine alone, and likewise water again, is hostile; but in the manner that wine mixed with water is sweet and accomplishes grace delightfully, so also the construction of the account delights the ears of those encountering the composition; and here will be the end.

## 1 Esdras

### Chapter 1

**1 Esdras 1:1**

And Josiah kept the Passover in Jerusalem to his Lord and sacrificed the Passover on the fourteenth day of the first month,

**1 Esdras 1:2**

having set the priests according to daily orders, clothed in the temple of the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:3**

And he told the Levites, temple servants of Israel, to sanctify themselves to the Lord in the placing of the holy ark of the Lord in the house that Solomon son of David the king had built:

**1 Esdras 1:4**

It shall not be for you to carry it on your shoulders. And now serve the Lord your God and minister to his nation Israel, and prepare yourselves according to your paternal houses and your tribes, according to the writing of David king of Israel and according to the greatness of Solomon his son.

**1 Esdras 1:5**

And standing in the holy place according to the division of your fathers, you Levites, before your brothers the sons of Israel,

**1 Esdras 1:6**

sacrifice the Passover in order, and prepare the sacrifices for your brothers, and do the Passover according to the commandment of the Lord that was given to Moses.

**1 Esdras 1:7**

And Josiah gifted to the people who were found there thirty thousand lambs and kids, and three thousand calves. These were given from the royal things according to promise, to the people and to the priests and Levites.

**1 Esdras 1:8**

And Hilkiah and Zechariah and Heskelos, the overseers of the temple, gave to the priests for Passover two thousand six hundred sheep and three hundred calves.

**1 Esdras 1:9**

And Jeconiah and Semeias and Nathanael the brother, and Asabias and Ochiel and Joram, captains of thousands, gave to the Levites for Passover five thousand sheep and seven hundred calves.

**1 Esdras 1:10**

And when these things had come to be, the priests and the Levites stood in good order, having the unleavened things according to the tribes and according to the divisions of the fathers before the people, to offer to the Lord according to the things written in the book of Moses; and so in the morning.

**1 Esdras 1:11**

And they roasted the Passover with fire, as is fitting, and they boiled the sacrifices in the bronze vessels and kettles with fragrance and carried them away to all those from the people.

**1 Esdras 1:12**

And after these things they prepared for themselves and for their brothers the priests, the sons of Aaron.

**1 Esdras 1:13**

For the priests were offering the fat until late, and the Levites prepared for themselves and for their brothers the priests, the sons of Aaron.

**1 Esdras 1:14**

And the temple singers, sons of Asaph, were in their order, according to the things arranged by David and Asaph and Zechariah and Eddinous, who was beside the king,

**1 Esdras 1:15**

and the gatekeepers were at each gate. It was not possible for each one to pass by his own daily order, for their brothers the Levites prepared for them.

**1 Esdras 1:16**

And the things of the sacrifice of the Lord were completed in that day, to keep the Passover and to bring the sacrifices upon the altar of the Lord according to the command of King Josiah.

**1 Esdras 1:17**

And the sons of Israel who were found at this time kept the Passover and the feast of unleavened bread seven days.

**1 Esdras 1:18**

And such a Passover had not been kept in Israel from the times of Samuel the prophet,

**1 Esdras 1:19**

and all the kings of Israel had not kept such a Passover as Josiah kept, and the priests and the Levites and the Jews and all Israel who were found in their dwelling in Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 1:20**

In the eighteenth year of Josiah's reign this Passover was kept.

**1 Esdras 1:21**

And the works of Josiah were set right before his Lord with a heart full of reverence.

**1 Esdras 1:22**

And the things concerning him also have been written in the former times, concerning those who sinned and acted impiously against the Lord beyond every nation and kingdom, and the things by which they grieved him in perception, and the words of the Lord rose up against Israel.

**1 Esdras 1:23**

And after all this act of Josiah, it happened that Pharaoh king of Egypt came to raise war in Carchemish upon the Euphrates, and Josiah went out to meet him.

**1 Esdras 1:24**

And the king of Egypt sent to him, saying, What is there between me and you, king of Judea?

**1 Esdras 1:25**

I have not been sent by the Lord God against you, for my war is upon the Euphrates. And now the Lord is with me, and the Lord is with me hastening; stand away and do not oppose the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:26**

And Josiah did not turn himself back upon his chariot, but attempted to fight him, not attending to the words of Jeremiah the prophet from the mouth of the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:27**

But he set battle with him in the plain of Megiddo, and the rulers came down to King Josiah.

**1 Esdras 1:28**

And the king said to his servants, Remove me from the battle, for I have become very weak. And immediately his servants removed him from the battle line,

**1 Esdras 1:29**

and he went up onto his second chariot. And having been restored to Jerusalem, he changed his life and was buried in his ancestral tomb.

**1 Esdras 1:30**

And in all Judea they mourned Josiah, and Jeremiah the prophet lamented for Josiah, and the chief men with women lamented him until this day; and this was given out to happen always for the whole race of Israel.

**1 Esdras 1:31**

And these things have been written in the book of the histories concerning the kings of Judea; and each thing done from the act of Josiah and his glory and his understanding in the law of the Lord, both the things done before by him and the present things, is recounted in the book of the kings of Israel and Judah.

**1 Esdras 1:32**

And those from the nation took up Jeconiah son of Josiah and appointed him king instead of Josiah his father, being twenty-three years old.

**1 Esdras 1:33**

And he reigned in Israel and Jerusalem three months. And the king of Egypt removed him from reigning in Jerusalem

**1 Esdras 1:34**

and penalized the nation one hundred talents of silver and one talent of gold.

**1 Esdras 1:35**

And the king of Egypt appointed Joakim his brother king, king of Judea and Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 1:36**

And Joakim bound the great men, but after seizing Zarakes his brother, he brought him up out of Egypt.

**1 Esdras 1:37**

And Joakim was twenty-five years old when he reigned over Judea and Jerusalem, and he did what was evil before the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:38**

And after him Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon came up, and having bound him with a bronze bond, he led him away to Babylon.

**1 Esdras 1:39**

And Nebuchadnezzar took from the holy vessels of the Lord and carried them away and set them aside in his temple in Babylon.

**1 Esdras 1:40**

And the things recounted concerning him and his uncleanness and impiety have been written in the book of the times of the kings.

**1 Esdras 1:41**

And Joakim his son reigned instead of him, for when he was appointed, he was eight years old.

**1 Esdras 1:42**

And he reigned three months and ten days in Jerusalem and did what was evil before the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:43**

And after a year Nebuchadnezzar sent and transferred him to Babylon together with the holy vessels of the Lord,

**1 Esdras 1:44**

and he appointed Zedekiah king of Judea and Jerusalem, being twenty-one years old; and he reigned eleven years.

**1 Esdras 1:45**

And he did what was evil before the Lord and was not turned back by the words spoken by Jeremiah the prophet from the mouth of the Lord.

**1 Esdras 1:46**

And having sworn by King Nebuchadnezzar in the name of the Lord, he perjured himself and revolted. And hardening his neck and his heart, he transgressed the ordinances of the Lord God of Israel.

**1 Esdras 1:47**

And the leaders of the people and of the priests also acted impiously in many things and beyond all the uncleannesses of all the nations, and they defiled the temple of the Lord, which had been sanctified in Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 1:48**

And the God of their fathers sent through his messenger to call them back, because he was sparing them and his dwelling.

**1 Esdras 1:49**

But they mocked among his messengers, and on the day when the Lord spoke, they were making sport of his prophets, until he, having become angry against his nation because of their impious deeds, commanded the kings of the Chaldeans to be brought up against them.

**1 Esdras 1:50**

These killed their young men with the sword around their holy temple and did not spare young man or virgin or elder or younger one, but they gave all into their hands.

**1 Esdras 1:51**

And taking up all the holy vessels of the Lord, the great and the small, and the chests of the Lord and the royal storehouses, they carried them away to Babylon.

**1 Esdras 1:52**

And they burned the house of the Lord with fire and tore down the walls of Jerusalem and burned its towers with fire,

**1 Esdras 1:53**

and they completed all her glorious things, to make them useless; and the ones left he led away with the sword to Babylon.

**1 Esdras 1:54**

And they were servants to him and to his sons until the Persians reigned, to fulfill the word of the Lord in the mouth of Jeremiah:

**1 Esdras 1:55**

Until the land should take pleasure in its Sabbaths, all the time of its desolation it will keep Sabbath, to complete seventy years.

### Chapter 2

**1 Esdras 2:1**

In the first year of Cyrus reigning over the Persians, for the completion of the word of the Lord in the mouth of Jeremiah,

**1 Esdras 2:2**

the Lord raised the spirit of Cyrus king of Persians, and he proclaimed in his whole kingdom, and also through writings, saying,

**1 Esdras 2:3**

Thus says Cyrus, king of Persians: The Lord of Israel, the Lord Most High, appointed me king of the inhabited world.

**1 Esdras 2:4**

And he signified to me to build him a house in Jerusalem, the one in Judea.

**1 Esdras 2:5**

If therefore any of you is from his nation, let his Lord be with him, and having gone up to Jerusalem in Judea, let him build the house of the Lord of Israel; this is the Lord who encamped in Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 2:6**

Therefore as many as dwell in their places, let those in his place help him with gold and with silver, with gifts, with horses and cattle, together with the other things added according to vows for the temple of the Lord in Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 2:7**

And the chief tribes of the paternal houses of Judah and Benjamin, and the priests and the Levites, and all whose spirit the Lord raised to go up to build the house for the Lord in Jerusalem, stood up,

**1 Esdras 2:8**

and those around them helped in everything, with silver and gold, horses, cattle, and very many vowed offerings of many whose mind was raised.

**1 Esdras 2:9**

And King Cyrus brought out the holy vessels of the Lord that Nebuchadnezzar had transferred from Jerusalem and had set aside in his idol temple.

**1 Esdras 2:10**

And Cyrus, king of Persians, brought them out and handed them over to Mithradates his treasurer.

**1 Esdras 2:11**

And through him they were handed over to Samanassar, governor of Judea.

**1 Esdras 2:12**

And this was the number of these: one thousand golden libation bowls, one thousand silver libation bowls, twenty-nine silver censers, thirty golden cups, two thousand four hundred ten silver cups, and one thousand other vessels.

**1 Esdras 2:13**

And all the vessels carried through, gold and silver, were five thousand four hundred sixty-nine.

**1 Esdras 2:14**

And they were brought up by Samanassar, together with those from the captivity, from Babylon into Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 2:15**

Now in the times of Artaxerxes king of the Persians, Belemus and Mithradates and Tabellius and Rathymus and Beeltethmus and Samellius the scribe, and the rest arranged with them, dwelling in Samaria and the other places, wrote against those dwelling in Judea and Jerusalem this written-under letter to him:

**1 Esdras 2:16**

To King Artaxerxes, lord, your servants Rathymus the recorder of matters, and Samellius the scribe, and the remaining ones of their council, and the judges in Coele-Syria and Phoenicia:

**1 Esdras 2:17**

And now let it be known to the lord king that the Jews who went up from you to us, having come into Jerusalem, are building the rebellious and evil city, repairing both its marketplaces and walls, and laying a foundation for a temple.

**1 Esdras 2:18**

Therefore if this city is built and the walls completed, they will certainly not endure giving taxation, but will also resist kings. And since the things concerning the temple are being worked, we suppose it good not to overlook such a thing, but to address the lord king, so that, if it appears good to you, search may be made in the books from your fathers.

**1 Esdras 2:19**

And you will find in the records written concerning these things, and you will know that that city was rebellious and troubling kings and cities, and that the Jews were rebels and establishing sieges in it from of old; for this reason also this city was made desolate.

**1 Esdras 2:20**

Now therefore we disclose to you, lord king, that if this city is built and its walls are raised up, there will no longer be a descent for you into Coele-Syria and Phoenicia.

**1 Esdras 2:21**

Then the king wrote back to Rathymus, the one writing the matters, and to Beeltethmus and Samellius the scribe and to the rest arranged with them and dwelling in Samaria and Syria and Phoenicia the things written below:

**1 Esdras 2:22**

I have read the letter that you sent to me. Therefore I commanded search to be made, and it was found that that city is from of old one that sets itself against kings,

**1 Esdras 2:23**

and the men in it were completing revolts and wars, and strong and hard kings were in Jerusalem, ruling and levying taxes on Coele-Syria and Phoenicia.

**1 Esdras 2:24**

Now therefore I commanded those men to be hindered from building the city, and for care to be taken that nothing beyond these things happen, and that the things of evil not proceed further to trouble kings.

**1 Esdras 2:25**

Then, when the writings from King Artaxerxes had been read, Rathymus and Samellius the scribe and those arranged with them set out to Jerusalem in haste with horse and a crowd in battle array, and began to hinder those building. And the building of the temple in Jerusalem was idle until the second year of the reign of Darius king of the Persians.

### Chapter 3

**1 Esdras 3:1**

And King Darius made a great reception for all those under him and all those born in his house and all the great men of Media and Persia,

**1 Esdras 3:2**

and for all the satraps and generals and toparchs under him, from India as far as Ethiopia, in the one hundred twenty-seven satrapies.

**1 Esdras 3:3**

And they ate and drank and, having been filled, departed. But King Darius departed into his own bedroom and slept and became awake.

**1 Esdras 3:4**

Then the three young men, the bodyguards who guarded the body of the king, said one to another,

**1 Esdras 3:5**

Let each of us speak one word that will prevail, and whoever's saying appears wiser than the other's, King Darius will give him great gifts and great victory prizes:

**1 Esdras 3:6**

to be clothed in purple, to drink in golden vessels, to sleep upon gold, a chariot with a golden bridle, a linen turban, and a collar around the neck.

**1 Esdras 3:7**

And he shall sit second to Darius because of his wisdom, and he shall be called kinsman of Darius.

**1 Esdras 3:8**

And then each wrote his own word, sealed it, and placed it under the pillow of King Darius, and they said,

**1 Esdras 3:9**

When the king rises, they will give him the writing; and whoever the king and the three great men of Persia judge that his word is wiser, to him the victory will be given, just as it is written.

**1 Esdras 3:10**

The first wrote, Wine prevails.

**1 Esdras 3:11**

The other wrote, The king prevails.

**1 Esdras 3:12**

The third wrote, Women prevail, but over all things truth conquers.

**1 Esdras 3:13**

And when the king was awakened, they took the writing and gave it to him, and he read it.

**1 Esdras 3:14**

And having sent out, he called all the great men of Persia and Media and the satraps and generals and toparchs and consuls, and he sat in the council hall, and the writing was read before them.

**1 Esdras 3:15**

And he said, Call the young men, and they will make clear their words. And they were called and entered.

**1 Esdras 3:16**

And they said to them, Report to us concerning the things written.

**1 Esdras 3:17**

And the first, the one who had spoken concerning the strength of wine, began and said thus: Men, how does wine prevail? It leads astray the mind of all humans who drink it.

**1 Esdras 3:18**

It makes the mind of the king and the orphan one, and that of the household servant and the free person, and that of the poor person and the rich person.

**1 Esdras 3:19**

And it turns every mind into feasting and gladness, and it does not remember any grief or any debt.

**1 Esdras 3:20**

And it makes all hearts rich, and it does not remember king or satrap, and it makes everyone speak in talents.

**1 Esdras 3:21**

And when they drink, they do not remember being friendly with friends and brothers, and after not long they draw swords.

**1 Esdras 3:22**

And when they rise up from the wine, they do not remember what they did.

**1 Esdras 3:23**

Men, does not wine prevail, because it compels them to do thus? And having spoken thus, he was silent.

### Chapter 4

**1 Esdras 4:1**

And the second, the one who had spoken concerning the strength of the king, began to speak:

**1 Esdras 4:2**

Men, do not humans prevail, holding down the earth and the sea and all things in them?

**1 Esdras 4:3**

But the king prevails and rules over them and masters them, and whatever he says to them, they obey.

**1 Esdras 4:4**

If he tells them to make war one against another, they do it. And if he sends them out against the enemies, they go and work down the mountains and the walls and the towers.

**1 Esdras 4:5**

They murder and are murdered, and they do not transgress the word of the king. And if they conquer, they bring everything to the king, whatever they plunder and all the other things.

**1 Esdras 4:6**

And as many as do not serve as soldiers or make war, but farm the land, again when they sow and reap, they bring to the king; and one compelling another, they bring the taxes to the king.

**1 Esdras 4:7**

And he himself is one alone. If he says to kill, they kill; if he says to release, they release.

**1 Esdras 4:8**

He said to strike, they strike; he said to make desolate, they make desolate; he said to build, they build.

**1 Esdras 4:9**

He said to cut out, they cut out; he said to plant, they plant.

**1 Esdras 4:10**

And all his people and his forces obey. And besides these things, he reclines, eats and drinks and sleeps,

**1 Esdras 4:11**

but they keep watch around him, and each one is not able to go away and do his own works, nor do they disobey him.

**1 Esdras 4:12**

Men, how does the king not prevail, because he is obeyed thus? And he was silent.

**1 Esdras 4:13**

But the third, the one who had spoken concerning women and truth, this is Zerubbabel, began to speak: Men,

**1 Esdras 4:14**

is not the king great, and are not humans many, and does not wine have strength? Who then is the master over them, or who rules over them? Is it not women?

**1 Esdras 4:15**

Women gave birth to the king and to all the people who rule the sea and the earth.

**1 Esdras 4:16**

And from them they came to be, and these women nourished the ones who planted the vineyards from which wine comes to be.

**1 Esdras 4:17**

And these make the robes of humans, and these make glory for humans, and humans are not able to be without women.

**1 Esdras 4:18**

And if they gather gold and silver and every beautiful thing and see one woman beautiful in appearance and beauty,

**1 Esdras 4:19**

leaving all these things, they gape at her, and with open mouth they gaze at her, and all choose her rather than gold and silver and every beautiful thing.

**1 Esdras 4:20**

A human leaves behind his own father, who nourished him, and his own country, and is joined to his own wife,

**1 Esdras 4:21**

and with his wife he leaves his soul and remembers neither father nor mother nor country.

**1 Esdras 4:22**

And from this you must know that women rule over you. Do you not labor and toil and give and bring everything to women?

**1 Esdras 4:23**

And a human takes his sword and goes out to make expeditions and rob and steal and to sail into the sea and rivers.

**1 Esdras 4:24**

And he sees a lion and walks in darkness, and when he steals and seizes and strips, he carries it back to the woman he loves.

**1 Esdras 4:25**

And a human loves his own wife more than father and mother.

**1 Esdras 4:26**

And many became senseless in their own minds because of women and became slaves because of them,

**1 Esdras 4:27**

and many perished and stumbled and sinned because of women.

**1 Esdras 4:28**

And now do you not trust me? Is not the king great in his authority? Do not all countries fear to touch him?

**1 Esdras 4:29**

I was watching him and Apame, daughter of Bartacus the admirable, the concubine of the king, sitting at the right of the king,

**1 Esdras 4:30**

and taking the diadem from the head of the king and placing it on herself, and she slapped the king with her left hand.

**1 Esdras 4:31**

And besides these things, the king, with open mouth, was gazing at her. And if she smiles at him, he laughs; but if she becomes bitter toward him, he flatters her, so that she may be reconciled to him.

**1 Esdras 4:32**

Men, how are women not strong, since they act thus?

**1 Esdras 4:33**

And then the king and the great men were looking at one another. And he began to speak concerning truth.

**1 Esdras 4:34**

Men, are not women strong? The earth is great, and Heaven is high, and the sun is swift in its course, because it turns in the circle of Heaven and again runs back to its own place in one day.

**1 Esdras 4:35**

Is not the one who does these things great? And truth is great and stronger than all things.

**1 Esdras 4:36**

All the earth calls upon truth, and Heaven blesses it, and all works shake and tremble, and there is no unjust thing with it.

**1 Esdras 4:37**

Wine is unjust, the king is unjust, women are unjust, all the sons of humans are unjust, and all their works like these are unjust; and there is no truth in them, and in their injustice they will perish.

**1 Esdras 4:38**

But truth remains and is strong forever, and lives and rules forever and ever.

**1 Esdras 4:39**

And with her there is no receiving faces, nor distinctions, but she does righteous things away from all unrighteous and evil things; and all are pleased with her works, and in her judgment there is nothing unjust.

**1 Esdras 4:40**

And hers is the strength and the kingdom and the authority and the greatness of all the ages. Blessed is the God of truth.

**1 Esdras 4:41**

And he stopped speaking. And all the people then shouted, and then they said, Great is truth, and it prevails.

**1 Esdras 4:42**

Then the king said to him, Ask what you wish, more than the things written, and we will give it to you, since you were found wiser, and you shall sit next to me and be called my kinsman.

**1 Esdras 4:43**

Then he said to the king, Remember the vow that you vowed, to build Jerusalem on the day when you received your kingdom,

**1 Esdras 4:44**

and to send out all the vessels taken from Jerusalem, which Cyrus separated when he vowed to cut off Babylon and vowed to send them there.

**1 Esdras 4:45**

And you vowed to build the temple that the Idumeans burned when Judea was made desolate by the Chaldeans.

**1 Esdras 4:46**

And now this is what I claim from you, lord king, and what I ask of you, and this is the greatness that is from you. Therefore I beg that you do the vow that you vowed to the King of Heaven to do from your mouth.

**1 Esdras 4:47**

Then King Darius rose and kissed him and wrote letters for him to all the stewards and toparchs and generals and satraps, that they should send him forward, and all those with him who were going up to build Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 4:48**

And to all the toparchs in Coele-Syria and Phoenicia and to those in Lebanon he wrote letters to transfer cedar wood from Lebanon to Jerusalem, and that they should build the city with him.

**1 Esdras 4:49**

And he wrote to all the Jews going up from the kingdom into Judea concerning freedom: no powerful one or toparch or satrap or steward was to come to their doors,

**1 Esdras 4:50**

and all the country that they hold was to be tax-free for them, and that the Idumeans should release the villages of the Jews that they were holding down,

**1 Esdras 4:51**

and that twenty talents be given yearly for the building of the temple until it should be built,

**1 Esdras 4:52**

and for whole-burnt offerings to be offered upon the altar day by day, just as they have command to offer seventeen, another ten talents yearly,

**1 Esdras 4:53**

and for all those coming up from Babylonia to build the city, freedom was to belong to them, both to them and to their descendants, and to all the priests who were coming up.

**1 Esdras 4:54**

And he also wrote the provision and the priestly robe in which they serve in it.

**1 Esdras 4:55**

And for the Levites he wrote to give the provision until the day when the house should be completed and Jerusalem should be built,

**1 Esdras 4:56**

and for all those guarding the city, he wrote to give them allotments and wages.

**1 Esdras 4:57**

And he sent out all the vessels that Cyrus had separated from Babylon. And everything that Cyrus said to do, he also commanded to be done and sent out to Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 4:58**

And when the young man went out, having lifted his face toward Heaven opposite Jerusalem, he blessed the King of Heaven, saying,

**1 Esdras 4:59**

From you is victory, and from you is wisdom, and yours is the glory, and I am your household servant.

**1 Esdras 4:60**

Blessed are you, who gave me wisdom, and to you I confess, Master of the fathers.

**1 Esdras 4:61**

And he took the letters and went out and came into Babylon and reported to all his brothers.

**1 Esdras 4:62**

And they blessed the God of their fathers, because he had given them relief and release

**1 Esdras 4:63**

to go up and build Jerusalem and the temple, where his name had been named upon it. And they feasted with music and joy seven days.

### Chapter 5

**1 Esdras 5:1**

And after these things the leaders of the house of the paternal houses were chosen to go up according to their tribes, and their wives and their sons and daughters and their male servants and female servants and their cattle.

**1 Esdras 5:2**

And Darius sent with them 1,000 horsemen, until he restored them to Jerusalem with peace and with music, drums, and flutes.

**1 Esdras 5:3**

And all their brothers were making music, and he made them go up together with those.

**1 Esdras 5:4**

And these are the names of the men going up, according to their paternal houses, into their tribes, by their division.

**1 Esdras 5:5**

The priests, sons of Phinehas son of Aaron: Jesus son of Josedek, son of Saraios, and Joakim son of Zerubbabel son of Salathiel, from the house of David, from the generation of Phares, and from the tribe of Judah,

**1 Esdras 5:6**

who spoke wise words before Darius king of Persians in the second year of his reign, in the month Nisan, the first month.

**1 Esdras 5:7**

And these are those from Judea who went up from the captivity of the sojourn, whom Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon had resettled into Babylon,

**1 Esdras 5:8**

and who returned to Jerusalem and the rest of Judea, each into his own city, those who came with Zerubbabel and Jesus: Nehemiah, Zaraios, Resaios, Enenios, Mordecai, Beelsaros, Aspharasos, Reelios, Roimos, Baana, their leaders.

**1 Esdras 5:9**

The number of those from the nation and their leaders: sons of Phoros, 2,172; sons of Saphat, 472.

**1 Esdras 5:10**

Sons of Ares, 756.

**1 Esdras 5:11**

Sons of Phaath Moab, among the sons of Jesus and Joab, 2,812.

**1 Esdras 5:12**

Sons of Elam, 1,254; sons of Zathoui, 975; sons of Chorbe, 705; sons of Bani, 648.

**1 Esdras 5:13**

Sons of Bebai, 633; sons of Argai, 1,322.

**1 Esdras 5:14**

Sons of Adonikan, 637; sons of Bagoi, 2,606; sons of Adinou, 454.

**1 Esdras 5:15**

Sons of Ater of Ezekias, 92; sons of Kilan and Azenan, 67; sons of Azarou, 432.

**1 Esdras 5:16**

Sons of Annis, 101; sons of Arom, 32; sons of Basai, 323; sons of Arsiphourith, 102.

**1 Esdras 5:17**

Sons of Baiterous, 3,005; sons from Baithlomon, 123.

**1 Esdras 5:18**

Those from Netophas, 55; those from Anathoth, 158; those from Baithasmon, 42.

**1 Esdras 5:19**

Those from Kariathiri, 25; those from Kapheira and Berog, 743.

**1 Esdras 5:20**

The Chadiasai and Ammidioi, 422; those from Kirama and Gabbe, 621.

**1 Esdras 5:21**

Those from Makalon, 122; those from Betolion, 52; sons of Niphis, 156.

**1 Esdras 5:22**

Sons of Kalamolalos and Onous, 725; sons of Jerechou, 245.

**1 Esdras 5:23**

Sons of Sanaas, 3,301.

**1 Esdras 5:24**

The priests: sons of Jeddou son of Jesus, among the sons of Sanasib, 872; sons of Emmerouth, 252.

**1 Esdras 5:25**

Sons of Phassourou, 1,047; sons of Charmi, 217.

**1 Esdras 5:26**

The Levites: the sons of Jesus and Kadmiel and Bannou and Soudios, 74.

**1 Esdras 5:27**

The temple singers: sons of Asaph, 128.

**1 Esdras 5:28**

The gatekeepers: sons of Saloum, sons of Atar, sons of Tolman, sons of Dakoub, sons of Ateta, sons of Tobis, all 139.

**1 Esdras 5:29**

The temple servants: sons of Esau, sons of Asipha, sons of Tabaoth, sons of Keras, sons of Souda, sons of Phalaiou, sons of Labana, sons of Agraba,

**1 Esdras 5:30**

sons of Akoud, sons of Outa, sons of Ketab, sons of Akkaba, sons of Sybai, sons of Anan, sons of Kathoua, sons of Geddour,

**1 Esdras 5:31**

sons of Jairou, sons of Daisan, sons of Noeba, sons of Chaseba, sons of Kazera, sons of Oziou, sons of Phinoe, sons of Asara, sons of Basthai, sons of Assana, sons of Mani, sons of Naphisi, sons of Akouph, sons of Achiba, sons of Asoub, sons of Pharacem, sons of Basalem,

**1 Esdras 5:32**

sons of Meedda, sons of Koutha, sons of Charea, sons of Barchoue, sons of Serar, sons of Thomoi, sons of Nasi, sons of Atepha.

**1 Esdras 5:33**

Sons of the servants of Solomon: sons of Assaphioth, sons of Pharira, sons of Jeieli, sons of Lozon, sons of Israel, sons of Saphoui,

**1 Esdras 5:34**

sons of Agia, sons of Phachareth, sons of Sabie, sons of Sarothi, sons of Misaias, sons of Tas, sons of Addous, sons of Souba, sons of Apherra, sons of Barodis, sons of Saphag, sons of Allon.

**1 Esdras 5:35**

All the temple servants and the sons of the servants of Solomon were 372.

**1 Esdras 5:36**

These went up from Thermeleth and Thelersas, Charaathalan and Aalar leading them.

**1 Esdras 5:37**

And they were not able to report their paternal houses and generations, whether they were from Israel: sons of Dalan son of Baenan, sons of Nekodan, 652.

**1 Esdras 5:38**

And from the priests, those claiming priesthood and not found: sons of Obdia, sons of Akbos, sons of Jaddou, who took Augia as wife from the daughters of Phaezeldaios and was called by his name.

**1 Esdras 5:39**

And when the genealogical writing of these was sought in the enrollment and was not found, they were separated from serving as priests.

**1 Esdras 5:40**

And Nehemiah and Attharias told them not to share in the holy things until a high priest should arise clothed with the Disclosure and the Truth.

**1 Esdras 5:41**

And all Israel, from twelve years old and upward, apart from male servants and female servants, were 42,360; their male servants and female servants were 7,337; singers and singing women, 245.

**1 Esdras 5:42**

Camels, 435; horses, 7,036; mules, 245; pack animals, 5,525.

**1 Esdras 5:43**

And some of the leaders according to the paternal houses, when they came to the temple of God in Jerusalem, vowed to raise the house on its place according to their ability

**1 Esdras 5:44**

and to give into the temple treasury for the works 1,000 minas of gold and 5,000 minas of silver and 100 priestly robes.

**1 Esdras 5:45**

And the priests and the Levites and those from his people were settled in Jerusalem and the country, both the temple singers and the gatekeepers and all Israel in their villages.

**1 Esdras 5:46**

And when the seventh month was standing and the sons of Israel were each in their own places, they were gathered with one accord into the broad place of the first gate toward the east.

**1 Esdras 5:47**

And Jesus son of Josedek stood up, and his brothers the priests, and Zerubbabel son of Salathiel and his brothers, and they prepared the altar of the God of Israel,

**1 Esdras 5:48**

to offer whole-burnt offerings upon it, according to the things declared in the book of Moses, the man of God.

**1 Esdras 5:49**

And some from the other nations of the land were gathered against them, and they set the altar upright upon their place, because they were in hostility with them. And all the nations on the land prevailed against them, and they offered sacrifices according to the time, and whole-burnt offerings to the Lord, morning and evening,

**1 Esdras 5:50**

and they kept the feast of tabernacle-pitching, as it has been commanded in the law, and sacrifices day by day, as was proper,

**1 Esdras 5:51**

and after these things continual offerings and sacrifices of Sabbaths and new moons and all sanctified feasts.

**1 Esdras 5:52**

And as many as vowed a vow to God, from the new moon of the seventh month they began to offer sacrifices to God, and the temple of God had not yet been built.

**1 Esdras 5:53**

And they gave silver to the stonecutters and craftsmen, and drinks and foods and carts to the Sidonians and Tyrians, for them to bring cedar wood from Lebanon and carry rafts to the harbor of Joppa, according to the commandment written for them by Cyrus king of the Persians.

**1 Esdras 5:54**

And in the second year, coming to the temple of God in Jerusalem, in the second month, Zerubbabel son of Salathiel and Jesus son of Josedek and their brothers began, and the priests, the Levites, and all those who had come from the captivity into Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 5:55**

And they laid the foundation of the temple of God at the new moon of the second month, in the second year, after coming into Judea and Jerusalem.

**1 Esdras 5:56**

And they set the Levites from twenty years old over the works of the Lord. And Jesus stood, and his sons and brothers, and Kadmiel the brother, and the sons of Emadaboun, and the sons of Joda son of Eliadoud with their sons and brothers, all the Levites, with one accord as work supervisors for the works in the house of the Lord. And the builders built the temple of the Lord.

**1 Esdras 5:57**

And the priests stood clothed, with musical instruments and trumpets, and the Levites, sons of Asaph, having the cymbals, hymning the Lord and blessing according to David king of Israel.

**1 Esdras 5:58**

And they called out with hymns, blessing the Lord, because his kindness and his glory are forever in all Israel.

**1 Esdras 5:59**

And all the people sounded trumpets and shouted with a great voice, hymning the Lord at the raising of the house of the Lord.

**1 Esdras 5:60**

And there came from the priests, the Levites, and the leaders according to their paternal houses, the elders who had seen the former house, to the building of this one, with weeping and a great cry,

**1 Esdras 5:61**

and many with trumpets and joy with a great voice,

**1 Esdras 5:62**

so that the people could not hear the trumpets because of the weeping of the people, for the crowd was sounding trumpets greatly, so that it was heard from far away.

**1 Esdras 5:63**

And when the enemies of the tribe of Judah and Benjamin heard, they came to recognize what the sound of the trumpets was.

**1 Esdras 5:64**

And they recognized that those from the captivity were building the temple for the Lord God of Israel.

**1 Esdras 5:65**

And coming to Zerubbabel and Jesus and the leaders of the paternal houses, they said to them, Let us build together with you.

**1 Esdras 5:66**

For like you, we listen to your Lord and sacrifice to him from the days of Asbasareth king of Assyrians, who transferred us here.

**1 Esdras 5:67**

And Zerubbabel and Jesus and the leaders of the paternal houses of Israel said to them, It is not for us and you to build the house for the Lord our God.

**1 Esdras 5:68**

For we alone will build to the Lord of Israel according to the things Cyrus king of the Persians commanded us.

**1 Esdras 5:69**

But the nations of the land, lying upon those in Judea and besieging them, hindered them from building.

**1 Esdras 5:70**

And by stirring up counsels and making confederacies, they prevented the building from being completed all the time of the life of King Cyrus. And they were hindered from building for two years, until the reign of Darius.

### Chapter 6

**1 Esdras 6:1**

And in the second year of the reign of Darius, Haggai and Zechariah son of Addo, the prophets, prophesied to the Jews who were in Judea and Jerusalem, upon them in the name of the Lord God of Israel.

**1 Esdras 6:2**

Then Zerubbabel son of Salathiel stood up, and Jesus son of Josedek, and they began to build the house of the Lord in Jerusalem, the prophets of the Lord being with them and helping them.

**1 Esdras 6:3**

At that same time Sisinēs, the governor of Syria and Phoenicia, came to them, and Sathrabouzanes and the fellow-companions, and they said to them,

**1 Esdras 6:4**

Who ordered you to build this house and complete this roof and all the other things? And who are the builders completing these things?

**1 Esdras 6:5**

And the elders of the Jews had favor, a visitation having come upon the captivity from the Lord,

**1 Esdras 6:6**

and they were not hindered from the building until Darius should be notified concerning them and addressed.

**1 Esdras 6:7**

Copy of a letter that they wrote to Darius and sent: Sisinēs, governor of Syria and Phoenicia, and Sathrabouzanes and the fellow-companions, the leaders in Syria and Phoenicia, to King Darius, greeting.

**1 Esdras 6:8**
