And since many times, when our fathers were afflicted, you helped them in their humiliation and rescued them out of great dangers,

**3 Maccabees 2:13**

behold now, holy King, because of our many and great sins we are being worn down and have been subjected to our enemies and are slack in weaknesses.

**3 Maccabees 2:14**

But in our downfall, this insolent and profane one is attempting to insult the holy place appointed upon the earth by the name of your glory.

**3 Maccabees 2:15**

For your dwelling place, heaven of heaven, is unreachable by humans.

**3 Maccabees 2:16**

But since, being pleased with your glory in your people Israel, you sanctified this place,

**3 Maccabees 2:17**

do not punish us by the uncleanness of these, nor correct us by profanation, lest the lawless boast in their wrath, nor exult in the pride of their tongue, saying,

**3 Maccabees 2:18**

We trampled the house of sanctification, as the houses of abominations are trampled.

**3 Maccabees 2:19**

Wipe away our sins and scatter our errors, and make your mercy appear at this hour.

**3 Maccabees 2:20**

May your compassions quickly come before us, and give praises in the mouth of those cast down and crushed in souls, making peace for us.

**3 Maccabees 2:21**

Here the God who watches over all things, and before all is holy in holies, having heard the lawful supplication, struck him who was greatly lifted up with insult and insolence.

**3 Maccabees 2:22**

Shaking him this way and that like a reed by wind, so that he was on the ground useless, and also paralyzed in his limbs and unable even to speak, entangled by righteous judgment.

**3 Maccabees 2:23**

Therefore his friends and bodyguards, seeing the swift and sharp correction that overtook him, fearing lest even his life fail, quickly dragged him out, being struck with overwhelming fear.

**3 Maccabees 2:24**

But later, after he gathered himself, he came in no way to repentance though rebuked, but departed with bitter threat.

**3 Maccabees 2:25**

And being brought into Egypt and increasing the things of evil, through the previously designated drinking companions and associates, who were separated from all justice,

**3 Maccabees 2:26**

he was not only satisfied with countless wanton acts, but also went forward to such insolence that blasphemies were set up in the places, and many of the friends, looking closely to the king's purpose, themselves also followed his will.

**3 Maccabees 2:27**

And he purposed publicly to spread blame against the nation; and on the tower belonging to the court, he set up a pillar and engraved a writing,

**3 Maccabees 2:28**

that none of those not sacrificing should enter into their sacred places, and that all Jews should be brought into registration and servile condition, and that those speaking against it, being carried by force, should be removed from life,

**3 Maccabees 2:29**

and that those registered should be branded by fire on the body with the ivy-leaf emblem of Dionysus, and that they should be registered into the previously arranged restricted status.

**3 Maccabees 2:30**

But so that he might not appear hateful to all, he wrote beneath: But if any of them choose to conduct themselves among those initiated according to the rites, these shall be equal citizens with the Alexandrians.

**3 Maccabees 2:31**

Therefore some, hating the city's foundations of piety because of the city, easily gave themselves up, as if they would share in some great good fame from their future association with the king.

**3 Maccabees 2:32**

But most strengthened themselves with a noble soul and did not depart from piety, and, exchanging their money for life, they tried fearlessly to rescue themselves from the registrations.

**3 Maccabees 2:33**

And they were set in good hope that they would obtain help; and those withdrawing from them they abhorred and judged as enemies of the nation and deprived them of common association and usefulness.

### Chapter 3

**3 Maccabees 3:1**

And receiving these things, the impious one was so greatly enraged that he was not only angry with those in Alexandria, but also more heavily opposed those in the country, and ordered that they hurry to gather all into the same place and remove them from life by the worst fate.

**3 Maccabees 3:2**

And while these things were being arranged, a hostile report was sounding out against the race among humans of one mind for doing evil, occasion being given for disposition, as though they were hindered by their lawful customs.

**3 Maccabees 3:3**

But the Jews were preserving unturned their goodwill and faith toward the kings,

**3 Maccabees 3:4**

and, reverencing God and living by his law, they made a separation concerning foods; for this reason they appeared hateful to some.

**3 Maccabees 3:5**

But adorning their common conduct with the good practice of the righteous, they had been established as approved among all humans.

**3 Maccabees 3:6**

Therefore the foreigners in no way reckoned the good practice concerning the race that was being talked of among all,

**3 Maccabees 3:7**

but they kept talking of the difference concerning acts of worship and foods, saying that these humans had become companions in libation neither with the king nor with the forces, but were hostile and opposing matters in some great way; and they attached no ordinary blame.

**3 Maccabees 3:8**

But the Greeks in the city, having been wronged in nothing, seeing an unexpected disturbance around the people and unobstructed rushes coming to be, did not have strength to help, for the disposition was tyrannical; but they encouraged them and were distressed and supposed that these things would change.

**3 Maccabees 3:9**

For such a great body, ignorant of nothing, would not be overlooked in this way.

**3 Maccabees 3:10**

And already some neighbors and friends and fellow-workers, secretly drawing some aside, were giving pledges to defend with them and to bring every earnest effort for help.

**3 Maccabees 3:11**

Therefore that one, made arrogant by his present prosperity and not seeing the might of the greatest God, but supposing that he would remain continually in the same counsel, wrote this letter against them:

**3 Maccabees 3:12**

King Ptolemy Philopator, to the generals and soldiers throughout Egypt and in every place, greeting and good health.

**3 Maccabees 3:13**

And I myself am in good health, and so are our affairs.

**3 Maccabees 3:14**

Since the campaign into Asia that came to be for us, which you yourselves know, was brought to the best end according to reason by the unfailing alliance of the gods toward us and by our own might,

**3 Maccabees 3:15**

we considered that we should nurture the nations inhabiting Coele-Syria and Phoenicia not by force of spear, but by fairness and much kindness to humanity, and gladly do them good.

**3 Maccabees 3:16**

And having assigned very many revenues to the temples in the cities, we were brought on also, going up to Jerusalem, to honor the temple of the accursed ones who never cease from folly.

**3 Maccabees 3:17**

But they received our presence in word, yet falsely in deed, when we were eager to enter into their shrine and honor it with outstanding and most beautiful dedications.

**3 Maccabees 3:18**

Carried by older conceits, they barred us from the entrance, falling short of our might because of the kindness to humanity that we have toward all humans.

**3 Maccabees 3:19**

And making evident their hostility toward us, as the only ones of the nations stiff-necked toward kings and their own benefactors, they wish to bear nothing genuine.

**3 Maccabees 3:20**

But we, having dealt with their folly and having been brought through with victory, and having met all nations in Egypt kindly as was proper, did what was fitting.

**3 Maccabees 3:21**

Among these things, making known to all our forgetfulness of wrong toward their fellow tribesmen, because of the alliance and the countless affairs entrusted to them from the beginning with simplicity, we dared to change matters and wished to deem them worthy also of Alexandrian citizenship and to make them participants in the perpetual sacred rites.

**3 Maccabees 3:22**

But they, receiving the opposite and pushing away the good with their inborn evil character, and continually inclining toward the worthless,

**3 Maccabees 3:23**

not only turned away the priceless citizenship, but also abhor in word and silence the few among them who are genuinely disposed toward us, suspecting at every point that through their most infamous way of life they will quickly overturn our successes.

**3 Maccabees 3:24**

Therefore, being well persuaded by proofs that these people are in every way ill-minded toward us, and taking forethought lest ever, if sudden disorder later arises for us, we should have these impious people behind our back as traitors and barbarian enemies,

**3 Maccabees 3:25**

we have commanded that as soon as this letter arrives, those dwelling there be sent to us immediately with women and children, with insults and abuses, enclosed on every side in iron bonds, for incurable and infamous murder fitting for enemies.

**3 Maccabees 3:26**

For when these are punished together, we have understood that for the remaining time our affairs will be established completely in stability and the best disposition.

**3 Maccabees 3:27**

Whoever shelters any of the Jews, from elder to infant to those at the breast, shall be beaten to death with most shameful tortures, with his whole household.

**3 Maccabees 3:28**

And whoever wishes may inform, on condition that he shall receive the property of the one falling under correction, and 2,000 drachmas from the royal silver, and shall obtain freedom and be crowned.

**3 Maccabees 3:29**

And every place where a Jew is found being sheltered at all shall become impassable and burned with fire, and shall appear entirely useless to every mortal nature for all time.

**3 Maccabees 3:30**

And the form of the letter had been written thus.

### Chapter 4

**3 Maccabees 4:1**

And everywhere, wherever this decree arrived, a public feast was organized among the nations, with shoutings and joy, as the hatred long before hardened in their mind against them was now being displayed openly with boldness.

**3 Maccabees 4:2**

But for the Jews there was incurable grief and all-lamenting cry with tears, their heart on every side burning with groans, as they bewailed the unexpected destruction suddenly adjudged against them.

**3 Maccabees 4:3**

What nome or city, or what inhabited place altogether, or what streets, were not filled with beating and groanings over them?

**3 Maccabees 4:4**

For with bitterness of a merciless soul they were sent out together by the commanders in each city, so that at the extraordinary punishments even some of the enemies, taking common pity before their eyes and considering the uncertain overthrow of life, wept over their thrice-wretched deportation.

**3 Maccabees 4:5**

For a multitude of aged women covered with gray hair was led, bent forward by the foot-slowness that comes from old age, driven by a violent impulse of overturning into a sharp march without any regard for shame.

**3 Maccabees 4:6**

And the young women who had just entered the bridal chamber for fellowship of life, receiving wailings instead of delight and sprinkling their myrrh-wet hair with dust, were led unveiled, and all together began a dirge instead of wedding songs, as ones torn by foreign outrages.

**3 Maccabees 4:7**

And as public prisoners they were dragged with violence until the boarding into the ship.

**3 Maccabees 4:8**

And their husbands, having their necks wrapped with nooses instead of crowns, in the prime of youthful age, instead of feasting and youthful ease, passed the remaining days of the wedding in lamentations, seeing Hades already lying at their feet.

**3 Maccabees 4:9**

And they were brought down, being led like beasts by iron-bound constraints, some with their necks fastened to the ship benches, and others with their feet secured by unbreakable fetters,

**3 Maccabees 4:10**

and also with the dense planking arranged above, so that, with their eyes darkened on every side, they might receive the transport of plotters through the whole voyage.

**3 Maccabees 4:11**

And when these had been brought to the place called Schedia and the sailing alongside had been completed, just as it had been decreed by the king, he ordered them to encamp in the hippodrome before the city, which had a boundless perimeter and was exceedingly suitable for making an example to all those going down into the city and to those being sent out from it into the country for travel, so that they might neither have fellowship with his forces nor be counted worthy of enclosures at all.

**3 Maccabees 4:12**

And when this happened, after he heard that their fellow-countrymen from the city were secretly going out more often to lament the inglorious misery of their brothers,

**3 Maccabees 4:13**

he was enraged and ordered that the same thing also be done carefully to these together as to those, in no way falling short of their punishment,

**3 Maccabees 4:14**

and that the whole race be registered by name, not for the laborious service of works previously declared a little before, but so that, after being tortured with the ordered abuses, they might at last be made to vanish within the time of a single day.

**3 Maccabees 4:15**

So their registration was being made with bitter zeal and ambitious attendance from rising of sun until setting, taking its end unaccomplished for forty days. [16] And the king, greatly and continually filled with joy, held banquets before all the idols; with a mind led far astray from the truth and with a profane mouth, he praised the mute things that could not speak to them or help, and against the greatest God he spoke things not fitting.

**3 Maccabees 4:17**

And after the aforesaid interval of time the scribes reported to the king that they no longer had strength to make the registration of the Jews because of their immeasurable multitude,

**3 Maccabees 4:18**

though the greater number were still in the country, some still gathered in houses, and others also by place, so that it had become impossible for all the commanders throughout Egypt.

**3 Maccabees 4:19**

And when he threatened them more harshly, as if they had been bribed for a device of escape, it happened that he was plainly persuaded about this,

**3 Maccabees 4:20**

when they said with proof that even the paper supply and the writing reeds that they were using had already run out.

**3 Maccabees 4:21**

And this was an operation of the invincible providence from heaven of the One helping the Jews.

### Chapter 5

**3 Maccabees 5:1**

Then, having summoned Hermon, the one set over the care of the elephants, being filled with heavy anger and bile, unchangeable in every way,

**3 Maccabees 5:2**

he commanded that by the coming day all the elephants, 500 in number, be given to drink with generous handfuls of frankincense and much unmixed wine, and that, after being made savage by the abundant supply of drink, they be brought in to meet the doom of the Jews.

**3 Maccabees 5:3**

And the one giving these orders turned to the feast, having gathered those of his friends and of the army who were especially hateful toward the Jews.

**3 Maccabees 5:4**

And Hermon the elephantarch steadfastly completed the thing ordered.

**3 Maccabees 5:5**

And the ministers assigned to these things went out in the evening and bound the hands of the wretched ones, and devised the remaining security around them, supposing that by night the race would receive the end of destruction together.

**3 Maccabees 5:6**

But the Jews, seeming to the nations to be bereft of every shelter because of the constraint with bonds enclosing them on every side,

**3 Maccabees 5:7**

all with tears called upon the Almighty Lord, the ruler of every power, their merciful God and Father, with a cry hard to stop, begging

**3 Maccabees 5:8**

him to turn back the unholy counsel against them and, with a magnificent manifestation, to rescue them from the doom ready at their feet.

**3 Maccabees 5:9**

So then their entreaty went up earnestly into heaven.

**3 Maccabees 5:10**

And Hermon, after making the pitiless elephants drink, filled with the great supply of wine and stuffed with frankincense, came at dawn to the court to report about these things to the king.

**3 Maccabees 5:11**

But the good creature from everlasting time, a portion of sleep in night and day, appointed by the One who gives to all to whom he wills, he sent upon the king,

**3 Maccabees 5:12**

and by the operation of the Master he was held fast by a very sweet and deep sleep, being far removed from his lawless purpose and greatly deceived in his unchangeable reasoning.

**3 Maccabees 5:13**

And the Jews, having escaped the hour marked beforehand, praised their holy God and again asked the easily reconciled One to show the might of his great-strengthened hand to arrogant nations.

**3 Maccabees 5:14**

And when the tenth hour was already almost at midpoint, the one appointed over the invitations, seeing the invited guests gathered together, came near and nudged the king.

**3 Maccabees 5:15**

And after waking him with difficulty, he showed him that the time of the banquet was already passing by, making the word about these things.

**3 Maccabees 5:16**

And after considering this and turning to the drinking, the king commanded those who had come to the banquet to recline directly opposite him.

**3 Maccabees 5:17**

And when this had happened, he urged them to give themselves to feasting and to set down the present portion of the banquet for gladness, honoring it greatly.

**3 Maccabees 5:18**

And when the conversation advanced further, the king summoned Hermon and, with a bitter threat, inquired for what cause the Jews had been allowed to live through the present day.

**3 Maccabees 5:19**

And when he showed that from the night he had carried the thing ordered to completion, and the friends bore witness with him,

**3 Maccabees 5:20**

the king, having cruelty worse than Phalaris, said that they had today's sleep to thank for this. But prepare the elephants in the same way without delay for the coming day, for the vanishing of the lawless Jews.

**3 Maccabees 5:21**

And when the king had spoken, all who were present together consented gladly with joy, and each departed to his own house.

**3 Maccabees 5:22**

And they did not spend the time of the night so much for sleep as for devising all sorts of mockeries against those who seemed wretched.

**3 Maccabees 5:23**

Now the early rooster had just crowed, and Hermon, having armed the beasts, was setting them in motion in the great peristyle.

**3 Maccabees 5:24**

And the crowds throughout the city had gathered for the most pitiful spectacle, waiting for the morning with eagerness.

**3 Maccabees 5:25**

But the Jews, having their souls drawn through the unbroken time, stretching out their hands to heaven in a many-teared supplication with mournful songs, begged the greatest God again to help them swiftly.

**3 Maccabees 5:26**

But the rays of the sun were not yet being scattered, and while the king was receiving his friends, Hermon stood by and called him to the going out, showing that the object of the king's zeal lay ready.

**3 Maccabees 5:27**

And when the king received this and was struck with amazement at the unlawful going out, being mastered in every way by ignorance, he inquired what the matter was, for which this had been completed for him with haste.

**3 Maccabees 5:28**

And this was the operation of God, who masters all things, who had put forgetfulness into his mind concerning the things previously devised by him.

**3 Maccabees 5:29**

And Hermon and all the friends showed him that the beasts and the forces had been prepared, O king, according to your earnest purpose.

**3 Maccabees 5:30**

But he, filled with deep bile at the things spoken, because all his mind about these things had been scattered by the providence of God, looked intently and said with threat,

**3 Maccabees 5:31**

If your parents were present to you, or offspring of children, I would have prepared this rich feast for the wild beasts, instead of the Jews, who are blameless to me and to my ancestors, having shown a whole and sure faith especially.

**3 Maccabees 5:32**

And yet, if it were not because of the affection of shared upbringing and of usefulness, you would have been deprived of life instead of these.

**3 Maccabees 5:33**

Thus Hermon endured an unexpected and dangerous threat, and he was drawn together in sight and face.

**3 Maccabees 5:34**

And each of the friends withdrew gloomily, and they dismissed those who had gathered, each to his own occupation.

**3 Maccabees 5:35**

And when the Jews heard the things from the king, they praised the manifest God and King of kings, having obtained this help also from him.

**3 Maccabees 5:36**

And according to the same customs, the king again organized the banquet and urged them to turn to gladness.

**3 Maccabees 5:37**

And having summoned Hermon, he said with threat, How many times must I command you concerning these same things, most wretched one?

**3 Maccabees 5:38**

Even now, arm the elephants for tomorrow, for the vanishing of the Jews.

**3 Maccabees 5:39**

And the relatives reclining together, marveling at his unstable mind, put forward these things:

**3 Maccabees 5:40**

King, until when do you test us as irrational beings, commanding already a third time to make them vanish, and again, when the matters come, by a change dissolving the things decreed by you?

**3 Maccabees 5:41**

Because of these things the city is in turmoil through expectation, and, being filled with gatherings, is already often in danger of being plundered.

**3 Maccabees 5:42**

Therefore the king, a Phalaris in every way, being multiplied with irrationality and considering as nothing the changes of soul happening in him for the oversight of the Jews, confirmed a most unfinished oath, determining to send these without delay into Hades, mangled under the knees and feet of beasts,

**3 Maccabees 5:43**

and, after making an expedition against Judea, to make it level with fire and spear quickly, and to make their temple, untrodden by us, prostrate with fire, and quickly make it deserted for all time of those who complete sacrifices there.

**3 Maccabees 5:44**

Then the friends and relatives departed overjoyed, and with confidence arranged the forces at the most suitable places of the city for guarding.

**3 Maccabees 5:45**

And the elephantarch, having brought the beasts almost to a maddened condition with most fragrant drinks of frankincensed wine, equipped with fearsome gear,

**3 Maccabees 5:46**

about dawn, when the city was already filled with innumerable multitudes around the hippodrome, entered into the court and urged the king on to the thing set before him.

**3 Maccabees 5:47**

And he, having filled his impious mind with heavy anger, rushed out with all the weight together with the beasts, wishing with an unwounded heart and the pupils of his eyes to behold the painful and wretched overthrow of those marked beforehand.

**3 Maccabees 5:48**

But when the Jews saw the dust of the elephants going out around the gate, and of the armed force following with them, and of the movement of the crowd, and when they heard the heavy-sounding uproar,

**3 Maccabees 5:49**

they supposed that this was the last turn of life for them, the end of the most wretched expectation, and, turning to pity and groans, they kissed one another, embracing their relatives and falling on their necks, fathers with children and mothers with young daughters, while others had newborn infants at their breasts, drawing their last milk.

**3 Maccabees 5:50**

Nevertheless, also considering the helps from heaven that had happened for them before, they threw themselves down facedown together and separated the infants from the breasts,

**3 Maccabees 5:51**

and cried out with an exceedingly great voice, entreating the ruler of all power to have pity with manifestation on those already standing at the gates of Hades.

### Chapter 6

**3 Maccabees 6:1**

And Eleazar, a certain notable man from the priests of the country, already having reached the age of eldership and adorned with every virtue in life, quieted the elders around him and prayed that they call upon the holy God, saying these things:

**3 Maccabees 6:2**

O King of great might, Most High, Almighty God, steering all creation with compassions,

**3 Maccabees 6:3**

look upon the seed of Abraham, upon the children of sanctified Jacob, the people of your sanctified portion, perishing unjustly as strangers in a strange land, Father.

**3 Maccabees 6:4**

You destroyed Pharaoh, abounding in chariots, the former ruler of this Egypt, lifted up with lawless boldness and a great-speaking tongue, together with his proud army, drowned in the sea, making a light of mercy shine upon the race of Israel.

**3 Maccabees 6:5**

You shattered Sennacherib, heavy king of the Assyrians, boasting in innumerable forces, having already taken all the earth subject to him by spear and being lifted up against your holy city, speaking heavy things with boasting and boldness; you, Master, showed your might manifest to many nations.

**3 Maccabees 6:6**

You cooled the blazing furnace for the three companions in Babylonia, who had freely given their soul to fire so as not to serve empty things, and you rescued them unharmed to a hair, sending the flame upon all those opposed.

**3 Maccabees 6:7**

You brought Daniel up unharmed into light, when through slanders of envy he had been thrown down to the lions on the ground as food for beasts,

**3 Maccabees 6:8**

and Jonah, wasting away unsparingly in the belly of the sea-fed monster, you showed unharmed to all his own, Father.

**3 Maccabees 6:9**

And now, hater of outrage, rich in mercy, shelterer of all things, appear swiftly to those from the race of Israel who are being outraged by abominable lawless nations.

**3 Maccabees 6:10**

But if our life has been entangled with impieties during the exile, rescue us from the hand of enemies, and, as you choose, Master, destroy us by doom.

**3 Maccabees 6:11**

Do not let the vain-minded bless vain things over the destruction of your beloved ones, saying, Not even their God rescued them.

**3 Maccabees 6:12**

But you, eternal One, who have all strength and all sovereignty, now look upon us. Have mercy on us, who are being removed from life by the senseless outrage of lawless men in the manner of plotters.

**3 Maccabees 6:13**

Let nations cower at your invincible power today, honored One, you who have power for the salvation of the race of Jacob.

**3 Maccabees 6:14**

The whole multitude of infants and their parents entreat you with tears.

**3 Maccabees 6:15**

Let it be shown to all the nations that you are with us, Lord, and that you have not turned your face away from us, but just as you said that not even when they were in the land of their enemies would you overlook them, so accomplish it, Lord.

**3 Maccabees 6:16**

And when Eleazar was just ending the prayer, the king with the beasts and with all the arrogant noise of the force was passing along toward the hippodrome.

**3 Maccabees 6:17**

And when the Jews saw it, they cried out greatly into heaven, so that even the valleys lying nearby, sounding together, made an uncontrollable wailing through the whole camp.

**3 Maccabees 6:18**

Then the greatly glorious Almighty and true God, making his holy face appear, opened the heavenly gates, from which two glorified angels of fearsome appearance came down, visible to all except the Jews,

**3 Maccabees 6:19**

and they stood against them and filled the force of those opposed with confusion and cowardice, and bound them with immovable fetters.

**3 Maccabees 6:20**

And even the body of the king became shuddering, and forgetfulness took his heavy-hearted boldness.

**3 Maccabees 6:21**

And the beasts turned back upon the armed forces following with them and trampled them down and destroyed them utterly.

**3 Maccabees 6:22**

And the king's anger was turned into pity and tears over the things previously devised by him.

**3 Maccabees 6:23**

For after hearing the cry and seeing them all facedown for destruction, he wept and threatened the friends with anger, saying,

**3 Maccabees 6:24**

You are ruling beside the king and have surpassed tyrants in cruelty, and you are attempting to remove me myself, your benefactor, already from rule and from breath, secretly devising things not profitable to the kingdom.

**3 Maccabees 6:25**

Who irrationally separated each one from his house and gathered here those who held the strongholds of the country for us in faith?

**3 Maccabees 6:26**

Who surrounded with such lawless abuses those who from the beginning have surpassed all nations in goodwill toward us in every way, and who have most often accepted the worst dangers among men?

**3 Maccabees 6:27**

Loose, release the unjust bonds. Send them away to their own places with peace, begging pardon for the things done before.

**3 Maccabees 6:28**

Release the sons of the Almighty heavenly living God, who from our ancestors until now provides our affairs with unhindered stability with glory.

**3 Maccabees 6:29**

So he spoke these things. And they, being loosed in an indivisible time, blessed their holy savior God, having just escaped death.

**3 Maccabees 6:30**

Then the king, after departing into the city, summoned the one over the revenues and commanded that wines and the remaining things useful for feasting be supplied to the Jews for seven days, judging that in the place where they had supposed they would receive destruction, in that same place they should keep deliverance-feasts in all gladness.

**3 Maccabees 6:31**

Then those who before were reproached and near Hades, or rather standing upon it, instead of a bitter and hard-to-avoid doom, organized a saving cup, and they divided by couches the place prepared for their fall and burial, being full of joy.

**3 Maccabees 6:32**

And having ceased the all-lamenting song of mourning, they took up a fatherly song, praising God the savior and wonder-worker; and, putting away every wailing and shriek, they organized dances as a sign of peaceful gladness.

**3 Maccabees 6:33**

In the same way also the king, after gathering a great banquet concerning these things, unceasingly returned thanks magnificently toward heaven for the unexpected salvation that had happened to him.

**3 Maccabees 6:34**

And those who before supposed that they would be for destruction and bird-food, after registering them with joy, groaned deeply, putting shame upon themselves and ingloriously extinguishing their fire-breathing daring.

**3 Maccabees 6:35**

And the Jews, just as we said before, having organized the aforesaid dance, were passing the time with feasting in cheerful thanksgivings and psalms,

**3 Maccabees 6:36**

and, having appointed a common ordinance concerning these things for all their sojourning community through generations, they established the aforesaid days to be kept as glad days, not for the sake of drinking and delicacy, but because of the salvation that had happened to them through God.

**3 Maccabees 6:37**

And they petitioned the king, asking for their release to their own places.

**3 Maccabees 6:38**

And they were registered from the twenty-fifth of Pachon until the fourth of Epeiphi, for forty days, and their destruction was being arranged from the fifth of Epeiphi until the seventh, for three days,

**3 Maccabees 6:39**

during which also the ruler of all things, having made his mercy appear with great glory, rescued them all together without stumbling.

**3 Maccabees 6:40**

And they feasted, all things being supplied by the king, until the fourteenth, on which day also they made the petition concerning their release.

**3 Maccabees 6:41**

And the king consented to them and wrote for them the letter written below to the commanders in each city, having its earnestness in a high-souled way.

### Chapter 7

**3 Maccabees 7:1**

King Ptolemy Philopator to the commanders throughout Egypt and to all those appointed over affairs: greetings and be strong.

**3 Maccabees 7:2**

And we ourselves and our children are strong, the great God having directed our affairs for us just as we choose.

**3 Maccabees 7:3**

Some of the friends, through ill-nature lying more often beside us, persuaded us to gather the Jews under the kingdom into a body and punish them with foreign punishments of rebels,

**3 Maccabees 7:4**

putting forward that our affairs would never be stable, because of the hostility these people have toward all nations, until this should be completed.

**3 Maccabees 7:5**

They also brought them down in bonds with mistreatments as slaves, or rather as plotters, and without any inquiry or examination attempted to remove them, having fastened on themselves cruelty more savage than the law of Scythians.

**3 Maccabees 7:6**

But we, after threatening more harshly over these things, according to the gentleness that we have toward all humans, scarcely granted life to them; and having surely recognized that the heavenly God has shielded the Jews, always fighting on their behalf as a father for sons,

**3 Maccabees 7:7**

and having considered the firm goodwill of a friend that they have toward us and our ancestors, we have justly released them from every charge in whatever way,

**3 Maccabees 7:8**

and we have ordered each one, all of them, to return to their own places, with no one anywhere harming them at all, nor reproaching them concerning the things that happened contrary to reason.

**3 Maccabees 7:9**

For know that if we devise any evil thing against them or grieve them at all, we will not have a human, but the Most High God, who rules every power, opposed to us for vengeance of the affairs, inescapably in every way through all time. Be strong.

**3 Maccabees 7:10**

And when they received this letter, they did not hasten to be immediately about the departure, but petitioned the king that those from the race of the Jews who had voluntarily transgressed the holy God and the law of God should receive through them the owed punishment,

**3 Maccabees 7:11**

putting forward that those who had transgressed the divine commands for the sake of the belly would never be well disposed, not even toward the king's affairs.

**3 Maccabees 7:12**

And he accepted that they spoke the truth and consented, giving them full permission so that they might utterly destroy those who had transgressed the law of God in every place under his kingdom, with boldness, without any royal authority or inspection.

**3 Maccabees 7:13**

Then, having acclaimed him as was fitting, their priests and the whole multitude, shouting Hallelujah, departed with joy.

**3 Maccabees 7:14**

Then they punished any defiled fellow-countryman who fell in along the way, and they put them to death with examples.

**3 Maccabees 7:15**

And on that day they put to death more than 300 men, and they kept gladness with joy, having subdued the profane.

**3 Maccabees 7:16**

And those themselves who had held to God until death, having received the complete enjoyment of salvation, set out from the city crowned with all sorts of most fragrant flowers, with gladness and shouting, in praises and all-melodious hymns giving thanks to the God of their fathers, the eternal savior of Israel.

**3 Maccabees 7:17**

And when they arrived at Ptolemais, the one named rose-bearing because of the special nature of the place, where the fleet waited for them seven days according to their common counsel,

**3 Maccabees 7:18**

there they made a saving drinking-feast, the king having supplied to them generously all the things for the arrival of each one as far as his own house.

**3 Maccabees 7:19**

And after being brought down with peace in fitting thanksgivings, likewise there also they established that these days be kept as glad days for the time of their sojourning community.

**3 Maccabees 7:20**

And having consecrated them on a pillar at the place of the banquet, and having established a place of prayer, they departed unharmed, free, overjoyed, being preserved through land and sea and river by the command of the king, each to his own place,

**3 Maccabees 7:21**

and having greater authority than before among the enemies, with glory and fear, being shaken in none of their possessions at all by anyone.

**3 Maccabees 7:22**

And all of them recovered all their own things from the registration, so that those who had anything gave it back to them with the greatest fear, the great things of the greatest God having been done perfectly for their salvation.

**3 Maccabees 7:23**

Blessed be the rescuer of Israel into the times always. Amen.

## 4 Maccabees

### Chapter 1

**4 Maccabees 1:1**

Being about to display a most philosophical word, whether reverent reasoning is master over the passions, I would rightly advise you to give eager attention to philosophy.

**4 Maccabees 1:2**

For the word is necessary for knowledge to everyone, and in another way it contains praise of the greatest virtue, I mean prudence.

**4 Maccabees 1:3**

If, then, reasoning appears to prevail over the passions that hinder temperance, both gluttony and desire,

**4 Maccabees 1:4**

but also is shown to rule over the passions that impede justice, such as malice, and over the passions that impede courage, both anger and pain and fear,

**4 Maccabees 1:5**

how then, perhaps some may say, if reasoning has power over the passions, does it not master forgetfulness and ignorance? They attempt to say something laughable.

**4 Maccabees 1:6**

For reasoning does not have power over its own passions, but over those opposed to justice and courage and temperance and prudence, and over these not so as to destroy them, but so as not to yield to them.

**4 Maccabees 1:7**

So from many places and other places I could show you that reverent reasoning is sovereign over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:8**

But much more could I prove this from the manly goodness of those who died for virtue, Eleazar and the seven brothers and their mother. [9] For all these, looking beyond pains even to death, displayed that reasoning rules completely over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:10**

So then, for their virtues, it is possible for me to praise the men who at that time died with their mother for nobility and goodness, and for their honors I would bless them.

**4 Maccabees 1:11**

For those men, admired not only by all humans for courage and endurance, but also by those who abused them, became causes for the tyranny against the nation to be destroyed, having conquered the tyrant by endurance, so that through them the fatherland was cleansed.

**4 Maccabees 1:12**

But concerning this also it will be possible to speak immediately now, after I have begun the subject, just as I am accustomed to do; and so I will turn to the word about them, giving glory to the all-wise God.

**4 Maccabees 1:13**

Therefore we are now seeking whether reasoning is sovereign over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:14**

And we distinguish what reasoning ever is, and what passion is, and how many forms of passions there are, and whether reasoning prevails over all these.

**4 Maccabees 1:15**

Reasoning, then, is mind with right word, preferring the life of wisdom.

**4 Maccabees 1:16**

Wisdom, then, is knowledge of divine and human matters and of the causes of these.

**4 Maccabees 1:17**

This, then, is the training of the law, through which we learn divine things reverently and human things advantageously.

**4 Maccabees 1:18**

And the forms of wisdom have been set as four: prudence and justice and courage and temperance.

**4 Maccabees 1:19**

And most ruling of all is prudence, from which reasoning prevails over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:20**

And the most comprehensive natures of passions are two: pleasure and pain. Each of these is naturally concerned with both the body and the soul.

**4 Maccabees 1:21**

And there are many sequences of passions around pleasure and pain.

**4 Maccabees 1:22**

Before pleasure, then, there is desire; and after pleasure, joy.

**4 Maccabees 1:23**

And before pain there is fear; and after pain, grief.

**4 Maccabees 1:24**

And anger is a common passion of pleasure and pain, if someone considers that it has befallen him.

**4 Maccabees 1:25**

And in pleasure there is also the malicious disposition, being the most many-formed of all the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:26**

According to the soul, it is boasting, love of money, love of glory, love of strife, faithlessness, and envy.

**4 Maccabees 1:27**

And according to the body, it is eating everything, gluttony, and eating alone.

**4 Maccabees 1:28**

Therefore, just as pleasure and pain are two plants of the body and the soul, there are many offshoots of these plants,

**4 Maccabees 1:29**

each of which the all-cultivating reasoning clears around and prunes away and binds around and waters and changes in every way, taming the materials of the habits and passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:30**

For reasoning is guide of the virtues and sovereign over the passions. Therefore first consider, through the works that hinder temperance, that reasoning is master over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 1:31**

Temperance, then, is mastery over desires.

**4 Maccabees 1:32**

And of desires, some are soul-like and some are bodily, and reasoning appears to prevail over both of these.

**4 Maccabees 1:33**

For since, being moved toward forbidden foods, we turn away from the pleasures from them, is it not because reasoning is able to prevail over the appetites? I think so.

**4 Maccabees 1:34**

Therefore, desiring water creatures and birds and four-footed animals and all kinds of foods forbidden to us according to the law, we abstain because of the mastery of reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 1:35**

For the passions of the appetites are restrained, being bent back by the temperate mind, and all the movements of the body are muzzled by reasoning.

### Chapter 2

**4 Maccabees 2:1**

And what is marvelous if the desires of the soul toward participation in beauty are made invalid?

**4 Maccabees 2:2**

For in this way temperate Joseph is praised, because by reasoning and understanding he completely mastered the pleasure-passion.

**4 Maccabees 2:3**

For being young and flourishing for intercourse, by reasoning he made invalid the sting of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 2:4**

And reasoning appears to prevail not only over the frenzy-driving of pleasure-passion, but also over every desire.

**4 Maccabees 2:5**

For the law says, You shall not desire the wife of your neighbor, nor as many things as belong to your neighbor.

**4 Maccabees 2:6**

And yet, when the law has told us not to desire, much more could I persuade you that reasoning is able to have power over desires, just as also over the passions that hinder justice.

**4 Maccabees 2:7**

For in what way is someone who is solitary-eating in character, and gluttonous, and a drunkard, retrained, if it is not clear that reasoning is lord over the passions?

**4 Maccabees 2:8**

For immediately, when someone conducts citizenship by the law, even if he is a lover of money, he forces his own manner by lending to those in need without interest, and by cancelling the loan when the sevens arrive.

**4 Maccabees 2:9**

And even if someone is sparing, he is held by the law through reasoning, neither gleaning the harvests nor gathering leftover clusters from the vineyards. And from the other cases also it is possible to recognize this, that reasoning has power over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 2:10**

For the law has power even over goodwill toward parents, by not betraying virtue for their sake,

**4 Maccabees 2:11**

and it prevails over love toward a wife, by exposing her because of lawlessness,

**4 Maccabees 2:12**

and it rules over love of children, by punishing them because of vice, and it masters the familiar ways of friends, by exposing them because of wickedness.

**4 Maccabees 2:13**

And do not suppose it to be strange, since reasoning is able even to prevail over enmity through the law,

**4 Maccabees 2:14**

neither cutting down the cultivated plants of those at war, but preserving the things of enemies from those who would destroy them, and raising up the things that have fallen.

**4 Maccabees 2:15**

And reasoning also appears to prevail over the more violent passions: love of rule, empty glory, boasting, great boasting, and envy.

**4 Maccabees 2:16**

For the temperate mind, urging all these malicious passions toward good, pushes them away and constrains them, just as also anger; for it masters this also.

**4 Maccabees 2:17**

Indeed Moses, when angry against Dathan and Abiram, did nothing against them by anger, but ordered the anger by reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 2:18**

For the temperate mind is able, as I said, to act nobly against the passions, and to change some of them, and to make others invalid.

**4 Maccabees 2:19**

For why does our all-wise father Jacob accuse those around Simeon and Levi, because they slaughtered the Shechemites as a nation not by reasoning, saying, Cursed be their anger?

**4 Maccabees 2:20**

For if reasoning were not able to have power over anger, he would not have spoken this way.

**4 Maccabees 2:21**

For when God fashioned the human, he planted his passions and habits around him.

**4 Maccabees 2:22**

And then he enthroned over all things the sacred ruling mind through the senses within,

**4 Maccabees 2:23**

and to this he gave a law, according to which, when it conducts citizenship, it will reign a temperate and just and good and courageous kingdom.

**4 Maccabees 2:24**

How then, someone may say, if reasoning has power over the passions, does it not have power over forgetfulness and ignorance?

### Chapter 3

**4 Maccabees 3:1**

But the word is altogether laughable; for reasoning does not appear to prevail over its own passions, but over the bodily ones.

**4 Maccabees 3:2**

For example, no one can cut desire out of us, but reasoning can provide that we not be enslaved to desire.

**4 Maccabees 3:3**

No one can cut anger out of our soul, but reasoning is able to help against anger.

**4 Maccabees 3:4**

No one can cut malice out of us, but reasoning could fight alongside us so that we not be bent by malice.

**4 Maccabees 3:5**

For reasoning is not a rooter-out of the passions, but an antagonist.

**4 Maccabees 3:6**

This, then, can be considered more clearly through the thirst of King David.

**4 Maccabees 3:7**

For when David had fought against the foreigners through the whole day and had killed many of them with the soldiers of the nation,

**4 Maccabees 3:8**

then, when evening came, sweating and exceedingly weary, he came to the royal tent, around which the whole army of the fathers had encamped.

**4 Maccabees 3:9**

So all the others were at supper,

**4 Maccabees 3:10**

but the king, being very thirsty, though he had abundant springs, was not able through them to heal the thirst.

**4 Maccabees 3:11**

But a certain irrational desire for the water among the enemies, intensifying, dried him up, and loosening him, burned him.

**4 Maccabees 3:12**

Therefore, when the shield-bearers were distressed over the king's desire, two strong young soldiers, reverencing the king's desire, armed themselves with full armor and, taking a water jar, went over the stockades of the enemies,

**4 Maccabees 3:13**

and, escaping the notice of the gate sentries, went through searching throughout the whole camp of the enemies.

**4 Maccabees 3:14**

And when they had found the spring, they boldly filled the drink from it for the king.

**4 Maccabees 3:15**

But he, though burning with thirst, reasoned that the drink, reckoned equal in power to blood, was an all-dreadful danger to the soul;

**4 Maccabees 3:16**

therefore, setting reasoning against desire, he poured out the drink to God.

**4 Maccabees 3:17**

For the temperate mind is able, as I said, to conquer the constraints of the passions,

**4 Maccabees 3:18**

and to quench the inflammations of the stings, and to wrestle down the pains of bodies that are excessive, and by the nobility and goodness of reasoning to spit away all the dominions of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 3:19**

And now the time also calls us to the proof of the theory of temperate reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 3:20**

For since our fathers had deep peace because of good law-order, and were doing well, so that even Seleucus Nicanor, the king of Asia, both set apart money for them for the temple-service and accepted their citizenship,

**4 Maccabees 3:21**

then certain men, having made a new move against the common harmony, made use of many-formed misfortunes.

### Chapter 4

**4 Maccabees 4:1**

For a certain Simon, opposing Onias, who once held the high priesthood through life, a noble and good man, when he was not strong enough to harm him though slandering in every way concerning the nation, went away as a fugitive, intending to betray the fatherland.

**4 Maccabees 4:2**

Therefore, when he came to Apollonius, the commander of Syria and Phoenicia and Cilicia, he said, Being well disposed to the king's affairs, I have come

**4 Maccabees 4:3**

to disclose that many myriads of private moneys have been stored in the treasuries of Jerusalem, not sharing with the sacred things, and that these belong to King Seleucus.

**4 Maccabees 4:4**

And when Apollonius had learned each of these things, he praised Simon for his care toward the king, and, going up to Seleucus, informed him of the treasure of moneys.

**4 Maccabees 4:5**

And after receiving the authority concerning it, he quickly went up to our fatherland with the accursed Simon and a very heavy army,

**4 Maccabees 4:6**

and when he came near, he said that he had come by the commands of the king so that he might take the private moneys of the treasury.

**4 Maccabees 4:7**

And when the nation was distressed at the word and was speaking against it, considering it all-dreadful if those who had entrusted deposits to the sacred treasury should be deprived, they hindered him as far as possible.

**4 Maccabees 4:8**

But Apollonius went away into the temple with threats.

**4 Maccabees 4:9**

And when the priests, with women and children, entreated God in the temple to shield the sacred place being treated with contempt,

**4 Maccabees 4:10**

and when Apollonius was going up with the armed army for the seizure of the moneys, angels on horseback appeared beforehand from heaven, flashing around with their weapons and putting much fear and trembling into them.

**4 Maccabees 4:11**

Indeed Apollonius, having fallen half-dead in the all-tribal enclosure of the temple, stretched out his hands to heaven and with tears called upon the Hebrews, so that by praying for him they might propitiate the heavenly army.

**4 Maccabees 4:12**

For he said that he had sinned so as to be worthy even to die, and that, if saved, he would hymn before all humans the blessedness of the sacred place.

**4 Maccabees 4:13**

Moved by these words, Onias the high priest, though otherwise cautious lest King Seleucus should ever think Apollonius had been taken off by a human plot and not by divine justice, prayed for him.

**4 Maccabees 4:14**

And he, after being saved unexpectedly, went away to report to the king the things that had happened to him.

**4 Maccabees 4:15**

And when King Seleucus died, his son Antiochus Epiphanes, a proud and terrible man, succeeded to the rule.

**4 Maccabees 4:16**

He removed Onias from the high priesthood and appointed his brother Jason high priest, after Jason agreed to give,

**4 Maccabees 4:17**

if he would permit the rule to him, 3,660 talents each year.

**4 Maccabees 4:18**

And he permitted him both to serve as high priest and to lead the nation.

**4 Maccabees 4:19**

And he changed the way of life of the nation of the Jews and changed their citizenship toward every lawlessness,

**4 Maccabees 4:20**

so that he not only constructed a gymnasium on the very citadel of our fatherland, but also dissolved the care of the temple.

**4 Maccabees 4:21**

At these things divine justice, being indignant, made Antiochus himself war against them.

**4 Maccabees 4:22**

For when he was warring against Ptolemy in Egypt, and heard that, when a rumor had spread concerning his death, the Jerusalemites were rejoicing as much as possible, he quickly set out against them.

**4 Maccabees 4:23**

And when he had ravaged them, he set a decree that if any of them should appear to conduct citizenship by the ancestral law, they should die.

**4 Maccabees 4:24**

And when in no way was he strong enough to dissolve through decrees the goodwill of the nation,

**4 Maccabees 4:25**

but saw all his threats and punishments being dissolved, so that even women, because they had circumcised their children, were thrown down with their infants, knowing beforehand that they would suffer this,

**4 Maccabees 4:26**

therefore, since his decrees were being despised by the people, he himself, through tortures, compelled each one of the nation, while tasting defiled foods, to renounce Judaism.

### Chapter 5

**4 Maccabees 5:1**

Indeed the tyrant Antiochus, having seated himself beforehand with the councilors on a certain high place, and with the armed troops standing around him in a circle, commanded the spear-bearers

**4 Maccabees 5:2**

to drag up each one of the Hebrews and to compel them to taste swine's flesh and things sacrificed to idols.

**4 Maccabees 5:3**

But if any should not wish to eat defiled food, these were to be broken on the wheel and put to death.

**4 Maccabees 5:4**

And when many had been seized together, one Hebrew first from the band, Eleazar by name, a priest by race, a lawyer in knowledge, advanced in age and known to many of those around the tyrant because of his age, was brought near him.

**4 Maccabees 5:5**

And when Antiochus saw him, he said,

**4 Maccabees 5:6**

Before beginning the tortures against you, old man, I would advise you in these things, that by tasting the swine's flesh you may be saved; for I respect your age and your gray hair, which after having so long a time you do not seem to me to philosophize, using the religion of the Jews.

**4 Maccabees 5:7**

For why do you abhor the flesh-eating of this animal, since nature has graciously given it as most beautiful?

**4 Maccabees 5:8**

For this seems senseless, not to enjoy pleasant things without reproach, and unjust to turn away from the favors of nature.

**4 Maccabees 5:9**

And you seem to me about to do something even more senseless, if, empty-glorying about the truth, you despise even me for your own punishment.

**4 Maccabees 5:10**

Will you not wake up from your babbling philosophy

**4 Maccabees 5:11**

and scatter the nonsense of your reasonings, and, taking up a mind worthy of your age, philosophize the truth of what is profitable,

**4 Maccabees 5:12**

and, doing reverence to my humane encouragement, pity your own old age?

**4 Maccabees 5:13**

For consider this also, that if there is any overseeing power of this religion of yours, it would pardon you for every lawlessness that comes through compulsion.

**4 Maccabees 5:14**

When the tyrant was urging him in this way toward the outlawed eating of flesh, Eleazar asked for the word,

**4 Maccabees 5:15**

and after receiving authority to speak, he began to address the people this way:

**4 Maccabees 5:16**

We, Antiochus, being persuaded to conduct citizenship by a divine law, consider no constraint to be more violent than good obedience to our law.

**4 Maccabees 5:17**

Therefore in no way do we think it worthy to transgress the law.

**4 Maccabees 5:18**

And yet if our law were not truly divine, as you suppose, though we otherwise think it to be divine, not even so would it be permitted for us to invalidate our reputation for piety.

**4 Maccabees 5:19**

Therefore do not suppose this to be a small sin, if we should eat defiled food.

**4 Maccabees 5:20**

For transgressing the law in small things and in great things is equal in power,

**4 Maccabees 5:21**

for through each of the two the law is despised in the same way.

**4 Maccabees 5:22**

And you mock our philosophy, as though we do not live in it with good reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 5:23**

For it teaches us temperance, so as to have power over all pleasures and desires, and to train courage, so as willingly to endure every pain,

**4 Maccabees 5:24**

and it instructs justice, so as to practice equal law through all habits, and it teaches piety, so as magnificently to reverence only the God who is.

**4 Maccabees 5:25**

Therefore we do not eat defiled food. For believing that the law has been established by God, we know that the Creator of the world, in legislating, sympathizes with us according to nature.

**4 Maccabees 5:26**

And he permitted us to eat the things that would become familiar to our souls, but he forbade us to eat the flesh of things that would become opposed.

**4 Maccabees 5:27**

And it is tyrannical not only to compel us to transgress the law, but also to eat, so that you may laugh still more at this most hateful defiled eating of ours.

**4 Maccabees 5:28**

But you will not laugh this laughter against me, nor will I pass by the sacred oaths of the fathers concerning the keeping of the law,

**4 Maccabees 5:29**

not even if you should cut out my eyes and melt my inward parts.

**4 Maccabees 5:30**

I am not so old and unmanly that my reasoning should not grow young through piety.

**4 Maccabees 5:31**

For these things, make wheels ready and blow the fire more violently.

**4 Maccabees 5:32**

I do not pity my own old age so much that through myself I would dissolve the ancestral law.

**4 Maccabees 5:33**

I will not lie to you, O training law, nor will I flee you, nor will I deny you, beloved self-control.

**4 Maccabees 5:34**

Nor will I shame you, philosophical word, nor will I renounce you, honorable priesthood and knowledge of legislation.

**4 Maccabees 5:35**

Nor will you defile my august mouth of old age, nor the age of a lawful life.

**4 Maccabees 5:36**

But the fathers will receive me pure, not having feared your constraints even to death.

**4 Maccabees 5:37**

For you will tyrannize over the impious, but over my reasonings concerning piety you will master neither through words nor through works.

### Chapter 6

**4 Maccabees 6:1**

When he had spoken against the tyrant's encouragements in this way, the spear-bearers stood by and bitterly dragged Eleazar to the instruments of torture.

**4 Maccabees 6:2**

And first they stripped the old man, who was adorned with the dignity around piety.

**4 Maccabees 6:3**

Then, binding him around the elbows on each side, they abused him with whips,

**4 Maccabees 6:4**

while a herald cried out from the other side, Be persuaded by the commands of the king.

**4 Maccabees 6:5**

But great-minded and truly noble Eleazar, as though being tortured in a dream, was changed in no way.

**4 Maccabees 6:6**

But lifting his eyes high into heaven, the old man was scraped in the flesh by the whips and was flowing down with blood,

**4 Maccabees 6:7**

and his sides were being pierced; and falling to the ground because his body could no longer bear the pains, he kept his reasoning upright and unbent.

**4 Maccabees 6:8**

Indeed one of the bitter spear-bearers, leaping in with his heel, struck him in the flanks so that he would rise after falling.

**4 Maccabees 6:9**

But he endured the pains and despised the constraint,

**4 Maccabees 6:10**

and he persevered through the abuses; and like a noble athlete, being struck, the old man conquered those torturing him.

**4 Maccabees 6:11**

Indeed, sweating in the face and gasping hard, he was marveled at by the very ones torturing him because of his good courage.

**4 Maccabees 6:12**

Therefore, partly pitying the things of his old age,

**4 Maccabees 6:13**

partly being in sympathy because of acquaintance, and partly in wonder at his endurance, some of the king's men approached him and said,

**4 Maccabees 6:14**

Why do you senselessly destroy yourself with these evils, Eleazar?

**4 Maccabees 6:15**

We will set before you some of the cooked foods, and you, pretending to taste swine's flesh, be saved.

**4 Maccabees 6:16**

And Eleazar, as though abused more bitterly through the counsel, cried out,

**4 Maccabees 6:17**

May we children of Abraham not think so wickedly that, becoming soft-souled, we would act out an improper drama for us.

**4 Maccabees 6:18**

For it would be senseless if, after living the life to old age according to truth and lawfully guarding the reputation upon it,

**4 Maccabees 6:19**

we should now change, and we ourselves should become a pattern of impiety to the young, so that we become an example of defiled eating.

**4 Maccabees 6:20**

And it would be shameful if we should live a little time longer, and that while being laughed at by all for cowardice,

**4 Maccabees 6:21**

and should be despised by the tyrant as unmanly, and not shield our divine law even to death.

**4 Maccabees 6:22**

Therefore, as to these things, children of Abraham, die nobly for piety.

**4 Maccabees 6:23**

But you spear-bearers of the tyrant, why do you delay?

**4 Maccabees 6:24**

Seeing him so great-minded toward the constraints, and not changing even at their pity, they led him to the fire.

**4 Maccabees 6:25**

There, burning him with evil-skilled instruments, they threw him down and poured foul-smelling liquids into his nostrils.

**4 Maccabees 6:26**

And when he had already been burned down to the bones and was about to faint, he lifted his eyes to God and said,

**4 Maccabees 6:27**

You know, God, that though it is available to me to be saved, I am dying by burning tortures because of the law.

**4 Maccabees 6:28**

Therefore be gracious to your nation, being satisfied with our penalty on their behalf.

**4 Maccabees 6:29**

Make my blood their purification, and receive my soul as a life in exchange for theirs.

**4 Maccabees 6:30**

And when he had said these things, the sacred man nobly died in the tortures,

**4 Maccabees 6:31**

and even to the tortures of death he stood against them by reasoning because of the law. Therefore, confessedly, reverent reasoning is master over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 6:32**

For if the passions had mastered reasoning, we would have given to these the testimony of prevailing;

**4 Maccabees 6:33**

but now, since reasoning has conquered the passions, we appropriately assign to it the authority of leadership.

**4 Maccabees 6:34**

And it is just for us to confess that the might belongs to reasoning, since it even prevails over pains from outside;

**4 Maccabees 6:35**

otherwise it would be laughable. And I show not only that reasoning has mastered pains, but also that it has power over pleasures and yields nothing to them.

### Chapter 7

**4 Maccabees 7:1**

For like the best pilot, the reasoning of our father Eleazar, steering the ship of piety in the sea of the passions,

**4 Maccabees 7:2**

and being abused by the threats of the tyrant and flooded by the storm-waves of the tortures,

**4 Maccabees 7:3**

in no way turned the rudders of piety until he sailed into the harbor of immortal victory.

**4 Maccabees 7:4**

No city ever withstood this way when besieged with many and various machines, as all-holy Eleazar did. Though his sacred soul was being burned with abuses and racks, he conquered those besieging him through reasoning that shielded piety.

**4 Maccabees 7:5**

For just as Father Eleazar stretched out his own understanding like a cliff-hung headland, he broke around him the mad-surging waves of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 7:6**

O priest worthy of the priesthood, you did not defile the sacred teeth, nor did you make common with defiled eating the stomach that had made room for God-reverence and lawful cleansing.

**4 Maccabees 7:7**

O one harmonious with the law and philosopher of divine life.

**4 Maccabees 7:8**

Such indeed ought those who minister the law to be, defending it with their own blood and noble sweat against sufferings even to death.

**4 Maccabees 7:9**

You, father, through your endurance confirmed our good law-order into glory, and, speaking solemnly of holiness, you did not dissolve it; and through your works you certified the words of divine philosophy,

**4 Maccabees 7:10**

O old man more forceful than tortures, elder stronger-toned than fire, Eleazar, greatest king of passions.

**4 Maccabees 7:11**

For just as Father Aaron, armed with the censer, running through the multitude of the nation, conquered the fire-kindling angel,

**4 Maccabees 7:12**

so Eleazar, son of Aaron, though being melted through by the fire, did not turn his reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 7:13**

And yet the most marvelous thing is that, though he was an old man, with the strengths of the body already loosened, the flesh slackened all around, and the nerves also weary, he grew young again

**4 Maccabees 7:14**

in the spirit through reasoning, and by Isaac-like reasoning he made invalid the many-headed rack.

**4 Maccabees 7:15**

O blessed old age and solemn gray hair and lawful life, which the faithful seal of death completed.

**4 Maccabees 7:16**

If, then, an old man despised tortures even to death through piety, confessedly reverent reasoning is leader of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 7:17**

But perhaps some may say, Not all rule completely over the passions, because not all have prudent reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 7:18**

But as many as take forethought for piety from the whole heart, these alone are able to have power over the passions of the flesh,

**4 Maccabees 7:19**

believing that they do not die to God, just as neither do our patriarchs Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, but they live to God.

**4 Maccabees 7:20**

Therefore nothing stands against the fact that some appear to be mastered by passions because of weak reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 7:21**

For who, philosophizing piously according to the whole rule of philosophy,

**4 Maccabees 7:22**

and having trusted God and knowing that to endure every pain because of virtue is blessed, would not rule completely over the passions because of God-reverence?

**4 Maccabees 7:23**

For the wise, temperate, and courageous person alone is lord of the passions.

### Chapter 8

**4 Maccabees 8:1**

For this reason indeed even young boys, philosophizing by the reasoning of piety, prevailed over more difficult instruments of torture.

**4 Maccabees 8:2**

For since in the first trial the tyrant had been manifestly conquered, not being able to compel an old man to eat defiled food, then with very passionate anger he commanded others from the age-group of the Hebrews to be brought, and if they should eat defiled food, to release them after eating; but if they should speak against it, to torture them more bitterly.

**4 Maccabees 8:3**

When the tyrant had ordered these things, seven brothers were brought forward with their aged mother, beautiful and reverent and noble and graceful in every way.

**4 Maccabees 8:4**

When the tyrant saw them surrounding their mother in the middle like a chorus, he was pleased with them; and being struck by their seemliness and noble birth, he smiled at them and called them near and said,

**4 Maccabees 8:5**

Young men, I kindly admire each one of you, highly valuing the beauty and the number of so many brothers. I not only advise you not to go mad with the same madness as the old man tortured before, but also urge you to yield to my counsel and enjoy my friendship.

**4 Maccabees 8:6**

For just as I would be able to punish those who disobey my commands, so also I can do good to those who obey me well.

**4 Maccabees 8:7**

So, if you are persuaded by me, you will receive both offices and leaderships over my affairs, after denying the ancestral ordinance of your citizenship.

**4 Maccabees 8:8**

And after taking part in Greek life and changing your way of life, indulge in your youths.

**4 Maccabees 8:9**

But if you provoke me angrily through your disobedience, you will compel me with terrible punishments to destroy each one of you through tortures.

**4 Maccabees 8:10**

Therefore have pity on yourselves, whom even I, the enemy, pity because of your age and good form.

**4 Maccabees 8:11**

Will you not reason this out, that nothing is laid up for you if you disobey, except to die with racks?

**4 Maccabees 8:12**

And while saying these things, he commanded the instruments of torture to be set before them in front, so that through fear also he might persuade them to eat defiled food.

**4 Maccabees 8:13**

And when the spear-bearers set before them wheels and joint-dislocators, racks and wheel-pulleys and catapults and kettles, pans and finger-screws and iron hands and wedges and the live coals of the fire, the tyrant took up the word and said,

**4 Maccabees 8:14**

Be afraid, young boys, and the justice that you reverence will be gracious to you when you transgress the law through compulsion.

**4 Maccabees 8:15**

But after hearing persuasive things and seeing dreadful things, they not only were not afraid, but even counter-philosophized against the tyrant and through good reasoning dissolved his tyranny.

**4 Maccabees 8:16**

And yet let us consider: if any among them had been cowardly-souled and unmanly, what words would they have used? Would they not be these?

**4 Maccabees 8:17**

O wretched and very senseless ones that we are! When the king urges us and calls us to benefaction, should we not be persuaded by him,

**4 Maccabees 8:18**

if we cheer ourselves with empty wishes and dare death-bearing disobedience?

**4 Maccabees 8:19**

Shall we not fear the instruments of torture, men and brothers, and consider the threats of the tortures, and flee this empty glory and destruction-bearing boasting?

**4 Maccabees 8:20**

Let us have mercy on our own ages and let us pity our mother's old age,

**4 Maccabees 8:21**

and let us consider that if we disobey, we will die.

**4 Maccabees 8:22**

And divine justice also will pardon us because through compulsion we feared the king.

**4 Maccabees 8:23**

Why do we lead ourselves out of the sweetest life and deprive ourselves of the sweet world?

**4 Maccabees 8:24**

Let us not force the compulsion, nor love glory over our own rack.

**4 Maccabees 8:25**

Not even the law itself puts us to death unwillingly when we have feared the instruments of torture.

**4 Maccabees 8:26**

From where has such love of strife been melted into us, and why does death-bearing endurance please us, when it is present to live without disturbance by being persuaded by the king?

**4 Maccabees 8:27**

But the young men who were about to be tortured said none of these things, nor did they consider them.

**4 Maccabees 8:28**

For they were despisers of the passions and sovereigns over pains,

**4 Maccabees 8:29**

so that as soon as the tyrant ceased advising them to eat defiled food, all together through one voice, as though from the same soul, spoke to him.

### Chapter 9

**4 Maccabees 9:1**

Why do you delay, tyrant? For we are ready to die rather than transgress our ancestral commands.

**4 Maccabees 9:2**

For we would rightly shame our ancestors if we did not use good obedience to the law and Moses as counselor.

**4 Maccabees 9:3**

Tyrant, counselor of lawlessness, do not, while hating us, pity us more than we pity ourselves.

**4 Maccabees 9:4**

For we consider your pity toward our lawless salvation to be more difficult than death itself.

**4 Maccabees 9:5**

And you terrify us by threatening us with death through tortures, as though you had not learned a little while ago from Eleazar.

**4 Maccabees 9:6**

But if the old men of the Hebrews, after enduring tortures because of piety, acted piously, we young men would die more justly, looking beyond the tortures of your constraints, which our old teacher also conquered.

**4 Maccabees 9:7**

Therefore test us, tyrant; and if you put our souls to death because of piety, do not suppose that you harm us by torturing.

**4 Maccabees 9:8**

For we, through this suffering and endurance, will have the prizes of virtue and will be with God, because of whom we also suffer.

**4 Maccabees 9:9**

But you, because of our polluted murder, will endure from divine justice an adequate eternal torture through fire.

**4 Maccabees 9:10**

When they had said these things, the tyrant not only became harsh toward them as toward disobedient ones, but was angered as toward ungrateful ones.

**4 Maccabees 9:11**

Therefore the shield-bearers, being commanded, brought forward the eldest of them, and tearing his tunic, bound his hands and arms with straps on each side.

**4 Maccabees 9:12**

And when they grew weary beating him with whips, accomplishing nothing, they threw him onto the wheel,

**4 Maccabees 9:13**

around which the noble young man, being stretched tight, became disjointed.

**4 Maccabees 9:14**

And being broken in every limb, he denounced them, saying,

**4 Maccabees 9:15**

Most defiled tyrant, enemy of heavenly justice, and cruel-minded one, you abuse me in this way, not as one who has murdered a man, nor as one who has acted impiously, but as one shielding the divine law.

**4 Maccabees 9:16**

And when the spear-bearers said, Confess to eat, so that you may be released from the tortures,

**4 Maccabees 9:17**

he said to them, Your wheel is not so strong, defiled servants, as to choke my reasoning. Cut my limbs and burn my flesh and twist my joints.

**4 Maccabees 9:18**

For through all the tortures I will persuade you that sons of Hebrews alone are invincible for virtue.

**4 Maccabees 9:19**

As he said these things, they spread fire under him and, stretching him still more, they inflamed the wheel.

**4 Maccabees 9:20**

And the wheel was stained on every side with blood, and the heap of coals was extinguished by the drops of bodily fluids, and the flesh flowed around the axles of the instrument.

**4 Maccabees 9:21**

And though the framework of his bones had already been cut around, the great-minded and Abrahamic young man did not groan.

**4 Maccabees 9:22**

But as though being transformed in fire into incorruption, he nobly endured the racks,

**4 Maccabees 9:23**

saying, Imitate me, brothers. Do not desert my life-course, nor deny the brotherhood of my good courage. Serve a sacred and noble warfare for piety,

**4 Maccabees 9:24**

through which our just and fatherly Providence, becoming gracious to the nation, may punish the accursed tyrant.

**4 Maccabees 9:25**

And when he had said these things, the sacredly fitting young man broke off his soul.

**4 Maccabees 9:26**

And when all had marveled at his enduring soul, the spear-bearers led the second by age after the first, and having fitted iron hands with sharp claws, they bound him to the instrument and catapult.

**4 Maccabees 9:27**

And when they inquired if he wished to eat before being tortured, they heard his noble judgment.

**4 Maccabees 9:28**

After pulling with the iron hands from the tendons up to the chin, the leopard-like beasts stripped off all the flesh and the skin of the head.

**4 Maccabees 9:29**

But he, enduring this heavy pain, said, How sweet is every manner of death because of our ancestral piety. And he said to the tyrant,

**4 Maccabees 9:30**

Do you not think, cruelest of all tyrants, that you are now being tortured more than I am, seeing the proud reasoning of your tyranny being conquered by our endurance because of piety?

**4 Maccabees 9:31**

For I am lightened from pain by the pleasures that come through virtue,

**4 Maccabees 9:32**

but you are tortured in the threats of impiety. And you will not escape, most defiled tyrant, the punishments of divine wrath.

### Chapter 10

**4 Maccabees 10:1**

And after this one had endured the death worthy of song, the third was being led, urged much by many to taste and be saved.

**4 Maccabees 10:2**

But he cried out and said, Or are you ignorant that the same father sowed me with those who have died, and the same mother bore me, and we were brought up on the same decrees?

**4 Maccabees 10:3**

I will not deny by oath the noble kinship of my brotherhood.

**4 Maccabees 10:4**

Therefore, if you have any instrument of punishment, bring it to my body; for even if you wish, you are not able to touch my soul.

**4 Maccabees 10:5**

But they, bearing bitterly the boldness of the man, disjointed his hands and feet with joint-dislocating instruments, and, levering them from their sockets, tore him limb from limb.

**4 Maccabees 10:6**

And they broke around his fingers and arms and legs and elbows.

**4 Maccabees 10:7**

And since in no way were they strong enough to choke him, they dragged off the skin, together with the very tips of the fingers, and flayed him; and immediately they led him onto the wheel,

**4 Maccabees 10:8**

around which, being torn apart from the vertebrae, he saw his own flesh being ripped to pieces and drops of blood flowing from his inward parts.

**4 Maccabees 10:9**

And when he was about to die, he said,

**4 Maccabees 10:10**

We, most defiled tyrant, suffer these things because of God's training and virtue,

**4 Maccabees 10:11**

but you, because of impiety and polluted murder, will endure indissoluble tortures.

**4 Maccabees 10:12**

And when this one had died in a brother-fitting way, they dragged up the fourth, saying,

**4 Maccabees 10:13**

Do not also go mad together with your brothers in the same madness, but be persuaded by the king and save yourself.

**4 Maccabees 10:14**

But he said to them, You do not have fire more burning against me in such a way as to make me cowardly.

**4 Maccabees 10:15**

By the blessed death of my brothers, and the eternal destruction of the tyrant, and the everlasting life of the pious, I will not deny the noble brotherhood.

**4 Maccabees 10:16**

Devise tortures, tyrant, so that even through these you may learn that I am brother of those tortured before.

**4 Maccabees 10:17**

When blood-devouring and murderous and all-defiled Antiochus heard these things, he commanded that his tongue be cut out.

**4 Maccabees 10:18**

But he said, Even if you take away the instrument of the voice, God hears also those who are silent.

**4 Maccabees 10:19**

Behold, the tongue has been put forward loose; cut it, for not by this will you cut the tongue of our reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 10:20**

Gladly, for the law of God, we have the members of the body mutilated.

**4 Maccabees 10:21**

But God will swiftly pursue you, for you cut out the tongue that sings divine hymns.

### Chapter 11

**4 Maccabees 11:1**

And when this one also, having been abused by the tortures, died in them, the fifth leaped forward, saying,

**4 Maccabees 11:2**

I am not about to ask off, tyrant, from the torture for virtue,

**4 Maccabees 11:3**

but I have come forward of myself, so that by killing me also you may owe punishment to heavenly justice for more wrongdoings.

**4 Maccabees 11:4**

O hater of virtue and hater of mankind, what have we done that you ravage us in this way?

**4 Maccabees 11:5**

Or does it seem evil to you that we reverence the Creator of all things and live according to his virtuous law?

**4 Maccabees 11:6**
