But these things are worthy of honors, not tortures,

**4 Maccabees 11:7**

if indeed you perceived human longing and had hope of salvation from God.

**4 Maccabees 11:8**

But now, being alien from God, you war against those who reverence God.

**4 Maccabees 11:9**

And as he was saying such things, the spear-bearers bound him and dragged him to the catapult,

**4 Maccabees 11:10**

on which, after binding him on the knees and fitting these into iron foot-cuffs, they bent his waist down around a wheel-shaped wedge, around which, being wholly bent backward around the wheel like a scorpion, he was torn limb from limb.

**4 Maccabees 11:11**

Being constrained in spirit and choked in body in this way,

**4 Maccabees 11:12**

he said, Fine favors, tyrant, you grant us against your will, providing for us through nobler pains to display endurance toward our law.

**4 Maccabees 11:13**

And when this one also had died, the sixth young boy was being led, and when the tyrant asked whether he wished to eat and be released, he said,

**4 Maccabees 11:14**

I am younger than my brothers in age, but equal in understanding.

**4 Maccabees 11:15**

For since we were born and nourished into the same things, we also owe it to die alike for the same things.

**4 Maccabees 11:16**

So if it seems good to you to torture one who does not eat defiled food, torture.

**4 Maccabees 11:17**

When he had said these things, they brought him to the wheel,

**4 Maccabees 11:18**

on which, being stretched limb from limb and dislocated from the vertebrae, he was burned underneath.

**4 Maccabees 11:19**

And after heating sharp spits, they applied them to his back, and piercing his sides, they burned through his inward parts.

**4 Maccabees 11:20**

But while being tortured he said, O contest worthy of sacred things, into which because of piety so many brothers have been called for training of pains, and we have not been conquered.

**4 Maccabees 11:21**

For pious knowledge is invincible, tyrant.

**4 Maccabees 11:22**

Armed with nobility and goodness, I also will die with my brothers.

**4 Maccabees 11:23**

But you, tyrant, will take to yourself a great avenger, innovator of tortures and enemy of those who truly reverence.

**4 Maccabees 11:24**

We six young boys have dissolved your tyranny.

**4 Maccabees 11:25**

For is not your dissolution this, that you have not been able either to persuade our reasoning or to force us toward defiled eating?

**4 Maccabees 11:26**

Your fire is cold to us, and the catapults painless, and your violence powerless.

**4 Maccabees 11:27**

For the spear-bearers over us have been stationed not by a tyrant, but by divine law. Because of this we have reasoning invincible.

### Chapter 12

**4 Maccabees 12:1**

And when this one also had blessedly died in them, after being thrown into a kettle, the seventh came forward, youngest of all.

**4 Maccabees 12:2**

The tyrant, pitying him, though he had been terribly reviled by his brothers, and seeing that he was already surrounded with bonds,

**4 Maccabees 12:3**

sent for him nearer and tried to encourage him, saying,

**4 Maccabees 12:4**

You see the end of your brothers' madness; for through disobedience they have died after being racked. But if you are not persuaded, wretch, you also will die before your hour after being tortured.

**4 Maccabees 12:5**

But if you are persuaded, you will be a friend and will lead the affairs of the kingdom.

**4 Maccabees 12:6**

And while urging these things, he sent for the boy's mother, so that, having pity on her, deprived of so many sons, he might stir the one left over toward saving obedience.

**4 Maccabees 12:7**

But after his mother urged him in the Hebrew voice, as we will say a little later, he says, Release me,

**4 Maccabees 12:8**

that I may say something to the king and to all the friends with him.

**4 Maccabees 12:9**

And the king and those with him, rejoicing greatly at the promise of the boy, quickly loosed him.

**4 Maccabees 12:10**

And running near to the pans, he said,

**4 Maccabees 12:11**

Unholy one, he says, and most impious tyrant of all the wicked, were you not ashamed, after receiving good things and the kingdom from God, to kill his servants and to rack those trained in piety?

**4 Maccabees 12:12**

In return for these things, divine justice will store you up for denser and eternal fire and tortures, which will not release you for the whole age.

**4 Maccabees 12:13**

Were you not ashamed, being a human, most beast-like one, to cut out the tongues of those of like passion and made from the same elements, and to torture them by abusing them in this way?

**4 Maccabees 12:14**

But they, having died nobly, fulfilled piety toward God,

**4 Maccabees 12:15**

and you, evil one, will wail evilly, having killed the athletes of virtue without cause.

**4 Maccabees 12:16**

Therefore, being about to die himself also, he said,

**4 Maccabees 12:17**

I will not desert from the excellence of my brothers.

**4 Maccabees 12:18**

And I call upon the God of the fathers, that he may become gracious to my race.

**4 Maccabees 12:19**

But you he will punish both in the present life and after you die.

**4 Maccabees 12:20**

And after praying these things, he threw himself against the pans, and thus gave back the soul.

### Chapter 13

**4 Maccabees 13:1**

If, then, the seven brothers looked beyond pains even to death, it is confessed together from every side that reverent reasoning is master over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 13:2**

For if, enslaved to the passions, they had eaten defiled food, we would have said that they had been conquered by these.

**4 Maccabees 13:3**

But now it was not so; rather, by reasoning praised by God, they got the better of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 13:4**

It is not possible to overlook the leadership of their understanding, for they prevailed over both passion and pains.

**4 Maccabees 13:5**

How then is it not possible to confess in these the passion-mastery of good reasoning, since they did not turn away from pains through fire?

**4 Maccabees 13:6**

For just as towers standing before harbors cut off the threats of the waves and provide a calm anchorage for those sailing in,

**4 Maccabees 13:7**

so the seven-towered good reasoning of the young men, having fortified the harbor of piety, conquered the lack of discipline of the passions.

**4 Maccabees 13:8**

For setting up a sacred chorus of piety, they were encouraging one another, saying,

**4 Maccabees 13:9**

Let us die brotherly, brothers, concerning the law. Let us imitate the three young men in Assyria, who despised the equal-wrestling furnace.

**4 Maccabees 13:10**

Let us not act cowardly toward the proof of piety.

**4 Maccabees 13:11**

And one said, Have courage, brother; another, Endure nobly;

**4 Maccabees 13:12**

and another, being reminded, said, Remember from where you are, or by what father's hand Isaac endured to be slaughtered because of piety.

**4 Maccabees 13:13**

And each one and all together, looking at one another, bright and very courageous, said, Let us consecrate ourselves from the whole heart to God, the One who gave the souls, and let us use the bodies for guarding around the law.

**4 Maccabees 13:14**

Let us not fear the one who seems to kill the body.

**4 Maccabees 13:15**

For a great contest and danger of soul, lying in eternal torture, belongs to those who transgress the command of God.

**4 Maccabees 13:16**

Therefore let us arm ourselves with the passion-mastery of divine reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 13:17**

For if we die this way, Abraham and Isaac and Jacob will receive us into their bosoms, and all the fathers will praise us.

**4 Maccabees 13:18**

And to each one of their brothers being dragged away, the ones left behind would say, Do not shame us, brother, nor lie against our brothers who died before us.

**4 Maccabees 13:19**

And you are not ignorant of the affections of brotherhood, which divine and all-wise providence distributed through the fathers to those who were born, and planted through the motherly womb,

**4 Maccabees 13:20**

in which brothers dwelt the equal time and were formed in the same time and increased from the same blood and were carried to completion through the same soul,

**4 Maccabees 13:21**

and were born after equal times and drank milk from the same springs, from which brother-loving souls are drawn together by embraces,

**4 Maccabees 13:22**

and grow more intensely through being nourished together and daily familiarity and the other training and our discipline in the law of God.

**4 Maccabees 13:23**

So then, with brotherly love established as sympathetic in this way, the seven brothers had their harmony toward one another more sympathetically.

**4 Maccabees 13:24**

For being trained in the same law, and having practiced the same virtues, and being nourished together in the just life, they loved one another more.

**4 Maccabees 13:25**

For shared zeal for nobility and goodness intensified their goodwill and harmony toward one another.

**4 Maccabees 13:26**

For together with piety they made brotherly love more desirable to themselves.

**4 Maccabees 13:27**

But nevertheless, though nature and familiarity and the habits of virtue were increasing the affections of brotherhood in them, because of piety the brothers left behind endured seeing their brothers being abused and tortured even to death.

### Chapter 14

**4 Maccabees 14:1**

Further, they urged one another toward the abuse, so that they not only despised pains, but also had power over the passions of brotherly love for the brothers.

**4 Maccabees 14:2**

O reasonings more kingly than a king and freer than free men.

**4 Maccabees 14:3**

O sacred and well-harmonized agreement of the seven brothers concerning piety.

**4 Maccabees 14:4**

None of the seven young boys acted cowardly, nor shrank back toward death,

**4 Maccabees 14:5**

but all, as though running on a road of immortality, hurried toward death through tortures.

**4 Maccabees 14:6**

For just as hands and feet move in harmony with the leadings of the soul, so those sacred young boys, as though moved by one soul, the immortal soul of piety, agreed together toward death for it.

**4 Maccabees 14:7**

O all-holy seven of harmonious brothers. For just as seven days of world-making are around piety,

**4 Maccabees 14:8**

so the young boys, dancing around the seven, circled around the fear of tortures, dissolving it.

**4 Maccabees 14:9**

Now we shudder when hearing the affliction of those young men; but they, not only seeing, and not only hearing the word of threat immediately, but also suffering, endured, and this with pains through fire.

**4 Maccabees 14:10**

What could become more painful than these? For the power of fire, being sharp and swift, quickly dissolved the bodies.

**4 Maccabees 14:11**

And do not consider it marvelous if reasoning completely mastered those men in the tortures, since even the mind of a woman looked beyond more many-formed pains.

**4 Maccabees 14:12**

For the mother of those seven young men endured the racks upon each one of her children.

**4 Maccabees 14:13**

But observe how much-entwined the affection of child-love is, drawing everything toward the sympathy of the inward parts,

**4 Maccabees 14:14**

since even irrational animals have sympathy and affection like humans toward those begotten from them.

**4 Maccabees 14:15**

For among birds, the tame ones that roof-roost around houses shield their chicks,

**4 Maccabees 14:16**

and others, having made nests on the peaks of mountains and clefts of ravines and holes of trees and their tips, lay eggs and prevent the one approaching.

**4 Maccabees 14:17**

And if they are not able to prevent him, flying around them in a circle, pained by affection and calling out with their own voice, they help the children in whatever way they can.

**4 Maccabees 14:18**

And why is it necessary to demonstrate sympathy toward children through irrational animals?

**4 Maccabees 14:19**

Since even bees, around the time of wax-making, defend against those approaching, and with their sting as with iron strike those approaching their nest, and defend it until death.

**4 Maccabees 14:20**

But sympathy for the children did not move the mother of the young men, who was of one soul with Abraham.

### Chapter 15

**4 Maccabees 15:1**

O reasoning, tyrant over the passions of children, and piety more desirable to a mother than children.

**4 Maccabees 15:2**

When two things lay before the mother, piety and the temporary salvation of her seven sons according to the promise of the tyrant,

**4 Maccabees 15:3**

she rather loved piety, which saves into eternal life according to God.

**4 Maccabees 15:4**

O in what way might I describe the child-loving passions of parents? We wonderfully seal likeness of soul and form into the small character of a child, especially because, by the passions, mothers have been established as more sympathetic than fathers toward those born.

**4 Maccabees 15:5**

For insofar as mothers are weaker-souled and more given to bearing children, so much more child-loving they are.

**4 Maccabees 15:6**

And of all mothers, the mother of the seven children became most child-loving, she who through seven pregnancies planted the affectionate love toward them,

**4 Maccabees 15:7**

and through the many birth-pangs for each one of them was compelled to have sympathy toward them,

**4 Maccabees 15:8**

yet because of the fear toward God, she looked beyond the temporary salvation of the children.

**4 Maccabees 15:9**

Not only this, but also because of the nobility and goodness of the sons and their good obedience toward the law, she had greater affectionate love in them.

**4 Maccabees 15:10**

For they were just and temperate and courageous and great-souled and brother-loving and mother-loving in such a way that they obeyed her while keeping the lawful things even to death.

**4 Maccabees 15:11**

But nevertheless, though there were so many things around child-love drawing the mother into sympathy, over none of them were the all-various tortures strong enough to turn her reasoning.

**4 Maccabees 15:12**

Rather, both by each child and by all together, the mother was urged forward toward death for piety.

**4 Maccabees 15:13**

O sacred nature and affections of parents and child-loving birth and nurtures and unconquerable passions of mothers.

**4 Maccabees 15:14**

The mother, seeing each one being racked and burned, was not changed because of piety.

**4 Maccabees 15:15**

She saw the flesh of the children being melted around the fire, and the fingers of feet and hands twitching on the ground, and the flesh of the heads as far as the chins lying before her like masks.

**4 Maccabees 15:16**

O mother now tested by pains more bitter than the birth-pangs over them.

**4 Maccabees 15:17**

O woman alone who gave birth to piety whole.

**4 Maccabees 15:18**

The firstborn breathing out did not turn you, nor the second looking pitifully toward you in the tortures, nor the third growing cold.

**4 Maccabees 15:19**

Nor, when you saw the eyes of each one looking like bulls upon the tortures at their own abuse, and their nostrils signaling beforehand their death, did you weep.

**4 Maccabees 15:20**

Seeing flesh of children burned away upon flesh of children, and hands cut off upon hands, and heads flayed off upon heads, and dead bodies falling upon dead bodies, and seeing the chorus of your children become a many-man grave through the tortures, you did not shed tears.

**4 Maccabees 15:21**

Not in this way do Siren melodies, nor swan voices for love of hearing, draw those who hear, as voices of children calling to a mother with tortures.

**4 Maccabees 15:22**

By how great and how many tortures was the mother then tortured as the sons were tortured by wheels and cauteries?

**4 Maccabees 15:23**

But reverent reasoning, making her inward parts courageous in the passions themselves, stretched her to overlook temporary child-love.

**4 Maccabees 15:24**

And though she saw the destruction of seven children and the much-entwined variety of the racks, the noble mother released them all because of faith toward God.

**4 Maccabees 15:25**

For just as in a council chamber within her own soul, seeing dreadful counselors, nature and birth and child-love and the racks of children,

**4 Maccabees 15:26**

the mother held two votes for the children, one death-bearing and one saving.

**4 Maccabees 15:27**

She did not recognize the salvation that would save seven sons for a little time,

**4 Maccabees 15:28**

but the daughter of God-revering Abraham remembered his endurance.

**4 Maccabees 15:29**

O mother of a nation, avenger of the law and defender of piety and prize-bearer of the contest through inward parts;

**4 Maccabees 15:30**

O more noble than males toward steadfastness and more manly than men toward endurance.

**4 Maccabees 15:31**

For just as Noah's ark, carrying the world in the world-filled flood, steadfastly endured the surging waves,

**4 Maccabees 15:32**

so you, the law-keeper, being drenched on every side in the flood of the passions and held together by strong winds, the tortures of sons, nobly endured the storms for piety.

### Chapter 16

**4 Maccabees 16:1**

If, then, even a woman, and an aged one, and mother of seven children, endured the tortures of her children even to death while seeing them, confessedly reverent reasoning is sovereign over the passions.

**4 Maccabees 16:2**

I have shown, therefore, that not only men prevailed over the passions, but also a woman looked beyond the greatest tortures.

**4 Maccabees 16:3**

And the lions around Daniel were not so savage, nor the furnace burning against Mishael with most violent fire, as the nature of child-love burned around that woman, seeing her seven sons tortured in such varied ways.

**4 Maccabees 16:4**

But by the reasoning of piety the mother quenched so many and such great passions.

**4 Maccabees 16:5**

For consider this also, that if the woman, though being mother, had been cowardly-souled, she would have lamented over them and perhaps spoken this way:

**4 Maccabees 16:6**

O miserable me, and often thrice-wretched, who after bearing seven children have become mother of none.

**4 Maccabees 16:7**

O vain seven pregnancies, and useless seven ten-month periods, and fruitless nursings, and miserable milk-feedings.

**4 Maccabees 16:8**

In vain for you, children, I endured many birth-pangs and the more difficult cares of upbringing.

**4 Maccabees 16:9**

O my children, some unmarried and others married without profit. I will not see your children, nor be blessed when called grandmother.

**4 Maccabees 16:10**

O I, a woman with many children and beautiful children, am a widow and alone, full of many laments.

**4 Maccabees 16:11**

Not even if I die will I have any of my sons to bury me. But the sacred and God-revering mother lamented none of them with this lament,

**4 Maccabees 16:12**

nor did she turn any of them away so that they might not die, nor was she grieved over them as dying.

**4 Maccabees 16:13**

But as though having a mind of adamant and bearing again the number of her sons into immortality, she was rather urging them forward, entreating them to death for piety.

**4 Maccabees 16:14**

O mother, soldier of God through piety, elder and woman, through endurance you conquered even a tyrant, and in deeds and words you were found more powerful than a man.

**4 Maccabees 16:15**

For when you were seized with the children, you stood watching Eleazar being tortured and spoke to the children in the Hebrew voice:

**4 Maccabees 16:16**

Children, noble is the contest, to which, being called for the solemn witness of the nation, contend eagerly for the ancestral law.

**4 Maccabees 16:17**

For it is shameful for this old man to endure pains because of piety, but for you young men to be struck with terror at the tortures.

**4 Maccabees 16:18**

Remember that through God you received a share of the world and enjoyed life,

**4 Maccabees 16:19**

and because of this you owe it to endure every pain because of God,

**4 Maccabees 16:20**

because of whom also our father Abraham was hastening to slaughter Isaac, his race-father son. And Isaac, seeing the fatherly sword-bearing hand being brought down upon him, did not shrink.

**4 Maccabees 16:21**

And righteous Daniel was thrown into lions, and Hananiah and Azariah and Mishael were hurled into a furnace of fire, and they endured because of God.

**4 Maccabees 16:22**

Therefore we also, having the same faith toward God, do not grow harsh.

**4 Maccabees 16:23**

For it is senseless for those who know piety not to resist pains.

**4 Maccabees 16:24**

Through these words, the mother of seven, encouraging each one of her sons, persuaded them to die rather than transgress the command of God.

**4 Maccabees 16:25**

And still also knowing these things, that those who die because of God live to God, just as Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and all the patriarchs.

### Chapter 17

**4 Maccabees 17:1**

And some of the spear-bearers also said that when she herself was about to be seized for death, so that no one might touch her body, she threw herself upon the pyre.

**4 Maccabees 17:2**

O mother, with seven children you dissolved the violence of the tyrant, and made invalid his evil designs, and displayed the nobility of faith.

**4 Maccabees 17:3**

For just as a roof nobly established upon the pillars of the children, you endured unbent the earthquake through the tortures.

**4 Maccabees 17:4**

Therefore have courage, mother of sacred soul, having toward God the hope of endurance as firm.

**4 Maccabees 17:5**

Not so august has the moon been set in heaven with the stars, as you, having led your seven star-equal children to piety as lights, have been set as honored with God and established in heaven with them.

**4 Maccabees 17:6**

For your childbearing was from Abraham the father.

**4 Maccabees 17:7**

And if it were permitted for us, as on some tablet, to paint the history of your piety, would those looking not shudder at a mother of seven children who, because of piety, endured various tortures even to death?

**4 Maccabees 17:8**

For it would be worthy to inscribe even on the tomb itself these things also, spoken for remembrance to those from the nation:

**4 Maccabees 17:9**

Here an old priest and an aged woman and seven children have been buried because of the violence of a tyrant who wished to dissolve the citizenship of the Hebrews.

**4 Maccabees 17:10**

They also vindicated the nation, looking to God and enduring the tortures even to death.

**4 Maccabees 17:11**

For truly the contest that happened through them was divine.

**4 Maccabees 17:12**

For virtue was setting the contest prize then, testing through endurance; the victory was incorruption in long-lasting life.

**4 Maccabees 17:13**

And Eleazar was contesting before them, and the mother of the seven children was competing, and the brothers were fighting the contest.

**4 Maccabees 17:14**

The tyrant was fighting against them, and the world and the life of humans were watching.

**4 Maccabees 17:15**

And God-reverence was conquering, crowning her own athletes.

**4 Maccabees 17:16**

Who did not marvel at the athletes of divine legislation?

**4 Maccabees 17:17**

Who was not struck with amazement? Indeed the tyrant himself and their whole council marveled exceedingly at their endurance,

**4 Maccabees 17:18**

because of which they also now stand beside the divine throne and live the blessed age.

**4 Maccabees 17:19**

For Moses also says, And all the sanctified are under your hands.

**4 Maccabees 17:20**

So then these who were sanctified because of God have been honored not only with this honor, but also in that through them the enemies did not prevail over our nation,

**4 Maccabees 17:21**

and the tyrant was punished, and the fatherland was cleansed,

**4 Maccabees 17:22**

as they became a life in exchange for the sin of the nation. And through the blood of those pious ones and their propitiatory death, divine Providence saved Israel, which had been harmed before.

**4 Maccabees 17:23**

For when the tyrant Antiochus looked to the courage of their virtue and to their endurance under the tortures, he proclaimed their endurance to his soldiers for an example.

**4 Maccabees 17:24**

And he had them noble and courageous for infantry battle and siege, and after storming, he conquered all the enemies.

### Chapter 18

**4 Maccabees 18:1**

O Israelite children, offspring of Abrahamic seeds, be persuaded by this law and be pious in every way,

**4 Maccabees 18:2**

knowing that reverent reasoning is master of the passions, and not only of pains from within, but also of pains from outside.

**4 Maccabees 18:3**

In return for these things, because of piety those people handed their bodies over to pains, and they were marveled at not only by humans, but were also counted worthy of a divine portion.

**4 Maccabees 18:4**

And through them the nation had peace; and renewing the good law-order in the fatherland, they drove out the enemies by siege.

**4 Maccabees 18:5**

And the tyrant Antiochus was punished on earth and is punished after death. For since in no way at all was he strong enough to compel the Jerusalemites to become foreign and to change their way of life away from the ancestral customs, then, departing from Jerusalem, he encamped against the Persians.

**4 Maccabees 18:6**

And the mother of the seven children also spoke these righteous ordinances to the children:

**4 Maccabees 18:7**

I was born a pure virgin, and I did not step beyond the fatherly house, but guarded the built side.

**4 Maccabees 18:8**

No destroyer of desolation, a corrupter in a field, corrupted me, nor did a deceitful serpent, a destroyer, ruin the pure things of my virginity; but I remained through the time of prime with a husband.

**4 Maccabees 18:9**

And when these had become grown, their father died. Blessed was he, for after living the life of good children, he did not suffer the time of childlessness.

**4 Maccabees 18:10**

While he was still with you, he taught you the law and the prophets.

**4 Maccabees 18:11**

He read to you Abel, killed by Cain, and Isaac, being offered as whole-fruit, and Joseph in prison.

**4 Maccabees 18:12**

And he spoke to you of zealous Phinehas, and he taught you Hananiah and Azariah and Mishael in the fire.

**4 Maccabees 18:13**

And he also glorified Daniel in the lions' den, whom he also blessed.

**4 Maccabees 18:14**

And he reminded you also of the writing of Isaiah, which says, Even if you pass through fire, flame will not burn you up.

**4 Maccabees 18:15**

He melodized to you David the hymn-writer, who says, Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will rescue them.

**4 Maccabees 18:16**

He spoke Proverbs to you from Solomon, who says, It is a tree of life to all who do his will.

**4 Maccabees 18:17**

He certified Ezekiel, who says, Will these dry bones live?

**4 Maccabees 18:18**

For he did not forget the song that Moses taught, which teaches and says, I will kill, and I will make alive.

**4 Maccabees 18:19**

This is your life and the length of days.

**4 Maccabees 18:20**

O bitter was that day, and not bitter, when the bitter tyrant of the Greeks, having kindled fire with cruel kettles and boiling passions, brought the seven children of the Abrahamite woman to the catapult and all his tortures,

**4 Maccabees 18:21**

and gouged out the pupils of their eyes, and cut out tongues, and killed them by various tortures.

**4 Maccabees 18:22**

For these things divine justice pursued and will pursue the accursed tyrant.

**4 Maccabees 18:23**

But the Abrahamic children, with the prize-bearing mother, are gathered together into the chorus of the fathers, having received pure and immortal souls from God,

**4 Maccabees 18:24**

to whom be the glory into the ages of the ages. Amen.

## Psalms

### Chapter 151

**Psalms 151:1**

I was small among my brothers and younger in the house of my father; I shepherded my father's sheep.

**Psalms 151:2**

My hands made an instrument, and my fingers fitted together a psaltery.

**Psalms 151:3**

And who will report to my Lord? The Lord himself, he himself will listen.

**Psalms 151:4**

He himself sent out his angel and took me from my father's sheep and anointed me with the oil of his anointing.

**Psalms 151:5**

My brothers were beautiful and great, and the Lord did not take pleasure in them.

**Psalms 151:6**

I went out to meet the foreigner, and he cursed me by his idols;

**Psalms 151:7**

but I, drawing the sword from him, beheaded him and took away reproach from sons of Israel.
